# The core loop: collisions, damage, capture, the wave end, weapons and the economy

This page holds the rules of play inside a wave: who collides with whom and what a hit does, how a ship takes damage, repairs and dies, how an icon is captured and scored, how a wave ends, how the gun and the weapon systems fire, and how fuel, credit and the ambulance work. Addresses without a module name are in INSPACE.EXE, whose DGROUP is seg19. The update loop, the movers' order, the rock mover, the flight model and the sprites are in [world.md](world.md), the pools and the object and ship records in [structs.md](structs.md), the score and rating tables in [globals.md](globals.md#scoring), `rand()` in [rng.md](rng.md), the sound ids in [sound.md](sound.md), the saved game in [formats.md](formats.md#saved-games) and the screen states in [shell.md](shell.md#screen-states). [DESIGN]

## Notation
- `/`, `>>` and `lo16` are as in [world.md](world.md#notation). Object fields are named as in [structs.md](structs.md#object-record-0x70-bytes); ship-record fields are `+0xNN` relative to DGROUP:0x7090 + 0x15a·i ([structs.md](structs.md#ship-records-8--0x15a-bytes-at-dgroup0x7090)), design words `DGROUP:0x96xx + 0xad·n` relative to the design record of `design_index` n ([structs.md](structs.md#design-records)), and roster bytes relative to the 0x7c-byte record at far DGROUP:0x6e24 ([formats.md](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)). [DESIGN]
- **Calls** list their arguments in declaration order, the rule of [pitfalls.md](pitfalls.md#reading-code) ("Calls are written in declaration order"): for a Pascal callee (`retf n`) the first argument is the first one pushed, for a cdecl callee (`retf`) the last one pushed. Where the stack slot matters it is named as [bp+n]. [DESIGN]
- Money is counted in hundredths of an icon: 150 is "1.50 ICONS". [BYTES seg14:0x3cc] [ORACLE wave-0001]
- Symbols used below:

  | Symbol | Global | Meaning | Evidence |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | player | record DGROUP:0x9646 of the ship pool DGROUP:0x9324 (14) | the player's ship | [BYTES seg13:0xb7d seg3:0x6fa] |
  | now | DGROUP:0x1e50 | the tick clock in ms ([world.md](world.md#notation)) | [BYTES seg8:0x4659] |
  | frame# | dword DGROUP:0x1e68 | the wave frame counter, 36 per second ([world.md](world.md#update-loop), step 8) | [BYTES seg11:0x1d92 seg11:0x1c24] |
  | s | DGROUP:0x9150 | 4 − `Size`; one pixel is `1 << s` world units ([world.md](world.md#notation)) | [BYTES seg8:0x5785] |
  | W, H | DGROUP:0x90ea, DGROUP:0x90ec | play width and height in pixels | [BYTES seg8:0x25b seg8:0x266] |
  | `PlayMode` | DGROUP:0x9134 | INI `PlayMode`: 0 Action, F12 toggles it ([formats.md](formats.md#inspaceini-inner-space)) | [BYTES seg11:0xadf0 seg11:0x4dba] |
  | demo | DGROUP:0x95bc = 0 | the computer-pilot demo; ≠ 0 is a player game | [BYTES seg11:0x6d19 seg16:0xabc] |
  | seg18 mode | DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0 | the separate game mode of seg18 ([structs.md](structs.md#pool-roles)); every rule below is for DGROUP:0x9130 = 0 unless it says otherwise | [BYTES seg8:0x50d7 seg8:0x530f] |
  | served ship | DGROUP:0x952e | the ship the ambulance is serving, −1 when idle | [BYTES seg16:0xbf8 seg16:0x8a0] |
  | amb_state | DGROUP:0x9e49 | the ambulance's state ([The ambulance](#the-ambulance)) | [BYTES seg16:0xbfb seg9:0x610b] |
  | W(w)+n | word DGROUP:0x2f54 + 0x50·w + n | field n of the weapon record of weapon w ([structs.md](structs.md#weapon-records-28--0x50-bytes-at-dgroup0x2f54)); n = −8 … −2 are the last words of block w − 1 | [BYTES seg10:0x22d8 seg11:0xad10] |

## Collisions

### Who tests whom (seg8:0x50aa)
- Each mover calls the pair finder seg8:0x50aa(pool·256 + rec) for its own on-screen records, after storing its pool in DGROUP:0x943a and the record in DGROUP:0x943c. [BYTES seg13:0xb75 seg13:0xb7d seg13:0xb8a]
- **Callers and pools.** The movers run in the order of [world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 21 and register these pools: [BYTES seg7:0x3151 seg7:0x339a seg8:0x864b seg8:0x8e2d seg7:0x3f2c seg12:0xb6d seg7:0x43c9 seg12:0x332 seg16:0x7e1 seg12:0x1cc1 seg12:0x3d8b seg12:0x62c8 seg12:0x5459 seg12:0x3307 seg12:0x6c91 seg13:0xb7d]

  | Mover | Pool | Sites |
  |---|---|---|
  | seg13:0x0 | 14 (DGROUP:0x9324, ships) | seg13:0xb7d |
  | seg7:0x3bae | 3 (DGROUP:0x934a, rocks) | seg7:0x3f2c |
  | seg12:0x532c | 9 | seg12:0x5459 |
  | seg12:0x633e and seg12:0x1776, seg12:0x390e | 8 | seg12:0x62c8, seg12:0x1cc1, seg12:0x3d8b |
  | seg8:0x8256 | 2 | seg8:0x864b, seg8:0x8e2d |
  | seg7:0x309c | 0 (icons) | seg7:0x3151 |
  | seg7:0x32d0 | 1 | seg7:0x339a |
  | seg7:0x40c4 | 5 (DGROUP:0x9328) | seg7:0x43c9 |
  | seg12:0x0 | 6 | seg12:0x332 |
  | seg12:0x3148 | 10 | seg12:0x3307 |
  | seg12:0x6b1e | 11 | seg12:0x6c91 |
  | seg12:0xae4 | 4 | seg12:0xb6d |
  | seg16:0xa2 (the ambulance) | 7 | seg16:0x7e1 |

  - seg18 registers pools 15 and 20. [BYTES seg18:0x3ce0 seg18:0x4945]
- **Pool order.** In the visibility-list loop seg8:0x50aa skips candidates whose pool is below the caller's, and in the caller's own pool skips the caller itself. A pair of pools p < q is therefore found only by pool p's mover. [BYTES seg8:0x52ae seg8:0x52b3 seg8:0x52b6 seg8:0x52b8 seg8:0x52bf seg8:0x52c2]
  - The ship mover (pool 14) finds only other ships in a normal wave, since no normal-wave mover registers a pool above 14. Ship against rock is found by the rock mover, ship against icon by the icon mover, ship against shots by the pool-5, 6 and 10 movers, shot against rock by the rock mover. [BYTES seg13:0xb79 seg13:0xb8a seg8:0x52b6]
- **One collision per object per frame.** seg8:0x50aa returns 0xFFFF at once when the caller's `collided` (+0x1c) ≠ 0, and the visibility loop skips candidates whose `collided` ≠ 0. A hit sets `collided` = 1 on both parties, and the [visibility pass](world.md#visibility-pass-seg80x4e14) clears it before the movers, so the first mover to find a pair uses up both objects for that frame. [BYTES seg8:0x50d7 seg8:0x50de seg8:0x52e3 seg8:0x5305 seg8:0x545c seg8:0x6cda seg8:0x6cfa]
- **Candidates.** The normal path walks the visibility list DGROUP:0x507c (pool) / DGROUP:0x50e0 (record), indices 0 … DGROUP:0x9146 − 1, and returns the first hit as pool·256 + rec, else 0xFFFF. [BYTES seg8:0x5224 seg8:0x52a0 seg8:0x545c]
- **Race-track path.** In a kind-6 wave (DGROUP:0x9320 = 6), a caller whose `on_screen` is 0 with `Detail` DGROUP:0x9208 > 1 re-projects its own world position into `scr_x`/`scr_y` through seg8:0x29ea and builds the same squares. It then tests every ship 0 … DGROUP:0x9316, skipping those with `on_screen` ≠ 0 or `state` ≠ 0 and itself when the caller is a ship, and returns 0x0e00 | i on a hit. This path has no pool-order rule and no `collided` test. Ships never take it, because the ship mover calls only for on-screen ships. [BYTES seg8:0x50e1 seg8:0x50eb seg8:0x50f2 seg8:0x511c seg8:0x5165 seg8:0x51a7 seg8:0x51be seg8:0x51c5 seg8:0x51cc seg8:0x520b seg8:0x5218]
- **seg18 path.** In seg18 mode seg8:0x530f–0x545a sets DGROUP:0x9446 = 20000 and can return 0x1000 ([world.md](world.md#collisions)). [BYTES seg8:0x530f seg8:0x545a]

### Pair test (seg8:0x6afa)
- **Squares.** In screen pixels, the caller C has square A (DGROUP:0x5160) = (`scr_x`, `scr_y`, `scr_x` + `size`, `scr_y` + `size`). [BYTES seg8:0x5224 seg8:0x5253]
- **Search box.** Box B (DGROUP:0x5148) = (`scr_x` − h, `scr_y` − h, `scr_x` + `size` + q, `scr_y` + `size` + q) with h = (70 << `Size`) >> 1 and q = (15 << `Size`) >> 1, `Size` = DGROUP:0x9156. [BYTES seg8:0x525c seg8:0x5260 seg8:0x5263 seg8:0x5265 seg8:0x5283 seg8:0x5288 seg8:0x5294]
- seg8:0x6afa(K, C) (Pascal, `retf 4`) returns 0, no hit, when both have `fixed` (+0x54) ≠ 0. [BYTES seg8:0x6b3b seg8:0x6b4d seg8:0x6b53]
- It then needs the candidate K's `state` = 0, K's top-left point in box B (PtInRect) and a non-empty IntersectRect of A with K's square. [BYTES seg8:0x6b65 seg8:0x6b7d seg8:0x6ba6 seg8:0x6bb7]
- **Exclusions,** each in both roles:
  - a pool-10 object and its owner (`owner_pool`/`owner_rec`) never collide; [BYTES seg8:0x6bc0 seg8:0x6bcf seg8:0x6bd8 seg8:0x6be1 seg8:0x6bfa seg8:0x6c03]
  - an object with byte +0x2e ≠ 0 does not collide unless it is a ship whose ship word +0x2e (DGROUP:0x70be) = 2; [BYTES seg8:0x6c1a seg8:0x6c24 seg8:0x6c31 seg8:0x6c49 seg8:0x6c5d]
  - a pool-6 missile does not collide with its launcher (DGROUP:0x66e8/0x66ea + 0x1c·rec) while its age DGROUP:0x66ec + 0x1c·rec < 10. [BYTES seg8:0x6c67 seg8:0x6c71 seg8:0x6c7b seg8:0x6c84 seg8:0x6c97]
- **On a hit** a party in pool 7 with DGROUP:0x9e49 = 1 sets DGROUP:0x9e49 = 0, and both parties get `collided` = 1. [BYTES seg8:0x6cb3 seg8:0x6cbf seg8:0x6cc6 seg8:0x6cda seg8:0x6cfa]
- **Roles A and B.** The orientation flag [bp−0xc] is written by four tests in this order, the last write winning: K is the player → 1, C is the player → 0, K `fixed` (+0x54) ≠ 0 → 1, C `fixed` (+0x54) ≠ 0 → 0. Nothing initialises it. [BYTES seg8:0x6b04 seg8:0x6d0e seg8:0x6d23 seg8:0x6d3d seg8:0x6d57 seg8:0x6d5c]
  - Flag 1 makes K party A: pool DGROUP:0x944a, record DGROUP:0x944e, velocity DGROUP:0x52e0/0x52e2, screen position DGROUP:0x52e4/0x52e6, mass DGROUP:0x9446. Flag 0 makes K party B: DGROUP:0x944c, DGROUP:0x9450, DGROUP:0x52dc/0x52de, DGROUP:0x52e8/0x52ea, DGROUP:0x9448. Only K's velocity, position and mass are stored here. [BYTES seg8:0x6d79 seg8:0x6db9 seg8:0x6dd0 seg8:0x6e44 seg8:0x6e5e seg8:0x6e9e seg8:0x6ea8 seg8:0x6f29]
  - A `fixed` object (a gate post) is therefore always party A, and otherwise the player's ship is A. [BYTES seg8:0x6d3d seg8:0x6d57]
  - Both extents (A: DGROUP:0x9442, B: DGROUP:0x9444) are `extent` + 2, or `size` in seg18 mode. [BYTES seg8:0x6ddb seg8:0x6dee seg8:0x6df2 seg8:0x6e18 seg8:0x6ec0 seg8:0x6f11]
  - **The argument order.** seg8:0x50aa pushes the candidate K before its own argument C, so in seg8:0x6afa [bp+8] is K and [bp+6] is C, and the four tests above read them so. The race-track call seg8:0x5218 uses the same order. [BYTES seg8:0x52f0 seg8:0x52f3 seg8:0x52f4 seg8:0x52f8 seg8:0x6b09 seg8:0x6b21 seg8:0x5218]
  - **Where the flag lies.** On every call from one seg8:0x50aa search the flag is the word at seg8:0x50aa's bp − 0x30, below that function's stack pointer. Only the four stores and the test at seg8:0x6d5c touch it. The stores follow the setting of both `collided` words, and the search returns on the first hit, so no call of a search reads a value that an earlier call of the same search wrote; a value from an earlier search survives unless the loop's other callees reach that depth, as seg8:0x29ea's frame does. [BYTES seg8:0x50b4 seg8:0x50b7 seg8:0x50b8 seg8:0x52f3 seg8:0x52f7 seg8:0x6afd seg8:0x6b04 seg8:0x6cda seg8:0x6cfa seg8:0x6d0e seg8:0x52fb seg8:0x5302]
  - **The decided pairs.** The rock mover's search (C a rock, K the player) writes only "K is the player", so the player is A and the rock B. The gate post's search (C the post, `fixed`, K the player) ends on "C `fixed`" = 0, so the post is A and the player B. A pair with neither the player nor a `fixed` object reads the stale word; [ADR 0015](../decisions/0015-collision-orientation.md) sets the port's rule for it. [BYTES seg8:0x6d0e seg8:0x6d36 seg8:0x6d57 seg8:0x6d5c seg8:0x6dc0 seg8:0x6dd7 seg8:0x6ea5 seg8:0x6ebc] [DESIGN]

### Response (seg8:0x546c)
- **Call.** seg8:0x546c (Pascal, `retf 0xe`) takes [bp+6] the caller's `mass`, [bp+8] its `extent` + 2 (never read), [bp+0xa] a far pointer to its `vy`, [bp+0xe] one to its `vx`, and [bp+0x12] the hit object from seg8:0x50aa. It returns the damage to the caller, or −1. [BYTES seg8:0x5aab seg8:0x57f5 seg8:0x57fe seg13:0xbaf seg13:0xbb8 seg13:0xbc8]
1. **Shot bookkeeping.** When party A is a pool-5 (DGROUP:0x9328) object, it returns −1 if A's last-hit entry DGROUP:0x5a54/0x5a56 + 0x14·rec already names B; otherwise it plays B's hit sound (B's `hit_sound` through seg10:0x754a, or for a ship B the design word DGROUP:0x9700 + 0xad·n through seg10:0x7944) and stores B there. The same holds with A and B swapped. [BYTES seg8:0x549a seg8:0x54b4 seg8:0x5520 seg8:0x5530 seg8:0x5580 seg8:0x559c seg8:0x55ac seg8:0x55b3]
2. **Ambulance pass-through.** With DGROUP:0x9e49 in 3 … 6, a pool-7 party against the ship whose record is DGROUP:0x952e returns −1, in both orientations. [BYTES seg8:0x55b7 seg8:0x55be seg8:0x55c5 seg8:0x55d5 seg8:0x55e1 seg8:0x55f1]
3. **Branches.** When the hit object is party A (seg8:0x5611 path) the caller is B: B's mass is the caller's mass, A's mass is multiplied by 10 when A is pool 0 and B pool 2, and A's mass becomes 20000 when A's `fixed` (+0x54) ≠ 0. When the caller is A (seg8:0x5870 path), A's mass is the caller's mass and becomes 20000 for a `fixed` party; this branch has no pool-0/pool-2 rule. [BYTES seg8:0x5611 seg8:0x56a8 seg8:0x56af seg8:0x56b9 seg8:0x56bd seg8:0x56d0 seg8:0x56d7 seg8:0x5870 seg8:0x58ef seg8:0x5905]
4. **Capture pass-through.** It returns −1, no bounce and no damage, when one party is a ship with bytes +0xea = 0 and +0xe8 = 0 and design word DGROUP:0x9714 (Captures Icons) ≠ 0, and the other has `capturable` (+0x53) ≠ 0 and signed byte +0x2f ≥ 0; both orientations, both branches. [BYTES seg8:0x56dd seg8:0x56ec seg8:0x56f3 seg8:0x5700 seg8:0x5719 seg8:0x5720 seg8:0x5727 seg8:0x572a seg8:0x5774 seg8:0x5912 seg8:0x595c seg8:0x595f seg8:0x59a9]
5. **Units.** Both extents become (extent >> s) + 1. [BYTES seg8:0x5777 seg8:0x5785 seg8:0x5787 seg8:0x578e seg8:0x5790 seg8:0x59b3 seg8:0x59c6]
6. **Physics.** It calls [the bounce](#bounce-ispacedll-ordinal-29) through seg10:0x7dd4, except that with a pool-5 party it takes the result as 1 without calling it. [BYTES seg8:0x5794 seg8:0x579b seg8:0x57a1 seg8:0x57db seg8:0x59c9 seg8:0x59d6 seg8:0x547b]
7. **Result 0 (separating).** It calls seg8:0x698a(0) or seg8:0x6a44(0): the hit object takes its slot velocity, as the bounce left it, and `collided` = 1 and is not dispatched; the caller's velocity is not written, and the function returns −1. The callers pass that −1 to the hit dispatcher seg8:0x5df2, which tests for −1 at entry. [BYTES seg8:0x57e3 seg8:0x57e9 seg8:0x5a18 seg8:0x5a1e seg8:0x5e46 seg8:0x5e93]
8. **Result 1.** First the caller's `vx`/`vy` take its own party's slot through the pointers, the B slot DGROUP:0x52dc/0x52de when the hit object is A and the A slot DGROUP:0x52e0/0x52e2 when it is B, also for a `fixed` caller. [BYTES seg8:0x57f2 seg8:0x57f8 seg8:0x5801 seg8:0x5a27 seg8:0x5a2d seg8:0x5a36] Then both branches write B's world `x`/`y` from seg8:0x2bbe of the screen point (DGROUP:0x52e8, DGROUP:0x52ea) unless B's `fixed` (+0x54) ≠ 0; B is the caller in the first branch and the hit object in the second. The hit party's damage gains 5 × DGROUP:0x9454 when it is A (into DGROUP:0x9438) or 4 × DGROUP:0x9454 when it is B (into DGROUP:0x9436). Then seg8:0x698a(1) or seg8:0x6a44(1) writes the hit party's `collided` = 1 and its velocity from its slot (0 for a `fixed` party) and calls seg8:0x5df2(hit party, other party, its damage). The function returns DGROUP:0x9436 when the caller is B and DGROUP:0x9438 when it is A. [BYTES seg8:0x5824 seg8:0x5837 seg8:0x5853 seg8:0x5a59 seg8:0x5a6c seg8:0x5a88 seg8:0x585b seg8:0x5860 seg8:0x5a90 seg8:0x5a96 seg8:0x5867 seg8:0x586a seg8:0x5a9d seg8:0x5aa0 seg8:0x698a seg8:0x69dc seg8:0x69ff seg8:0x6a1f seg8:0x6a44 seg8:0x6a96 seg8:0x6ab5 seg8:0x6ad5]
- **The two writers.** seg8:0x698a(flag) and seg8:0x6a44(flag) (Pascal, `retf 2`) work on party A and party B. Each sets the party's `collided` = 1 and copies its slot velocity (A: DGROUP:0x52e0/0x52e2, B: DGROUP:0x52dc/0x52de) into `vx`/`vy` unchanged. With flag ≠ 0 a `fixed` party's `vy` and `vx` are then zeroed, and the party is dispatched: seg8:0x5df2(A, B, DGROUP:0x9438) or seg8:0x5df2(B, A, DGROUP:0x9436), the return discarded. With flag 0 a `fixed` party keeps the slot velocity. [BYTES seg8:0x69a8 seg8:0x69c1 seg8:0x69d8 seg8:0x69dc seg8:0x69ff seg8:0x6a1b seg8:0x6a1f seg8:0x6a34 seg8:0x6a62 seg8:0x6a7b seg8:0x6a92 seg8:0x6a96 seg8:0x6aae seg8:0x6ad5 seg8:0x6aea]
- So on result 1 the hit object is dispatched inside seg8:0x546c, before the caller's own dispatch at its [site](#the-three-sites). [BYTES seg8:0x5867 seg8:0x5a9d seg7:0x4060 seg12:0x1dbe]
- **DGROUP:0x9454** is the moving rock's own signed heat byte +0x2f: the rock mover sets it before seg8:0x546c, after its pool-5 heating step, and clears it after. Every other caller leaves it 0. [BYTES seg7:0x400c seg7:0x4011 seg7:0x4049 seg7:0x4068]

- **Pool-5 parties keep stale damage words.** A pool-DGROUP:0x9328 party skips the bounce (step 6), and nothing in seg8:0x546c or seg8:0x6afa writes DGROUP:0x9436/0x9438 except the two heat additions. Their other writers are the bounce calls of seg8:0x39e5, seg8:0x7417 and seg18:0x2ddb, the halving at seg8:0x7abb and the floor of 3 at seg18:0x2dee. So a SNOWBALL or FIREBALL collision deals the damage that the last bounce or shot impact left, and the port keeps both words as persistent globals. [BYTES seg8:0x5794 seg8:0x579b seg8:0x57a1 seg8:0x547b seg8:0x5860 seg8:0x5a96 seg8:0x39e5 seg8:0x7417 seg8:0x7abb seg18:0x2ddb seg18:0x2dee] An oracle check of a FIREBALL hit's damage against the previous bounce is [OPEN].

### Bounce (ISPACE.DLL ordinal 29)
- seg10:0x7dd4 forwards its 12 dwords unchanged through the far pointer DGROUP:0x3c6c, which holds ISPACE.DLL ordinal 29, and pops 0x30 bytes. [BYTES seg10:0x7dde seg10:0x7e0a seg10:0x7e0e seg10:0x7e18]
- Its parameters (A's and B's damage words DGROUP:0x9438/0x9436, masses, extents, positions DGROUP:0x52e4 … 0x52ea and velocities DGROUP:0x52dc … 0x52e2), the slow-contact step, the moving-apart test that returns 0 and the elastic exchange that returns 1 and writes both damages are in [ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes) (29), as an integer sequence a port follows step by step. It draws no `rand()`. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg2:0x3c] [BYTES seg8:0x57a3 seg8:0x57db]
- core-0001 watched only the systems words, so no oracle run has yet compared a bounce's velocities and positions with that sequence; a watch of DGROUP:0x52dc … 0x52ea and DGROUP:0x9436/0x9438 around seg8:0x57db would. [ORACLE core-0001] [OPEN]

### Ship against ship (seg13:0x0)
- Per ship i, after its move, flight and draw ([world.md](world.md#ships-seg130x0)), a ship whose `on_screen` ≠ 0 calls seg8:0x50aa(14·256 + i) and, on a hit, amount = seg8:0x546c(hit, &`vx`, &`vy`, `extent` + 2, `mass`). A ship whose object word +0x1a is 0 jumps past the whole block, the weapon calls below included. [BYTES seg13:0xb69 seg13:0xb6e seg13:0xb75 seg13:0xb7d seg13:0xb8a seg13:0xbcc]
- The response runs on every hit before the branches below, so a touch still gets the bounce and the dispatch of the hit object inside seg8:0x546c ([Response](#response-seg80x546c), step 8); a touch skips only the final dispatcher call. [BYTES seg13:0xbcc seg13:0xbee]
- A hit object with `capturable` (+0x53) ≠ 0, while the ship's byte +0xea = 0, goes to the [touch routine](#the-touch-routine-seg130x16ac) seg13:0x16ac(i, hit) instead of the dispatcher. [BYTES seg13:0xbee seg13:0xbfa seg13:0xc08 seg13:0xc0e]
- A pool-6 hit object whose weapon kind (W(w)+0x24, DGROUP:0x2f78 via DGROUP:0x66e4) is 6 while the ship's selected weapon's kind is also 6 gets `capturable` = 1 and goes to seg13:0x16ac. After the call the ship's count byte +0xae + `weapon_slot` (DGROUP:0x713e) rises by 1 with no test, whatever the touch did. [BYTES seg13:0xc11 seg13:0xc18 seg13:0xc5b seg13:0xc69 seg13:0xc6c seg13:0xc73 seg13:0xc77]
- Otherwise the damage goes to seg8:0x5df2(DGROUP:0x9324, i, hit pool, hit rec, amount), and the mover drops its return. [BYTES seg13:0xc79 seg13:0xc89 seg13:0xc8d seg13:0xc92 seg13:0xc95]
- Both branches above see only ships in a normal wave ([pool order](#who-tests-whom-seg80x50aa)); the pool-6 missile mover reaches seg13:0x16ac itself ([Pool-6 mover](#pool-6-mover-seg120x0)). [BYTES seg8:0x52b6 seg12:0x320] A ship's object byte +0x53 stays 0, so the first branch never runs for a ship in a normal wave: none of the byte stores to an object's +0x53 goes through the pool-14 pointer DGROUP:0x1e30, the indexed ones go through DGROUP:0x9328, DGROUP:0x934a and, at seg13:0xc5b, a hit pool that seg13:0xc18 restricts to 6, no word or dword store covers the byte, and the pool block is zero-filled ([structs.md](structs.md#allocation)). [BYTES seg12:0x2eee-0x2ef9 seg7:0x53a4-0x53af seg7:0x581f-0x582a seg18:0x680f-0x681a seg13:0xc11-0xc5b]
- **Weapons 24 … 26.** Every path, a miss, either touch or the dispatcher, reaches seg13:0xc95, which reads the selected weapon w = byte +0xb8 + `weapon_slot` (DGROUP:0x7148). With w = 24 or 26 it calls seg18:0x551a(DGROUP:0x9324, i). With w = 25 or 26 it calls seg18:0x59ec(DGROUP:0x9324, i) on frames where byte DGROUP:0x1e68 & 7 = 0 while GetAsyncKeyState(VK_RETURN) or GetAsyncKeyState('Z') has bit 0x8000; that test does not check that i is the player. So these weapons act per on-screen ship in the ship mover, not through seg11:0xac86. What seg18:0x551a and seg18:0x59ec do is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg13:0xc0e seg13:0xc77 seg13:0xc95 seg13:0xc9e seg13:0xca6 seg13:0xcab seg13:0xcb7 seg13:0xcbf seg13:0xcc5 seg13:0xccb seg13:0xcd4 seg13:0xce0 seg13:0xcf1]

### Hit dispatcher (seg8:0x5df2)
seg8:0x5df2(T pool, T rec, X pool, X rec, a) (cdecl, `retf`) applies amount a from the attacker X to the target T and calls T's `on_damage`. Its locals: [bp−6] the dispatch flag (1 at entry), [bp−8] the 2000-range flag, [bp−0xa] the negated flag, [bp−0x10]/[bp−0x12] the credited pool and record, [bp−4] the result. [BYTES seg8:0x5df2 seg8:0x5e00 seg8:0x697f]
- **Entry.** X's mass is 20000 when X pool = 0x1f, else X's `mass`. An amount below −1 is negated with [bp−0xa] = 1; −1 itself is kept. [BYTES seg8:0x5e1f seg8:0x5e25 seg8:0x5e3a seg8:0x5e3e seg8:0x5e46 seg8:0x5e4a seg8:0x5e4c seg8:0x5e51]
- **Pools above 14.** With X pool > DGROUP:0x9324 (signed) it stores X pool as the credited pool and goes straight to the handler call with the dispatch flag 1, skipping every rule below: the −1 test, the credit rules, the pool-2 negation and the pool-10 rule. The credited record is never written on that path, so seg8:0x7be8, seg8:0x99ce and the rest receive an uninitialised stack word. Only the seg18 pools take it. [BYTES seg8:0x5e54 seg8:0x5e5c seg8:0x642c seg8:0x642f seg8:0x6432]
- **Amount rules** for X pool ≤ 14, in order:
  1. 2000 < a < 3000: a −= 2000 and [bp−8] = 1; when X is a ship whose design word DGROUP:0x970a + 0xad·n is 7, a = 1. An amount outside that range, a plain 2000 or 2000 + A/2 for A ≤ −2 as the [lightning strike](#lightning-seg110x9fdc-seg110xa234-seg110xa9ea) can send, keeps its raw value and sets no flag. [BYTES seg8:0x5e5f seg8:0x5e66 seg8:0x5e6d seg8:0x5e72 seg8:0x5e87 seg8:0x5e8e]
  2. a = −1 returns 0 at once, with no sound, no handler and no seg8:0x99ce. This is the −1 of the [response](#response-seg80x546c)'s pass-through and separating paths. [BYTES seg8:0x5e93 seg8:0x5e97 seg8:0x5e99 seg8:0x5e9b]
  3. **OverThruster ram.** When the player's ship word +0x1c (DGROUP:0x70ac) > 2 (signed), X is the player, T pool is DGROUP:0x934a, 8 or 9 and `PlayMode` DGROUP:0x9134 < 2, it calls seg3:0x6ec(player, 7, 0, 0xd) and then seg13:0xb6f2(player, 9). The rule sits on the rock's, post's or virus's own dispatch, with the player as X. [BYTES seg8:0x5ea4 seg8:0x5eab seg8:0x5eb3 seg8:0x5eb8 seg8:0x5ec0 seg8:0x5ec6 seg8:0x5ecc seg8:0x5edd seg8:0x5eeb]
     - Message 0xd (switch case 12) plays sound 0x67 through seg10:0x744c only while the dword DGROUP:0x1cb4 is below now (unsigned), and then sets DGROUP:0x1cb4 = now + 4000; it always formats string 350 "Be More Careful, %s. Avoid Dangerous Hazards." with the pilot name DGROUP:0x380c. [BYTES seg3:0xcfd seg3:0xd05 seg3:0xd0a seg3:0xd0c seg3:0xd12 seg3:0xd19 seg3:0xd1b seg3:0xd22 seg3:0xd27] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 22] id 350
  4. **Credit.** The credit starts at −1, becomes X's (`owner_pool`, `owner_rec`) when X's `owner_pool` is 14, then T's when T's is 14. [BYTES seg8:0x5ef3 seg8:0x5f18 seg8:0x5f3b]
  5. **Tethered target.** When T's `owner_pool` ≠ −1, the word DGROUP:0x65da + 0x20·s toggles between 0 and 1 when X's mass + 100 > T's mass (signed), s being T's signed byte +0x30. Then one `rand()` (seg5:0xdc) is always drawn, and seg11:0x947a(DGROUP:0x65d0 + 0x20·s, DGROUP:0x65d2 + 0x20·s, 2) runs when `rand()` % 12 + 30 < a, a ≠ 100 and T is a ship. s is T's signed byte +0x30, the slot of the tether holding T (the zero of a never-written byte for a pool-10 hook record dispatched as T, so slot 0 whichever slot the hook belongs to), and the toggled word is the slot's swing direction +0x0e, which reverses a hooked object's swing. The call passes w = 2 even for HANDCUFFS: the holder lets go (the release, which tumbles a held ship for 800 ms) only if it is on screen, holds a 2 or 15 slot and has `fuel` ≥ 25, and it pays 25 ([The tether weapons](#the-tether-weapons-seg110x947a)). [BYTES seg8:0x5f4e seg8:0x5f58 seg8:0x5f5b seg8:0x5f63 seg8:0x5f6d seg8:0x5f74 seg8:0x5f94 seg8:0x5f9a seg8:0x5f9f seg8:0x5fa5 seg8:0x5fad seg8:0x5fb6 seg8:0x5fd5 seg8:0x5fd9 seg8:0x5fdd]
  6. **X pool DGROUP:0x9328 (5).** When X's `mass` is 120 (FIREBALL), a = max(2a, 20), signed. The credit becomes the pool-5 object's shooter, (DGROUP:0x5a50, DGROUP:0x5a52) + 0x14·X rec. [BYTES seg8:0x5fe8 seg8:0x5ffa seg8:0x6004 seg8:0x6006 seg8:0x600b seg8:0x6015 seg8:0x601c]
  7. **X pool 6.** The credit becomes the launcher (DGROUP:0x66e8, DGROUP:0x66ea) + 0x1c·X rec. When the missile's weapon kind (DGROUP:0x2f78 + 0x50·DGROUP:0x66e4[0x1c·rec]) is 6, T is a ship and T's selected weapon has kind 6, the dispatch flag is cleared. [BYTES seg8:0x6023 seg8:0x602d seg8:0x6034 seg8:0x6040 seg8:0x6047 seg8:0x604a seg8:0x6060 seg8:0x6067]
  8. X pool 10, or X's `owner_pool` ≠ −1: the credit becomes X's (`owner_pool`, `owner_rec`), overriding rules 4 … 7. A credit still −1 then becomes X itself. [BYTES seg8:0x606c seg8:0x6080 seg8:0x6097 seg8:0x609e seg8:0x60a5 seg8:0x60ab seg8:0x60b1]
- **Credit bookkeeping** (seg8:0x60b7 … 0x6303) runs after the credit rules only when the credited pool cp ([bp−0x10]) is 9, 2 or DGROUP:0x9324 (the ship pool, 14 in a wave); cr is the credited record. [BYTES seg8:0x60b7 seg8:0x60bd seg8:0x60c3 seg8:0x60c9 seg8:0x60cb]
  1. **The goal stamp.** With cp = DGROUP:0x9324 the credited ship cr gets dword +0xa4 (DGROUP:0x7134) = now when its word +0x38 (DGROUP:0x70c8) is 1 and its goal (+0x32, +0x34) is T. Then every other ship j ≤ DGROUP:0x9316, j ≠ cr, with `alive` (DGROUP:0x717c) ≠ 0, goal = T and +0x38 = 1 gets seg13:0x2714(j, cr, 5, 10, 0, −1), whether or not cr's stamp was written. [BYTES seg8:0x60ce seg8:0x60d9 seg8:0x60de seg8:0x60e8 seg8:0x60f1 seg8:0x60fe seg8:0x6102 seg8:0x6106 seg8:0x6146 seg8:0x6157]
  2. **Defenders.** When T is a ship (pool DGROUP:0x9324) or pool 0, DGROUP:0xb0c ≠ 0, and cp is DGROUP:0x9324, or is 2 with the defender owner (DGROUP:0x5304, DGROUP:0x5306) + 0x24·cr ≠ T, each defender entry k < DGROUP:0x940a whose owner is T gets seg8:0x9028(k, 10, cp, cr); cp 9 skips the block. [BYTES seg8:0x6159 seg8:0x616a seg8:0x6174 seg8:0x6189 seg8:0x6198 seg8:0x619b seg8:0x61a9 seg8:0x61c3 seg8:0x61d4] Inside the same block:
     - with cp = DGROUP:0x9324 and T a ship, seg4:0x5e8e(T rec, cr, a, 1) runs unless X is a ship with [bp−8] = 0; when cr is the player, T's +0x38 is 4 and T's `goal_rec` is the player (the goal pool untested), DGROUP:0x9432 += a; then seg4:0x7d36(cr, T rec, a); [BYTES seg8:0x61d6 seg8:0x61e3 seg8:0x61e8 seg8:0x61f9 seg8:0x6201 seg8:0x620e seg8:0x6218 seg8:0x6221 seg8:0x622e]
     - when T is an icon (pool 0), the credit is the player (cp = DGROUP:0x9324, cr = DGROUP:0x9646) and X pool ≠ 10, it calls seg8:0x81f8(T pool, T rec), which shifts the eight-entry hit history (words DGROUP:0x937a, bytes DGROUP:0x938a and DGROUP:0x9392) down one place and stores seg5:0x2b4(frame#, 10), T pool and T rec in entry 0 ([The vanish](#the-vanish-seg70x13b2-and-seg70x158e)). [BYTES seg8:0x6159 seg8:0x6161 seg8:0x616a seg8:0x6236 seg8:0x6242 seg8:0x6247 seg8:0x624d seg8:0x6259 seg8:0x8205 seg8:0x823b seg8:0x8240 seg8:0x8246 seg8:0x824c]
  3. **The player's hit.** With the credit (DGROUP:0x9324, the player) and T not the player, DGROUP:0x943e/0x9440 = (T pool, T rec) and the dword DGROUP:0x52d8 = now. [BYTES seg8:0x625f seg8:0x626e seg8:0x6276 seg8:0x6283 seg8:0x6286 seg8:0x628c seg8:0x6298 seg8:0x629c]

### The three sites
- **Rock mover** (seg7:0x3efb–0x4068). After the pair search and the pool-5 heating ([world.md](world.md#rocks-seg70x3bae)) it sets DGROUP:0x9454 = the rock's signed heat +0x2f, calls amount = seg8:0x546c(hit, &`vx`, &`vy`, `extent` + 2, `mass`), then seg8:0x5df2(DGROUP:0x934a, rock, hit pool, hit rec, amount), drops the return and clears DGROUP:0x9454. [BYTES seg7:0x4011 seg7:0x4049 seg7:0x404e seg7:0x4060 seg7:0x4065 seg7:0x4068]
  - So a rock against the player: the player (A) is dispatched first inside seg8:0x546c with DGROUP:0x9438 + 5 × heat, then the rock with the returned DGROUP:0x9436. An amount of −1 makes the rock's dispatch return at once. [BYTES seg8:0x5860 seg8:0x5867 seg8:0x5aa0 seg8:0x5e93]
- **Gate post** (seg12:0x1d79–0x1dc3, in seg12:0x1776). The same seg8:0x546c call on the pool-8 record, then seg8:0x5df2(8, post, hit pool, hit rec, amount), the return dropped. It does not write DGROUP:0x9454, whose only writers are the rock mover's two stores, so the heat bonus is 0. [BYTES seg12:0x1da9 seg12:0x1dae seg12:0x1dbc seg12:0x1dbe seg12:0x1dc3 seg7:0x4011 seg7:0x4068]
  - Against the player the post is A ([Pair test](#pair-test-seg80x6afa)): the player is dispatched first with DGROUP:0x9436 and moved by seg8:0x2bbe, then the post with DGROUP:0x9438. [BYTES seg8:0x6d57 seg8:0x5a96 seg8:0x5a9d]
  - The post template sets `on_damage` to seg7:0x5090, `mass` 3500 and `hit_sound` 8, pool 8 through DGROUP:0x1e18. [BYTES seg9:0x11e7 seg9:0x120e seg9:0x1232 seg9:0x1238]
- **Ship mover** (seg13:0xb5a–0xc92): [Ship against ship](#ship-against-ship-seg130x0). [BYTES seg13:0xb69 seg13:0xc8d]

## Damage

### The ship's systems
- The `systems` words +0x26, +0x28, +0x2a and +0x2c are the health 0 … 100 of lamps 0 … 3; the lamp rows are design words DGROUP:0x96f4 + 2k ([world.md](world.md#damage-panel)). [BYTES seg11:0x1df7 seg11:0x632]
- The systems are named ENGINE, GUN, THRUST, WINGS and CLOAK. [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 53] ids 840–844 The damage display shows Engine, Guns and Thruster, and the wings or the cloak as the fourth system. [MANUAL General] The TRANSFORMER's panel reads Engine, Gun, Thrust and Shield. [ORACLE wave-0001]
- **Cloak.** Byte +0xe8 is the cloak-on flag, toggled by seg11:0x4c72 ([The cloak](#the-cloak-seg110x4c72)), and `has_cloak` (+0xe7), which picks lamp row 4 CLOAK, is the ship's cloak. [BYTES seg11:0x4c72 seg11:0x4d08 seg11:0x4da0 seg11:0x3d0e]
- **Shield.** Byte +0xe9 picks lamp row 5 SHIELD and the shop's SHIELD repair, so it is the ship's shield, and byte +0xea is the shield-on flag, toggled by seg11:0x4b00 ([The shield](#the-shield-seg110x4b00)). [BYTES seg11:0x3db2 seg16:0x4bd8 seg11:0x4ba1 seg11:0x4bdf]

### Damage to a ship (seg8:0x45e0)
seg8:0x45e0 is the ship pool's `on_damage` (cdecl: pool, ship, d). In order: [BYTES seg8:0x45e0 seg8:0x4d5d]
1. **No damage** for a ship with byte +0x147 = 1 outside seg18 mode, and for the player while DGROUP:0x104c ≠ 0. [BYTES seg8:0x45f4 seg8:0x4607 seg8:0x4614]
2. **Dead ship.** A ship with `alive` (+0xec) = 0 hit by anything but itself (DGROUP:0x943a/0x943c) and not by pool 11 explodes at once through seg12:0x274a when dword +0x84 − 5500 < now; in seg18 mode it also sets DGROUP:0x9530 = 6 and calls seg18:0x633c. It returns 0. [BYTES seg8:0x461b seg8:0x4622 seg8:0x4638 seg8:0x463f seg8:0x4659 seg8:0x4662]
3. **Scale.** d = d·7/8; for the player d = d·(DGROUP:0x9000 + 6)/8 and the rating loss of [globals.md](globals.md#scoring). [BYTES seg8:0x468a seg8:0x469d seg8:0x46a5]
   - In a Demon's Gate wave (DGROUP:0x92e8 ≠ 0) a hit on ship 1 sets DGROUP:0x8e30 = now + 20000. [BYTES seg8:0x4776 seg8:0x4790]
4. **Attacker.** A gun shot (DGROUP:0x943a = 0x1e, the [shot slot](#shot-slots) DGROUP:0x943c) owned by a ship calls seg4:0x5e8e; when that owner is the player and `PlayMode` = 0 it records DGROUP:0x943e/0x9440 and DGROUP:0x52d8. [BYTES seg8:0x47c3 seg8:0x47e4 seg8:0x4817 seg8:0x4827]
   - Any other attacker scales d by 3/4, and halves it again in a kind-6 wave when the attacker is a ship. [BYTES seg8:0x4865 seg8:0x486f seg8:0x4875 seg8:0x4887]
   - The shot rules below are keyed on the local [bp−0xc] = 2, which a shot sets and which a non-shot attacker whose word +0x22 is 2 also produces. [BYTES seg8:0x47c3 seg8:0x485e seg8:0x49e4]
5. **Last hit.** Ship dword +0x14a = the ship's world `x`/`y` and dword +0x14e = now. [BYTES seg8:0x48aa seg8:0x48b9]
6. **Side.** Object word +0x26 = +1 when the 32-bit cross product of the relative position and velocity, [bp−0x12]·[bp−0x10] − [bp−0x14]·[bp−0xe], is above 0, else −1. [BYTES seg8:0x4931 seg8:0x4948]
7. **Sound.** With DGROUP:0xb0a ≠ 0 it plays bounce sound 7 here; DGROUP:0xb0a stays 0 ([globals.md](globals.md#global-table)), so this never runs. [BYTES seg8:0x494e seg8:0x4960 seg8:0x4d43]
8. **Mode factor.** With byte +0xea ≠ 0, d = 4d/3, at least 1; else with `cloaked` (+0xe8) ≠ 0, d = 3d, at least 2; else d = 2d, at least 1. It stores d in `damage`. [BYTES seg8:0x496d seg8:0x497a seg8:0x497e seg8:0x4987 seg8:0x498e seg8:0x49a9 seg8:0x49b8]
9. With DGROUP:0xb0a = 0 it plays the design's hit sound, design word +0x2e (DGROUP:0x9700 + 0xad·n), through seg10:0x7944; the object's `hit_sound` +0x42 is not read here ([structs.md](structs.md#object-record-0x70-bytes), Callbacks of the ship pool). [BYTES seg8:0x49bc seg8:0x49d8 seg8:0x49dc]
10. **Systems.** Each `systems` word +0x26 + 2k (k = 0 … 3) loses max(1, d / divisor), the divisor being design word DGROUP:0x96e2 + 2k (+0x10 + 2k), or DGROUP:0x96da + 2k (+0x08 + 2k) for a shot. With a roster record (`roster_index` ≠ −1) and p = roster byte +0x34 + k, the quotient is first scaled by (20 − 3p)/20, or (10 − p)/10 for a shot. All four words are then clamped at ≥ 0. [BYTES seg8:0x4a1e seg8:0x4a2a seg8:0x4a31 seg8:0x4a55 seg8:0x4a6f seg8:0x4a99 seg8:0x4aa5 seg8:0x4aae seg8:0x4ad1 seg8:0x4aeb seg8:0x4b07 seg8:0x4b11]
11. **Wings.** For a wing ship (design word DGROUP:0x96fa = 3): [BYTES seg8:0x4b23 seg8:0x4b58 seg8:0x4b69 seg8:0x4b82 seg8:0x4bc2 seg8:0x4be1 seg8:0x4bec seg8:0x4c06 seg8:0x4c14 seg8:0x4c34]
    - at word +0x2c = 0 it tears off every wing still on (seg8:0x2cd8 side 1 for byte +0xad, side 0 for +0xac), clears ship words +0x10 and byte +0xea, and sets ship words +0x20 = design word +0x2a − 14, +0x22 = 7, +0x24 = min(+0x24, 1) and byte +0xe0 = 1;
    - below 50 with both wings on it tears the wing on the hit side (object +0x26 = 1: side 1, +0xad; else side 0, +0xac), clears +0x10 and +0xea, sets +0xe0/+0xe1 when +0x24 = 2, and sets object +0x26 = 0.
12. **Thruster out.** Word +0x2a = 0 with design word DGROUP:0x96f8 = 2 and `thrusting` ≠ 0 sets `thrusting` = 0 and calls seg13:0x6716 (flame off). [BYTES seg8:0x4c6c seg8:0x4c78 seg8:0x4c7f seg8:0x4c86 seg8:0x4c8f]
13. **Death.** Engine word +0x26 < 1, unless the attacker is the ship itself or pool 11, calls the ship's `on_destroy` seg16:0x8e4. Every [defender entry](structs.md#other-wave-tables) k < DGROUP:0x940a, DGROUP:0x52fe + 0x24k, aimed at this ship then gets +0 = 15 and the killer, and the function returns 1. [BYTES seg8:0x4c9a seg8:0x4ca4 seg8:0x4cc9]
    - With the ship itself or pool 11 as attacker the ship keeps flying at engine 0. [BYTES seg8:0x4cb5 seg8:0x4cbf]
14. **Otherwise** it clears DGROUP:0xb0a and calls seg13:0x33d6, which rewrites `kill_value` ([world.md](world.md#ship-rating-seg130x33d6)), and returns 0. [BYTES seg8:0x4d43 seg8:0x4d4c seg13:0x3662 seg13:0x366a]

### Lamp messages
- The lamp states and when seg11:0x5fe raises a message are in [world.md](world.md#damage-panel). The message switch seg3:0x1351 indexes its 192-entry jump table at seg3:0x1356 with code − 1. [BYTES seg3:0x1346 seg3:0x1347 seg3:0x134e]
- **Codes 10, 11, 12** play sound 0x6e and then 0x2d (code 10) or 0x2c (11, 12) through seg10:0x744c. [BYTES seg3:0xd2d seg3:0xd2f seg3:0xd35 seg3:0xd41]
- They format string 134 into DGROUP:0x8db0 with %S = string code + 0x75 (127 ENGINE, 128 GUN, 129 THRUSTER), %? = (code = 10, 519, 135) and the %< %> part on W > 700: code 10 reads "DANGER: ENGINE TROUBLE. REPAIR OR ABORT MISSION.", codes 11 and 12 "DANGER: GUN FAILURE. …" and "DANGER: THRUSTER FAILURE. …". [BYTES seg3:0xd47 seg3:0xd57 seg3:0xd5d seg3:0xd6e seg3:0xd74 seg3:0xd7b] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 8] id 127 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 9] ids 128, 129, 134, 135 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 33] id 519
- **Codes 82, 83** format string 517 "%S SYSTEM OK REPAIRS COMPLETE" with %S = code + 0x2e (128 GUN, 129 THRUSTER). [BYTES seg3:0xd86 seg3:0xd89 seg3:0xd8f] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 33] id 517
- **Code 84** formats string 138 "Repairs Complete, %s. Try To Avoid Trouble." with the pilot name DGROUP:0x380c, with no sound. [BYTES seg3:0xd95 seg3:0xd9b] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 9] id 138

### Wings torn off (seg8:0x2cd8)
- seg8:0x2cd8(ship, side) (cdecl) sets ship dword +0x156 = now − 10000 and clears byte +0xad (side ≠ 0) or +0xac (side 0). Its local `cut` [bp−4] is 1 for side 0 (wing +0xac goes) and 0 otherwise, and the debris flag [bp−6] starts at 0. [BYTES seg8:0x2cea seg8:0x2cee seg8:0x2cf6 seg8:0x2d01 seg8:0x2d0e seg8:0x2d12 seg8:0x2d1e]
- With DGROUP:0x95a6 = 0, that is while the MASTER.SHP block is not loaded ([globals.md](globals.md#global-table)), it recomputes design word +0x3c (DGROUP:0x970e), the wing band's half-width D, through seg14:0x3eda and seg14:0x4074, and does not test seg14:0x3eda's return. D = trunc(((sext BOD byte +0x1a << `Size`) sar 1) / 2) of the ship's body is the same arithmetic as the design fill seg14:0x1b84's; the only other writer of the word is seg18:0x3307, which stores trunc(lo16(sext(BOD +0x1a)·(2·`Size` + 1))/6) in seg18 mode. So outside seg18 mode the recompute writes the value already there whenever the SHP index it reads names the ship the design was built from; whether it can name another is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg8:0x2d23 seg8:0x2d29 seg8:0x2d6f-0x2d8d seg8:0x2d91 seg14:0x2070-0x208b seg18:0x32f5-0x3307]
- **The wing cut.** Every tear calls ISPACE.DLL [ordinal 15](ispace.md#ordinal-notes) once, through seg10:0x7370(DGROUP:0x3a38[ship], `cut`, object `size`, D, object `gap`, object `frame`, flag), on the ship's own sheet: with flag 1 when it starts a debris entry (seg8:0x30d4), and with flag 0 at its end while [bp−6] is still 0, that is with no free entry or with DGROUP:0x92e4 ≠ 0 (seg8:0x347d). Ordinal 15 blacks the torn side of row 0 of the 16 heading cells, keeping the far side and a band of D pixels past the ship's axis; rows ≥ 1 and the extra column 16 keep their wings. After both tears only the band is left. [BYTES seg8:0x2d96 seg8:0x2d9b seg8:0x309a-0x30d9 seg8:0x33de seg8:0x3391 seg8:0x343d-0x3482] core-0001's sprite after the first tear (heading 2, wing +0xac) and after the second (heading 3) lies inside the regions ordinal 15 keeps. [ORACLE core-0001]
- The placement brackets seg14:0x0(−1) with seg14:0x3eda and seg14:0x4074 (seg9:0x6883, seg9:0x6890), and DGROUP:0x95a6 ≠ 0 there skips the recompute at seg8:0x2d23, so seg14:0x0's restore re-cut (seg14:0xcb0, seg14:0xcc9) does not recompute. Inside the bracket only the new-body path seg16:0x35d3 frees the block, and the set-up never reaches it (DGROUP:0x95a6 has two writers, seg14:0x3fa1 and seg14:0x40a1). [BYTES seg8:0x2ce7 seg8:0x2d23 seg8:0x2d27 seg9:0x6883 seg9:0x6890 seg14:0x3fa1 seg14:0x40a1 seg16:0x35d3]
- The taunts and the debris below need DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0. [BYTES seg8:0x2d96]
- **Taunts.** Each ship j with `goal_pool` = 14, `goal_rec` = the damaged ship and word +0x38 = 1 gets one taunt seg3:0x6ec(j, 14, ship, code), code 0x19 … 0x1e, chosen by both ships' wing bytes and seg4:0x5d9c(ship, j) > 0x5f: strings 373, 375, 383, 377, 379 and 381. [BYTES seg8:0x2da6 seg8:0x2dcb seg8:0x2ddc seg8:0x2df5 seg8:0x2e0a seg8:0x2e34 seg8:0x2e57 seg3:0xda9 seg3:0xdb9 seg3:0xdc1 seg3:0xdd1 seg3:0xde1 seg3:0xde9] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 24] ids 373–383
- **Debris.** With a free entry (counter word DGROUP:0x9176 + 0x22k = 0) in the [debris table](structs.md#other-wave-tables) the torn wing plays sound 0x6a through seg10:0x754a(0x6a, 14, ship, 1), and seg8:0x2cd8 starts the entry with the dword 0x00460001 there: counter 1, end 70. [BYTES seg8:0x33c2 seg8:0x33cd seg8:0x33da seg8:0x33e9 seg8:0x2f1d]
  - **Placement** (outside seg18 mode). The entry's `extent` +0x1e is the object's `extent` E. With R = 8·trunc(D/2) + trunc((E − 8D)/4) and m = (f + 12) % 16 for `cut` 1 and (f + 4) % 16 for `cut` 0, f the object's `frame`, the wing's point is P = trunc(E/2) + trunc(lo16(T[m]·R)/128) on each axis, T being the 16 word pairs at DGROUP:0x66e, (0, −128), (48, −110), (91, −91), (110, −48), (128, 0) … (−48, −110) clockwise. The copy window runs from x0 = max(Px − 128, 0) to x1 = min(Px + 128, E) (y alike), its offset is ox = max(128 − Px, 0) (oy alike), and the entry's position gains (Px − 128, Py − 128). So the 256-unit debris square is centred R world units from the ship's centre on the torn side; the 128 is half the cell [bp−0xc] = 256. [BYTES seg8:0x2f3b seg8:0x33f4-0x343a seg8:0x2f0b-0x2f16 seg8:0x2f90-0x301b seg8:0x301e-0x3096 DGROUP:0x66e]
  - **The strip.** The flag-1 call returns the torn wing's cell: heading f of row 0 before the cut, with the kept side erased. seg8:0x2cd8 gives the entry a new DC +0x0c holding a 5p × p bitmap +0x10, p = 256 sar s (32 at `Size` 1), blacked with BLACKNESS, then five SRCCOPY BitBlts of the window to ((256j + ox) sar s, oy sar s), j = 0 … 4, size ((x1 − x0) sar s, (y1 − y0) sar s), from (x0 sar s, y0 sar s). It then makes the strip's ordinal-6 mask in bitmap +0x12, selects it into a new DC +0x0e, deletes the wing cell and plays 0x6a. [BYTES seg8:0x310f-0x3177 seg8:0x3183-0x31de seg8:0x3344-0x338d seg8:0x33c2-0x33cd]
  - In seg18 mode the strip comes from the same five BitBlts at seg8:0x3334, rop 0x00CC0020, with p = trunc((64·`Size` + 32)/3), the half-cell trunc(lo16(seg5:0x110(p·dword DGROUP:0x88cc, sext DGROUP:0x88c0))/2) and every shift replaced by sc(v) = trunc(lo16(seg5:0x110(v·(2·`Size` + 1)·sext DGROUP:0x88c0, dword DGROUP:0x88cc))/3), seg5:0x110(a, b) being the RTL's signed 32-bit a / b. [BYTES seg8:0x317c-0x3181 seg8:0x2e67-0x2e9b seg8:0x31e0-0x324c seg8:0x3254-0x3340]
  - **Mover.** seg13:0x447a, called at the end of every wave frame (seg7:0x118d), handles each entry k < 3 whose counter is > 0 (signed): it draws it with seg8:0x8a6(k), adds the velocity DGROUP:0x9164/0x9166 + 0x22k to the position DGROUP:0x915c/0x915e + 0x22k, and takes velocity >> 4 (sar) off the velocity. [BYTES seg7:0x118d seg13:0x463d seg13:0x4649 seg13:0x4652 seg13:0x465a seg13:0x4662 seg13:0x4670]
  - At counter 1 it loads graphic 230 (0xe6) and burns it into the entry's 5-cell strip DC DGROUP:0x9168 + 0x22k (SRCAND from the flame's mask, SRCPAINT, then rop 0x220326 from the strip's mask DC DGROUP:0x916a + 0x22k), and calls seg7:0x1208. At counters 50, 56, 61, 63, 65 and 66 it calls seg7:0x5d6a(x + 0x80, y + 0x80, 1, `rand()` % 60 − 30, `rand()` % 60 − 30), a smoke puff in the 20 × 0x14 table DGROUP:0x58bc ([world.md](world.md#smoke-seg70x5b68-and-seg70x5e04)) that is skipped when `rand()` % 100 < 20, 45 or 70 for `Detail` 0, 1 or 2. Then the counter rises by 1, and when it equals the end word DGROUP:0x9178 + 0x22k it is cleared, the square at DGROUP:0x9160/0x9162 is blacked and seg10:0x37d0(k) releases the DCs. [BYTES seg13:0x4674 seg13:0x4683 seg13:0x44bd seg13:0x44df seg13:0x44f6 seg13:0x4508 seg13:0x455a seg13:0x457d seg13:0x45c0 seg13:0x45cc seg13:0x45d4 seg13:0x4604 seg13:0x4620 seg13:0x4628 seg7:0x5d95 seg7:0x5da0 seg7:0x5da3 seg7:0x5dbc]
  - **Draw.** seg8:0x8a6(k) shows frame (counter / 2) % 5 of the strip, cells p = (32 << `Size`) >> 1 wide. Below counter 2 it only stores the screen position seg8:0x29ea(position) in DGROUP:0x9160. Otherwise, inside (−40, −40) … (W + 40, H + 40), it draws masked (the old square SRCAND, the new SRCAND then SRCPAINT) when the square meets any visibility-list rectangle, and else a BLACKNESS PatBlt of the old square and SRCCOPY at the new. [BYTES seg8:0x8a6 seg8:0x8c0]
  - Debris does not collide: no call of the pair finder seg8:0x50aa lies in seg13:0x447a or seg8:0x8a6. [BYTES seg8:0x50aa seg13:0x447a seg8:0x8a6]
- A torn-off wing cannot be repaired by the ship. [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]

### Death and the explosion
- **seg16:0x8e4(pool, ship)**, the ship's `on_destroy`, returns at once for byte +0x147 = 1 outside seg18 mode. [BYTES seg16:0x8f8]
- It starts up to 8 entries of the [effect table](structs.md#effect-table-45--0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x1e90) DGROUP:0x1e90, each in a free one of the 45 slots, with start times now + 20k, word +0x02 = 10, and DGROUP:0x9430 + 1 each. [BYTES seg16:0x924 seg16:0x939 seg16:0x9c1 seg16:0x9d8 seg16:0x9e7]
  - Entry 0 takes screen offset (16, 16); entries 1 … 7 draw two `rand()` % 60 − 12 offsets, x first. [BYTES seg16:0x95d seg16:0x96e seg16:0x991 seg16:0x9a4]
- It then sets `tumbling` (+0xe2) = 1 (tumbling), object +0x26 = 1, dword +0x84 = now + 6000, calls seg13:0x4710 and sets `alive` (+0xec) = 0 (dead). [BYTES seg16:0x9ff seg16:0xa19 seg16:0xa2d seg16:0xa37 seg16:0xa3d]
- In a kind-7 (duel) wave it sets DGROUP:0x668a = −1 when the player dies or no other live, idle ship remains. [BYTES seg16:0xa42 seg16:0xa9b]
- It plays seg10:0x754a(0x45, 14, ship, 1). [BYTES seg16:0xaac]
- **The player's death.** A player game sets DGROUP:0x1e84 = now + 8000 and the rating to 4/5; the demo sets DGROUP:0x1e80 = now + 8000, DGROUP:0x95a8 = 1 and halves the rating, and with DGROUP:0x951a = 0 calls seg11:0xaece. [BYTES seg16:0xabc seg16:0xacd seg16:0xae6 seg16:0xaea seg16:0xb19 seg16:0xb24 seg16:0xb2b]
- Outside seg18 mode it ends with seg13:0x498e(ship, 1). [BYTES seg16:0xb3c]
- **The entries.** Entry k gets type +0x0a = 5 for k = 0 and 1 for k = 1 … 7, frame counter +0x00 = 1, start dword +0x10 = now + 20k, frame count +0x02 = 10 and source (DGROUP:0x9324, ship); a k without a free slot is skipped. Their world point is the ship's `x` + 0x80, `y` + 0x80. It does not raise DGROUP:0x9434, the loop's last index ([structs.md](structs.md#effect-table-45--0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x1e90), Counts). [BYTES seg16:0x91e seg16:0x914 seg16:0x9e9 seg16:0x9f1 seg16:0x924 seg16:0x935 seg16:0x939 seg16:0x942 seg16:0x949 seg16:0x9b2 seg16:0x9bd seg16:0x9d8 seg16:0x9e7 seg7:0x98b]
  - Type 1 entries wait for their start time and play KABOOM at their world point when they start; entry 0 (type 5) starts at once. Each entry draws frames 1 … 8 of the fireball strip and blacks its square at frame 9 ([world.md](world.md#effect-frame-seg80x3fbe)). Entry 0 also calls the blast seg8:0x5ab0 at frame 6. [BYTES seg8:0x3fe5 seg8:0x4006 seg8:0x4037 seg8:0x4234 seg8:0x4250]
- **The blast seg8:0x5ab0(e)** (Pascal, `retf 2`) works from effect entry e: its source (+0x06, +0x08), its type +0x0a and its world point DGROUP:0x5224/0x5226 + 4e. [BYTES seg8:0x5ab0 seg8:0x5de6]
  1. It returns at once when DGROUP:0x1318 > 6 (signed). [BYTES seg8:0x5aec seg8:0x5af1 seg8:0x5af3]
  2. **Targets.** It visits pools DGROUP:0x9326 … DGROUP:0x9324 and the records below each in-use count DGROUP:0x9406 + 2·pool, skipping a record with `state` ≠ 0 or `mass` = 0, the served ship (DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x952e) while its `on_screen` = 0, and the source itself only when the type is 0xff06. [BYTES seg8:0x5c9f seg8:0x5cbf seg8:0x5cc6 seg8:0x5cd6 seg8:0x5cdc seg8:0x5ce3 seg8:0x5cf5 seg8:0x5dd6]
  3. **Distance.** d = seg8:0x285a(the target's `x`/`y`, the world point), which writes x1 − x2 (target − point) to its first-pushed pointer and y1 − y2 to its second. It replaces d and both offsets by (2v) sar DGROUP:0x9156, doubles all three when DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x90f4 = 6, and then raises d to at least 100 (signed), so its d = 0 test at seg8:0x5d93 never fires. [BYTES seg8:0x5cfa seg8:0x5d20 seg8:0x5d28 seg8:0x5d2c seg8:0x5d2e seg8:0x5d38 seg8:0x5d42 seg8:0x5d47 seg8:0x5d4e seg8:0x5d55 seg8:0x5d5e seg8:0x5d64 seg8:0x5d93 seg8:0x2981 seg8:0x2984 seg8:0x298d seg8:0x29e6]
  4. **Range.** With B the source's `blast_size` (+0x48), a target is skipped unless (min(B, 7) + 8)·15 > d. [BYTES seg8:0x5d77 seg8:0x5d7d seg8:0x5d85 seg8:0x5d88 seg8:0x5d8b]
  5. **Amount** = ((B·600 / d)·15) / max(1, M), tripled for a pool-2 target, with B unclamped and M the low word of seg8:0x1290(mass, 25) = (mass/25 + 25) >> 1 ([The intercept](#the-intercept-seg80x2658)) for the target's `mass` sign-extended; each product, the tripling and both divisions are 16-bit signed (`imul`, `cwd`, `idiv`). [BYTES seg8:0x5d9c seg8:0x5da1 seg8:0x5db5 seg8:0x5dbc seg8:0x5dc0 seg8:0x5dc5 seg8:0x5dcd seg8:0x5b22 seg8:0x5b27 seg8:0x5b2c seg8:0x5b35 seg8:0x129d seg8:0x12b2]
  6. **Heat and dispatch.** It adds 2 to the target's byte +0x2f, 2 more when d < 150, and caps it at 9 (a signed byte compare); sets DGROUP:0x943a/0x943c to the source; sets DGROUP:0xb0c = 1, then 0 when the entry's frame counter +0x00 is not 0; calls seg8:0x5df2(pool, rec, source pool, source rec, −amount); and sets DGROUP:0xb0c = 1 after the call. [BYTES seg8:0x5b49 seg8:0x5b4e seg8:0x5b5d seg8:0x5b70 seg8:0x5b77 seg8:0x5b7f seg8:0x5b85 seg8:0x5b88 seg8:0x5b92 seg8:0x5b99 seg8:0x5ba2 seg8:0x5bb2 seg8:0x5bb8]
  7. **Push.** It then re-reads the target record and adds (v·dx)/d to `vx` and (v·dy)/d to `vy` (32-bit, seg5:0x314 and seg5:0x110), with v = ((B·250)/(d/10))/((mass + 100)/100) from 16-bit `imul` and `idiv` and the `mass` read after the dispatch; d, dx and dy are the values of step 3. [BYTES seg8:0x5bd0 seg8:0x5bdc seg8:0x5bed seg8:0x5c0c seg8:0x5c15 seg8:0x5c18 seg8:0x5c42 seg8:0x5c49 seg8:0x5c70 seg8:0x5c77 seg8:0x5c87 seg8:0x5c98]
- **The burnt sheet.** seg13:0x4710(ship), called before `alive` = 0, plays seg10:0x754a(0x6a, 14, ship, 1) and loads IGRAPHIC graphic 230 (0xe6, five 32-pixel flame cells) with seg10:0x6c02(0xe6, 0, −1). It ANDs the mask of the ship's sheet with itself shifted up by (9 << `Size`) >> 1 rows, and for each heading h = 0 … 15 pastes flame cell h % 5 (side min(`size`, p), p = (32 << `Size`) >> 1, centred by max(0, (`size` − p)/2)) into the sheet's cell h with the flame's mask (SRCAND) and the flame (SRCPAINT), then applies rop 0x220326 (destination AND NOT source) from the shifted mask over the whole cell, which clears what lies outside the ship's silhouette extended upward. The sheet stays burnt for the rest of the wave. [BYTES seg13:0x4710 seg13:0x4870] [BYTES IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 230]
  - core-0001 shows the dead ship's sprite red and burnt from 0.2 s after the death until it explodes. [ORACLE core-0001]
- **The dropped pod.** seg13:0x498e(ship, 1) takes the first pool-11 record (pool pointer DGROUP:0x1e24, count DGROUP:0x9302) whose `state` ≠ 0; when none is, it takes the one with the largest word DGROUP:0x8564 + 0x34·rec (strictly greater, from 0), calls its `on_destroy` as (11, rec) and, after the fill, seg7:0x2080(11, −1). [BYTES seg13:0x498e seg13:0x49ca seg13:0x4a1d seg13:0x4a2e]
  - seg13:0x4ba0(ship, rec, flag) fills it. With flag ≠ 0, when DGROUP:0x92e8 = 0 or the ship is the player, the pod takes kind DGROUP:0x8560 + 0x34·rec = 1, the ship's weapon slot bytes +0xb8 + j and +0xae + j for j = 1 … 9 below byte +0xcb (0xffff for the others) in DGROUP:0x856c and DGROUP:0x8580 + 2(0x1a·rec + j), and all the ship's `resources` (+0xce) in DGROUP:0x8562 + 0x34·rec; the ship's slot bytes are cleared, +0xcb = 1 and `resources` = 0. With flag 0 the kind is 3 and the pod takes min(`resources`, 400). Either way the fill then reads the pod's word back and subtracts it from the ship's `resources` (DGROUP:0x715e). [BYTES seg13:0x4ba0 seg13:0x4bb3 seg13:0x4bbc seg13:0x4bd1 seg13:0x4c4d seg13:0x4c73 seg13:0x4c7b seg13:0x4c84]
  - The pod record: `extent` 184 (0xb8), `mass` 101 (0x65), `state` 0, `capturable` 0, +0x51 = 0, `size` (24 << `Size`) >> 1, `gap` (4 << `Size`) >> 1, +0x4a = 0x1d, and the velocity of the ship plus 5 × the heading vector DGROUP:0x1050/0x1052 of (heading + 8) & 15. The dword DGROUP:0x8564 + 0x34·rec starts at 0x01f40000. [BYTES seg13:0x4cf0 seg13:0x4cfa seg13:0x4e4f seg13:0x4e95 seg13:0x4e9d seg13:0x4ea7 seg13:0x4bb3]
  - **The pod's position.** Per axis the offset is lo16(the heading-table entry of (heading + 8) & 15 · the ship's `extent`)/16, toward zero. seg13:0x4ba0 adds the x offset unreduced and then takes the x sum % (8·DGROUP:0x5a0 = 25600); it takes the y offset % (8·DGROUP:0x5a2 = 25600) before adding it and the y sum % 25600 again after. All three are signed 16-bit remainders (`cwd`, `idiv`), so a negative value stays negative. [BYTES seg13:0x4d6d seg13:0x4d71 seg13:0x4d7b seg13:0x4d84 seg13:0x4d88 seg13:0x4d9a seg13:0x4d9c seg13:0x4e05 seg13:0x4e13 seg13:0x4e16 seg13:0x4e20 seg13:0x4e28 seg13:0x4e2c seg13:0x4e3e seg13:0x4e40]
  - The pool-11 mover is in [Pods](#pods-pool-11-seg120x6b1e) and the pickup in [Other touches](#other-touches). [DESIGN]
- **Tumble.** A dead ship tumbles in the ship mover ([world.md](world.md#ships-seg130x0)) and explodes through seg12:0x274a once dword +0x84 < now, clearing `tumbling` (+0xe2) and object +0x26 first: 6 s after death. A hit in the last 5.5 s explodes it early (step 2 of [Damage to a ship](#damage-to-a-ship-seg80x45e0)). [BYTES seg13:0x14b seg13:0x156 seg13:0x160 seg13:0x174 seg13:0x181 seg16:0xa2d seg8:0x463f]
- **Fragments.** seg12:0x274a, the generic destroy handler ([structs.md](structs.md#object-record-0x70-bytes)), makes 1 fragment for `blast_size` 1, else 30, 60, 100 or 130 % of `blast_size` for `Detail` 0 … 3, at least 2; with DGROUP:0x9430 > 10 the count becomes (35 − DGROUP:0x9430)·n/25, at least 1. A `Detail` outside 0 … 3 leaves the count unset. [BYTES seg12:0x2816 seg12:0x2824 seg12:0x2834 seg12:0x2844 seg12:0x2849 seg12:0x2852 seg12:0x2860 seg12:0x2880]
  - Each fragment j, when a free slot exists, draws `rand()` % 110 for its start time now + r + 30j; for j > `Detail`/2 + 2 it draws `rand()` % 10 and, when that is ≤ 6, `rand()` % 4; for j > 0 it draws x and then y as (`rand()` % (2(n + 40)) − n − 40)·8. [BYTES seg12:0x28ec seg12:0x292d seg12:0x2938 seg12:0x293d seg12:0x29a0 seg12:0x29f6]
  - Fragment j > 0 has the world point `x` + `extent`/2 + (`rand()` % (2(n + 40)) − n − 40)·8 (and `y` alike), fragment 0 the plain `x`, `y`; the screen centre DGROUP:0x5170 + 4e is seg8:0x29ea of that point. A fragment j > `Detail`/2 + 2 whose `rand()` % 10 ≤ 6 shortens +0x02 by `rand()` % 4 + 4 and starts 60 ms later. Each filled entry adds 1 to DGROUP:0x9430 and raises DGROUP:0x9434 to its index. [BYTES seg12:0x297c seg12:0x298e seg12:0x29cb seg12:0x2a0c seg12:0x2a34 seg12:0x2921 seg12:0x292d seg12:0x2938 seg12:0x293d seg12:0x28de seg12:0x28e9 seg12:0x2a3c]
- **The player's death timing.** seg16:0x8e4 sets DGROUP:0x1e84 = now + 8000, and the explosion 6 s later (seg12:0x274a) moves it to now + 1500, so in the normal case the tick takes the death up 7.5 s after it. core-0001 measures the explosion 6.01 s and the MessageBox 7.52 s after the death's KABOOM. [BYTES seg16:0xacd seg13:0x14b seg12:0x2ac1] [ORACLE core-0001]
  - Until then the wave runs on: the tick tests DGROUP:0x1e84 at seg7:0x6ac and goes on to the wave pass while it is 0 or not due. When due it shows the MessageBox seg16:0x68d6 with strings 2328 and 2329 only when DGROUP:0x920c = 0, clears DGROUP:0x1e84, calls seg6:0x5d58 and ends the pass at seg7:0x1192. A second test of DGROUP:0x1e84 at seg7:0x1c5 enters seg7:0x1d8 without the MessageBox. What follows on screen is in [shell.md](shell.md#screen-states), State 5. [BYTES seg7:0x6ac seg7:0x6b2 seg7:0x6bd seg7:0x6bf seg7:0x6d2 seg7:0x1c5 seg7:0x1d8 seg7:0x1e1 seg7:0x1e6] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 146] ids 2328–2329

### Rocks (seg7:0x5090)
- seg7:0x5090 is the rock pool's `on_damage` (cdecl: pool, rec, d). It reads the attacker from DGROUP:0x944c/0x9450, the B party of the last collision, so a hit that did not come through a collision sees stale values. [BYTES seg7:0x509f seg7:0x50e7 seg7:0x50f0]
- It adds no damage when DGROUP:0x9450 exceeds the word DGROUP:0x9406 + 2·DGROUP:0x944c, the in-use count of pool DGROUP:0x944c. A gun hit sets B = (30, slot j) ([Shot hit test](#shot-hit-test-seg80x3688-and-seg80x6f3c)), so the guard then reads DGROUP:0x9406 + 60 = DGROUP:0x9442, the A-party extent of the last pair test, and the hit counts when j ≤ that extent. [BYTES seg7:0x509f seg7:0x50a2 seg7:0x50a6 seg7:0x50a8 seg7:0x50ac seg8:0x725b seg8:0x726d]
- **Heat.** For a rock with d > 1, d becomes 3d/2. Unless B is a pool-5 (DGROUP:0x9328) object of mass 200, the heat byte +0x2f rises by d/4, at most 9. Every such hit sets dword +0x6a = now + 3000. [BYTES seg7:0x50c5 seg7:0x50e7 seg7:0x50fe seg7:0x5104 seg7:0x5106 seg7:0x5129 seg7:0x5131 seg7:0x5135 seg7:0x5150 seg7:0x5154]
- **Break.** It adds d to `damage` at seg7:0x5163; a rock breaks only when `damage` > `max_damage` and its heat before the hit was above 6 (or in seg18 mode). [BYTES seg7:0x50df seg7:0x50e4 seg7:0x5163 seg7:0x5171 seg7:0x5179 seg7:0x517f]
  - A small rock (`mass` 350) is destroyed through seg12:0x274a and seg7:0x2080(pool, −1), returning 1. [BYTES seg7:0x5196 seg7:0x51a2 seg7:0x51af]
  - A big rock splits into three small rocks k = 1, 2, 3: `mass` 350, `extent` 168, `max_damage` 300 (100 in seg18 mode), `damage` 0, heat 9, each at the parent record's `x`, `y` as they stand after the previous small rock was written, plus 8·(dx, dy) with (dx, dy) = (0, −10), (15, 12) and (−15, 13). [BYTES seg7:0x521b seg7:0x5223 seg7:0x5234 seg7:0x524c seg7:0x525e seg7:0x5450 seg7:0x547f seg7:0x55cd seg7:0x5611 seg7:0x5627 seg7:0x5632]
    - The split first sets the parent's `state` to 1, so its own record is free for a small rock ([The break's picture](#rocks-seg70x5090), below), and the small rock that lands in it moves the base of the rocks after it: when rock 1 takes it, rocks 2 and 3 are offset from parent + 8·(0, −10); when rock 2 takes it, rock 3 is offset from parent + 8·(15, 12). [BYTES seg7:0x51cc seg7:0x526f seg7:0x5280 seg7:0x52be seg7:0x5444]
- **Otherwise** it adds d again at seg7:0x564e, so a rock hit with d > 1 that neither destroys nor splits adds 2 × (3d/2). With d ≤ 1, or outside the rock pool, d is added once. [BYTES seg7:0x5175 seg7:0x563d seg7:0x564e]
- **Gate posts** take seg7:0x5090 too ([The three sites](#the-three-sites)). For a pool other than DGROUP:0x934a it adds the amount to `damage` once (seg7:0x563d–0x564e) and returns 0, after the in-use guard above, so a post never breaks. [BYTES seg7:0x509f seg7:0x50b0 seg7:0x50b9 seg7:0x563d seg7:0x564e seg7:0x5652 seg7:0x50ae]
- **Results.** seg7:0x5090 returns 1 at seg7:0x51b7, which both a destroyed small rock and a split big rock reach, and 0 otherwise. [BYTES seg7:0x51b7 seg7:0x529e]
- **The break's picture.** A big rock that splits makes no effect-table entry: it gets `state` 1, a BLACKNESS PatBlt of its square and seg10:0x754a(0x45, DGROUP:0x934a, rec, 1); only the small rock's path calls seg12:0x274a. Each small rock takes the lowest rock record with `state` ≠ 0, searching from record 0 for each piece and growing the pool by one record past its count, and its `vx`, `vy`, +0x26 and `frame` are not written, so they keep the reused record's values. [BYTES seg7:0x5196 seg7:0x51a2 seg7:0x51cc seg7:0x51f9 seg7:0x5209 seg7:0x52a2 seg7:0x52c2 seg7:0x541f seg7:0x5444 seg7:0x5449]
  - core-0001 hears five 0x45 sounds within 0.3 s when rocks break. [ORACLE core-0001]
- The rock mover's heating step for pool-5 hits runs before the response ([world.md](world.md#rocks-seg70x3bae), Heating). [BYTES seg7:0x3f65 seg7:0x3ff7]

### Auto-repair (seg11:0x1d7c)
- seg11:0x1bcc calls seg11:0x1d7c at its end. It acts when frame# exceeds dword DGROUP:0x5014 and then sets DGROUP:0x5014 = frame# + 180, so it runs every 181 frames, about 5 s. [BYTES seg11:0x1d71 seg11:0x1d92 seg11:0x1d97 seg11:0x1d9b seg11:0x1da1]
- **Countdown.** Per ship i the word DGROUP:0x1858 + 2i is decremented on every tick where it is > 0. When it is ≤ 0 that tick does no repair and reloads it to max(1, 9 − `mass`/75). [BYTES seg11:0x1eab seg11:0x1e6f seg11:0x1e75 seg11:0x1e97]
- **Repair** runs on a tick with the countdown > 0 when design word DGROUP:0x971e (Repair System) ≠ 0, the ship is the player or the mode is not seg18, and `fuel` > 90, tested once before the lamps. [BYTES seg11:0x1ebd seg11:0x1ece seg11:0x1edc]
  - It skips a lamp whose row DGROUP:0x96f4 + 2k is −1, a health ≥ 82, and lamp 3 at health 0 or 50. [BYTES seg11:0x1df7 seg11:0x1e08 seg11:0x1e15 seg11:0x1e1c]
  - Each repaired lamp gains 2 (the player, k ≠ 3) or 1, one more in a kind-6 wave, and costs 2 `fuel`, so one tick can spend 8 and end below 90. [BYTES seg11:0x1db0 seg11:0x1dba seg11:0x1dbf seg11:0x1dd0 seg11:0x1dd9 seg11:0x1e3d]
- On every tick with the countdown > 0, repaired or not, ship dword +0x8c = max(27, 4·(75 − word +0x28)) / 27; +0x8c is the gun's extra interval ([The gun](#the-gun-seg110x4e0a)). [BYTES seg11:0x1e44 seg11:0x1e4c seg11:0x1e53 seg11:0x1e62 seg11:0x4e51]
- Repair stops at 82 or 83, so a lamp passes 75 and gives its completion message, and only the ambulance brings a system back to 100. [BYTES seg11:0x1e08 seg11:0x6a7]
- The manual stops auto-repair at 15 % fuel; the bytes test 90 absolute, about 7 % of the TRANSFORMER's 1320. [MANUAL Special Ship Systems] [MANUAL Resource Management] [BYTES seg11:0x1edc] [ORACLE probe-0001]

## Capture

### The touch routine (seg13:0x16ac)
- seg13:0x16ac(ship, pool·256 + rec) (cdecl) takes the ship at [bp+6] and the object at [bp+8]. Early exits, in order: [BYTES seg13:0x16be seg13:0x1fb8]
  1. the object's `capturable` (+0x53) = 0; [BYTES seg13:0x16d7 seg13:0x16db]
  2. the ship is DGROUP:0x952e while DGROUP:0x9e49 is 1 … 6; [BYTES seg13:0x16df seg13:0x16f3]
  3. a dead ship (`alive` (+0xec) = 0) takes seg8:0x45e0(14, ship, 1), one point of damage, and returns, unless the object is pool 11 with DGROUP:0x856a + 0x34·rec = ship; [BYTES seg13:0x16fc seg13:0x1703 seg13:0x170f seg13:0x1715 seg13:0x171e]
  4. ship byte +0xea ≠ 0 or `cloaked` (+0xe8) ≠ 0; [BYTES seg13:0x172e seg13:0x1737 seg13:0x1740]
  5. a pool-0 object whose byte +0x2f is negative. [BYTES seg13:0x174d seg13:0x1752]
- Every touch that gets this far PatBlts the object's square BLACKNESS, calls seg13:0x6716(ship) and [seg8:0x7d70](#the-push-out-seg80x7d70), and sets the **player's** ship dword +0x156 = now − 1, whichever ship touched. [BYTES seg13:0x1756 seg13:0x177e seg13:0x1782 seg13:0x178b seg13:0x17f6 seg13:0x1802 seg13:0x180e]
- **seg13:0x6716(ship)**, the flame-off call, calls seg7:0x1208 when the ship is the player, DGROUP:0x951a = 0 and DGROUP:0x238c = 0, and otherwise sets ship dword +0x156 (DGROUP:0x71e6) = now − 1, a 32-bit subtraction. [BYTES seg13:0x6720 seg13:0x6728 seg13:0x672f seg13:0x6736 seg13:0x673d seg13:0x674f]
- **The tail** seg13:0x1f98 sets ship word +0x52 (DGROUP:0x70e2) = 7 when the ship is the player, DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0 and `PlayMode` ≠ 0. Every path from the PatBlt on reaches it except the virused-icon branch, whose two exits seg13:0x1aa4 and seg13:0x1ad6 jump past it. [BYTES seg13:0x1f98 seg13:0x1f9e seg13:0x1fa0 seg13:0x1fa7 seg13:0x1fb2 seg13:0x1aa4 seg13:0x1ad6]
- The manual: icons are captured by flying into them, frozen icons cannot be captured until thawed, and neither cloak nor shield lets a ship capture. [MANUAL QuickStart] [MANUAL Icons] [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]

### Who calls the touch routine
- seg13:0x16ac has eight call sites; seg8:0x50aa hands a pair only to the lower pool's mover ([Who tests whom](#who-tests-whom-seg80x50aa)), so the player captures an icon in the icon mover, not in the ship mover. [BYTES seg7:0x4a7f seg12:0x3a3 seg12:0x3fb seg12:0xbba seg12:0x6ce0 seg4:0x1439 seg13:0xc08 seg13:0xc69]

  | Site | Routine | Pair |
  |---|---|---|
  | seg7:0x4a7f | seg7:0x4a38, the icon mover's hit handler | an icon finds a ship |
  | seg12:0x3a3, seg12:0x3fb | the pool-6 mover seg12:0x0 ([Pool-6 mover](#pool-6-mover-seg120x0), step 13) | a missile finds a ship |
  | seg12:0xbba | the pool-4 mover seg12:0xae4 | a fuel tank finds a ship |
  | seg12:0x6ce0 | the pool-11 mover seg12:0x6b1e | a pod finds a ship |
  | seg4:0x1439 | the computer pilot seg4:0xbde | an off-screen ship meets its goal |
  | seg13:0xc08, seg13:0xc69 | the ship mover ([Ship against ship](#ship-against-ship-seg130x0)) | a ship finds a ship |

- **The icon mover** seg7:0x309c, for an icon with `on_screen` ≠ 0 and `collided` = 0, sets DGROUP:0x943a = 0 and DGROUP:0x943c = the icon, calls seg8:0x50aa and, on a hit, seg7:0x4a38(hit pool, hit rec, icon). [BYTES seg7:0x3143 seg7:0x314a seg7:0x3151 seg7:0x315a seg7:0x315e seg7:0x3177]
- **seg7:0x4a38(P, R, icon)** (cdecl) calls seg13:0x16ac(R, icon) only when P is the ship pool DGROUP:0x9324, ship R's design word DGROUP:0x9714 (Captures Icons) ≠ 0, ship byte +0xea (DGROUP:0x717a) = 0 and the icon's byte +0x51 = 0, and then jumps to seg7:0x4c54. There, unless P = DGROUP:0x9328 and the hit object's `mass` is 120 (a FIREBALL), it calls a = seg8:0x546c(P·256 + R, &icon `vx`, &icon `vy`, icon `extent` + 2, icon `mass`) and seg8:0x5df2(0, icon, P, R, a). So a capture is followed by the response and by the icon's own dispatch, with the ship as attacker, which the `capturable` gate of the [Hit dispatcher](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2) then decides. [BYTES seg7:0x4a47 seg7:0x4a4d seg7:0x4a5a seg7:0x4a61 seg7:0x4a70 seg7:0x4a7f seg7:0x4a87 seg7:0x4c54 seg7:0x4c69 seg7:0x4ca9 seg7:0x4cb8]
  - A ship whose design does not capture, or with byte +0xea ≠ 0, never reaches seg13:0x16ac from an icon; the touch routine's own design test ([Who captures](#who-captures)) is redundant on this path. [BYTES seg7:0x4a4d seg7:0x4a61 seg13:0x1836]
- **The computer pilot** seg4:0xbde calls seg13:0x16ac(ship, goal pool·256 + goal rec) for a ship whose object word +0x1a = 0 when IntersectRect of the goal's (x − 0x90, y − 0x90, x + 0x180, y + 0x180) and the ship's world square (x, y, x + `size`, y + `size`) is not empty. [BYTES seg4:0x13a5 seg4:0x13aa seg4:0x13c9 seg4:0x13d3 seg4:0x13d7 seg4:0x13dc seg4:0x1421 seg4:0x1428 seg4:0x1439]
- **A dead ship's touch.** Exit 3 calls seg8:0x45e0 with the caller's DGROUP:0x943a/0x943c still set, so rule 2 of [Damage to a ship](#damage-to-a-ship-seg80x45e0) decides by site: from the ship mover, which stored the ship itself, the hit is a self-hit and does not explode it; from the icon, pool-4 and pool-6 movers, which stored their own records, a dead ship that touches the object explodes once dword +0x84 − 5500 < now. [BYTES seg13:0x1715 seg13:0xb75 seg13:0xb7d seg7:0x3151 seg7:0x315a seg12:0xb6d seg12:0x332 seg12:0x33b seg8:0x461b]

### The push-out (seg8:0x7d70)
seg8:0x7d70 (Pascal, `retf 0x24`) pushes the touched object out of the ship. The touch routine passes, from [bp+6] up: the ship's `size`, the object's `size`, far pointers to the ship's +0x02 and +0x00, to the object's +0x02 and +0x00, to the ship's `vy` and `vx`, and to the object's `vy` and `vx`. It writes only the object's `vx`, `vy`, `scr_x` and `scr_y`. Every step is 16-bit unless marked; `/` truncates toward zero. [BYTES seg13:0x1794-0x17f6 seg8:0x8046 seg8:0x7e25 seg8:0x7e3b seg8:0x802d seg8:0x803c]
1. hs = ship `size`/2, ho = object `size`/2; dx = object `scr_x` + ho − ship `scr_x` − hs, dy alike; d = seg8:0x35d0(dx, dy) on the sign-extended values, 1 when it is 0. [BYTES seg8:0x7d7e-0x7db1 seg8:0x7de2 seg8:0x7df1]
2. When |object `vx` − ship `vx`| < 10 and |object `vy` − ship `vy`| < 10 (seg9:0x88ba, signed), object `vx` = ship `vx` − lo16(15·dx)/d and `vy` alike. [BYTES seg8:0x7df9 seg8:0x7dff seg8:0x7e07 seg8:0x7e0d seg8:0x7e12-0x7e3b]
3. With the relative velocity (rvx, rvy) taken again and s = seg8:0x35d0(rvx, rvy) (1 for 0), it returns when the low word of seg5:0x110(seg5:0x314(rvx·dx + rvy·dy in 32 bits, 100), s·d in 32 bits) is above 15 (signed): the object already moves away, and the velocity of step 2 stays. [BYTES seg8:0x7e7c-0x7ea9 seg8:0x7eae seg8:0x7eb1 seg8:0x7eb5]
4. Otherwise ux = lo16(rvx·100)/s and uy alike. Of the y penetration py, divided by max(|uy|, 1), and the x penetration px, divided by max(|ux|, 1), it takes t = px when |py| > |px|, else py (py on a tie), and adds lo16(t·ux)/−100 to the object's `scr_x` and lo16(t·uy)/−100 to its `scr_y`; the `cwd` after each `imul` cuts the product to 16 bits. [BYTES seg8:0x7ff0-0x8010 seg8:0x801e-0x803c] The penetration itself, ((rvy > 0 ? dy : −dy) + hs + ho)·100 for y and the same from x, has an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s2-capture.md §2b). [OPEN]
- The PatBlt before it blacks the object's old square, and the [dissolve](world.md#icon-dissolve-seg70x3a42) draws at the moved `scr_x`/`scr_y`. [BYTES seg13:0x1756 seg13:0x17f6]

### Who captures
- The pool-0 branch runs only when the icon's byte +0x51 = 0 (not dissolving) and the ship's design word DGROUP:0x9714 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg13:0x1816 seg13:0x1822 seg13:0x1830 seg13:0x1836]
- seg14 writes DGROUP:0x9714 = 1 or 0 from the SHP flag 0x10, Captures Icons ([formats.md](formats.md#mastershp-ships)). seg7:0x1e4e and seg11:0xaffe force 1 for the player's design, so the player always captures; seg13:0xb63a writes 0. [BYTES seg14:0x1eb4 seg14:0x1ebb seg14:0x1ec8 seg7:0x1e4e seg11:0xaffe seg13:0xb63a]

### Capturing an icon
1. The icon gets `state` = 1, byte +0x51 = 11 and word +0x48 = the capturing ship. [BYTES seg13:0x1842 seg13:0x184a seg13:0x1854]
2. seg13:0x3008(pool·256 + rec, ship), the [rivals](#the-rivals-seg130x3008). [BYTES seg13:0x185d]
3. In a kind-6 wave, when the capturer is not seg4:0x8d3a(), it calls seg13:0xb6f2(ship, 8) and twice seg13:0x2714(seg4:0x8d3a(), ship, −50, −80, 1, 2). [BYTES seg13:0x1863 seg13:0x18a5]
4. seg7:0x5ae8(rec, 1), the [counters](#counters-seg70x5ae8-and-seg70x5a46). [BYTES seg13:0x18ac]
5. seg7:0x3a42(rec), the first [dissolve](world.md#icon-dissolve-seg70x3a42) step, at once. [BYTES seg13:0x18b7]
6. seg13:0x4eec(ship, 35). seg13:0x4eec(ship, v) adds (1000 − w)·v / 1000 to ship word +0x82 (32-bit, seg5:0x110) for v > 0, and for v ≤ 0 sets w = (w + v)·1000 / (v + 1000). [BYTES seg13:0x18c3 seg13:0x4eec seg13:0x4ef7 seg13:0x4f18]
7. **Virused icon** (`capturable` (+0x53) = 2), then return without score:
   - `resources` (+0xce) −= 100, floored at 0, and seg3:0x6ec(ship, 7, 0, 0x48): sound 0x67 through seg10:0x744c and string 652 "You're In Trouble! You Captured A Virused Icon!"; [BYTES seg13:0x18d7 seg13:0x18e5 seg13:0x18e9 seg13:0x18ee seg13:0x18f4 seg13:0x18fd seg3:0xfdd seg3:0xfdf seg3:0xfe7] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 41] id 652
   - for the player with `PlayMode` < 2: in a player game DGROUP:0x95bc = 0 and `PlayMode` = 2 (from 0) or 3 (from non-zero); in the demo with `PlayMode` ≠ 0, `PlayMode` = 4. When `PlayMode` is then > 2 it puts strings 1700 + 1701 "Out Of Control. That" "Icon Didn't Taste So Good!" into DGROUP:0x8630 with DGROUP:0x58c = 1 and DGROUP:0x5c0 = now + 1200; [BYTES seg13:0x1917 seg13:0x1925 seg13:0x192b seg13:0x1941 seg13:0x1947 seg13:0x195d seg13:0x1963 seg13:0x196e seg13:0x198f seg13:0x19ac seg13:0x19bc] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 107] ids 1700–1701
   - ship dword +0x94 = now + 15000 and DGROUP:0x8690 = now + 7500; the nearest `state`-0 record of pools 3, 8 (word DGROUP:0x3d6c + 0x34·rec = 2 only) and 9, by seg8:0x285a from 20000, goes into ship words +0x06 and `goal_pool` (pool) and +0x08 and `goal_rec` (record), with 2 in +0x36 … +0x3c. [BYTES seg13:0x19c7 seg13:0x19d2 seg13:0x19de seg13:0x19e4 seg13:0x19ed seg13:0x1a03 seg13:0x1a4d seg13:0x1a87 seg13:0x1a8d seg13:0x1a93 seg13:0x1ab0 seg13:0x1ac3 seg13:0x1ad6]
   - The manual: a virused icon gives no resources, and the ship flies out of control and bashes into things while auto-repair purges the virus. [MANUAL Icons]
8. **Good icon:**
   - **Value.** DGROUP:0x9604 = (`damage` × −90)/210 + 100 (16-bit imul, signed idiv); a negative value increments DGROUP:0x9498 and becomes 50. [BYTES seg13:0x1ae7 seg13:0x1aec seg13:0x1af0 seg13:0x1af2 seg13:0x1afc seg13:0x1b00]
   - The capturer's `resources` gain DGROUP:0x9604 and its captured count +0xd2 gains 1. [BYTES seg13:0x1b0e seg13:0x1b12]
   - **Teammate.** A non-player capturer whose team (design word DGROUP:0x970a) is the player's sends seg3:0x6ec(player, 14, ship, 0x6f), string 1062 "I've Captured An Icon. Score 1 For The %s." with the team name from seg13:0x218a, and adds 1000 to the score and to the rating, each with a 32-bit `add dword`. [BYTES seg13:0x1b19 seg13:0x1b28 seg13:0x1b32 seg13:0x1b38 seg13:0x1b42 seg13:0x1b4a seg13:0x1b53 seg3:0x118f seg3:0x11a3 seg3:0x11b2] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 67] id 1062
   - **Defenders.** Every pool-2 record k < DGROUP:0x940a with `state` 0, DGROUP:0x5304 + 0x24k = 0 and DGROUP:0x5306 + 0x24k = the icon's record passes to the capturer (DGROUP:0x5304 = 14, DGROUP:0x5306 = ship) and counts in DGROUP:0x9602, cleared first. With DGROUP:0x9602 ≠ 0 it calls seg2:0x170e and seg10:0x744c(0x69). [BYTES seg13:0x1b5e seg13:0x1b6e seg13:0x1b79 seg13:0x1b83 seg13:0x1b8c seg13:0x1b93 seg13:0x1b97 seg13:0x1ba4 seg13:0x1ba6 seg13:0x1bad seg13:0x1bb2 seg13:0x1bb4] The manual: the defenders of a captured icon become yours. [MANUAL Icons]
   - **Sound** seg10:0x754a(id, 0, rec, 1): 0x23 when (DGROUP:0x9602 = 0 and the icon's `fixed` (+0x54) = 0) or the wave is kind 6; else 0x26 when DGROUP:0x9602 < 2; else 0x66. [BYTES seg13:0x1bba seg13:0x1bcf seg13:0x1bd6 seg13:0x1be5 seg13:0x1be9 seg13:0x1bf8 seg13:0x1c04 seg13:0x1c06]
     - 0x23 is CAPTURED.WAV. ISPACE.DLL's switch sends 0x26 to the voice C_NOTBAD.WAV ("Not bad."), or 0x23 while its DGROUP:0x696 (VoiceFX) = 0, and 0x66 to one of 0x47 … 0x49 (YORE_HOT, PERFORM, SLICK) by its own `rand()` % 3, a repeat of the last moving to the next. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg9:0xc72 seg9:0xd7d seg9:0xd0e seg3:0x668 DGROUP:0x696 DGROUP:0xcfc] [REF re/artifacts/sounds.tsv]
   - **Message.** seg3:0x6ec(ship, 7, 0, 0x55), shown only for the player ([Messages](#messages-seg30x6ec)): with DGROUP:0x9602 = 0 string 574 "Good Icon Capture! %s Resource Gained." with seg6:0x1010(DGROUP:0x9604, 100, 2); with defenders string 575 "Good Icon Capture! %# Acquired." with (DGROUP:0x9602, string 2530 "Defender"), %# taking 12530 "Defenders" for a count ≠ 1. [BYTES seg13:0x1c0e seg13:0x1c10 seg13:0x1c12 seg13:0x1c17 seg3:0xe21 seg3:0xe26 seg3:0xe2d seg3:0xe2f seg3:0xe31 seg3:0xe35 seg3:0xe44 seg3:0xe4a seg3:0xe4d seg3:0xe53] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 36] ids 574–575 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 159] id 2530 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 784] id 12530
   - **Score.** For the player, (DGROUP:0x9602 + 4) × 1000 is added to the score DGROUP:0x1e58 and to the rating DGROUP:0x5078: 4000 without defenders. The product is a 16-bit `imul` sign-extended by `cwd` into the two 32-bit add/adc pairs, so it wraps for 29 or more defenders. [BYTES seg13:0x1c1f seg13:0x1c25 seg13:0x1c27 seg13:0x1c2a seg13:0x1c2d seg13:0x1c32 seg13:0x1c3a seg13:0x1c3e] [ORACLE wave-0001] [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Number format.** seg6:0x1010(v, d, n, dest) writes "-" (DGROUP:0x491) and negates when v < 0, then decimal(v / d), "." (DGROUP:0x493), and decimal(v % d) through seg6:0x10ce cut to n characters, without zero padding: 100 → "1.0", 150 → "1.50", 7 → "0.7". [BYTES seg6:0x1010 seg6:0x102e seg6:0x1066 seg6:0x10ce] [ORACLE wave-0001]
- The oracle's two captures scored 4000 each and the next ambulance transferred 1.75, credit 3.25; which capture was worth what is not observed. A capture worth 0.75 has `damage` 60, because the icon's damage moves in steps of 3. [ORACLE wave-0001] [BYTES seg13:0x1ae7 seg7:0x2375]

### Other touches
- **Pool 4 (fuel tank).** With ship byte +0xea = 0 and pickup byte +0x51 = 0: `state` 1, +0x48 = ship, `fuel` = min(tank, `fuel` + 250), seg13:0x4eec(ship, 30), seg10:0x754a(0x71, 14, ship, 1), +0x51 = 10, seg13:0x3008, seg12:0xe5c(rec) and DGROUP:0x698c = now + 10000. [BYTES seg13:0x1c42 seg13:0x1c4d seg13:0x1c6d seg13:0x1c8a seg13:0x1c90 seg13:0x1c9e seg13:0x1cb5 seg13:0x1cb7 seg13:0x1cc3 seg13:0x1cd8 seg13:0x1ce8]
  - **The tank's dissolve** seg12:0xe5c(rec) and the mover that calls it per frame are in [The fuel tank](#the-fuel-tank-pool-4-seg120xae4). [BYTES seg13:0x1cd8 seg12:0xc4b]
  - **The tank's cool-down DGROUP:0x698c.** The race respawn seg12:0x4f4e ([The fuel tank](#the-fuel-tank-pool-4-seg120xae4)) respawns a pending record only when now > dword DGROUP:0x698c (unsigned 32-bit). When its loop finds no pending record it sets DGROUP:0x698c = now + 10000 without testing the timer; a failed timer test or a respawn suppresses that store. The writers are seg12:0x279a, seg12:0x514e and the touch's seg13:0x1ce8. [BYTES seg12:0x505a seg12:0x5039 seg12:0x5068 seg12:0x506c seg12:0x5070 seg12:0x5074 seg12:0x513b seg12:0x514e seg12:0x5151 seg12:0x279a seg13:0x1ce8]
- **Pools 11 and 6, the common path.**
  - **Exits.** It jumps to the tail seg13:0x1f98 without writing the record when the record's byte +0x51 ≠ 0, or, for a pod (pool 11) of this ship (DGROUP:0x856a + 0x34·rec = ship), when the ship's `alive` is 0: the ship gets the PatBlt, seg13:0x6716 and seg8:0x7d70 and nothing else. Its test of ship byte +0xea at seg13:0x1cff never succeeds, because exit 4 has already returned for a shielded ship. [BYTES seg13:0x1cec seg13:0x1cff seg13:0x1d1a seg13:0x1d24 seg13:0x1d31 seg13:0x1d39 seg13:0x1d3e seg13:0x1733]
  - **The record.** Otherwise the record gets byte +0x51 = 10, `state` = 1 and word +0x48 = the touching ship. [BYTES seg13:0x1d50 seg13:0x1d5d seg13:0x1d6c]
  - **A kind-2 pod** (kind word DGROUP:0x8560 + 0x34·rec = 2) sets DGROUP:0x1318 = 1, the **player's** object byte +0x54 (`fixed`) = 0 through DGROUP:0x1e30 whichever ship touched, DGROUP:0x913e = 1 when DGROUP:0x94b2 ≠ 0, and calls seg3:0x6ec(player, 7, 0, 0xb5). [BYTES seg13:0x1d70 seg13:0x1d7a seg13:0x1d81 seg13:0x1d90 seg13:0x1d9c seg13:0x1dad]
  - **A kind-1 pod** sets DGROUP:0x9604 = its resources DGROUP:0x8562 + 0x34·rec, adds them to the ship's `resources` and calls seg3:0x6ec(ship, 7, 0, 0x56) ([Messages](#messages-seg30x6ec)). Then for j = 1 … 9 it hands the pod's weapon W = DGROUP:0x856c + 0x34·rec + 2j with count C = DGROUP:0x8580 + 0x34·rec + 2j to the ship unless W = −1: a slot k = 1 … byte +0xcb − 1 holding W (byte +0xb8 + k) gets count byte +0xae + k = min(count + C, 100) (signed) and the pod's W becomes −1; otherwise seg12:0x6d64(ship, W) adds the weapon and byte +0xad + (byte +0xcb) = the low byte of C. The pod's entry j = 0 is never handed over, as the fill never writes it. [BYTES seg13:0x1db5 seg13:0x1dc9 seg13:0x1dd5 seg13:0x1de2 seg13:0x1dea seg13:0x1e01 seg13:0x1e14 seg13:0x1e23 seg13:0x1e2f seg13:0x1e4e seg13:0x1e68 seg13:0x1e73 seg13:0x4bd5]
  - **Pool 6.** A pool-6 record whose weapon word W(w)+0x42 (DGROUP:0x2f96) ≠ 0 gets all four writes: the ship's object word +0x26 = 1, `tumbling` (+0xe2, DGROUP:0x7172) = 1, dword +0x84 (DGROUP:0x7114) = now + 1200, and `fuel` (+0x1e) = min(design word DGROUP:0x96f0, `fuel` + 20); with W(w)+0x42 = 0 the jump at seg13:0x1ea1 skips all four. [BYTES seg13:0x1e9c seg13:0x1ea1 seg13:0x1eb2 seg13:0x1ebd seg13:0x1ecc seg13:0x1ee8]
