Then it calls seg13:0x3008(pool·256 + rec, ship), seg13:0x4eec(ship, 50) and seg12:0x129e(pool, rec). [BYTES seg13:0x1ef0 seg13:0x1efa seg13:0x1f0d seg13:0x1f19 seg13:0x1f1d seg13:0x1f2c seg13:0x1f30 seg13:0x1f32 seg13:0x1f41 seg13:0x1f47 seg13:0x1f4d seg13:0x1f59]
  - **seg13:0xb6f2.** After seg12:0x129e it calls seg13:0xb6f2(ship, 2) for a kind-1 pod (pool 11) outside Demon's Gate (DGROUP:0x92e8 = 0) when the ship's design team (DGROUP:0x970a) is not 7. [BYTES seg13:0x1f59 seg13:0x1f6c seg13:0x1f73 seg13:0x1f7d seg13:0x1f84 seg13:0x1f90]
  - **A kind-3 pod**, the flag-0 fill of [the dropped pod](#death-and-the-explosion), gives its taker nothing: the touch tests the kind word only for 2 and 1, and any kind but 1 jumps from seg13:0x1dc6 to seg13:0x1e8d, past the resources and the weapon hand-over (a kind-2 pod after its own block). The fill has taken the pod's min(`resources`, 400) off the dying ship, and no code gives it back. The pod word DGROUP:0x8562 + 0x34·rec has two indexed readers, seg13:0x1dc9 and the fill's seg13:0x4c7b; seg3:0x23e9 reads record 0's word DGROUP:0x8562 itself. [BYTES seg13:0x1d70-0x1dc6 seg13:0x1d7a seg13:0x1dbf seg13:0x1dc9 seg13:0x1e8d seg13:0x4c7b seg3:0x23e9]

### The rivals (seg13:0x3008)
seg13:0x3008(pool·256 + rec, ship) (cdecl) runs after every capture and pickup. The parts that reach the player: [BYTES seg13:0x185d seg13:0x1f41]
- **Escort case.** Its loop over the other ships compares the same word +0x06 (DGROUP:0x7096) with the pool and then with the record, so only pool = rec can match. [BYTES seg13:0x309e seg13:0x30a7]
- **Rivals.** Otherwise each ship k ≠ ship with goal words +0x32 = pool, +0x34 = rec and +0x38 = 2, `state` 0, `alive` (+0xec) ≠ 0, bytes +0x148/+0x149 = (pool, rec) and pool 0 or 11 gets seg3:0x6ec(ship, DGROUP:0x9324, k, 0x49) and seg4:0x5e8e(k, ship, 90, 2). A team-7 k (design word DGROUP:0x970a = 7) takes that path too, except that the player as k with pool 6 makes it call seg13:0xb6f2(ship, 4) instead. [BYTES seg13:0x313e seg13:0x3148 seg13:0x3152 seg13:0x3158 seg13:0x316a seg13:0x318f seg13:0x31b2 seg13:0x31b8 seg13:0x31c2 seg13:0x31db seg13:0x31e4 seg13:0x31f1 seg13:0x31fa seg13:0x3203 seg13:0x320e]
  - Message 0x49 (case 72) formats string 654 "Hey, I Wanted That %D! " in mode 2 with the dword of the sender k's words +0x32/+0x34 and a word 0. Its recipient is the touching ship, so the player sees it when the player takes a computer ship's goal. [BYTES seg3:0xfed seg3:0xfef seg3:0xff4 seg3:0xff9 seg3:0xffb seg3:0xa5f] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 41] id 654
- **`rand()`.** It draws `rand()` (seg5:0xdc) only for a ship other than the player, with `resources` (+0xce) > 350, DGROUP:0x9208 + 3 > DGROUP:0x9316, design team ≠ 3 and DGROUP:0x992 + 20 < DGROUP:0x932a. A player capture draws none here. [BYTES seg13:0x3219 seg13:0x321c seg13:0x3228 seg13:0x3236 seg13:0x3242 seg13:0x324f seg13:0x329f]

### Counters (seg7:0x5ae8 and seg7:0x5a46)
- seg7:0x5ae8(rec, captured) first calls seg7:0x5a46. With captured ≠ 0 it adds 1 to directory word +0x150 (CAPTURED) and to DGROUP:0x945c; with captured = 0 it adds 1 to +0x14e (DESTROYED) and DGROUP:0x945e. [BYTES seg7:0x5af4 seg7:0x5b07 seg7:0x5b0c seg7:0x5b1c seg7:0x5b3d]
  - With captured = 0, when the icon's byte DGROUP:0x57b2 + 0x23·rec is the ship pool, ship word +0xd0 (destroyed) of ship DGROUP:0x57b3 + 0x23·rec gains 1. [BYTES seg7:0x5b21 seg7:0x5b39]
  - It sets directory byte +0x1d0 = 2 (complete) when DGROUP:0x91fc = 0 and the byte is not 1. [BYTES seg7:0x5b41 seg7:0x5b5a]
- seg7:0x5a46 sets **DGROUP:0x91fc, the live-icon count**, to the number of pool-0 records 0 … 7 (always 8) with `state` 0, byte +0x2e = 0 and byte +0x51 = 0. [BYTES seg7:0x5a55 seg7:0x5a5f seg7:0x5a66 seg7:0x5a78 seg7:0x5a7f seg7:0x5a86 seg7:0x5a8b]
- For every other record 0 … 7 whose icon number n = DGROUP:0x57ae + 0x23·i is ≥ 0 it sets bit n % 8 of byte +0x1e2 + n/8 of the current directory record DGROUP:0x69f4 + 0x297·DGROUP:0x94a2: captured and destroyed icons alike. [BYTES seg7:0x5a8d seg7:0x5a91 seg7:0x5a98 seg7:0x5aa4 seg7:0x5ab1 seg7:0x5ab7 seg7:0x5ad8]
- At the briefing seg9:0xb2a sets DGROUP:0x57ae + 0x23·i = −1 for every pool-0 record below the in-use count and `state` 1 on pools 0 and 1, and calls seg9:0x0(8); in a new directory (DGROUP:0x94b0 ≠ 0) it also sets icon `damage` and directory word +0x1d2 + 2i to −1, clears directory byte +0x1d1 and word +0x150, and copies +0x150 to +0x14e. [BYTES seg9:0xb43 seg9:0xb49 seg9:0xb58 seg9:0xb77 seg9:0xbc6 seg9:0xbd6 seg9:0xbf0]
  - The in-use count is 8 at every briefing: the load seg9:0x22b4, which seg9:0x5cca calls at seg9:0x5d3c before seg9:0xb2a at seg9:0x5d66, stores 8 in DGROUP:0x9406 on every path. DGROUP:0x94b0's only writers are seg6:0x36b6 (1) and seg6:0x36ed (0 when the entered directory has a record). [BYTES seg9:0x24b5 seg9:0x5d3c seg9:0x5d66 seg6:0x36b6 seg6:0x36ed]
  - seg9:0xb2a ends with the icon walk seg9:0x0(8) (seg9:0xbf7). Its activation seg9:0x9b0(s) sets a negative directory word +0x1d2 + 2s to 0 and copies the word into pool-0 record s's `damage`, so a revisited directory's icons take their damage back. [BYTES seg9:0xb49-0xb77 seg9:0xbf5-0xbf7 seg9:0xa08-0xa1c seg9:0xa2f-0xa43]
- **The briefing's crack loop.** After seg9:0xb2a and the backup seg10:0x506c ([Icon effects](#icon-effects)), seg9:0x5cca calls seg7:0x233c(0, k, 0) for k = 0 … 7 (seg9:0x5d70–0x5d8b). With amount 0, seg7:0x233c copies the record's `damage`, unchanged, into directory word +0x1d2 + 2k unless the icon's word DGROUP:0x57a8 + 0x23·k ≠ 0 or its heat is −100 or −2, and with `damage` > 0 and heat ≥ 0 it draws the crack cell into the strip with S = 32 and g = 6 fixed, the unscaled CRACKS bitmap; it plays no sound and never shatters, since both need an amount ≠ 0. [BYTES seg9:0x5d66 seg9:0x5d6b seg9:0x5d70-0x5d8b seg7:0x234f-0x2372 seg7:0x2375-0x23a5 seg7:0x23cf-0x2488 seg7:0x2404 seg7:0x24a6-0x24aa seg7:0x24c8-0x24cc]
  - So the records the walk does not activate keep the `damage` they last had, the −1 of the last new directory's seg9:0xb2a or the value that record index had in the last directory that used it, and the loop writes it into their slots at every briefing; the wave end's packing (seg9:0x2828, [Remaining icons](#seg90x2828-the-wave-into-the-directory-record)) zeroes them again. Damaged icons show their cracks from the next wave's first frame. The writers of directory word +0x1d2 + 2k are seg7:0x23a5, seg7:0x26f7, seg9:0xa1c, seg9:0xb77, seg9:0x3014 and seg9:0x305f. [BYTES seg7:0x23a5 seg7:0x26f7 seg9:0xa1c seg9:0xb77 seg9:0x3014 seg9:0x305f seg9:0x2fff-0x3014 seg9:0x3048-0x305f]
  - core-0003's ORACLE3X, saved in the wave after a revisit, holds −1 in directory slots 3 … 7, and its wave-2 start shows the third icon (`damage` 54) cracked in the icon column. [ORACLE core-0003]

### The icon after capture
- `Detail` sets the dissolve step DGROUP:0x8ffe (seg6:0xdaa): 3 for `Detail` 0, 2 for 1, 1 for 2 and 3, and nothing for a higher value (and DGROUP:0x9154 to 4, 7, 13, 18). seg6:0xdaa stores the step as a word; the dissolve seg7:0x3a42 reads its low byte and seg12:0xe5c the word. [BYTES seg6:0xe36 seg6:0xe41 seg6:0xe4f seg6:0xe5d seg6:0xe6d seg6:0xe72 seg6:0xe75 seg6:0xe78 seg7:0x3a63 seg12:0xe85]
  - From the counter 11 of step 1 the dissolve therefore runs 4, 6 or 11 times (11 → 8 → 5 → 2 → 0, 11 → 9 → … → 1 → 0, 11 → 10 → … → 0): once inside the touch and then once per frame in [world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 18. [BYTES seg13:0x184a seg13:0x18b7 seg7:0x3a6a seg7:0x3a79 seg7:0xc1a] core-0002 catches one shrunken, scrambled frame of it and black within 0.4 s, with the icon's column row dissolving in step. [ORACLE core-0002]
- An off-screen icon (word +0x1a = 0) for which seg13:0x59e8(0, i) returns 0 still loses its step but returns before the end. Since the frame calls seg7:0x3a42 only while +0x51 ≠ 0 ([world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 18), an icon whose counter reaches 0 there never runs its end PatBlts, seg7:0x65aa(0, i) or seg7:0x2080(0, −1). [BYTES seg7:0x3a86 seg7:0x3a92 seg7:0x3a9c seg7:0x3b93 seg7:0xc14]
- The dissolve's end call seg7:0x2080(0, −1) does nothing: seg7:0x2080 changes in-use counts only for pools 1, 2, 4 … 11 and 19. [BYTES seg7:0x208d seg7:0x20c9 seg7:0x20cd seg7:0x3b9d]
- **Icon column.** seg7:0x65aa(0, rec) calls seg7:0x64ac(0, 1, rec), which returns 1 at once when DGROUP:0x9458 = 0 and otherwise draws row rec through seg13:0x5a6a(hdc, 0, 46·rec + 4, 0, rec, rec + 3, 1, DGROUP:0xb6e). Then it returns 1 while DGROUP:0x91fc ≠ 0; with DGROUP:0x91fc = 0 it destroys the column with seg7:0x63f2, but only when it took and released its own DC (hdc 0 after the release), and returns 0. [BYTES seg7:0x65aa seg7:0x65c5 seg7:0x64ac seg7:0x64be seg7:0x6577 seg7:0x6589 seg7:0x658e seg7:0x6593 seg7:0x6597 seg7:0x659e seg7:0x63f2]
  - With flag 1, seg13:0x5a6a ([world.md](world.md#object-pictures-seg130x5a6a)) clears the cell to black and always calls seg13:0x5c22. For a pool-0 record with `state` ≠ 0, seg13:0x5c22 draws the four-quarter picture (seg13:0x5f49 … 0x6057) when word DGROUP:0x57c1 + 0x23·rec ≠ 0, whatever +0x51 holds; with that word 0 it draws the strip cell while +0x51 ≠ 0 and nothing once +0x51 = 0. [BYTES seg13:0x5ad3 seg13:0x5be2 seg13:0x5f2c seg13:0x5f35 seg13:0x5f3f seg13:0x6070 seg13:0x6086 seg13:0x608d seg13:0x6092 seg13:0x6096]
  - So a dissolving icon's row is drawn from its strip cell, and the black row after a capture holds because the dissolve calls seg7:0x65aa only after +0x51 has reached 0 and the cell is blacked. [BYTES seg7:0x3b88 seg7:0x3b93] That a shattered icon keeps its four quarters in the row because seg7:0x2768 sets DGROUP:0x57c1 + 0x23·rec = 1 has an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s2-gun.md §6.2). [OPEN] core-0002 shows the captured icon's row black from the dissolve's end and a shattered icon's row as four quarter pictures. [ORACLE core-0002]
  - **The last icon.** A capture recounts DGROUP:0x91fc (seg7:0x5ae8, step 4) before the dissolve ends, so capturing the last icon destroys the column at the dissolve's end. The shatter seg7:0x26c4 calls seg7:0x65aa before seg7:0x5ae8, so shooting the last icon leaves the column standing until the next own-DC draw of seg7:0x64ac. [BYTES seg13:0x18ac seg7:0x3b93 seg7:0x2725 seg7:0x2758]
  - seg7:0x65aa also calls seg13:0x6450 for a non-player ship, and seg13:0x58dc(0, 1) when DGROUP:0xa76 is 2 or 3 and the mark DGROUP:0xa76 − 2, the words DGROUP:0x6e54 (pool) and DGROUP:0x6e52 (record) + 0x1e·(DGROUP:0xa76 − 2), holds the object: the target mark in target mode, mark 1 (DGROUP:0x6e72/0x6e70) in message mode. [BYTES seg7:0x65aa seg7:0x65ea seg7:0x65f1 seg7:0x65fd seg7:0x6603 seg7:0x660c seg7:0x6616]
- **Retarget.** The 280 ms IS_INFO step seg13:0x24aa(0), outside a race and with the player's `target_pool` ≠ −1, calls seg3:0xf4(player, high byte, low byte) of seg13:0x2206()'s result, both bytes zero-extended, when seg3:0x0(target) = 0 and seg13:0x371c(target) ≠ 1. A captured icon (`state` 1) fails seg3:0x0, so the display moves on within 280 ms; no AUTO/MANUAL test is on this path. What seg3:0xf4 does with a target pool of −1 for the player's ship is in [structs.md](structs.md#ship-records-8--0x15a-bytes-at-dgroup0x7090) (`target_pool`): its "NO TARGETS" branch is never reached. [BYTES seg13:0x24b9 seg13:0x24bf seg13:0x24ce seg13:0x24e2 seg13:0x24ec seg13:0x24fa seg13:0x2500 seg13:0x2504 seg13:0x250a seg13:0x250d seg13:0x2513 seg13:0x2518]
  - After the capture message core-0002's display returns to "CURRENT TARGET (MANUAL)" with the target it showed before the capture. [ORACLE core-0002]

### Shooting an icon (seg7:0x233c)
- The icon's `on_damage` seg7:0x233c skips damage when DGROUP:0x57a8 + 0x23·rec ≠ 0 or byte +0x2f is −100 or −2; otherwise it adds 3 × amount to `damage`, capped at 211, and copies it to directory word +0x1d2 + 2·rec. [BYTES seg7:0x234f seg7:0x2370 seg7:0x2375 seg7:0x2386]
- It shatters the icon through seg7:0x26c4 when the amount ≠ 0 and `damage` > 208, or when byte +0x2f < 0. [BYTES seg7:0x24da seg7:0x24ef]
- seg7:0x26c4 sets `damage` and directory word +0x1d2 + 2·rec to −1, blacks the square, plays seg10:0x754a(0x1f, 0, rec, 1), sets `state` 1, calls seg7:0x2768(rec) and seg7:0x65aa(0, rec), and then seg7:0x5ae8(rec, 0), a destroyed icon. [BYTES seg7:0x26c4 seg7:0x26d8 seg7:0x2758]
- **The fragments.** seg7:0x2768(rec) makes four pool-1 records (DGROUP:0x1dfc), reusing one with `state` ≠ 0 or growing the pool, with the fields of [structs.md](structs.md#pool-1-fragments). They take the icon's position plus (0, 0), (176, 0), (0, 176) and (176, 176) and its velocity plus (−10, −10), (20, −10), (−10, 20) and (10, 10). [BYTES seg7:0x2cc0-0x2d0a seg7:0x2d0e-0x2d57 seg7:0x2d5b-0x2da4 seg7:0x2da8-0x2df4]
  - core-0002 shows a shattered icon breaking into four small fragments that drift apart, with SHATTER 0x1f after the last CRACK 0x12. [ORACLE core-0002]
- With `damage` ≤ 211 a captured icon is worth at least 100 − 90 = 10, so the "negative → 50" fallback does not arise from these paths. [BYTES seg7:0x2386 seg13:0x1afc]
- Destroying a virused icon is legal and destroying a good one is vandalism. [MANUAL Icons] [MANUAL Laws of Inner Space]

### Icon effects
The melt, the burn, the freeze and the virus overlay work on the icon strip DC DGROUP:0x3a02 (slot 13), where icon i's cell is at x = (S + g)·i + g, y = g, with S and g the `size` and `gap` of pool-0 record 0 unless stated. [BYTES seg7:0x479e-0x47ad seg7:0x4d95-0x4da4]
- **The words.** Word +0 of the [icon entry](structs.md#other-wave-tables) (DGROUP:0x57a8 + 0x23·i) counts an effect down, 80 for a melt and 89 for a burn; word +2 (DGROUP:0x57aa) is the flame frame, 0 while the icon does not burn. The icon record's signed heat byte +0x2f is −100 while the icon is frozen and −2 right after a thaw or an extinguish. [BYTES seg7:0x4ce0 seg7:0x47b4 seg7:0x4b39 seg7:0x4c46 seg7:0x4afd seg7:0x4bc7]
- **The melt.** In the icon mover's hit handler seg7:0x4a38, a hit by the rock pool DGROUP:0x934a whose rock has heat above 4 (signed) sets word +0 = 80 and plays seg10:0x754a(0x6a, 0, icon, 1). While word +0 ≠ 0 the icon mover calls seg7:0x4d86(i) on every pass, and when the word is then below 7 it sets it to 0 and calls seg7:0x4ffe(i) at the end of the same iteration; a melt therefore runs 74 passes, 80 down to 6. [BYTES seg7:0x4cc0-0x4cef seg7:0x322d-0x324b seg7:0x31fc-0x3206] core-0002 shows an icon cracked and then, after 0x20 IGNITE, its picture torn into vertical strips. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **seg7:0x4d86(i)** (cdecl) draws four `rand()`, r1 = ((r % 12 + 10) << `Size`) >> 1, r2 likewise, r3 = (r % 10) << `Size` with no shift back, r4 = ((r % 10 + 22) << `Size`) >> 1, and takes d = (2 << `Size`) >> 1. [BYTES seg7:0x4da7-0x4e0d]
  - With word +2 = 0 (the melt) it copies four columns d wide and S high of the strip cell at x + r, r = r1 … r4, from y = g − d to y = g (SRCCOPY), so each column slides down by d and takes the black gap in at the top, and lowers word +0 by 1. [BYTES seg7:0x4e14-0x4ed1]
  - With word +2 ≠ 0 (the burn) it draws a fifth `rand()` and puts r4 in r3's place when its bit 0 is set, copies the columns at r1 and r3 from y = g − trunc(d/2) to y = g in the strip cell and in each of the five cells x0 = (S + g)·(k − 1) + g, k = 1 … 5, of the icon's flame image DGROUP:0x3a04 + 2i, lowers word +0 only while word +2 < 7, and calls the [smoke start](world.md#smoke-seg70x5b68-and-seg70x5e04) seg7:0x5b68(0, i, 0) whenever word +0 < 50 and word +0 % 5 = 0. [BYTES seg7:0x4ed4-0x4ee0 seg7:0x4ee3-0x4f3f seg7:0x4f44-0x4fbf seg7:0x4fc1-0x4fcc seg7:0x4fd0-0x4fef]
- **seg7:0x4ffe(i), burnt out** (cdecl, called only at seg7:0x3206), PatBlts DGROUP:0x13dc BLACKNESS at record i's screen point with its `size`, sets `state` 1, calls seg7:0x5b68(0, i, 1) twice, seg7:0x5ae8(i, 0) (destroyed, [Counters](#counters-seg70x5ae8-and-seg70x5a46)), seg7:0x65aa(0, i) and seg8:0x99ce(0, i, −1, −1, 1). [BYTES seg7:0x500c-0x5031 seg7:0x503a seg7:0x5040-0x505a seg7:0x505c-0x5084]
- **seg7:0x477e(i), ignite** (cdecl), whose callers set word +2 = 2 first, plays seg10:0x754a(0x6a, 0, i, 1), sets word +0 = 89 and loads graphic 230 (0xe6, five 32 × 32 flame cells) with seg10:0x6c02(0xe6, 0, −1), the rescale seg10:0x6308(−1, −1, h, −1) and its ordinal-6 mask. Only while DC DGROUP:0x3a04 + 2i is 0 it creates that DC with the bitmap DGROUP:0x3aec + 2i of (5S + 6g) × (2g + S); it always blacks it. For k = 0 … 4 it copies the icon's strip cell below row h12 = (12 << `Size`) >> 1, S × (S − h12) from y = g + h12, into cell k at ((S + g)k + g, g + h12), then pastes flame cell k (source x = S·k) over the whole S × S cell with the mask (SRCAND 0x8800C6) and the graphic (SRCPAINT 0xEE0086). [BYTES seg7:0x478d-0x4796 seg7:0x47b0 seg7:0x47b4 seg7:0x47ba-0x47dc seg7:0x4832-0x4865 seg7:0x4896-0x48b6 seg7:0x48c0-0x490d seg7:0x4912-0x495e]
  - While word +2 ≠ 0 the icon is drawn from slot 14 + i, cell word +2 / 2 − 1 ([world.md](world.md#object-pictures-seg130x5a6a)), and seg7:0x49b6 releases the eight DCs at the wave end ([The tick takes it up](#the-tick-takes-it-up-seg70x610), step 7). [BYTES seg13:0x5d5b]
- **seg7:0x2512(i, k), the overlay** (cdecl): k = 1 ice, k = 0 the virus's green. While word +0 ≠ 0 it only calls seg7:0x65aa(0, i). Otherwise it loads graphic 360 (0x168, a 64 × 32 DIB whose cell 0 holds greens and cell 1 blues and white) with seg10:0x6c02(0x168, 0, −1), the rescale and its mask, pastes cell k (source x = S·k) at ((g + S)·i + g, g) with the mask (SRCAND) and the graphic (SRCPAINT), using record i's own `size` and `gap`, sets byte +0x1c8 + i of the current directory record to k + 1 and, for k = 0, the icon's +0x53 = 2, and ends with seg7:0x65aa(0, i). It plays no sound. [BYTES seg7:0x2521-0x252a seg7:0x252e-0x2547 seg7:0x2558-0x2581 seg7:0x258d-0x25c0 seg7:0x25d0-0x25ec seg7:0x2612-0x2628 seg7:0x262d-0x2637 seg7:0x263c-0x2642] [BYTES IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 360]
  - The push of 0x168 before seg10:0x6c02 is graphic id 360, not the string at DGROUP:0x168 ([pitfalls.md](pitfalls.md#reading-code)). [BYTES seg7:0x255f]
  - Its callers are the freeze (seg7:0x4c3c), the briefing (seg9:0x94ef, seg9:0x9540) and seg11:0xa3db in seg11:0xa234, a virus bolt on an icon: the green overlay with k = 0 ([Lightning](#lightning-seg110x9fdc-seg110xa234-seg110xa9ea), strike step 8). [BYTES seg7:0x4c3c seg9:0x94ef seg9:0x9540 seg11:0xa3db]
  - **At the briefing** seg9:0x94ba calls seg7:0x2512(k, byte − 1) for each k = 0 … 7 whose directory byte +0x1c8 + k is non-zero, and sets heat −100 for a byte of 2; in a kind-2 wave (DGROUP:0x9320 = 2) it then calls seg7:0x2512(k, 0) for every k when DGROUP:0x9334 = 0, without `rand()`, and otherwise only when `rand()` % 3 = 0 ([rng.md](rng.md#callers-and-range-mapping)). [BYTES seg9:0x94cd-0x94f4 seg9:0x94f7-0x9519 seg9:0x951e-0x9545]
- **The backup, image slot 32.** seg10:0x506c, called at seg9:0x5d6b right after seg9:0xb2a in every briefing, copies the strip cells (38k + 6, 6), 32 × 32, k < DGROUP:0x92ec, to DC DGROUP:0x3a28 at (32k, 0), creating the DC and the bitmap DGROUP:0x3b10 of 32·DGROUP:0x92ec × 32 when DGROUP:0x3a28 is 0. The load seg9:0x22b4 stores the dword 0x20008 at DGROUP:0x92ec, 8 records for pool 0, on every path. The copy comes before the crack loop and reads the unscaled geometry, so the backup holds the bare icons. [BYTES seg9:0x5d6b seg10:0x5083-0x50b5 seg10:0x50bf-0x50f9 seg9:0x24d3]
  - **seg7:0x5660(i), restore,** StretchBlts backup cell (32i, 0, 32 × 32) onto the strip cell (S × S, SRCCOPY) and calls seg7:0x233c(0, i, 0). Both callers, the mover when the heat reaches −2 (seg7:0x31cd) and the FIREBALL thaw (seg7:0x4afd), have just made the heat −2, so seg7:0x233c skips its damage and crack part: a thawed icon loses its cracks until its next hit. [BYTES seg7:0x566d-0x56a2 seg7:0x56a7-0x56af seg7:0x2364-0x2372 seg7:0x31cd-0x31d8 seg7:0x4afd-0x4b06]
  - **seg7:0x56ba(i)** copies the strip cell into the backup the other way; its only caller is the extinguish (seg7:0x4c21), so a melted picture becomes the backup. [BYTES seg7:0x56c7-0x56fe seg7:0x4c21]
- **SNOWBALLS and FIREBALLS.** seg7:0x4a38 takes these cases when the touch test fails, before its tail seg7:0x4c54, for P = DGROUP:0x9328 and R the pool-5 record, whose owner is pool DGROUP:0x5a50 + 0x14·R, record DGROUP:0x5a52 + 0x14·R ([Hit dispatcher](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2)): [BYTES seg7:0x4aa1 seg7:0x4bb9 seg7:0x4c54]
  - **Thaw.** `mass` 0x78 (a FIREBALL) on an icon with heat < 0: four seg7:0x5b68(0, icon, 1), seg10:0x754a(0x76, 0, icon, 1) only when the heat is below −2, heat −2 and seg7:0x5660(icon); when the owner pool is DGROUP:0x9324 the owner ship's words +0x36 and +0x38 (DGROUP:0x70c6/0x70c8) become 2. [BYTES seg7:0x4ab2 seg7:0x4ab9-0x4ad3 seg7:0x4add-0x4aed seg7:0x4afd-0x4b06 seg7:0x4b0a-0x4b24]
  - **Burn.** `mass` 0x78 with heat ≥ 0 does nothing more while word +2 ≠ 0. Otherwise word +2 = 2, the dword DGROUP:0x57a4 + 0x23·icon = now + 4000, seg7:0x477e(icon) and +0x53 = 0; before seg7:0x477e, when the icon's `damage` is 0, its +0x53 is 1, the owner pool is the literal 14 and that ship's words +0x32 = 0, +0x34 = the icon and +0x38 = 1, it calls seg13:0xb6f2(owner, 1). DGROUP:0x57a4 + 0x23·i has two writers, this one and seg7:0x45e5 in the pool-5 mover, and no reader at that displacement. [BYTES seg7:0x4b2b-0x4b49 seg7:0x4b4e-0x4b95 seg7:0x4b9d-0x4ba9 seg7:0x4c4b-0x4c4f seg7:0x45e5]
  - **Extinguish.** `mass` 0xc8 (a SNOWBALL) on an icon with word +2 > 0: words +0 and +2 = 0, heat −2, four seg7:0x5b68(0, icon, 1), +0x53 = 1, seg10:0x754a(0x76, 0, icon, 1) and seg7:0x56ba(icon). [BYTES seg7:0x4bb9-0x4c21]
  - **Freeze.** `mass` 0xc8 with word +2 ≤ 0 and heat ≥ 0: seg7:0x2512(icon, 1), heat −100 and +0x53 = 0; with heat < 0 neither. [BYTES seg7:0x4c27-0x4c4f]
  - The frozen icon is the manual's: it has icicles and cannot be captured until fire thaws it. Icons can be torched with fire, frozen with snow and melted by hot rocks. [MANUAL Icons] [MANUAL Dealing With Other Ships]
  - The pool-5 mover seg7:0x40c4 has a twin branch, seg7:0x45a9–0x463f, which tests only the hit object's `mass` 0xa0, in any pool: a FIREBALL (`mass` 0x78) on an icon entry whose flame word is 0 ignites it and a SNOWBALL (`mass` 0xc8) on one whose flame word is above 0 clears the entry's words +0 and +2 and calls seg7:0x5b68(hit pool, hit rec, 1) four times. Icons have `mass` 0xa0 but are pool 0, so in a wave only a pool-5 … 14 object of mass exactly 160 reaches it, indexing the icon entries with its own record number ([Snowballs and fireballs](#snowballs-and-fireballs-seg130x1318-seg70x40c4-seg70x570a)). [BYTES seg7:0x45a9 seg7:0x4623-0x463d seg7:0x45c2-0x45f0 seg7:0x45f2-0x463d]

### Target validity (seg3:0x0)
- seg3:0x0(pool, rec) returns 0 for a negative pool or record, a pool above DGROUP:0x9324 or a record at or above the allocated count DGROUP:0x92ec + 2·pool. [BYTES seg3:0xe seg3:0x14 seg3:0x1a seg3:0x2f seg3:0x33]
- For pool 14 (the constant) and a record other than the player it returns 1 for `state` 0, and for another state when ship byte +0x147 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg3:0x35 seg3:0x3b seg3:0x51 seg3:0x5d seg3:0x62 seg3:0x64]
- Otherwise `state` ≠ 0 gives 0; pools 0, 7, 1, 9 and 11 give 1; pool 2 gives 1 when DGROUP:0x52fe + 0x24·rec = 15 or (DGROUP:0x5304 + 0x24·rec = 14 and DGROUP:0x5306 + 0x24·rec = the player); pool 8 gives 1 when DGROUP:0x3d6c + 0x34·rec = 2 and DGROUP:0x3d5a + 0x34·rec > 0; any other record gives 1 only when seg13:0x371c returns 2. [BYTES seg3:0x78 seg3:0x7f seg3:0x85 seg3:0x8b seg3:0x9b seg3:0xa2 seg3:0xac seg3:0xb2 seg3:0xb8 seg3:0xc8 seg3:0xcf seg3:0xda seg3:0xe2 seg3:0xe5]
- seg13:0x371c(pool, rec) returns 0 outside pool 12, 1 for an even pool-12 record and 2 for an odd one. [BYTES seg13:0x3729 seg13:0x3733 seg13:0x3739 seg13:0x373e]
- **Names.** seg3:0x1542 names a pool-0 record "Icon " + its record index (string 152 with the last 2 characters cut, seg6:0xf1a), with caps ≠ 0 adding " (" + the file name DGROUP:0x57b4 + 0x23·rec + ")"; pool 12 (seg13:0x371c ≠ 0) is 585 "Exit Gate", pool 3 153 "Rock", pool 9 170 "Virus", pool 2 154 "Defender", pool 7 347 "Ambulance" and pool 1 892 "Icon Fragment". Pools 3 and 9 add 10000 to the id in mode 7. [BYTES seg3:0x15ce seg3:0x15e0 seg3:0x15e4 seg3:0x164c seg3:0x1659 seg3:0x166f seg3:0x167c seg3:0x168c seg3:0x169e seg3:0x1747 seg3:0x1772 seg3:0x16f8 seg3:0x16e6 seg3:0x16c6 seg3:0x16ab] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 10] ids 152–154 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 11] id 170 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 37] id 585 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 22] id 347 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 56] id 892 The oracle's icons read ALPHA 0, BRAVO 1, CHARLIE 2. [ORACLE wave-0001]

## Scoring
- The score and rating words, the kill scorer and the other awards are in [globals.md](globals.md#scoring). The capture awards are listed there as seg13:0x1c32 and seg13:0x1b4a; they are paid as [Capturing an icon](#capturing-an-icon) describes. [DESIGN]
- **Display.** seg11:0x1bcc calls seg11:0x4a0(0) when the score differs from DGROUP:0x5018, which reaches the digit routine seg11:0x4ea when seg11:0x2120 passes ([world.md](world.md#score)). [BYTES seg11:0x1bda seg11:0x1bde seg11:0x1be3 seg11:0x1be8 seg11:0x4b8]
- **The digits** are signed: seg6:0xeb8 prints a negative score with "-", and on the narrow layout the DIGITAL text blacks that sign's cell ([world.md](world.md#score)). core-0002's panel reads 000 at the start, 4000 and 8000 after captures and 900 after a rock kill, right-aligned. [BYTES seg6:0xec9 seg11:0x2225] [ORACLE core-0002]
- **The level.** seg9:0x484c, whose only caller is seg9:0x3229 at the start of the wave generator seg9:0x3202, sets DGROUP:0x9334 to k + 1 for the last k of the 12-dword table DGROUP:0x9bc below the rating, compared as signed 32-bit values (high words `jg`/`jl`, low words `jae`); a tie does not count. So a capture or a kill changes DGROUP:0x9334 only at the next wave's generation ([globals.md](globals.md#scoring)). [BYTES seg9:0x3229 seg9:0x485b seg9:0x4863 seg9:0x4870 seg9:0x4874 seg9:0x4876 seg9:0x4878 seg9:0x487c seg9:0x4882]
- **No wave-end bonus.** The additions to DGROUP:0x1e58 are seg4:0x2ef8, seg7:0x196a, seg8:0x6785, seg8:0x67a6, seg8:0x7971, seg8:0x7c3b, seg8:0x92cd, seg13:0x1b4a, seg13:0x1c32 and seg16:0x7228; the only other writes are the stores at seg9:0xc4e and seg6:0x968a. None lies in seg9:0x6bc2, seg9:0x6cc4, seg9:0x2828 or the debriefing seg9:0x6fee. [BYTES seg4:0x2ef8 seg7:0x196a seg8:0x6785 seg8:0x67a6 seg8:0x7971 seg8:0x7c3b seg8:0x92cd seg13:0x1b4a seg13:0x1c32 seg16:0x7228 seg9:0xc4e seg6:0x968a]

## The wave end

### Leaving through the gate (seg12:0x1776)
- The gate mover seg12:0x1776(p) tests gate p's beam with seg12:0x1f7a(p) ([world.md](world.md#gates)). [BYTES seg12:0x1dca]
- **The player.** When seg12:0x1f7a returns the player's ship with object byte +0x2e = 0 or `cloaked` (+0xe8) ≠ 0, it calls seg12:0x2288(14, player) and sets DGROUP:0x1e80 = now + 1000. Nothing on this path tests DGROUP:0x91fc or directory byte +0x1d0: the player can leave with icons left. [BYTES seg12:0x1dca seg12:0x1dd5 seg12:0x1dff seg12:0x1e11 seg12:0x1e17 seg12:0x1e28 seg12:0x1e35 seg12:0x1e3b]
- **Anything else** returned with byte +0x2e = 0 (or a +0xe8 ship) gets seg12:0x2288, a BLACKNESS PatBlt of its square and seg10:0x754a(0x27, pool, rec, 1). [BYTES seg12:0x1e45 seg12:0x1e63 seg12:0x1e81 seg12:0x1eb2 seg12:0x1ebb seg12:0x1ebd]
- **Exit sign.** While DGROUP:0x91fc = 0 the gate toggles DGROUP:0xd0a every 400 ms (deadline DGROUP:0x1928) and BitBlts two halves of bitmap OUT_SIGN (DGROUP:0xd0c) into DC DGROUP:0x39ea at frame x = 13·DGROUP:0xd0a, scaled. [BYTES seg12:0x1b3b seg12:0x1b42 seg12:0x1b54 seg12:0x1b6a seg12:0x1b79 seg12:0x1c02 seg12:0x1c58 seg12:0x1c64]
- The manual's "all icons must be captured or destroyed before you can return from Inner Space" is the game's end, not a condition for leaving a wave. [MANUAL Icons] [MANUAL General]

### seg12:0x2288(pool, rec): an object goes through
In this order: [BYTES seg12:0x2288]
1. **Shots.** A pool-DGROUP:0x9328 object with byte +0x2e ≠ 0 returns at once, without sound. Otherwise it plays seg10:0x754a(0x27, pool, rec, 1), sets word DGROUP:0x5a4c + 0x14·rec = word DGROUP:0x5a4e + 0x14·rec − 7 and +0x2e = 0xff, and returns; the word pair is the pool-5 entry's (age, life) ([structs.md](structs.md#pool-5-entries-0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x5a4c-and-trails-0x24-bytes-at-dgroup0x5eac)): the ball stops drawing and colliding and ends 12 passes later while its trail fades. [BYTES seg12:0x2297 seg12:0x22ad seg12:0x22b2 seg12:0x22c3 seg12:0x22cf seg12:0x22d3 seg12:0x22d6 seg12:0x22de seg12:0x22e3]
2. **A dead ship** (`alive` (+0xec) = 0) goes to seg12:0x274a(pool, rec) and returns. [BYTES seg12:0x22f3 seg12:0x22ff seg12:0x2305]
3. **The meeting note.** For a ship whose word +0x52 is 2 … 4, +0x58 = rec and +0x54 = 14, DGROUP:0x93b6 takes word +0xd6 (`roster_index`) of ship +0x56 when rec is the player and that ship's object has `state` 0, or rec's own +0xd6 when its +0x56 is the player; a value of −1 is not stored. The debriefing turns it into string 629 ([The debriefing](#the-debriefing)). [BYTES seg12:0x2313 seg12:0x231a seg12:0x2324 seg12:0x232a seg12:0x2331 seg12:0x2344 seg12:0x2359 seg12:0x235f seg12:0x236a]
4. **The buffer.** An on-screen object (`on_screen` (+0x1a) ≠ 0) gets seg12:0x2556(pool, rec), and a ship gets ship word +0x2e (DGROUP:0x70be) = 1. seg12:0x2556 draws nothing on DGROUP:0x13dc: unless the pool is 1 or +0x3c is already set, it gives the object a private compatible DC +0x3c and bitmap +0x3e of side `size` + 2·`gap` and copies the current sprite cell into it from DC +0x3a (SRCCOPY). [BYTES seg12:0x237b seg12:0x2389 seg12:0x238f seg12:0x239c seg12:0x2565 seg12:0x2579 seg12:0x2597 seg12:0x25cd seg12:0x25ee seg12:0x2694]
5. **The vanish.** On screen: seg7:0x13b2(1, pool, rec) ([The vanish](#the-vanish-seg70x13b2-and-seg70x158e)). Off screen: `state` 1, seg7:0x2080(pool, rec) and seg10:0x754a(0x27, pool, rec, 1). [BYTES seg12:0x23b0 seg12:0x23bf seg12:0x23d7 seg12:0x23e3 seg12:0x23f5]
6. **A team-7 ship with its prisoner.** A ship whose design word DGROUP:0x970a is 7, with ship word +0x54 = 14, object +0x36 = 14 and object +0x38 = ship word +0x56, takes (14, object +0x38) through seg12:0x2288 too; when that is the player it sets DGROUP:0x1e80 = now + 1000 and DGROUP:0x93a4 = 1, the Hall of Justice. [BYTES seg12:0x2410 seg12:0x2417 seg12:0x242c seg12:0x2436 seg12:0x2444 seg12:0x2458 seg12:0x2465 seg12:0x246b seg12:0x2472]
7. **The player held by team 7.** For the player with `owner_pool` (+0x32) = 14 and design word DGROUP:0x970a of ship `owner_rec` (+0x34) = 7, DGROUP:0x93a4 = 1. [BYTES seg12:0x2484 seg12:0x2498 seg12:0x249e seg12:0x24aa seg12:0x24b1]
8. With DGROUP:0x92e8 ≠ 0, ship 1 clears the player's `fixed` (+0x54). A pool-0 object calls seg7:0x5ae8(rec, 0): an icon that drifts through a gate counts as destroyed, with no score and no resource. In a kind-7 wave DGROUP:0x668a = −1 when no other live ship remains. [BYTES seg12:0x2288 seg12:0x24d7 seg12:0x24e2]

### The vanish (seg7:0x13b2 and seg7:0x158e)
- **Start.** seg7:0x13b2(mode, pool, rec) calls MessageBeep(0) for a negative pool. For a pool ≤ 14 it returns before any sound when the signed byte +0x2e > 0, and otherwise plays seg10:0x754a(0x27, pool, rec, 1); a pool above 14 skips the sound. [BYTES seg7:0x13c8 seg7:0x13cd seg7:0x13d1 seg7:0x13e1 seg7:0x13e6 seg7:0x13f2]
  - With a free slot of the four at DGROUP:0x226c + 0x5a·k (−1 = free) it stores +0x2e = mode. Neither mode blacks the screen DC at the start; mode 2 blacks the object's own copy DC +0x3c, mode 1 nothing. [BYTES seg7:0x1488 seg7:0x148c seg7:0x14b2 seg7:0x150d seg7:0x1511 seg7:0x1548]
  - With no free slot it leaves +0x2e unchanged and blacks the object's square on DGROUP:0x13dc (pool ≤ 14); the sound has still played. [BYTES seg7:0x154d seg7:0x1553 seg7:0x1580]
- **End of a mode-1 vanish** (seg7:0x158e, the object's +0x2e = 1). It blacks the object's square on DGROUP:0x13dc. [BYTES seg7:0x17f7 seg7:0x1819]
  - A ship whose ship word +0x2e is 2 instead gets +0x2e = 0xfe; with `PlayMode` ≠ 0, when the player's goal (+0x32/+0x34) is that ship and the player's +0x38 = 1, it also sets dword DGROUP:0x5c0 = now + 1200 and DGROUP:0x58c = 1 and formats string 1422 into DGROUP:0x8630. That path goes to the common tail seg7:0x1acb without seg12:0x26a4. The gate sets ship word +0x2e = 1, so a ship leaving through the gate does not take it. [BYTES seg7:0x181e seg7:0x182b seg7:0x1842 seg7:0x184c seg7:0x1850 seg7:0x1871 seg7:0x1898 seg12:0x239c] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 89] id 1422
  - Otherwise the object gets `state` 1 and seg7:0x65aa(pool, rec). [BYTES seg7:0x18b7 seg7:0x18c5]
  - **The 800 award.** For a pool other than 14, 6, 7 and 2 it searches the 8-entry hit history from its newest entry: word DGROUP:0x937a + 2k holds lo16(frame# / 10) of the hit, an unsigned 32-bit division (seg5:0x2b4), and bytes DGROUP:0x938a + k and DGROUP:0x9392 + k its pool and record. For each entry it takes the 32-bit difference frame# / 10 − sext(word) and ends the search at the first whose high word is non-zero or whose low word is ≥ 20 (unsigned); an entry whose sign-extended bytes name another object is skipped. The first entry naming this object plays seg10:0x754a(0x73, pool, rec, 1), calls seg3:0x6ec(player, 7, 0, 0x70), adds 800 (0x320) to the score dword DGROUP:0x1e58, not to the rating, and ends it. [BYTES seg7:0x18cd seg7:0x18ed seg7:0x18fb seg7:0x1905 seg7:0x190a seg7:0x191c seg7:0x191d seg7:0x191f seg7:0x1921 seg7:0x1923 seg7:0x1925 seg7:0x1928 seg7:0x1931 seg7:0x1936 seg7:0x1940 seg7:0x1941 seg7:0x1947 seg7:0x1950 seg7:0x1962 seg7:0x196a seg8:0x8240]
    - **The window.** With the push d frames before the vanish's end, the entry is always found for d ≤ 190, for 191 ≤ d ≤ 199 only when the push frame mod 10 ≤ 199 − d, and never for d ≥ 200: 190 frames are 5.3 s at 36 frames per second. That holds while the push frame is below 327 680 (frame# / 10 < 0x8000; beyond it every difference has a non-zero high word and nothing is found) and for a record below 128, since the stored bytes are compared sign-extended. An entry from an earlier wave, whose frame# was reset at the placement, gives a negative difference and ends the search behind the newer entries. [BYTES seg7:0x190a seg7:0x1923 seg7:0x1928 seg7:0x1931 seg7:0x1940 seg8:0x823b seg8:0x8240 seg9:0x6689]
    - seg8:0x81f8(pool, rec) moves entries 0 … 6 to 1 … 7 and writes the hit into entry 0. Its only callers are seg8:0x6259 in the hit dispatcher and seg8:0x7290 in the shot hit test seg8:0x6f3c. [BYTES seg8:0x820f seg8:0x8240 seg8:0x8246 seg8:0x824c seg8:0x6259 seg8:0x7290] The dispatcher pushes a hit on an icon credited to the player ([Hit dispatcher](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2), Credit bookkeeping). The shot hit test pushes (target pool, target rec) on a live barrel's hit when the slot's owner words +0x7a and +0x7c are DGROUP:0x9324 and DGROUP:0x9646 and the target pool is not DGROUP:0x9324, before the impact, so every player gun hit on anything but a ship enters the history, one whose impact result is 0 included ([Shot hit test](#shot-hit-test-seg80x3688-and-seg80x6f3c)). A rock therefore enters it only through a gun hit, never through a ram. [BYTES seg8:0x724e seg8:0x7254 seg8:0x7271 seg8:0x7279 seg8:0x7283 seg8:0x7289 seg8:0x7290]
    - seg8:0x81f8 stores lo16 of seg5:0x2b4(frame#, 10) in entry 0 after moving entries 6 → 7 down to 0 → 1. [BYTES seg8:0x8205 seg8:0x822d seg8:0x823b seg8:0x8240 seg8:0x8246 seg8:0x824c seg5:0x2b4]
    - **Message 0x70** is case 111 of seg3:0x6ec's switch: it pushes far DGROUP:0x380c (the pilot name), 0x2b0 (688), 4, mode 2 and 0x428 (1064) and joins seg3:0x915, which formats into DGROUP:0x8db0 with seg10:0xa332: string 1064 "He Shoots! He Scores! %R %s!!", `%R` drawing one `rand()` % 4 + 688 ("Well Done, ", "Way To Go, ", "That's The Ticket, " or "Right On, ") and `%s` the pilot name, as messages 0x5b and 0x5e do. The case plays no sound itself. The sender is pool 7, so the panel reads "MESSAGE from" / "Ambulance" ([Messages](#messages-seg30x6ec)). [BYTES seg3:0x1351 seg3:0x1089 seg3:0x108a seg3:0x108d seg3:0x1090 seg3:0x1092 seg3:0x1094 seg3:0x1097 seg3:0x915 seg3:0x919 seg3:0x921] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 67] id 1064 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 44] ids 688–691
    - **The sound.** seg10:0x754a's sequence switch plays 0x73 as slot 0x42 (CROWD) at once and slot 0x43 ("He shoots, he scores!") 700 ms later ([sound.md](sound.md#inspace-wrappers)), at the vanished object's position, so a trace shows the two slots and never 0x73. [BYTES seg10:0x765d seg10:0x7605 seg10:0x7617 seg7:0x1949]
  - A pool-6 object then gets its weapon slot reset and +0x2e = 0; any other gets +0x2e = 0xff and seg12:0x3778. Both then call seg12:0x26a4(pool, rec), seg7:0x2080(pool, rec) and seg2:0x170e. [BYTES seg7:0x1977 seg7:0x19ae seg7:0x19d1 seg7:0x19d6 seg7:0x19e6 seg7:0x19f4 seg7:0x19fa]
- **An icon that vanishes** has +0x2e = 1 from seg7:0x13b2 before seg12:0x2288 calls seg7:0x5ae8, so the recount seg7:0x5a46, which needs `state` 0, +0x2e = 0 and +0x51 = 0, no longer counts it as live. With all four slots busy +0x2e stays 0 and `state` stays 0: the icon counts as destroyed, still counts as live and stays in play. An off-screen icon gets `state` 1 first and is not counted. [BYTES seg7:0x14b2 seg12:0x23bf seg12:0x24e2 seg7:0x5a78 seg7:0x5a86 seg12:0x23d7]
- In core-0001 the player's sprite breaks up in place while the view scrolls on, after the HYPER sound (slot 0x27). [ORACLE core-0001]

### The tick takes it up (seg7:0x610)
- **Order.** The wave case tests DGROUP:0x1e80 at seg7:0x610, after the pending-input branch (DGROUP:0x8e60 ≠ 0, which leaves at seg7:0x1192), the DGROUP:0x9130 test at seg7:0x594 (which jumps to seg7:0x99a) and the DGROUP:0x1e88 block (seg7:0x5ce–0x60b). [BYTES seg7:0x4e6 seg7:0x519 seg7:0x594 seg7:0x5ce seg7:0x608 seg7:0x610]
- **The 1000 ms.** Until DGROUP:0x1e80 is due (unsigned) the pass goes on to the DGROUP:0x1e84 test at seg7:0x6ac, the DGROUP:0x5b4 and DGROUP:0x5b0 timers and the normal wave pass: the input router at seg7:0x863 when DGROUP:0x8e62 ≠ 0, and the fire poll seg11:0x4db0 when DGROUP:0x1a = 0 and the player's `state` = 0. So the wave keeps running for the second after the passage. [BYTES seg7:0x627 seg7:0x6ac seg7:0x863 seg7:0x87a seg7:0x881]
  - core-0001 shows the music paused (MCI_PAUSE) 1.004 s and 1.000 s after the HYPER sound in two exits. [ORACLE core-0001]
- **When due,** in a player game (DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0), the tick sets `PlayMode` = 1 when it is above 1, stores now into DGROUP:0x1e84 (death, [shell.md](shell.md#screen-states)) when the player's `alive` (+0xec) = 0, else calls seg9:0x6bc2, clears DGROUP:0x1e80 and ends the pass at seg7:0x1192, with no frame. [BYTES seg7:0x610 seg7:0x61a seg7:0x61e seg7:0x62b seg7:0x630 seg7:0x6dd seg7:0x6e4 seg7:0x6f0 seg7:0x6f7 seg7:0x6fa seg7:0x700 seg7:0x6a0 seg7:0x6a9]
- In the demo the same expiry runs seg6:0x92b8 when directory byte +0x1d0 = 2 and DGROUP:0xd26 ≠ 0, goes to state 8 when DGROUP:0x93a4 ≠ 0, and otherwise calls seg9:0x2828, seg6:0x885c(0, 0) when the player is alive and DGROUP:0x9516 = 0, and seg9:0xd6c. [BYTES seg7:0x62b seg7:0x652 seg7:0x659 seg7:0x66a seg7:0x675 seg7:0x67c seg7:0x687 seg7:0x68f seg7:0x69a]
- **seg9:0x6bc2** starts with seg10:0x7480(−1, 1) and clears DGROUP:0x1e74 before its kind-6 test. In a race wave each other ship with `goal_pool` = 0 is credited every pool-0 record still in `state` 0: `resources` += (`damage` × −90)/210 + 100, +0xd2 += 1 and seg7:0x5ae8(rec, 1), with no clamp and `state` left at 0, so every such ship is credited the same icons. [BYTES seg9:0x6bc2 seg9:0x6bd0 seg9:0x6bd4 seg9:0x6bdf seg9:0x6be3 seg9:0x6c22 seg9:0x6c59]
  - With DGROUP:0x93a4 ≠ 0 it sets state 8, DGROUP:0x929c = 0, calls seg16:0x6928(0), sets DGROUP:0x9346 = −1 and clears `alive` (+0xec) of team-7 ships escorting the player, and returns; otherwise it calls seg9:0x6cc4(0). [BYTES seg9:0x6c5b seg9:0x6c60 seg9:0x6c62 seg9:0x6cb5 seg9:0x6cb8]
- **seg9:0x6cc4(arg)**, in order: [BYTES seg9:0x6cc4]
  1. seg10:0x7db8, which calls ISPACE.DLL ordinal 7 through DGROUP:0x3c54 with command 2 and the dword DGROUP:0x5a0 ([ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes)). [BYTES seg9:0x6cd1 seg10:0x7dc2 seg10:0x7dc7 seg10:0x7dc9]
  2. seg13:0x65f2(1), whose case 1 calls seg13:0x5850, seg7:0x63f2 and seg13:0x6362, all without arguments; seg13:0x5850 destroys the window DGROUP:0x95fc and clears the word, and seg7:0x63f2 destroys the icon column ([Icon column](#the-icon-after-capture)). What seg13:0x6362 closes is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg9:0x6cd8 seg13:0x65f2 seg13:0x669b seg13:0x5850 seg7:0x63f2]
  3. DGROUP:0x1e74 = 0, state DGROUP:0x90f4 = 2 (the debriefing) and DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0; in a kind-6 wave seg9:0x6db0, which fills every ship's `fuel` to its tank. [BYTES seg9:0x6ce1 seg9:0x6ce5 seg9:0x6ceb seg9:0x6cee seg9:0x6cf6 seg9:0x6dc5 seg9:0x6dd4]
  4. When DGROUP:0x92e0 ≠ 0: GlobalUnlock, DGROUP:0x3d4c = 0, GlobalFree and DGROUP:0x92e0 = 0. The block's content is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg9:0x6cf9 seg9:0x6d04 seg9:0x6d09 seg9:0x6d16 seg9:0x6d1b]
  5. seg9:0x1092: DeleteObject of each non-zero word of the 32 at DGROUP:0x4e94, which it zeroes. [BYTES seg9:0x6d21 seg9:0x1092]
  6. seg12:0x26a4(p, rec) for p = 0 … 14 and every rec below the allocated count DGROUP:0x92ec + 2p, freeing the private buffers. [BYTES seg9:0x6d30 seg9:0x6d38 seg9:0x6d3e seg9:0x6d42 seg9:0x6d4f seg9:0x6d53]
  7. seg11:0x2f0 (the HUD windows) and seg7:0x49b6, which releases the 8 DCs DGROUP:0x3a04 + 2i that are non-zero: SelectObject DGROUP:0x3990 + 2i back, SelectPalette DGROUP:0x3a78 + 2i when non-zero, DeleteDC, DeleteObject of the bitmap DGROUP:0x3aec + 2i when non-zero, and both words zeroed. [BYTES seg9:0x6d5c seg9:0x6d61 seg7:0x49b6]
  8. PatBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, 0, 0, DGROUP:0x90ea, DGROUP:0x10, BLACKNESS), the whole client. [BYTES seg9:0x6d66 seg9:0x6d6e seg9:0x6d72 seg9:0x6d7a]
  9. DGROUP:0x93b2 = DGROUP:0x9332, its only writer in the module; its readers are [OPEN]. [BYTES seg9:0x6d82]
  10. seg9:0x2828 (below), then seg6:0x7266, which unlocks and frees DGROUP:0x94da and DGROUP:0x94dc when non-zero and clears the lock pointers DGROUP:0x6b08 and DGROUP:0x6b0c. [BYTES seg9:0x6d85 seg9:0x6d8a seg6:0x7266]
  11. DGROUP:0x94c4 = 1 and DGROUP:0x9330 = 0, and the debriefing frame seg9:0x6dec(1) only when arg ≠ 0; with arg 0 the first state-2 frame draws it. [BYTES seg9:0x6d8f seg9:0x6d95 seg9:0x6d9b seg9:0x6da4]

### seg9:0x2828: the wave into the directory record
- Its difficulty outputs (DGROUP:0x9332 and the one `rand()` of DGROUP:0x932c) are in [world.md](world.md#difficulty-inputs). [BYTES seg9:0x2a17 seg9:0x2b25 seg9:0x2b3d seg9:0x2b4d]
- It calls seg13:0x2688 for every ship and sets DGROUP:0x9346 from a team-7 ship. seg13:0x2688 only reads globals and returns a value, which the loop discards, so the loop's only effect is the team-7 flag. In kind-6 waves with DGROUP:0x91f8 ≠ 0 and (DGROUP:0x8f70 ≠ 0 or the demo) it writes the race tables through seg6:0x8b90, seg6:0x8cb2, seg6:0x8dfe and seg6:0x8eb4. [BYTES seg9:0x2828 seg9:0x2848 seg13:0x2688]
- **Meal re-check.** With DGROUP:0x91f8 > 0 it calls seg10:0x9672, which sets DGROUP:0x9214 to 3, 4, 1 or 2 when hhmm + 1 (seg5:0x4c0) lies strictly between the INI value and the value + 30 for `Lunch` (1200), `Breakfast` (800), `Coffee` (1000) and `Tea` (1530), tested in that order, and to 0 otherwise. seg10:0x552(4, 1) follows only when DGROUP:0x9214 changed, and seg10:0x565a always. [BYTES seg9:0x2aee seg9:0x2af0 seg9:0x2af6 seg9:0x2afe seg9:0x2b07 seg9:0x2b0f seg10:0x9672]
- **Per ship,** with DGROUP:0x91f8 > 0, every ship gets seg11:0xb09c(0, i), the flame off, and a ship with `roster_index` (+0xd6) ≠ −1 its roster writes ([formats.md](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)). Among them roster word +0x04 = `resources` (+0xce), and roster word +0x02 = the credit dword +0xdc, capped at 0x4fff6 (signed) and stored back, divided by 10 (seg5:0x110). So the wave end itself, not only the save, carries the credit and the resources into the roster, and the credit loses its last digit there. [BYTES seg9:0x2e64 seg9:0x2e69 seg9:0x2e73 seg9:0x2e78 seg9:0x2cdb seg9:0x2d09 seg9:0x2d15]
  - core-0001 carries the ship's damage into the next wave: 39, 9, 39, 1 at the start of wave 3. [ORACLE core-0001]
- It sets DGROUP:0x931c to the player's `roster_index`. [BYTES seg9:0x2e88 seg9:0x2e8c]
- **Remaining icons.** It packs the `state`-0 pool-0 records into directory slots 0, 1, …, storing `damage` in word +0x1d2 + 2s and byte +0x1c8 + s = 2 for heat +0x2f < 0 and 1 for `capturable` (+0x53) = 2 (1 overwriting 2), and zeroes the remaining slots up to 8; it does not clear +0x1c8 + s for a plain icon. [BYTES seg9:0x2f82 seg9:0x3077]
- **Directory counts.** It zeroes directory bytes +0xa3, +0xa0 and +0xa1, then counts into them the `state`-0 pool-9 records below DGROUP:0x9418 and the `state`-0 rocks below DGROUP:0x940c of `mass` 350 and of `mass` 1400. [BYTES seg9:0x3089 seg9:0x3099 seg9:0x30a9 seg9:0x30cf seg9:0x310b seg9:0x3135]
