## Weapons

### Two fire paths
- **Gun.** seg11:0x4db0 calls seg11:0x4e0a(player) only when `PlayMode` = 0 and the player's dword +0x84 is below now (unsigned), on GetAsyncKeyState(VK_SHIFT) bit 0x8000, else GetAsyncKeyState(VK_SPACE), else the joystick word DGROUP:0x1042 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg11:0x4dba seg11:0x4dc8 seg11:0x4dce seg11:0x4dd6 seg11:0x4ddc seg11:0x4de3 seg11:0x4de8 seg11:0x4def seg11:0x4df4 seg11:0x4e00]
  - The player-`state` test is in the caller seg7:0x87a, which skips the poll while the player's `state` ≠ 0 ([world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 4). [BYTES seg7:0x871 seg7:0x87a seg7:0x87f seg7:0x881]
- **Weapon system.** On WM_KEYUP, VK_RETURN and 'Z' reach seg11:0xac86 through seg11:0x6e11 when `PlayMode` = 0. On WM_KEYDOWN, Enter and Z call seg11:0xac86 only when `PlayMode` = 0 and the selected weapon byte is 13 (WILDCARD), so for weapon 8 a key press does nothing and the release launches. [BYTES seg11:0x6d2e seg11:0x6d30 seg11:0x6d4a seg11:0x6e11 seg11:0x6e16 seg11:0x6f58 seg11:0x6f5f seg11:0x6f69 seg11:0x6f6e seg11:0x6f73 seg11:0x6f74]
  - Both pass the general gate of seg11:0x6b64 ([world.md](world.md#key-messages-seg110x6b64)): a Z released while the player's lock-out +0x84 runs, during a collision tumble for example, is dropped, not kept. [BYTES seg11:0x6c75 seg11:0x6c9b seg11:0x6cd1]
  - In the demo seg11:0x6b64 ignores every WM_KEYUP after the ESC/'R' block, so Z and Enter fire nothing. [BYTES seg11:0x6d19 seg11:0x6d1e]
- The manual: the gun fires on SHIFT or SPACE and a weapon system on Z or RETURN, and Tab changes the weapon. [MANUAL QuickStart] [MANUAL General] The oracle's Z launches one missile per press and release. [ORACLE wave-0001] [ORACLE core-0002]

### The gun (seg11:0x4e0a)
- seg11:0x4e0a(s) (Pascal) stores 7 in DGROUP:0x594, the gun slot count. [BYTES seg11:0x4e19]
- **Gate**, all needed:
  - not (s = DGROUP:0x952e with DGROUP:0x9e49 in 3 … 6); [BYTES seg11:0x4e1f seg11:0x4e3a]
  - gun health, ship word +0x28, ≠ 0; [BYTES seg11:0x4e43]
  - ship dword +0x8c = 0 or +0x8c + dword +0xa0 ≤ frame#; [BYTES seg11:0x4e51 seg11:0x4e68]
  - dword +0xa0 + 5 ≤ frame#, so at most one shot per 5 frames; +0xa0 takes frame# on each shot; [BYTES seg11:0x4e6f seg11:0x4e7f seg11:0x50b7 seg11:0x50bb]
  - `on_screen` ≠ 0, or `goal_pool` ∈ {0, 9, 14}. [BYTES seg11:0x4e93 seg11:0x4eb4]
- **Aim.** A computer ship, or the player with `PlayMode` ≠ 0, fires only when the heading seg8:0x19b0 of the screen delta to object (`goal_pool`, `goal_rec`) equals its `frame`, unless that object is a ship with `cloaked` (+0xe8) ≠ 0, which always passes; it then calls seg11:0x5ab6(s, 5). The player in Action mode jumps past both. [BYTES seg11:0x4eb8 seg11:0x4ec5 seg11:0x4ec9 seg11:0x4fcb seg11:0x4fe2 seg11:0x4fe5]
- The player's shot stores now in DGROUP:0x8e28. [BYTES seg11:0x4ff1]
- **Slots.** The scan runs over j < DGROUP:0x594. A busy slot (+0x76 ≠ −1) owned by (DGROUP:0x9324, s) marks the shooter as owning one; when its age +0x76 ≤ W / ((DGROUP:0x594 − 2)·10) (16-bit `idiv`, 20 at W = 1024) the scan stops at once and the gun returns without a shot. The free slots are counted only up to that point. [BYTES seg11:0x5009 seg11:0x5011 seg11:0x501a seg11:0x5020 seg11:0x5025 seg11:0x5034 seg11:0x5038 seg11:0x503a seg11:0x503f seg11:0x5090 seg11:0x5094]
  - The player-owned flag is set by any slot j, free or busy, whose owner words are (DGROUP:0x9324, player): a free slot keeps the owner of its last shot. [BYTES seg11:0x5056 seg11:0x505e seg11:0x5067 seg11:0x506d seg11:0x508b seg11:0x508e]
  - A shot needs two free slots, the player-owned flag, or s = the player. [BYTES seg11:0x503f seg11:0x5045 seg11:0x504b seg11:0x5053]
- **Bookkeeping.** Before it looks for a free slot it writes ship byte +0xcc = max(1, 7 − 2·DGROUP:0x9000 − DGROUP:0x9334), dword +0xa0 = frame#, dword +0x14a = the ship's object dword +0x04 (`x`, `y`), dword +0x14e = now, and DGROUP:0x8e28 for the player; a player shot in Strategy mode calls seg7:0x1208. With every slot busy the player passes the scan, the bookkeeping runs and no slot is filled, so the 5-frame wait starts again without a shot. [BYTES seg11:0x4ff1 seg11:0x5098 seg11:0x509d seg11:0x50a2 seg11:0x50a6 seg11:0x50b3 seg11:0x50bb seg11:0x50cf seg11:0x50d5 seg11:0x50dc seg11:0x50e1 seg11:0x50e6 seg11:0x50fa seg11:0x55f2 seg11:0x55f8]
  - core-0002 sees byte +0xcc go 0 → 7 at the first shot of a game and stay 7. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Fill.** It takes the first free slot (+0x76 = −1) and sets +0x76, +0x7e and +0x80 to 0. [BYTES seg11:0x55ff seg11:0x5606 seg11:0x5608 seg11:0x560c seg11:0x5610]
  - **Trail.** For k = 0 … DGROUP:0x594 − 1 and a < ship word +0x24 it writes −100 to words +0x30 + 12a + 2k and +0x48 + 12a + 2k and calls seg8:0x764(j, k) after each pair. [BYTES seg11:0x510d seg11:0x5112 seg11:0x5118 seg11:0x511b seg11:0x5121 seg11:0x5129 seg11:0x512d seg11:0x5135 seg11:0x513f]
    - These calls run before the barrel count +0x78 is stored, so seg8:0x764 works with the slot's previous count while the loop uses the ship's. [BYTES seg11:0x5135 seg11:0x5192]
    - k = 6 lies past the six trail points: the −100 of barrel 0 lands on +0x3c and +0x54 (barrel 1's world head), that of barrel 1 on +0x48 (barrel 0's world y) and +0x60; seg8:0x764(j, 6) for barrel 1 writes its screen x into +0x18 (barrel 0's screen y, point 0) and its screen y into +0x30 (barrel 0's world x). The screen writes happen only when the slot's previous count was 2. The head stores below and the next seg8:0x764(j, 0) overwrite all of these words but +0x60. A port that keeps the slot as 0x4a raw words reproduces this. [BYTES seg11:0x511b seg11:0x5121 seg11:0x5129 seg11:0x512d seg8:0x7ac seg8:0x7b3 seg8:0x3b4b] [DESIGN]
  - Both barrels' world x (+0x30, +0x3c) = the ship's `x` and world y (+0x48, +0x54) = its `y`. [BYTES seg11:0x515f seg11:0x5168 seg11:0x516c seg11:0x5170 seg11:0x5174 seg11:0x5178]
  - Owner +0x7a = DGROUP:0x9324, +0x7c = s; barrel count +0x78 = `barrels` (ship word +0x24); barrel-alive words +0x84/+0x86 = the sign-extended bytes +0xe0/+0xe1. [BYTES seg11:0x517f seg11:0x5186 seg11:0x518e seg11:0x5192 seg11:0x5196 seg11:0x519b seg11:0x519f seg11:0x51a4]
  - Velocity +0x70 = lo16(3·DGROUP:0x1050[4·`frame`]) + ((`vx`/10)·10)/DGROUP:0x9006, every division a 16-bit `idiv`, and +0x72 the same with DGROUP:0x1052 and `vy`; DGROUP:0x1050 is the [heading table](world.md#heading-table). [BYTES seg11:0x51a8 seg11:0x51ac seg11:0x51af seg11:0x51c8 seg11:0x51d0 seg11:0x51d2 seg11:0x51d6 seg11:0x51da seg11:0x51ed]
  - Dword +0x90 = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x51f4]
  - The player's shot starts at the ship's position minus its velocity, a computer ship's at plus it. [BYTES seg11:0x51f9 seg11:0x5205 seg11:0x520b seg11:0x5243]
  - Lifetime +0x74 = W/10, the divisor 10 still in CX from the velocity: 102 at W = 1024. [BYTES seg11:0x51cc seg11:0x525f seg11:0x5263 seg11:0x526a]
  - Each player shot adds 1 to DGROUP:0x9432 unless `goal_pool` = 9. [BYTES seg11:0x5274 seg11:0x527a seg11:0x5281]
  - seg13:0x4eec(s, 1). [BYTES seg11:0x5285 seg11:0x528a]
  - +0x82 = 0, and byte +0x55 + j of every in-use record of pools DGROUP:0x9326 … 14 = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x5297 seg11:0x529d seg11:0x52a5 seg11:0x52b0 seg11:0x52c8 seg11:0x52df]
  - core-0002's TRANSFORMER shot reads velocity (18, −45), lifetime 102, one barrel, owner (14, 0), and its age rises by 5 per frame, so a shot lives about 20 frames. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Muzzle.** One barrel (ship word +0x24 = 1 exactly): x += lo16(8 × the word DGROUP:0x7b60 + 4·(64·`design_index` + `frame`)), y alike from DGROUP:0x7b62, then x = (x + 25600) mod 25600 through seg5:0x8c0 (shift) and seg5:0x1aa (the RTL signed 32-bit remainder) on the sign-extended x, y alike with DGROUP:0x5a2; 25600 is DGROUP:0x5a0 << 3. [BYTES seg11:0x52e6 seg11:0x52eb seg11:0x52ef seg11:0x530b seg11:0x5314 seg11:0x5318 seg11:0x532d seg11:0x5347 seg11:0x535b seg11:0x538c seg5:0x8c0 seg5:0x1aa]
- Any other barrel count takes the two-barrel path, which always places a = 0 and a = 1: barrel a adds 8 × the pair DGROUP:0x7be0/0x7be2 + 4·(16·(4·`design_index` + a) + `frame`) with the same wrap. With dx, dy = barrel 1 − barrel 0 (16-bit, then sign-extended), L = the sign-extended AX of seg8:0x1290(dx² + dy², |dx| + |dy|) (Pascal, `retf 8`; the squares from seg5:0x314, the absolute values from seg9:0x88dc), forced to 1 when L ≤ 1 (signed 32-bit), and +0x68 = lo16(sext(lo16(16·dx)) / L) through seg5:0x110, +0x6a alike; dword +0x6c = 0x003e001c. [BYTES seg11:0x549d seg11:0x54a0 seg11:0x54a4 seg11:0x53e8 seg11:0x5400 seg11:0x541b seg11:0x54a8-0x54da seg11:0x54de-0x5524 seg11:0x5529 seg11:0x5530-0x553b seg11:0x5555 seg11:0x5569 seg11:0x556e seg11:0x5572]
- **Sound.** The player's shot calls seg10:0x744c(0x69) first. Then seg10:0x754a(id, 0x1e, j, 2), from one call at seg11:0x55b2 on the two-barrel path: id 3 for one barrel; for two barrels id 4 (pushed at seg11:0x55a5) when bytes +0xe0 and +0xe1 are both ≠ 0, else 3. [BYTES seg11:0x5398 seg11:0x539a seg11:0x53a0 seg11:0x53a7 seg11:0x53a9 seg11:0x53ae seg11:0x5583 seg11:0x5585 seg11:0x558b seg11:0x5590 seg11:0x5595 seg11:0x5597 seg11:0x559c seg11:0x55a5 seg11:0x55a9 seg11:0x55b0 seg11:0x55b2]
  - core-0002 plays one 0x03 per TRANSFORMER shot and no 0x04. [ORACLE core-0002]
- A shooter that the scan saw owning a busy slot starts the new slot at age 15, set after the sound on both paths: the shot lives lifetime − 15 sub-steps. [BYTES seg11:0x5020 seg11:0x53b1 seg11:0x53bc seg11:0x55ba seg11:0x55c5]
- A race wave (kind 6 with byte DGROUP:0x9eb5 = 0) then calls seg11:0x5626(j); in seg18 mode seg18:0x49d8(j, lifetime − age) runs instead. [BYTES seg11:0x53c2 seg11:0x55ea]
- **seg11:0x5626** returns at once unless DGROUP:0x9320 = 6, and also when byte DGROUP:0x9eb5 ≠ 0. It is the only writer of slot words +0x88 and +0x8a and, besides the fire's 0, of dword +0x90 ([Shot update](#shot-update-seg80x3aec), Ricochet). [BYTES seg11:0x563d seg11:0x5737 seg11:0x575b seg11:0x5918 seg11:0x591c seg11:0x51f4]
- **`rand()`.** seg11:0x4e0a's own body calls none. The player in Action mode skips seg11:0x5ab6, and seg11:0x5ab6 writes its mode word only at seg11:0x5c43 (2 → 1), seg11:0x5f11 (5) and seg11:0x5f75 (1), while the only `rand()` of its callee seg11:0x63b4 is at seg11:0x6800. [BYTES seg11:0x4ec5 seg11:0x5c43 seg11:0x5f11 seg11:0x5f75 seg11:0x6800] Whether a computer ship's seg11:0x5ab6(s, 5) can reach seg11:0x63b4 is [OPEN]; the analyst's reading of seg11:0x5f9b … 0x5ffb is that mode 5 only brakes or drifts (work/analysis/m4/s2-gun.md §1.8).

### The muzzle tables (seg10:0x1460, seg10:0x1620, seg10:0x1502)
The 64 pairs per design at DGROUP:0x7b60 + 4·(64n + k) come from three routines that the ship set-up seg14:0x0 calls with the ship's `design_index` (ship word +0x4e) at seg14:0xcdc, seg14:0xce6 and seg14:0xcf0. [BYTES seg14:0xcd6 seg14:0xcdc seg14:0xce2 seg14:0xce6 seg14:0xcec seg14:0xcf0] S is the design word DGROUP:0x96fc + 0xad·n (+0x2a), cos128 and sin128 are seg6:0x720c and seg6:0x71b2 ([world.md](world.md#helpers)), and every /128 and /2 truncates toward zero.
- **seg10:0x1460(n)**, entries 0 … 15 (one barrel): with r = design word DGROUP:0x96d2 + 0xad·n + 3 and a starting at 450, entry k = (lo16(cos128(a)·r)/128 + S/2, S/2 − lo16(sin128(a)·r)/128), then a −= (k + 1) % 2 + 22. [BYTES seg10:0x146f-0x148a seg10:0x1491-0x14dd seg10:0x14e1-0x14f7 seg6:0x720c seg6:0x71b2]
- **seg10:0x1620(n)**, entries 16 … 31 (DGROUP:0x7ba0/0x7ba2), the rear launch's points: with r = design word +0x02 (DGROUP:0x96d4 + 0xad·n) and a starting at 270, entry k = (lo16(cos128(a)·r)/127 + S/2, S/2 + lo16(sin128(a)·r)/(−127)), each division signed toward zero, then a −= (k + 1) % 2 + 22. [BYTES seg10:0x1634 seg10:0x163b seg10:0x1647 seg10:0x1657 seg10:0x165e seg10:0x1668 seg10:0x1678 seg10:0x1689 seg10:0x1690 seg10:0x1694 seg10:0x16a2 seg10:0x16a7 seg10:0x16aa]
- **seg10:0x1502(n)**, entries 32 … 63 (DGROUP:0x7be0 + 4·(16·(4n + b) + k), two barrels b = 0, 1): with the barrel offset (ox, oy) = design words DGROUP:0x973a/0x973c + 4b + 0xad·n and a = 0, 23, 45, … (a += k % 2 + 22, restarting for each b), entry k = (S/2 + (lo16(ox·cos) − lo16(oy·sin))/128, S/2 + (lo16(oy·cos) + lo16(ox·sin))/128); the /128 applies to the 16-bit sum. [BYTES seg10:0x1511-0x1549 seg10:0x1553-0x1562 seg10:0x156e-0x15fb seg10:0x1602-0x1613]
- The shop seg16:0x5f78 calls seg10:0x1460 and seg10:0x1502 again, and seg10:0x9a2a calls seg10:0x1502. [BYTES seg16:0x610f seg16:0x6121 seg10:0xa042]
- **The barrel offsets.** Outside seg18 mode, design words +0x68 … +0x6e (DGROUP:0x973a … 0x9740) are written only by the sheet builder seg14:0x11a8 while DGROUP:0x95a6 ≠ 0 (seg18:0x3330 … 0x335a write them in seg18 mode), with c = trunc(side / 2) of the rendered sheet: +0x68 = sext8(BOD +0x1e) − c − `Size`, +0x6a = sext8(BOD +0x1f) − c, +0x6c = −(+0x68 + `Size`), +0x6e = +0x6a ([ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes), Ship sheets). In seg14:0x0's loop the sheet build (seg16:0x3784(i, 1) at seg14:0xd32) and the +0x2a store come before the three muzzle calls of the same iteration, so a wave's muzzle tables use that iteration's sheet. [BYTES seg14:0x1380-0x13e0 seg14:0xd32 seg14:0xd94 seg14:0xcdc seg14:0xce6 seg14:0xcf0 seg14:0xcf6]
  - MASTER.BOD's TALON (body 5, the SKY TALON's) holds +0x1e = 1 and +0x1f = 16, and TRANSFORMER (body 33) 4 and 6. With side 33 at `Size` 1 (c = 16) the words are (−16, 0, 15, 0) and (−13, −10, 12, −10), and the SKY TALON's two-barrel entries at heading 3 (a = 68) are (10, 2) and (21, 29). [BYTES MASTER.BOD MASTER.SHP DGROUP:0xf00] core-0006's DOUBLE GUNS volley on the SKY TALON at heading 3 leaves two parallel trails whose newest heads differ by (11, 27), the difference of those entries. [ORACLE core-0006]

### Shot slots
- The gun's shots live in nine 0x94-byte slots at DGROUP:0x6098, whose fields are in [structs.md](structs.md#shot-slots-9--0x94-bytes-at-dgroup0x6098). [BYTES seg8:0x3aec seg11:0x4e0a]

### Shot update (seg8:0x3aec)
- **The loop.** The wave frame sets DGROUP:0x9136 = 0 and then, for i = 0 … 4 and every j < DGROUP:0x598 + DGROUP:0x594 (read again on each iteration), stores the result of seg8:0x3aec(j, i) (Pascal, `retf 4`: i at [bp+6], j at [bp+8]) in DGROUP:0x9136, adding 1 to dword DGROUP:0x5cc after each i. So every slot gets sub-step i before any gets i + 1, and DGROUP:0x9136 holds the last call's result. [BYTES seg7:0x938 seg7:0x93a seg7:0x942 seg7:0x946 seg7:0x94b seg7:0x951 seg7:0x954 seg7:0x95d seg7:0x965]
  - The loop runs only when DGROUP:0x9144 ≥ DGROUP:0x8f76 (signed) and the pass's frame-skip local [bp−0xa] is 0, after the fire poll of the same pass ([world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 6), so a shot the poll fires gets its five sub-steps in the pass that fires it. [BYTES seg7:0x881 seg7:0x925 seg7:0x92d seg7:0x930 seg7:0x934]
- It acts while 0 ≤ age < lifetime. Trail length T = ((`Detail` + 1) >> 1) + 3; dot side e = (2 << `Size`) >> 1, or `Size` + 1 in seg18 mode, which also calls seg18:0x4dc4(j). [BYTES seg8:0x3b00 seg8:0x3b0f seg8:0x3b14 seg8:0x3b1d seg8:0x3b20 seg8:0x3b27 seg8:0x3b2b seg8:0x3b31 seg8:0x3b38 seg8:0x3b3f seg8:0x3b41]
- **Head.** seg8:0x764(j, 0) sets screen point 0 of every barrel a < +0x78 to seg8:0x29ea of its world head (+0x30, +0x48) + 0xc·a. [BYTES seg8:0x779 seg8:0x787 seg8:0x78e seg8:0x7a0 seg8:0x7ac seg8:0x7b3 seg8:0x3b46 seg8:0x3b49 seg8:0x3b4b]
- **Hit.** r = seg8:0x3688(j); any r ≠ 0 on a slot owned by the player increments +0x7e. r = 2 frees the slot (+0x76 = −1), blacks every trail point of each live barrel and returns 1. [BYTES seg8:0x3b54 seg8:0x3b5c seg8:0x3b75 seg8:0x3b7f seg8:0x3be4]
- **Ricochet.** When dword DGROUP:0x5cc ≠ 0 and either r = 1 or dword +0x90 = DGROUP:0x5cc: with +0x90 = DGROUP:0x5cc it plays seg10:0x754a(0x11, 0x1e, j, 2), takes the velocity (+0x88, +0x8a) and adds it to barrel 0's world head only; then a kind-6 wave calls seg11:0x5626(j). With DGROUP:0x5cc = 0 nothing in this block runs. [BYTES seg8:0x3bee seg8:0x3c0a seg8:0x3c0c seg8:0x3c0f seg8:0x3c13 seg8:0x3c40 seg8:0x3c44 seg8:0x3c48 seg8:0x3c4c seg8:0x3c54 seg8:0x3c5c seg8:0x3c60 seg8:0x3c6a]
  - Since only seg11:0x5626 writes +0x88/+0x8a/+0x90 besides the fire's 0 ([The gun](#the-gun-seg110x4e0a)), no shot ricochets outside a race wave. The 0x11 that core-0002 hears when a shot hits a rock and flies on with a new velocity is the impact sound of [Shot hit test](#shot-hit-test-seg80x3688-and-seg80x6f3c). [BYTES seg11:0x563d] [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Draw.** b = sext(age << 3) / lifetime (16-bit), 0 … 7 while the slot is active. A shot whose owner pool +0x7a is DGROUP:0x9324 selects brush DGROUP:0x3b4c + 2·(b + 10·row), row being the owner's signed ship byte +0xe3 (an `imul byte`); any other shot selects DGROUP:0x3b60 + 2b ([The brushes](#the-brushes-seg100x4dfe)). [BYTES seg8:0x3c75 seg8:0x3c79 seg8:0x3c7d seg8:0x3c85 seg8:0x3c91 seg8:0x3c99 seg8:0x3c9d seg8:0x3ca1 seg8:0x3caf]
  - Each live barrel (+0x84 + 2a ≠ 0) moves its screen point 0 by lo16(`vx`·i)/−50 and lo16(`vy`·i)/−50 of the owner object (+0x7a, +0x7c); `mov dx, 0xffce` and `cwd` replace the product's high word before the `idiv`. It draws the head with PatBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, x, y, e, e, PATCOPY 0x00F00021) when 0 < x < W and 0 < y < H (strict), or always in seg18 mode. [BYTES seg8:0x3cea seg8:0x3cf6 seg8:0x3cf9 seg8:0x3cfe seg8:0x3cff seg8:0x3d20 seg8:0x3d24 seg8:0x3d27 seg8:0x3d28 seg8:0x3d2e seg8:0x3d35 seg8:0x3d3e seg8:0x3d45 seg8:0x3d4e seg8:0x3d55 seg8:0x3d77]
- **Erase and shift.** When word +0x06 (barrel 0's screen x of point 3) ≠ −100 it blacks point T of each live barrel, e × e. Then it increments the age and moves screen points T … 1 of every barrel a < +0x78 from the point before, with no live test, so at most T + 1 heads show and each is erased when it reaches index T. [BYTES seg8:0x3d95 seg8:0x3d9a seg8:0x3dad seg8:0x3dbd seg8:0x3dd4 seg8:0x3def seg8:0x3e0c seg8:0x3e1a seg8:0x3e1e seg8:0x3e22 seg8:0x3e26]
  - core-0002 shows each TRANSFORMER shot as a trail of five 2 × 2 dots at `Size` 1, the newest #FFFF00 (brush 0) and the oldest seen #691900 (brush 5). [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Move.** Each live barrel's world point adds the velocity; outside seg18 mode a coordinate above DGROUP:0x5a0 << 3 (y: DGROUP:0x5a2 << 3), signed, becomes 0, and then a negative one becomes that edge, so 25600 itself stays. [BYTES seg8:0x3e68 seg8:0x3e6e seg8:0x3e72 seg8:0x3e74 seg8:0x3e83 seg8:0x3e88 seg8:0x3e90]
- **Convergence.** After the increment, when the age's low byte has (age & 0xf) = 0xf and +0x78 = 2 exactly, barrel 0's world head gains (+0x68 >> 2, +0x6a >> 2) and barrel 1's loses it, with no live test and no wrap. [BYTES seg8:0x3def seg8:0x3ef1 seg8:0x3ef5 seg8:0x3efd seg8:0x3f04-0x3f2c]
- **Expiry.** At age ≥ lifetime the age becomes −1 and 6 trail points are blacked. The routine returns 0 only when the slot is free and DGROUP:0x9136 = 0. [BYTES seg8:0x3aec]

### The brushes (seg10:0x4dfe)
seg10:0x4dfe deletes and creates again the 20 brushes DGROUP:0x3b4c + 2n; DGROUP:0x3b60 is brush 10. The colours depend on INI `Bright` (DGROUP:0x8ff2) and are PALETTERGB values 0x02bbggrr: [BYTES seg10:0x4e11-0x4e35 seg10:0x4e3c seg10:0x4e40-0x4f4f seg10:0x4f52-0x5060]

| n | `Bright` = 0 | `Bright` ≠ 0 | n | `Bright` = 0 | `Bright` ≠ 0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0x0200FFFF | 0x0200FFFF | 10 | 0x02FFFF00 | 0x02FFFF00 |
| 1 | 0x0200D7DC | 0x0200EBEB | 11 | 0x02E6D700 | 0x02F0EB00 |
| 2 | 0x02008CC8 | 0x0200D7DC | 12 | 0x02CDAF00 | 0x02E6D700 |
| 3 | 0x020064AF | 0x0200B4D2 | 13 | 0x02A08200 | 0x02D7CD00 |
| 4 | 0x02004187 | 0x02008CC8 | 14 | 0x02826400 | 0x02CDAF00 |
| 5 | 0x02001969 | 0x020078B9 | 15 | 0x026E3C00 | 0x02C6A500 |
| 6 | 0x02000550 | 0x020064AF | 16 | 0x025A1E00 | 0x02BE9B00 |
| 7 | 0x02000032 | 0x02004187 | 17 | 0x023C0000 | 0x02A08200 |
| 8 | 0x02000019 | 0x02004187 | 18 | 0x021A0000 | 0x02826400 |
| 9 | 0x0200000A | 0x02001969 | 19 | 0x020A0000 | 0x02826400 |

- With `Bright` = 0 row 0 (n = 0 … 9) runs yellow to dark red and row 1 (n = 10 … 19) cyan to dark blue. Both paths store brush 19 through the shared call at seg10:0x505b. [BYTES seg10:0x4f4f seg10:0x505b seg10:0x5060]
- **Which row.** A ship's shot takes the row in ship byte +0xe3, whose only writer, the set-up at seg14:0x21d6, stores design byte DGROUP:0x9716 + 0xad·n AND 1: the [flame row](formats.md#mastershp-ships), 0 (yellow to red) for a ship with SHP flag 0x01 set and 1 (cyan to blue) otherwise. So the index stays inside the 20 brushes, and a shot has its ship's flame colours. [BYTES seg14:0x21d0 seg14:0x21d4 seg14:0x21d6 seg8:0x3c99]

### Shot hit test (seg8:0x3688 and seg8:0x6f3c)
- **Heads.** seg8:0x3688(j) (`retf 2`) first stores seg8:0x29ea of each barrel's world head, the screen head, in DGROUP:0x5168/0x516a + 4a. [BYTES seg8:0x3697-0x36e6]
- It tests every visibility-list record outside pool DGROUP:0x9328 with seg8:0x6f3c(pool, rec, j, [bp−0xa]) and returns its 1 or 2 at once. [BYTES seg8:0x36fd-0x3730]
- **The goal.** When the owner pool +0x7a is the constant 14 it then tests the owner's goal, ship words +0x32/+0x34 (`goal_pool`, `goal_rec`), when that object's `on_screen` = 0; this runs for the player's shots too, whatever the player's goal words hold. [BYTES seg8:0x3738 seg8:0x373f seg8:0x3747 seg8:0x374c seg8:0x375b seg8:0x376a seg8:0x376e seg8:0x3779]
- **Gate beams.** It copies the world heads, without seg8:0x29ea, into DGROUP:0x5168/0x516a + 4a and returns 2 when the first or the second head lies in SetRect(G + 0x00, G + 0x02 − 5, G + 0x04, G + 0x06 + 5) of G = DGROUP:0x6678 + 0x1c·g, g < DGROUP:0x9318. That table is each gate's beam rectangle ([world.md](world.md#gates), Beam and centres), so shots stop at the beam, widened by 5 in y; the posts are pool-8 records that seg8:0x6f3c tests like any object. [BYTES seg8:0x378c-0x37be seg8:0x37d7-0x37f3 seg8:0x37fd seg8:0x3805 seg8:0x3816 seg8:0x381e]
  - Both PtInRect tests always run, so a one-barrel shot also tests the barrel-1 entry DGROUP:0x516c/0x516e that an earlier call left, a screen or a world point; the port keeps the four words as persistent globals. [BYTES seg8:0x3816 seg8:0x381a] [DESIGN]
- **Gates of seg8:0x6f3c(pool, rec, j, x)** (Pascal, `retf 8`): the target's `state` = 0; when its signed byte +0x55 + j > 1 while slot +0x82 = 0 outside seg18 mode, it lowers that byte by 1 and skips the record; byte +0x2e = 0, or a ship whose ship word +0x2e = 2; and not the shooter itself while age ≤ 23. [BYTES seg8:0x6f3c seg8:0x6f59 seg8:0x6f7d seg8:0x6f81 seg8:0x6f87 seg8:0x6f8e seg8:0x6f95 seg8:0x6f9a seg8:0x6f9c seg8:0x6fa0]
- **The test.** With (tx, ty) = seg8:0x29ea(`x`, `y`) of the target, a live barrel a hits when 0 ≤ DGROUP:0x5168[4a] − tx < `size` and 0 ≤ DGROUP:0x516a[4a] − ty < `size`. [BYTES seg8:0x700c seg8:0x7018 seg8:0x7b4c seg8:0x7b58 seg8:0x7b62 seg8:0x7b7a seg8:0x7b80 seg8:0x7b85 seg8:0x7b8b]
- **Near miss.** A miss with both offsets dx, dy inside −25 << `Size` … 40 << `Size` (inclusive, signed) sets byte +0x55 + j to 0. Any other miss sets it from s = seg8:0x35d0(dx + `size`/2, dy + `size`/2) − 5 (1 only when exactly 0) and q = (lo16((target `vy`/5 − shot `vy`)·dy) + lo16((target `vx`/5 − shot `vx`)·dx)) / (8s), 16-bit: lo8((s / q) / (`Detail` + 2)) through two seg5:0x110 divisions for q > 0, else lo8(s / ((`Detail` + 4)·10)) − lo8(`Detail`) + 10. The last live barrel's miss wins, and a stored value of 128 or more is negative at the skip test and never skips. [BYTES seg8:0x7026-0x7048 seg8:0x704b-0x7084 seg8:0x7089-0x70ca seg8:0x70dd-0x70fc seg8:0x7108-0x711c seg8:0x7131 seg8:0x714a seg8:0x6f7d]
- **Power.** p = 14 − age·14/lifetime, or 23 while age < 20; × 3/2 for the player's shot, or × 5/2 for a shooter whose team word DGROUP:0x970a = 7 and whose ship byte +0x5e = 0. For any ship's shot p = DGROUP:0x9704 (design word, the mass)·p / 250; × 3 in Demon's Gate when the owner record is not DGROUP:0x9646, the owner pool untested; and with a roster record p = (roster byte +0x32 + 5)·p·20/100. Every product is lo16. [BYTES seg8:0x7174 seg8:0x7197 seg8:0x71b7 seg8:0x71c5 seg8:0x71e5 seg8:0x71f8 seg8:0x71fb seg8:0x7202 seg8:0x7205 seg8:0x720a seg8:0x720d]
- **Parties.** Before the impact it sets DGROUP:0x944a/0x944e = the target, DGROUP:0x943a = DGROUP:0x944c = 30 and DGROUP:0x943c = DGROUP:0x9450 = j, so the target's handlers see a pool-30 attacker, the slot. [BYTES seg8:0x724e seg8:0x7251 seg8:0x7257 seg8:0x725b seg8:0x725e seg8:0x7262 seg8:0x7269 seg8:0x726d]
- **Impact.** It calls [ordinal 29](#bounce-ispacedll-ordinal-29) through seg10:0x7dd4 with, in push order, &(5·`vx`) and &(5·`vy`) of the shot, &target `vx`, &target `vy`, &head x, &head y, &target `x`, &target `y`, 30, target `extent` + 8, p, target `mass`, &DGROUP:0x9436 and &DGROUP:0x9438. In ordinal 29's order ([ispace.md](ispace.md#ordinal-notes), 29) the shot is party B (extent 30, mass p, damage DGROUP:0x9436) and the target party A (extent `extent` + 8, mass +0x44, damage DGROUP:0x9438), so the step that moves B moves the shot's head and never the target. [BYTES seg8:0x729b-0x72a8 seg8:0x73b3-0x741b seg8:0x7422 ISPACE.DLL seg2:0x4d5]
- **The seam fix.** seg8:0x6f3c reads its stack word [bp−0x1e] at seg8:0x72ab and seg8:0x742a and writes it nowhere. When it is ≠ 0 it adds DGROUP:0x5a0 << 3 to a head x below 1000 for a target x above 5000 and subtracts it from a head x above 5000 for a target x below 1000 (y alike with DGROUP:0x5a2), and after the impact stores seg5:0x1aa(head + 25600, 25600), the head taken back into 0 … 25599. [BYTES seg8:0x72ab seg8:0x72b4-0x7393 seg8:0x742a seg8:0x7430-0x747e]
  - The word lies where seg8:0x29ea, called from seg8:0x3688's head loop, leaves the return IP of its argument-less far calls to seg5:0x346, the CS of its call to seg9:0x88ba or the CX it pushes for seg9:0x88dc, whichever ran last in the last seg8:0x29ea call. [BYTES seg8:0x29f4 seg8:0x29f7 seg8:0x29f8 seg8:0x2a27 seg8:0x2a43 seg8:0x2a9d seg8:0x2ab7 seg8:0x2ade seg8:0x2b4a seg8:0x36c3 seg8:0x3692 seg8:0x6f46 seg8:0x3722]
  - **Its value.** seg8:0x3688's body runs at sp T = bp − 0x36 and calls seg8:0x29ea (three word pushes and a far call) and seg8:0x6f3c (four word pushes, `push cs` and a near call) from that sp, with no call or other push between the head loop's last seg8:0x29ea and the first seg8:0x6f3c. So seg8:0x6f3c's [bp−0x1e] is the word at T − 44, and seg8:0x29ea's sp after its prologue is T − 40. seg9:0x88ba, seg9:0x88dc and seg5:0x346 write nothing at or above their caller's sp, and seg8:0x29ea's tail from seg8:0x2ba1 stores its result through its pointer argument, seg8:0x3688's own [bp−0x1e] at T + 24, and makes no call. Every seg8:0x6f3c call of one seg8:0x3688 call reads the same word. [BYTES seg8:0x3692 seg8:0x3695 seg8:0x3696 seg8:0x36ba seg8:0x36bb seg8:0x36c2 seg8:0x36c3 seg8:0x36c8-0x3721 seg8:0x3714 seg8:0x3722 seg8:0x3779 seg8:0x6f40 seg8:0x29ee seg8:0x29f1-0x29f8 seg9:0x88c4-0x88f4 seg5:0x346-0x350 seg8:0x36bf seg8:0x2ba1-0x2bbb]
  - Outside seg18 mode the last write there is CX pushed at seg8:0x2ade, unless the far call at seg8:0x2b4a runs and leaves its return IP 0x2b4f. CX is the low word of off = sext32(y) − sext32(lo16(DGROUP:0x90ec << (byte DGROUP:0x9150 − 1))) − dword DGROUP:0x2410, y the world head of the slot's last barrel, word DGROUP:0x60e0 + 0x94j + 12·(+0x78 − 1). The call at seg8:0x2b4a runs when the 32-bit |off| (seg9:0x88dc) exceeds sext32(lo16(DGROUP:0x5a2 << 2)) = 12800, except in a kind-6 wave with byte DGROUP:0x9eb5 ≠ 1. DGROUP:0x2410 holds the player's y − (H << s)/2 of the last recentre ([world.md](world.md#view), Origin), so off is the head's y less that player y, and the word is 0, the seam fix skipped, only for a shot whose last head is level with it; any other shot runs the fix. [BYTES seg8:0x2a0e seg8:0x2a02-0x2a0b seg8:0x2abf-0x2ad9 seg8:0x2ade seg8:0x2ae9-0x2afc seg8:0x2b00-0x2b0c seg8:0x2b4a seg8:0x2b4f seg8:0x369e-0x36e6]
  - In seg18 mode seg8:0x29ea's two calls of seg5:0x110 put the low word of dword DGROUP:0x88cc at T − 44, and seg5:0x110 replaces a negative divisor in place by its negation, so the word ends as lo16(|DGROUP:0x88cc|). [BYTES seg8:0x2b51 seg8:0x2b7f seg8:0x2b8c-0x2b95 seg5:0x130-0x145]
- A zero impact result goes on to the next barrel with no damage. On a non-zero result a target with `fixed` (+0x54) ≠ 0 gets `vy` = 0 and `vx` = `vy`; any other gets each axis (new + old)/2 (`cwd; sub; sar 1`); the slot's velocity becomes the new one / 5. [BYTES seg8:0x7480 seg8:0x7484 seg8:0x7486 seg8:0x7499 seg8:0x74a0 seg8:0x74ac seg8:0x74b0 seg8:0x74c4-0x74e8 seg8:0x74ec seg8:0x7504]
- **Sound.** seg10:0x754a(0x1e, pool, rec, 1) for a pool-4 target and for a pool-8 record with word DGROUP:0x3d6c + 0x34·rec = 2 whose type byte DGROUP:0x4d93 + 0x14·(DGROUP:0x3d80 + 0x34·rec) is 5 (the CL left by `mov cx, 5` at seg8:0x74ef) or 4; otherwise seg10:0x754a(0x11, 0x1e, j, 2). [BYTES seg8:0x74ef seg8:0x7508-0x7546]
  - core-0002 hears 0x11 on every shot that hits a rock, and 0x11 and then 0x12 CRACK within 10 ms on a shot that hits an icon, whose `hit_sound` its own dispatch plays. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **After the sound**, in address order: [BYTES seg8:0x754e seg8:0x7b2e]
  1. The player's shot, on a target that is not the player, adds DGROUP:0x9438/3 (16-bit) to DGROUP:0x9432 for a target in pool DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x934a or 8 or a pool-0 record with `capturable` = 1, and a further DGROUP:0x9438/2 for a ship target whose word +0x38 = 4 and +0x34 = the player. [BYTES seg8:0x7603-0x7662 seg8:0x7666-0x768b]
  2. result = the target's `on_damage`(pool, rec, DGROUP:0x9438). [BYTES seg8:0x768f seg8:0x76a7 seg8:0x76ab]
  3. A [spark](#the-spark-seg80x804a) seg8:0x804a(j, v, a) with v = lo16(p·DGROUP:0x9436) when the result is 0, `Detail` ≥ 2 and v > 600 (signed). [BYTES seg8:0x76b1 seg8:0x76b5 seg8:0x76bc seg8:0x76bf seg8:0x76c3 seg8:0x76d0]
  4. A destroying player shot on a virused icon (`capturable` = 2) calls seg3:0x6ec(player, 7, 0, 0x5e) and adds 3000 to the score dword DGROUP:0x1e58, not to the rating. [BYTES seg8:0x792f-0x7977]
  5. Any destroying shot, whoever fired it, sets the player's ship dword +0x156 (DGROUP:0x71e6) = now − 1. [BYTES seg8:0x7a1f seg8:0x7a25 seg8:0x7a2c seg8:0x7a32 seg8:0x7a38 seg8:0x7a3c]
  6. The player's shot calls the kill scorer seg8:0x7be8(pool, rec, DGROUP:0x9438, result) whatever the result ([globals.md](globals.md#scoring)). [BYTES seg8:0x7a40-0x7a5e]
  7. A ship target with `alive` ≠ 0 has DGROUP:0x9438 halved in Demon's Gate unless it is the player, after `on_damage` and the kill scorer, so the halving shortens only its lock-out +0x84 = now + lo16(12·DGROUP:0x9438), with object +0x26 = 1 when it is 0 and `tumbling` (+0xe2) = 1. A pool-8 target gets DGROUP:0x3d54 + 0x34·rec = now + 120·DGROUP:0x9438. [BYTES seg8:0x768f seg8:0x7a5e seg8:0x7a95 seg8:0x7aa1 seg8:0x7aa3 seg8:0x7abb seg8:0x7ad6 seg8:0x7add seg8:0x7ae3 seg8:0x7afa seg8:0x7b00 seg8:0x7b1c]
  - The other steps of this block (the goal and escort calls of the shooter's ship, seg4:0x7d36, seg8:0x99ce, seg18:0x3f02 for pool 15, the race block, seg4:0x7636 and the player's +200 to DGROUP:0x9432 on a ship kill, and message 0x5b for a pool-9 kill) have an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s2-gun.md §4.7). [OPEN]
- **Result.** It returns 2 when `on_damage` reported destruction, else 1; only 2 frees the slot, so a shot that does not destroy flies on at the new velocity. [BYTES seg8:0x768f seg8:0x76ab seg8:0x7b20 seg8:0x7b2b]
- **Messages 0x5b and 0x5e** (cases 90 and 93 of seg3:0x6ec) format string 687 "You Destroyed A Virus! %R %s!!" and 692 "That Was A Nasty Icon. %R %s!!" in mode 2 with the count 4 and string 688 for `%R` and the pilot name DGROUP:0x380c for `%s`. seg10:0xa332 takes `%R` as rand() (seg5:0xdc) modulo the count, so each draws one `rand()` and picks 688 "Well Done, ", 689 "Way To Go, ", 690 "That's The Ticket, " or 691 "Right On, ". [BYTES seg3:0x1346 seg3:0x1351 seg3:0x140a seg3:0x1410 seg3:0x1078-0x1086 seg3:0x109a-0x10a8 seg3:0x915 seg3:0x919 seg10:0xa406 seg10:0xa7c4 seg10:0xa7d3] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 43] id 687 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 44] ids 688–692

### The spark (seg8:0x804a)
- seg8:0x804a(j, v, a) (cdecl) fills an entry e of the [effect table](structs.md#effect-table-45--0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x1e90) among 0 … 3 with frame counter +0x00 = 1, frame count +0x02 = min(v/630 + 2, 14), type +0x0a = 7, +0x0c = 2, +0x0e = 10 for a shot of a ship whose byte +0xe3 ≠ 0 and 15 otherwise, +0x04 = 2, +0x06 = 0x1e and +0x08 = j, and adds 1 to DGROUP:0x9430. [BYTES seg8:0x8109 seg8:0x810f-0x8122 seg8:0x8126 seg8:0x812c seg8:0x8154 seg8:0x815f seg8:0x816b seg8:0x8171 seg8:0x8179 seg8:0x817f seg8:0x8188]
- It takes the first of entries 0 … 3 that is free (−1) or of type ≤ 5 and returns when there is none. A busy one (+0x00 > −1) is first moved to the first free entry of 4 … 44, with DGROUP:0x9434 raised to that index, or overwritten when none is free; the move copies every word but +0x04. The new entry raises DGROUP:0x9434 to its own index. [BYTES seg8:0x81c1-0x81e7 seg8:0x8060-0x8101 seg8:0x8132-0x813d]
- It takes the world point from slot words +0x30/+0x48 + 12a (DGROUP:0x60c8/0x60e0) and the centre from +0x00/+0x18 + 12a (DGROUP:0x6098/0x60b0) of barrel a. Its other caller, seg8:0x3aa7 in seg8:0x3688, lies past the DGROUP:0x9130 = 0 return at seg8:0x382a, so in a wave every spark comes from the hit's step 3. [BYTES seg8:0x8109-0x81b8 seg8:0x382a seg8:0x3831 seg8:0x3aa7]
- seg8:0x3fbe draws a type-7 entry as 3 × 3 (step 2) squares of side 2 in brush DGROUP:0x576c[+0x0e + DGROUP:0xb6a] ([world.md](world.md#effect-frame-seg80x3fbe), Pieces). core-0002 shows a spark's first frame as nine 2 × 2 squares 4 px apart in brush 16's (196, 0, 0). [ORACLE core-0002]

### A gun hit's score
- A gun hit reaches the kill scorer with amount DGROUP:0x9438 ([globals.md](globals.md#scoring), Kill scorer). On a rock (pool 3) that the hit does not break the rating gains W[3]·d = 2d; a hit that destroys a small rock or splits a big one ([Rocks](#rocks-seg70x5090)) adds P[3] = 900 to the score and the rating. On an icon (pool 0) the rating gains d, a shatter adds P[0] = 1000, and a virused icon adds 3000 more to the score (step 4 above). [BYTES seg8:0x7a5e seg8:0x7c3b seg8:0x7c84 DGROUP:0xb0e DGROUP:0xb38 seg8:0x7971]
- In core-0002 no rock broke under gun fire alone; every 900 came from the ship ramming a rock the gun had heated. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **A ram's 900.** A rammed rock is party T of the rock mover's dispatch seg8:0x5df2(3, rock, 14, player, a), credited to the player's ship, so the kill scorer adds P[3] = 900 (word DGROUP:0xb14) when the rock's handler seg7:0x5090 breaks it ([Rocks](#rocks-seg70x5090)). seg7:0x5090 reads the old heat before its heat add and breaks a rock only when `damage` > `max_damage` and that old heat is above 6 (or in seg18 mode), so the first ram on a cold rock never breaks it, whatever its damage; a hard ram heats it, and a second within the heat's 3000 ms breaks it. [BYTES seg7:0x4060 seg7:0x50df seg7:0x5163 seg7:0x5175 seg7:0x5179 seg8:0x7c3b DGROUP:0xb0e] core-0005 scores +900 for two ram kills of rocks no gun had hit. [ORACLE core-0005]

### The weapon system (seg11:0xac86)
- It returns when ship byte +0xea or +0xe8 ≠ 0 or the player's object byte +0x2e ≠ 0. [BYTES seg11:0xac91 seg11:0xac97 seg11:0xac9e seg11:0xacb5] The manual: no weapon but the gun fires while cloaked or shielded. [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]
- w = the signed weapon byte +0xb8 + `weapon_slot`. When W(w)+0x46 = 0 and `goal_pool` ≠ 9, DGROUP:0x9432 += 3. [BYTES seg11:0xacc5 seg11:0xacc9 seg11:0xacd1 seg11:0xacd8 seg11:0xacdf]
- Every use then calls seg13:0x4eec(player, 4) and stores now in DGROUP:0x8e28. All three steps come before the launch test, so they run on every Z, also when no missile leaves (an empty magazine, a refusal or a full pool) and for w = 0. [BYTES seg11:0xace4 seg11:0xacea seg11:0xacfa seg11:0xad10]
- W(w)+0x0a ≠ 0 calls the [launcher](#launch-seg110x7c12) seg11:0x7c12(w, DGROUP:0x9324, player) and returns. [BYTES seg11:0xad10 seg11:0xad17 seg11:0xad18 seg11:0xad1c seg11:0xad21]
- Otherwise its 23-entry switch on w − 1 at seg11:0xadba sends 1 to seg11:0x8a42(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646) ([Ring of death](#ring-of-death-seg110x8a42)), 2 to seg11:0x947a(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646, 2) ([The tether weapons](#the-tether-weapons-seg110x947a)), 3 to seg11:0x9fdc(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646) ([Lightning](#lightning-seg110x9fdc-seg110xa234-seg110xa9ea)), 4 and 5 to seg13:0x1318(14, player, 0x1e, 4 or 5) (Pascal, declaration order: pool, rec, life, kind; [Snowballs and fireballs](#snowballs-and-fireballs-seg130x1318-seg70x40c4-seg70x570a)), 13 to seg11:0x9078(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646) ([Wildcard defense](#wildcard-defense-seg110x9078)), 15 to seg11:0x947a(…, 0xf) and 23 to seg11:0x9daa; every other case does nothing. The index is compared unsigned, so w = 0 (0xffff) does nothing. [BYTES seg11:0xadac seg11:0xadad seg11:0xadb0 seg11:0xadb5 seg11:0xadba seg11:0xad38-0xad56 seg11:0xad5a-0xad66 seg11:0xad6b-0xad8e seg11:0xad9e-0xada7]
- seg13:0x1318(4) makes a pool-5 (DGROUP:0x1e0c) record of `mass` 200, SNOWBALLS, and seg13:0x1318(5) one of `mass` 120, FIREBALLS; seg7:0x40c4 draws `mass` 120 from image slot 10 and 200 from slot 11. [BYTES seg13:0x1318 seg7:0x40c4]
- seg13:0x1318 searches slots 1 … for the first holding weapon w and subtracts W(w)+0x2e (DGROUP:0x2f82) from `fuel`, floored at 0, only when `fuel` > 40; with `fuel` ≤ 40, or no such slot, it returns with no ball, sound or fuel change, and the on-screen and cap tests come after the fuel is spent. [BYTES seg13:0x135e seg13:0x136c seg13:0x1370 seg13:0x1374 seg13:0x137d seg13:0x1381]
- seg11:0x947a (GRAPPLING HOOK, HANDCUFFS) makes pool-10 (DGROUP:0x1e20) records paired with [link slots](structs.md#link-slots-0x20-bytes-at-dgroup0x65cc) DGROUP:0x65cc + 0x20·k, whose weapon word is DGROUP:0x65d6 + 0x20·k. [BYTES seg11:0x947a seg11:0x957a seg11:0x9a34]
- core-0002: each launch adds 3 to DGROUP:0x9432. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **The SKY TALON's Z.** MASTER.SHP record 59, SKY TALON, carries weapon +0x06 = 7, HEATSEEKER MISSILES (string 186 + 7 = 193), on body +0x21 = 5; record 1, TRANSFORMER, carries weapon 8 on body 33. seg10:0x1fb2 gives weapon 7 kind W(7)+0x0a = 4, so seg11:0xac86 sends it to the launcher seg11:0x7c12 like weapon 8, with lifetime 200, launch sound 0x1c, price 800 and graphic W(7)−2 = 370, the same graphic as weapon 8 ([structs.md](structs.md#weapon-records-28--0x50-bytes-at-dgroup0x2f54)). [BYTES MASTER.SHP seg11:0xacc9 seg11:0xad10 seg11:0xad21 seg10:0x2389 seg10:0x239b seg10:0x23d4 seg10:0x243c seg10:0x23bf] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 13] id 193 core-0006's SKY TALON launches with 0x1c and counts 20 → 19 → 18, and its display reads "20 - HEATSEEKER MISSILES" at the wave start. [ORACLE core-0006]

### The shock (seg11:0x9daa)
- seg11:0x9daa, case 22 (code 23, UNCERTAINTY) of the switch, needs DGROUP:0x238c = 0 and for a ship user `fuel` ≥ 250, and sets DGROUP:0x2408 = now + 2000, DGROUP:0x238c = 9 and DGROUP:0x238e = 6. [BYTES seg11:0xad90 seg11:0xade6 seg11:0x9db8 seg11:0x9dce seg11:0x9de2 seg11:0x9de6]
- Every other ship gets `tumbling` (+0xe2) = 1, +0x84 = now + 3500 and object +0x26 = ±1 by record parity; live ships recover, since the tumble explodes only ships with `alive` = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x9e05 seg11:0x9e10 seg11:0x9e19 seg11:0x9e4c seg11:0x9e61 seg13:0x14b]
- Every record of the pool behind DGROUP:0x1e18 (DGROUP:0x9416 records, without the user's own when it is pool 8) gets +0x26 = ±1 and DGROUP:0x3d54 + 0x34i = now + 3000. [BYTES seg11:0x9e7d seg11:0x9e98]
- It then calls seg11:0x9078; in a Demon's Gate wave with one ship it sets the player's `fixed` (+0x54) = 1. [BYTES seg11:0x9ed4 seg11:0x9ef5]
- **View shake.** While DGROUP:0x238c ≠ 0, seg8:0x0 plays sound 0x69, a positioned sound seg10:0x754a(8, x, y, 4) with y = `rand()` % H drawn before x = `rand()` % W, adds DGROUP:0x238c/0x238e to the offsets DGROUP:0x2390/0x2392 and then a `rand()` % 3 − 2 jitter (−2 … 0) to each, bounces an offset past ±5 with velocity ∓9 (x) or ∓6 (y), adds 8 × offset to the view origin DGROUP:0x2400/0x2402, adds the offset to the player's `scr_x`/`scr_y` and redraws it with seg7:0x1208. [BYTES seg8:0x251 seg8:0x25b seg8:0x266 seg8:0x273 seg8:0x27b seg8:0x285 seg8:0x289 seg8:0x29a seg8:0x2b2 seg8:0x2c1 seg8:0x2ce seg8:0x2dd seg8:0x2e9 seg8:0x2f3]
- Once now > DGROUP:0x2408 it sets DGROUP:0x238c = 0, saves `scr_x`/`scr_y` into `old_scr_x`/`old_scr_y` and redraws the sprite. [BYTES seg8:0x357 seg8:0x35c seg8:0x360 seg8:0x367 seg8:0x3a0]
- No collision path starts the shake. [BYTES seg11:0x9daa]

### Ring of death (seg11:0x8a42)
seg11:0x8a42(pool, rec) (cdecl, `retf`: pool at [bp+6], rec at [bp+8]) is weapon 1, case 0 of the [weapon system](#the-weapon-system-seg110xac86)'s switch, called as seg11:0x8a42(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646). It draws no `rand()`, plays no sound and draws nothing itself: it only fills [effect-table](structs.md#effect-table-45--0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x1e90) entries, and the [effect frame](world.md#effect-frame-seg80x3fbe) does the rest. [BYTES seg11:0x8a65 seg11:0x8a74 seg11:0x9077 seg11:0xad5a-0xad66] Its locals are R = 80 (never changed), T = 30 (the ms step), the slot index l and the ok flag. [BYTES seg11:0x8a50 seg11:0x8a55 seg11:0x8a5c seg11:0x8a5f]
- **Gate.** For pool DGROUP:0x9324 it searches l = 1 … (signed byte +0xcb) − 1 for the first slot whose byte +0xb8 + l is 1; that slot decides: `fuel` (+0x1e) above 70 (signed) lets it act, `fuel` ≤ 70 refuses, and a later slot holding 1 is not tried. Any other pool acts at once, with l = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x8a62-0x8a96 seg11:0x8a98 seg11:0x8a9d-0x8aa1] The slot's count byte +0xae + l is never read or lowered. [BYTES seg11:0x8a42-0x9077]
- **Cost.** A ship user then adds lo16((−70 · `mass`) / 250) to `fuel`: a 16 × 16 signed `imul` of −70 by the pool-14 object `mass` (+0x44), divided by 250 in 32 bits through seg5:0x110 (250 pushed first). There is no floor, so `fuel` can go negative; the next 1000 ms tick floors it at 0 ([Consumption](#consumption-seg110x1bcc-every-1000-ms)). The TRANSFORMER at 1320 `fuel` has `mass` 325 and pays 91. [BYTES seg11:0x8aa5-0x8acd seg11:0x1d26] W(1)+0x46 = 2, so the weapon system adds nothing to DGROUP:0x9432. [BYTES seg10:0x211e seg11:0xacd1]
- **The ring.** X, Y = the user's `x`, `y` and SX, SY = its `scr_x`, `scr_y`, its top-left points with no centring offset. With a = 11, 32, 53 (step 21, while 800/14 = 57 > a; j = 0, 1, 2 counts the passes), b = lo16(seg8:0x1290(6400 − a², 80 − a/4)) (Pascal, the first push the radicand) = 79, 73, 60, A8 = a << 3, AS = (a << `Size`) >> 1, B8 and BS alike, it places 8 type-1 entries per pass: world point (X ± A8, Y ± B8) or (X ± B8, Y ± A8) and screen centre (SX ± AS, SY ± BS) or (SX ± BS, SY ± AS), all sums 16-bit with no wrap. The eight blocks in code order and their start multiplier m (start = now + 30·m ms): (+a, −b) m = j, (+b, −a) m = 5 − j, (+b, +a) m = j + 5, (+a, +b) m = 10 − j, (−a, −b) m = 20 − j, (−b, −a) m = j + 15, (−b, +a) m = 15 − j, (−a, +b) m = j + 10. Plotted with screen y downward they run clockwise from 12 o'clock over 0 … 600 ms. [BYTES seg11:0x8ad1-0x8af2 seg11:0x8f4d-0x8fbd seg11:0x8b02-0x8f42 seg11:0x8b52 seg11:0x8bda seg11:0x8c65 seg11:0x8cea seg11:0x8d79 seg11:0x8e04 seg11:0x8e8f seg11:0x8f1a seg8:0x1290-0x12c4]
- **The entries.** Each ring entry has dword +0x00 frame 1 and +0x02 count 10, type +0x0a = 1, start +0x10/+0x12, pool +0x06 and record +0x08; +0x04, +0x0c and +0x0e are not written. DGROUP:0x9430 rises by 1 for each of the 24 placements and for the two centre entries; only the centre entries raise DGROUP:0x9434, to max(DGROUP:0x9434, index), so the ring entries are visited because they lie below it. If both centre searches fail, ring entries above DGROUP:0x9434 stay idle until the effect frame's reset reaches them. [BYTES seg11:0x8b41-0x8b77 seg11:0x900d-0x9018 seg11:0x9053 seg8:0x4552 seg8:0x4560]
- **The search.** Each placement takes the first entry from the start index on with +0x00 = 0xffff and index below 45. The start index is l from the gate (the slot index, at least 1, for a ship; 0 for another pool) and becomes the index after the previous placement. A failed search stops at 45, so the next starts at 46 and every later search of the call fails: once the table is full from the current index, the rest of the ring and both centre entries are dropped, and a search does not resume after the last success. [BYTES seg11:0x8a5c seg11:0x8a6a seg11:0x8b7b-0x8b82 seg11:0x8f46-0x8f4a seg11:0x8fc0 seg11:0x9057-0x906a seg11:0x8b02-0x8b11]
- **The centre entries.** After the ring j = 3; for i = 0, 1 it places a type 0xff06 entry at the user's world and screen points with dword +0x00 = 0x000d0001 (frame 1, count 13) and start = now + lo16((15 − j)·30)·i, so now and now + 360, raising DGROUP:0x9434 and DGROUP:0x9430. [BYTES seg11:0x8fc9-0x9065 seg11:0x9007 seg11:0x902d seg11:0x9036-0x904f]
- **What the effect frame does with them.** Type 1 never blasts and draws a fireball of slot 10 for frames 1 … 8, so each ring fireball is a picture only. A type-0xff06 entry draws no fireball and calls the blast seg8:0x5ab0 only when f % 6 = 0 and the type is not 1, which with count 13 is once, at f = 6: its last frame (f = 12) jumps straight to the advance, so there is no second blast and no blackout. [BYTES seg8:0x3fe5-0x4256 seg8:0x41bc seg8:0x4234-0x4250 seg8:0x407b-0x4083]
- **The blast.** It hits at the entry's world point, fixed at creation, that is the user's top-left `x`, `y` at the moment of the call, not where the user is 360 ms later. It skips its source only for type 0xff06. B = the source's `blast_size` +0x48, for a ship design word DGROUP:0x9702 + 0xad·n = strength / 22 (11 for the TRANSFORMER); it does nothing while DGROUP:0x1318 > 6. [BYTES seg8:0x5ce3-0x5cf8 seg8:0x5aec seg14:0x1e93-0x1e99 seg14:0x210a] So the ring's damage is two blasts, at the sixth effect frame after now and after now + 360 ms; its fireballs hurt nothing.
- **Sound.** Every type-1 and type-0xff06 entry plays seg10:0x754a(0x45, 0, 0, 3) on its first frame after its start time, with DGROUP:0x5144/0x5146 set to its world point: up to 26 KABOOMs, in mode 3, so a point more than 800 cells from the player is silent. seg11:0x8a42 itself plays nothing. [BYTES seg8:0x400f-0x4037]
- **Other callers.** seg4:0x591c in the slot dispatch seg4:0x584c (literal pool 14; it first runs seg13:0x4eec(ship, 5) and the byte +0x2e gate) and seg12:0x6e6c in the pool-8 weapon use seg12:0x6dea, with weapon DGROUP:0x3d5a + 0x34·rec; a pool-8 user pays nothing and always acts. [BYTES seg4:0x591c seg4:0x584c seg4:0x1381 seg4:0x5408 seg4:0x583a seg13:0xa72b seg13:0xabd5 seg12:0x6e6c seg12:0x6dea seg12:0x611e seg12:0x6186 seg12:0x6df9]
- **The weapon's data.** W(1): name 187, picture graphic 0xc2, kind 0, fuel cost +0x2e = 0x14, price +0x34 = 600, +0x36 = 17 (5 when DGROUP:0x920c = 3), +0x46 = 2. The ambulance sells it while DGROUP:0x11aa ≠ 0. [BYTES seg10:0x20eb seg10:0x20f1 seg10:0x2124 seg10:0x211b seg10:0x2139 seg10:0x2131 seg10:0x211e seg10:0x20fd] The text is [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 110].

### Lightning (seg11:0x9fdc, seg11:0xa234, seg11:0xa9ea)
Weapon 3, case 2 of the weapon system's switch, called as seg11:0x9fdc(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646) (cdecl) on each Z or Enter release, the weapon system's own steps (DGROUP:0x9432 += 3 because W(3)+0x46 = 0, seg13:0x4eec(player, 4), DGROUP:0x8e28 = now) running first. [BYTES seg11:0xad82-0xad8e seg10:0x20c7]
- **The charge.** Object byte +0x50 is the electric charge c (signed) of any object. seg11:0x1bcc every 1000 ms, for each ship i ≤ DGROUP:0x9316, after the cloak step and before the `mass` update, while the ship's selected weapon byte +0xb8 + (word +0x18) is 3, adds (109 − c)/2 to the player's object byte (signed halving) and (109 − c)/5 to another ship's (8-bit add): the player's runs 54, 81, 95, 102, 105, 107, 108 and stays at 108, another ship's saturates at 105. Tab away from a slot holding 3 clears the player's byte ([Selection and the weapon display](#selection-and-the-weapon-display)). [BYTES seg11:0x1ca3-0x1cfa seg11:0x1cac seg11:0xae87] Other writers: a launch (seg11:0x8010), the placements (seg10:0x880, seg9:0xf43), the strike (below), a tank's `on_damage` seg12:0xe1f (its wobble rate max(1, damage/10)), a pool-6 missile whose W+0x14 = 3 (+4 per pass, cap 0x4b), a virus (+1 per DGROUP:0x1c2e 1000 ms tick up to 100, seg12:0x54dc-0x54f8) and the tether helper seg8:0x1e32 (sets 60 before its own strike, a dead path). [BYTES seg11:0x8010 seg10:0x880 seg9:0xf43 seg12:0xe1f seg12:0x217 seg12:0x227 seg12:0x54f8 seg8:0x2296]
- **The search seg11:0x9fdc(pool, rec).** For pool DGROUP:0x9324 it scans slots l = 1 … (signed byte +0xcb) − 1 and proceeds at the first slot holding 3 whose ship `fuel` is above W(3)+0x2e = 20 (signed): unlike the ring, a slot 3 with too little fuel lets the scan go on. A user in another pool always proceeds. [BYTES seg11:0x9ff0-0xa032 seg10:0x20bf] The range is R = sext8(user +0x50) << 2 (16-bit), at most W/2 (W = DGROUP:0x90ea, signed halving), and at least 150 for the player's ship. [BYTES seg11:0xa036-0xa078] Candidates are the `state`-0 records below the in-use count DGROUP:0x9406 + 2p of pools DGROUP:0x9326 … DGROUP:0x9324 except the literal pools 5 and 12, not (pool, rec) itself, at seg8:0x285a distance d < R (signed) from the user's top-left point, whose direction h = seg8:0x19b0(den = dx, num = dy) satisfies h − 1 ≤ user `frame` ≤ h + 1 (signed, no wrap, so a rock in frame 17 finds nothing). It picks the candidate of largest `mass` (strictly greater than the best so far, which starts at 0); the (R − d) · `mass` / R score, with a ×4 goal weight for literal pool 14, is computed and never read. No `on_screen` test: off-screen objects in range count, and the ambulance counts while its `state` is 0. [BYTES seg11:0xa085-0xa11c seg11:0xa120-0xa195 seg11:0xa198-0xa1c7 seg8:0x285a-0x2990]
- **After the pick.** With none, nothing is paid or called. Otherwise a literal-pool-14 user pays fuel = max(0, fuel − 20) (`jns`), and seg11:0xa234(pool, rec, tpool, trec) (cdecl) runs; a user of literal pool 6 whose missile entry's launcher is the picked ship (pool DGROUP:0x9324) sets that entry's age to W(id)+0x1e − 1, after the strike returns whether or not it struck. The fuel is paid before the strike, so a strike refused while DGROUP:0x1016 ≠ 0 still costs 20. [BYTES seg11:0xa1c9-0xa1f8 seg11:0xa1cf-0xa1e6 seg11:0xa1fb-0xa227 seg11:0xa243-0xa248]
- **The strike seg11:0xa234(pool, rec, tpool, trec).**
  1. DGROUP:0x1016 ≠ 0 returns at once (one bolt at a time). [BYTES seg11:0xa243-0xa248]
  2. DGROUP:0x9520 = rec and DGROUP:0x951e = pool are stored before the count test; rec at or above DGROUP:0x9406 + 2·pool increments DGROUP:0x9498 and returns. [BYTES seg11:0xa25e-0xa277] The stores of DGROUP:0x2400/0x2402 sar 3 into [bp−0x20]/[bp−0x1e] are never read. [BYTES seg11:0xa24c-0xa25b]
  3. Sound seg10:0x754a(0x22, pool, rec, 1), BOLT.WAV, at the source. [BYTES seg11:0xa27a-0xa28b]
  4. Source point P0 for a ship (pool DGROUP:0x9324): (x + 8·mx, y + 8·my) with the one-barrel muzzle entry DGROUP:0x7b60 + 4·(64·`design_index` + `frame`), no 25600 wrap; for another source (x + extent/2, y + extent/2). Target point P1 = (tx + textent/2, ty + textent/2). Each /2 is `cwd; sub ax, dx; sar ax, 1`. [BYTES seg11:0xa296-0xa338]
  5. s0 = seg8:0x29ea(P0), s1 = seg8:0x29ea(P1), then seg11:0xa4a6(s0.x, s0.y, s1.x, s1.y, pool) (cdecl, `add sp, 0xa`). seg8:0x29ea is `retf 6`, so the word pushed before its second call stays on the stack and is seg11:0xa4a6's fifth argument. [BYTES seg11:0xa33b-0xa374 seg8:0x29ea]
  6. seg11:0xa4a6 is the [bolt shape](shell.md#intro-and-title-screen) generator. It starts with srand(low word of GetCurrentTime()) (seg5:0xc4) before anything else, also on its early exits, so every bolt reseeds the wave's `rand()` ([rng.md](rng.md)). Its fifth argument is read only by the pen choice: pool 9 CreatePen(0, 2, 0x0000ff00) green, any other CreatePen(0, 2, 0x00ffffc3) for the main polyline, then the same colour at width 1 for the branches (DGROUP:0x6bbc + 20b, 4 lines of 5 points) and sub-branches (DGROUP:0x6cfc + 12s, 2 lines of 3 points), every line through seg12:0x7256, each pen deleted after use. It ends with DGROUP:0x1016 = N + 1; when it draws nothing (L/50 > 22 at seg11:0xa54d, or a 17th branch at seg11:0xa684) DGROUP:0x1016 stays 0, the return is ignored and the strike goes on with no picture. The bolt draws at once, in the frame of the strike. [BYTES seg11:0xa4b5-0xa4bb seg11:0xa89f-0xa8c1 seg11:0xa8a5-0xa8fc seg11:0xa910-0xa9c6 seg11:0xa9da-0xa9de seg11:0xa54d seg11:0xa684 seg11:0xa559-0xa7d1]
  7. Amount A: a virus source (pool 9) draws r = `rand()` (seg11:0xa37d, after the bolt's draws) and takes A = r % sext8(c/2) + sext8(c/3), the halvings 8-bit `idiv`s of the sign-extended charge and the % a 16-bit `idiv`; any other source takes A = sext8(+0x50). A virus charge of −1, 0 or 1 makes c/2 zero and divides by zero at seg11:0xa3a3, which faults the original; the port skips the division and its stores and logs once, [DESIGN] (ruling R1, [ADR 0016](../decisions/0016-undefined-reads.md), Proposed line). [BYTES seg11:0xa37d-0xa3af seg11:0xa3b4-0xa3c7 seg11:0xa3a3]
  8. A virus bolt on an icon (source pool 9, target pool 0) calls seg7:0x2512(trec, 0), the [overlay](#icon-effects), before the dispatch: only when the icon entry word DGROUP:0x57a8 + 0x23·icon is 0 it pastes cell 0 of graphic 360 (the green one), writes directory byte +0x1c8 + icon = 1 and sets the icon's `capturable` = 2, and it always ends with seg7:0x65aa(0, icon). [BYTES seg11:0xa3ca-0xa3e0 seg7:0x2512 seg7:0x2612-0x2628 seg7:0x2637]
  9. Dispatch: DGROUP:0x943c = rec, DGROUP:0x943a = pool, then seg8:0x5df2(tpool, trec, pool, rec, A/2 + 2000), the return dropped. The [dispatcher](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2)'s rule 1 subtracts 2000 and sets its 2000-range flag only for 2000 < amount < 3000, that is 2 ≤ A ≤ 1999; it then sets the amount to 1 when the attacker is in pool DGROUP:0x9324 and that ship's design team word DGROUP:0x970a + 0xad·n is 7. So a team-7 ship's bolt deals 1; A in −1 … 1 is a plain 2000 with no flag; A ≤ −2 is a plain 2000 + A/2 with no flag. [BYTES seg11:0xa3e3-0xa406 seg8:0x5e5f-0x5e8e]
  10. Transfer: a target outside the ship pool gets byte +0x50 = lo8(3A/5) (16-bit `imul`, `idiv` 5), whatever the dispatcher did, so a tank's `on_damage` charge is overwritten with no destroy test (A ≤ 1 leaves 0). The source's byte +0x50 −= lo8(A') with A' = A/3 for the player (pool DGROUP:0x9324, rec DGROUP:0x9646), else A (8-bit sub): another source ends at 0, the player keeps 2/3 (108 → 72). [BYTES seg11:0xa409-0xa45a]
  11. A ship source whose team word is 7 sets both the target's (any pool, ships included) and its own byte +0x50 to 0. [BYTES seg11:0xa45e-0xa497]
- **The bolt's life seg11:0xa9ea(1).** The wave frame calls it at seg7:0xca9, before the movers, while DGROUP:0x1016 ≠ 0 (cdecl, argument at [bp+6]); it starts with SetBkMode(DGROUP:0x13dc, 2). With D = DGROUP:0x1016 and F = DGROUP:0x1036: call 1 (D > 0, F = 0) redraws the main polyline and the branches black (pens of width 2 and 1), draws the branches again with the stock BLACK_PEN (GetStockObject(7)) and negates D. Call 2 (D < 0), argument ≠ 0: plays sound 0x22 as seg10:0x754a(0x22, DGROUP:0x951e, DGROUP:0x9520, 1) when DGROUP:0x1318 ≤ 1 and as seg10:0x754a(0x22, P0.x, P0.y, 4) at the bolt's first screen point DGROUP:0x6b5c when DGROUP:0x1318 > 1; redraws with 0x00ffff7f (0x0000ff00 for DGROUP:0x951e = 9) at both widths, sets F = 1 and negates D back to positive. Call 3 (D > 0, F = 1): black redraws, then D = F = 0. The visible sequence is drawn, erased, flashed with a second thunder, erased. How long a strike made by the visibility pass shows before call 1 erases it is [OPEN]: a frame-by-frame capture of a discharge settles it. DGROUP:0x1036 can carry over: its only writers are in seg11:0xa9ea, but DGROUP:0x1016 is also cleared by seg9:0x5ddc, seg10:0x31 and seg15:0x80fb, so a bolt cut between calls 2 and 3 leaves F = 1 and the next bolt ends after one call with an erase only. [BYTES seg7:0xca0-0xca9 seg11:0xa9f7-0xa9fd seg11:0xaa09-0xaa15 seg11:0xaa1d-0xaa39 seg11:0xaa41-0xaa65 seg11:0xaa79-0xaaac seg11:0xaac0-0xab93 seg11:0xaba7-0xac7b seg11:0xac61 seg11:0xac6e seg11:0xac76-0xac7b seg9:0x5ddc seg10:0x31 seg15:0x80fb seg9:0x88ba]
- **Who strikes without the search.** seg12:0x53fc in the virus mover seg12:0x532c: a virus with `on_screen` ≠ 0 and +0x50 > 20 strikes targets of pools 0 … DGROUP:0x9324 except 9 with `state` 0, `mass` ≠ 0, an icon not already `capturable` 2 and seg8:0x285a < 200; pools 0 and 6 are always struck, others only when sext8(+0x50) > `rand()` % 100, and after one call it moves to the next pool. seg8:0x22a8 in the tether helper is dead ([The tether weapons](#the-tether-weapons-seg110x947a)). [BYTES seg12:0x53bd-0x53fc seg12:0x54dc-0x54f8 seg12:0x55f5-0x5604 seg12:0x560b-0x5629 seg8:0x22a8]
- **The visibility discharge.** The [visibility pass](world.md#visibility-pass-seg80x4e14) takes a `rand()` % 9 after its listing; when it is 0, for each listed record whose byte +0x50 ≠ 0, outside the ship pool, and only when some other listed record has `state` 0, it calls seg11:0x9fdc(pool, rec) once ([rng.md](rng.md)). The test reads only +0x50 ≠ 0 and pool ≠ DGROUP:0x9324 with no kind filter, so a struck rock, a damaged tank (its wobble rate) and a virus all discharge. A virus charged to 1 a second after it appears, finding a candidate within R = 4 cells, divides by zero at seg11:0xa3a3, as does a struck virus whose A = 2 gives c = 1. A zero-charge player bolt (c in −1 … 1, for example weapon 3 fired in the first second after selecting it) has R ≥ 150, A/2 = 0 and a plain 2000. [BYTES seg8:0x4ffb-0x5008 seg8:0x5015-0x5058 seg8:0x508b-0x5096 seg12:0xe1f seg8:0x4e14]
- **Chains.** A player strike at 108 deals 54 (with the flag) and charges a rock 64; a rock chain runs 64 → 38 → 22 → 13 → 7 → 4 → 2 → 1 → 0, each discharge to the heaviest object within 4c cells in its frame cone, the player's ship included.
- **Other callers of seg11:0x9fdc.** seg4:0x594f (slot dispatch, pool 14), seg12:0x6ea1 (pool 8, which needs its own +0x50, otherwise R ≤ 0 and nothing is picked) and seg12:0x66e3 in seg12:0x64aa (a pool-6 record whose weapon has W+0x14 = 3 and whose signed +0x50 > 25 discharges). [BYTES seg4:0x594f seg12:0x6ea1 seg12:0x66e3 seg12:0x64aa seg12:0x233 seg12:0x66d7]
- **The weapon's data.** W(3): name 189, picture graphic 0xc5, kind 0, +0x2e = +0x30 = 0x14, price +0x34 = 950, +0x36 = 14 (5 when DGROUP:0x920c = 3), +0x42 = 1, +0x46 = 0. [BYTES seg10:0x2092 seg10:0x208c seg10:0x20bf seg10:0x20bc seg10:0x20ca seg10:0x20df seg10:0x20d7 seg10:0x20a4 seg10:0x20c7] The text is [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 112]; "lightning, are easy to target at a specific object" [MANUAL Special Ship Systems] and the virus's depleted charge [MANUAL Hazards].

### The tether weapons (seg11:0x947a)
GRAPPLING HOOK (weapon 2) and HANDCUFFS (weapon 15) are kind 0 and go to the switch: seg11:0xad76 calls seg11:0x947a(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646, 2 or 0xf) (cdecl: [bp+6] the user's pool P, [bp+8] its record R, [bp+0xa] the weapon w), the weapon system's steps running first (DGROUP:0x9432 += 3 for HANDCUFFS only, W(2)+0x46 = 1, W(15)+0x46 = 0). [BYTES seg11:0xad6b-0xad80 seg11:0xad10 seg11:0x9489-0x9493 seg11:0x9da7 seg10:0x217d seg10:0x21d7] A tether is a pool-10 record (the hook) paired with a [link slot](structs.md#link-slots-0x20-bytes-at-dgroup0x65cc) of the same index; the user O is the holder (end B), the object it meets is the held object T (end A). The manual has no per-weapon text; it says some weapons "can be used for other things besides destroying things" [MANUAL Special Ship Systems] and that Intercept-class Enforcers "try to handcuff the offender and take him out the gate to the Hall of Justice" [MANUAL Laws of Inner Space].
- **Costs and gates.** W(2): name 188, picture 190, fuel +0x2e = 25, price 1000, +0x36 = 15 (5 when DGROUP:0x920c = 3). W(15): name 201, picture 190, +0x2e = 20, price 750, +0x36 = −1 (5 when 3); the Standard edition sells HANDCUFFS in the hook's place. [BYTES seg10:0x213f-0x217d seg10:0x2177 seg10:0x2183-0x2198 seg10:0x219e-0x21ef seg10:0x21c8] The other words are stored by seg10:0x1fb2 and have no reader here. [OPEN]
  1. O's `on_screen` (+0x1a) = 0 returns with no other effect. [BYTES seg11:0x9497 seg11:0x949c seg11:0x9d9f]
  2. ok = 0; for P = DGROUP:0x9324, l = 1 … (signed byte +0xcb) − 1 sets ok at the first slot whose byte +0xb8 + l is 2 or 0xf, whichever w is (slot 0 is not looked at); any other P sets ok. [BYTES seg11:0x94a0-0x94d9]
  3. For P = DGROUP:0x9324, whatever ok is: W(w)+0x2e > `fuel` (signed) clears ok, otherwise `fuel` −= W(w)+0x2e. The charge is independent of the slot scan, so a ship with neither weapon in slots 1 … still pays. ok = 0 then returns. [BYTES seg11:0x94de-0x950d seg11:0x9511 seg11:0x9515]
  4. O's word +0x36 ≠ −1 (it holds something or its hook flies) takes the release; otherwise the fire. [BYTES seg11:0x9527 seg11:0x952c]
- **The fire.** The first slot k < DGROUP:0x9300 whose pool-10 record has `state` ≠ 0 and whose slot +0x00 is −1 is taken, or the call returns. Slot k +0x0a = w; record k `scr_y` = −1000 (stored in +0x02, copied to +0x00); sound seg10:0x754a(0x1d SLING, P, R, 1); O's byte +0x31 = k on both the arrest and the search exits. [BYTES seg11:0x9530-0x956d seg11:0x957a seg11:0x9586 seg11:0x9598 seg11:0x959b-0x95a8 seg11:0x95e1-0x95f2 seg11:0x9883]
  - **The Enforcer branch.** A ship user with design team word DGROUP:0x970a + 0xad·n (n = ship +0x4e) = 7 and w = 0xf aims at ship words (+0x54, +0x56) (DGROUP:0x70e4, 0x70e6) with no search; a local store of 200 is never read. seg14:0x1657-0x1667 gives an Enforcer its arrest target (+0x52 = 0x14, +0x54 = DGROUP:0x9324, +0x56 = the player, for byte +0x5e > 1); what a team-7 player ship holds in +0x54/+0x56 in Action mode is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg11:0x95ab-0x95dc seg14:0x1657-0x1667 seg14:0x2224]
  - **The search** (every other fire): tp = −1, best = 0, R0 = 300. For each pool q = DGROUP:0x9326 … DGROUP:0x9324 (signed) except 5, 12 and 2, and each record j below the in-use count DGROUP:0x9406 + 2q with `state` 0, not (P, R), and `fixed` (+0x54) = 0 unless q = 4: d = seg8:0x285a(C.x, C.y, O.x, O.y) (the candidate first), skipping d ≥ 300 (signed); score = min(`mass` + 400, 1200) (signed); h = seg8:0x19b0(dy, dx) of candidate − user, score doubled when h − 1 ≤ user `frame` ≤ h + 1 (signed, no wrap). Then, for a ship user (literal pool 14 at seg11:0x982b), before the ×6: the ship's goal (+0x32/+0x34) candidate is ×2 and ×3 more when the goal's `capturable` ≠ 0; else a pool-14 candidate with a pool-14 goal is /8 (`cwd; idiv`); else a candidate with `capturable` = 1 while ship word +0x38 ≠ 1 is ×2 (all 16-bit). A pool-6 candidate is ×6 (16-bit `imul`, AX kept). Last, score = lo16(((600 − d)·score)/600) with the 32-bit product and seg5:0x110 (sext(600) pushed first, the product last), and a strictly larger value replaces the best. [BYTES seg11:0x96a7-0x989e seg11:0x979f-0x97e8 seg11:0x97f2-0x9801 seg11:0x9804-0x9828 seg11:0x982b-0x9877 seg11:0x984d-0x9877 seg11:0x96bf-0x96e0 seg11:0x96e5-0x970c seg11:0x970f-0x974e seg5:0x110-0x1a7]
  - tp = −1 or DGROUP:0x92e8 ≠ 0 (Demon's Gate) takes the straight shot, otherwise the aimed shot. [BYTES seg11:0x95f6-0x9606]
  - **Aimed shot** (seg11:0x9606-0x9a67): record k takes O's `x`, `y`, `vx`, `vy`. The muzzle offset for a ship adds (lo16(mx·4)/3) << 3 to `x` and (lo16(my·4)/3) << 3 to `y` (16-bit `shl`, `cwd; idiv 3`, `shl 3`), (mx, my) = DGROUP:0x7b60 + 4·(64n + f) (entries 0 … 15) and f the record's own stale `frame` (+0x28, set by its last flight, 0 the first time); for another pool, e = O.`extent`/2: `x` += lo16(e·DGROUP:0x1050[4f])/16 + (DGROUP:0x10d0[4f] << 3), `y` alike with DGROUP:0x1052 and DGROUP:0x10d2 (each /16 `cwd; and dx, 0xf; add; sar 4`). seg8:0x348e(10, k, tp, tr, S) follows with S = 160 when tp = DGROUP:0x9324 and 110 otherwise ([the aim](#the-aim-seg80x348e)). When record k's `vx` and `vy` both still equal O's, they become O.v + 7·DGROUP:0x1050/0x1052[4·O.`frame`]; since the aim adds its terms to the target's velocity this compares target v + term with O.v. `frame` = seg8:0x19b0(den = `vx`, num = `vy`), `state` 0, byte +0x2e 0, `owner_pool`/`owner_rec` = (P, R). [BYTES seg11:0x960e-0x96a0 seg11:0x98a1-0x991f seg11:0x9923-0x994a seg11:0x994d-0x99d1 seg11:0x99d5-0x9a24 seg11:0x9661 seg11:0x99fb]
  - **Straight shot** (seg11:0x9a6a-0x9c2d): record k `state` 0, byte +0x2e 0, `vx` = O.`vx` + 7·DGROUP:0x1050[4·O.`frame`], `vy` likewise with DGROUP:0x1052, `frame` = O.`frame`, `x`, `y` = O's, the same muzzle offsets with f = O.`frame`. The hook leaves at 7 × the 16-unit heading vector, 112 world units per step, plus O's velocity. [BYTES seg11:0x9a6a-0x9af6 seg11:0x9afb-0x9bd3 seg11:0x9a9a seg11:0x9aba]
  - **Both shots:** slot k +0x08 = 0, +0x00 = 10, +0x02 = k, +0x04 = P, +0x06 = R; O +0x36 = 10, +0x38 = k; then seg7:0x2080(10, −1). The fire never writes slot +0x10 … +0x1e, so the first mover pass takes its frame from, and black-erases, the slot's stale points (zero at load, else the last tether's). [BYTES seg11:0x9a28-0x9a63 seg11:0x9bd7-0x9c2d seg11:0x9d93]
- **The release** (seg11:0x9c30-0x9d9c), on any call whose O holds something and that passed the gates, the fuel included: (1) O's +0x36 = DGROUP:0x9324 (O holds a ship c = O's +0x38): ship c dword +0x84 = now + 800 (32-bit `add`/`adc`), c's object word +0x26 = 1, byte +0xe2 `tumbling` = 1; (2) the held object (O's +0x36, +0x38) gets `owner_pool` −1 and O's +0x36 = −1, its velocity untouched (a swung object flies on, the throw); no store to the held object's +0x30 or slot +0x02/+0x06/+0x08; (3) s = signed byte O+0x31: slot s +0x00 = +0x04 = −1 and pool-10 record s `state` = 1, slot +0x08 not reset; (4) the last drawn line (+0x1c, +0x1e)-(+0x14, +0x16) is erased with BLACK_PEN through seg12:0x7256 with no roll; (5) PatBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, record s `scr_x`, `scr_y`, `size`, `size`, 0x00000042 BLACKNESS) blacks the hook's old square; (6) for the player with DGROUP:0x951a = 0, seg7:0x1208; (7) seg7:0x2080(10, −1) on every release. No sound. While the hook still flies (+0x36 = 10) the same path retracts it. [BYTES seg11:0x9c43-0x9c8c seg11:0x9ca3-0x9cc9 seg11:0x9cb7 seg11:0x9cd5-0x9d01 seg11:0x9d07-0x9d46 seg11:0x9d4b-0x9d72 seg11:0x9d77-0x9d97]

#### The aim (seg8:0x348e)
seg8:0x348e(P, r, tp, tr, S) (cdecl, `retf`) aims record (P, r) at the target (tp, tr). It is shared by this weapon, the [launcher](#launch-seg110x7c12)'s kind-5 aim and seg12:0x6902. With s = DGROUP:0x9150 it takes (ax, ay) = seg8:0x29ea(record x, y) and (bx, by) = seg8:0x29ea(target x, y), each word shifted left by s (16-bit), adds the target's `extent`/2 to bx and by (`cwd; sub; sar`), and forms ex = bx − ax, ey = by − ay (16-bit) and n = lo16(seg8:0x35d0(sext ex, sext ey)) (at least 1). It then adds lo16((S·n)/ex) to the TARGET's `vx` (a 32-bit `imul`, `cdq`, `idiv dword` of sext(ex), no zero test) and lo16((ey·S)/n) (through seg5:0x110) to the TARGET's `vy` and stores the sums into the record (P, r): the record's own velocity is discarded, not added to. The x term divides by ex, not by n, so it grows without bound as ex → 0 and wraps in 16 bits. ex = 0 faults the original; the port skips the division and the stores that depend on it, leaves the destination values unchanged and logs once, [DESIGN] (ruling R1, [ADR 0016](../decisions/0016-undefined-reads.md), Proposed line). [BYTES seg8:0x34ab-0x3535 seg8:0x3538 seg8:0x353f seg8:0x3546-0x3569 seg8:0x356c-0x3592 seg8:0x358e seg8:0x3595-0x35c3 seg8:0x35d0]

#### The pool-10 mover (seg12:0x3148)
Called once per wave frame at seg7:0xd16, after seg12:0x0 and the smoke and before the pods. For each pool-10 record k below DGROUP:0x941a (DGROUP:0x9406 + 2·10) with `state` 0, in this order: [BYTES seg12:0x3379-0x3397 seg7:0xd16]
1. `collided` (+0x1c) = 0. [BYTES seg12:0x3399]
2. `frame` = seg8:0x19b0(den = +0x10 − +0x18, num = +0x12 − +0x1a) of the slot: the heading of the previous pass's line. [BYTES seg12:0x33a5-0x33c4]
3. seg8:0x1b54(0x0a00 | k, 1), the integrator (it calls no seg8:0x1e32 for pool 10). [BYTES seg12:0x33c8-0x33d5 seg8:0x1ba7]
4. `src_x` = lo16((`size` + `gap`)·`frame`) + `gap`, then the draw seg8:0xb76(0x0a00 | k, 3, DGROUP:0x39ee, 0): image slot 3's DC, the hook sheet. [BYTES seg12:0x33e6-0x33f6 seg12:0x33fa-0x3407 seg8:0xb76]

Erase the previous line (BLACK_PEN, seg12:0x7256 of +0x1c, +0x1e, +0x14, +0x16); pen = CreatePen(PS_SOLID, 1, RGB(r2, 0, 128 + r1)) with r1 = `rand()` % 128 then r2 = `rand()` % 128 (two draws per pass, on or off screen); draw holder to hook (+0x18, +0x1a, +0x10, +0x12); DeleteObject; roll +0x14/+0x16 ← +0x10/+0x12 and +0x1c/+0x1e ← +0x18/+0x1a. Objects in pools below 10 meet a hook in their own movers; their [response](#response-seg80x546c) bounces both and on result 1 dispatches the hook inside it (`hit_sound` 6, CLUNK), and the mover then dispatches itself with X = the hook, whose pool-10 rule calls seg12:0x3498. seg12:0x3498 has a third caller, seg8:0x641d in the dispatcher (attacker X pool 10), which sets [bp−6] = 0 so that no damage handler runs. #### The hook meets T (seg12:0x3498)
seg12:0x3498(Tp, Tr, k) (cdecl, returns 0 or 1): [BYTES seg12:0x3498]
1. (Tp, Tr) = the slot's end B (its own holder), or Tp = 10 (another hook): return 0, nothing else. [BYTES seg12:0x34a7-0x34c7]
2. Erase the in-flight line (BLACK_PEN, seg12:0x7256 of +0x1c, +0x1e, +0x14, +0x16) and roll; record k `state` = 1; PatBlt BLACKNESS over the hook's square at `scr_x`, `scr_y`, `size`. [BYTES seg12:0x34ca-0x3528 seg12:0x352d-0x3558]
3. A ship-held hook (slot +0x04 = 14) meeting a ship (Tp = 14, both literal) misses unless the holder's goal (ship words +0x32, +0x34 of slot +0x06) is (14, Tr): then Tp = −1. A pool-6 missile's entry age DGROUP:0x66ec + 0x1c·Tr becomes age/4 (16-bit `cwd; idiv 4`) and the hook still hits. [BYTES seg12:0x355d-0x3580 seg12:0x3585-0x3599]
4. Miss when Tp = −1, or T's `fixed` ≠ 0 and Tp ≠ 4 (so tanks always attach). [BYTES seg12:0x359d-0x35c1]
5. Attach: seg10:0x754a(6 CLUNK, 10, k, 1); T's `fixed` = 0; when T's `owner_pool` ≠ −1 (another tether holds it) the hook steals it: s' = signed byte T+0x30, record s' `state` 1, T `owner_pool` −1, slot s' end B's +0x36 −1, slot s' +0x00 = +0x04 = −1, +0x08 = 0, its line erased and rolled. Then T `owner_pool`/`owner_rec` = slot +0x04/+0x06, T byte +0x30 = k, the holder's +0x36/+0x38 = (Tp, Tr), slot +0x00/+0x02 = (Tp, Tr), +0x08 = 0, return 1. The attach does not call seg7:0x2080, so DGROUP:0x941a keeps counting record k until the next recount. [BYTES seg12:0x35c4-0x35e3 seg12:0x35e8-0x36a4 seg12:0x36a9-0x3722 seg12:0x36ec seg12:0x3760-0x3769 seg12:0x3726]
6. Miss: holder (slot +0x04, +0x06) +0x36 = −1, slot +0x00 = +0x04 = −1, seg7:0x2080(10, −1), +0x08 = 0, return 1, silent. [BYTES seg12:0x3728-0x376f]
Only seg12:0x36ec writes an object's byte +0x30 (into T), no store writes a pool-10 record's, so a hook dispatched as T reads the zero of its zero-filled block (GlobalAlloc flags 0x42). Saved-game restores and RTL block copies are not traced. [BYTES seg12:0x36ec seg12:0x34bf seg6:0x1bb1 seg9:0x2619 seg5:0x1f6e]

#### The held object (seg8:0x1e32)
seg8:0x1e32(pool, rec) (Pascal, `retf 4`: pool at [bp+8], rec at [bp+6]) is called by the integrator seg8:0x1b54 for any record with `owner_pool` ≠ −1 outside pool 10, before its own move, as often as T's mover calls the integrator. T is the held object, s = signed byte T+0x30, hook = (slot s +0x0a = 2), H = (T `owner_pool`, T `owner_rec`) the holder. [BYTES seg8:0x1ba0-0x1baf seg8:0x1e52-0x1e5c seg8:0x2655 seg8:0x1bac seg8:0x1bad]
1. DGROUP:0x915a += 1, back to 0 above 3 (never reset; seg12:0x3e98 in kind-6 waves also advances it, seg12:0x3fb7-0x3fc2). [BYTES seg8:0x1e41-0x1e4c seg12:0x3fb7-0x3fc2]
2. T.x −= T.vx, T.y −= T.vy (16-bit); the function adds the possibly new velocity back in step 11. [BYTES seg8:0x1e90-0x1ea2 seg8:0x24cc-0x24ee]
3. hc = (H.x + H.`extent`/2, H.y + H.`extent`/2); slot +0x10/+0x12 = seg8:0x29ea(T.x, T.y) and +0x18/+0x1a = seg8:0x29ea(H.x, H.y), raw. d = lo16(seg8:0x285a(hc, T.x, T.y)), (o1, o2) = hc − T in cells; d = 0 becomes 1. The locals [bp−0x30] and [bp−0x2c] have one writer each, 0 (seg8:0x1eba) and 300 (seg8:0x1ec2). [BYTES seg8:0x1ed8-0x1ef5 seg8:0x1f1a-0x1f4f seg8:0x1f54-0x1f79 seg8:0x1eba seg8:0x1ec2]
4. **Holding cost.** H pool = 14 (literal): q = T.`mass`/(−200) + 8 (16-bit), at least 3; when frame# (dword DGROUP:0x1e68) % q = 0 (32-bit unsigned) the holder ship's `fuel` −= 3, and `fuel` ≤ 0 sets d = 1000. Mass 0 … 199 pays 3 every 8 frames, mass 1000 or more every 3. [BYTES seg8:0x1f7e-0x1fd5]
5. **Break** when d > 350 + 100·(hook) (signed: 350 for HANDCUFFS, 450 for the hook), when H's `state` ≠ 0, or when T's pool is 7: T `owner_pool` −1, the holder's +0x36 −1, slot +0x00 = +0x04 = −1, BLACK_PEN erase of (+0x1c, +0x1e)-(+0x14, +0x16) and of the entry-time (+0x18, +0x1a)-(+0x10, +0x12), seg7:0x2080(10, −1), seg10:0x744c(0x69), seg10:0x754a(0x14 STATIC, T pool, T rec, 1). Slot +0x08, T byte +0x30 and record s `state` are not touched, and the call does not return: steps 6 … 12 still run with this d (the force and the position restore apply, the rope draw is skipped because `owner_pool` is now −1, and the entry-time points are copied into +0x14/+0x1c). [BYTES seg8:0x1fda-0x2019 seg8:0x201d-0x20cd seg8:0x1e6d-0x1e7b seg8:0x20b3]
6. d ≤ 40: no force. [BYTES seg8:0x20d0-0x20d4]
7. **GRAPPLING HOOK** (d > 40): T `capturable` = 1 (icon, fuel tank, pod): T.vx += o1 sar 2, T.vy += o2 sar 2, a pull that grows with the distance. Otherwise the velocity is set: with slot +0x0e ≠ 0, T.vx = lo16((180·o2)/d) + lo16(o1 << 3)/d, T.vy = lo16((−180·o1)/d) + lo16(o2 << 3)/d; with +0x0e = 0, T.vx = lo16((−180·o2)/d) + lo16(o1 << 3)/d, T.vy = lo16((180·o1)/d) + lo16(o2 << 3)/d (each 180·o a 16 × 16 → 32-bit `imul` divided by sext(d) through seg5:0x110; each o << 3 a 16-bit `shl`, `cwd; idiv d`). T circles H at about 180 world units per step plus 8 toward it and spirals in to 40 cells. No force acts on H. [BYTES seg8:0x23b4-0x24c8 seg8:0x23c4-0x23e1 seg8:0x23e8-0x244d seg8:0x244f-0x24c8 seg8:0x23ee]
8. **HANDCUFFS** (slot +0x0a ≠ 2), only for d > 300: dvx = T.vx − H.vx, dvy likewise; v = lo16(seg8:0x1290(sext(lo16(lo16(dvx²) + lo16(dvy²))), |dvx| + |dvy|)) with 0 → 1; e = (+0x18 − +0x10, +0x1a − +0x12) in screen pixels; dot = ex·dvx + ey·dvy (32-bit); c = lo16((−100·dot)/(v·d)), r = lo16((sext(c)·v)/100); X = min(d − 300, 60)·50 (16-bit); v, c and r are computed once, before the sign test. When dot < 0 (the sign of its high word): aT = (100·r)/((100·T.mass)/H.mass + 100) (32-bit `idiv`, then seg5:0x110) and aH = r − aT; otherwise aT = aH = 0. Then aT += X/T.mass, aH += X/H.mass (16-bit `idiv`; a mass of 0 faults); T.vx += lo16((DGROUP:0x17bc/3 + aT)·ex)/d, T.vy likewise with ey; H.vx −= lo16((DGROUP:0x17be/3 + aH)·ex)/d, H.vy likewise (each product an `imul word` whose high word `cwd` replaces). DGROUP:0x17bc = aT, DGROUP:0x17be = aH: one pair for every link, never reset, so each call uses the previous call's impulses whichever link made them. [BYTES seg8:0x20d8-0x23b1 seg8:0x20e0-0x20e6 seg8:0x20ea-0x2125 seg8:0x2128-0x21a1 seg8:0x129d-0x12c4 seg8:0x215d-0x2176 seg8:0x2179-0x2198 seg8:0x21a6-0x21ed seg8:0x21f0-0x2201 seg8:0x2204-0x222b seg8:0x22b0-0x22f4 seg8:0x22f7-0x22fe seg8:0x2301-0x2386 seg8:0x2342-0x2349 seg8:0x238a-0x2393 seg9:0x88ba] The rope is slack up to 300 cells and beyond it stops the separating motion and pulls both ends together by mass ratio, with a spring of X/m; e is in pixels and d in cells, so e/d is a unit vector only at `Size` 1.
9. **Dead branches.** [bp−0x30] = 0 and [bp−0x2c] = 300 are the only values, so the two mass/3 steps (seg8:0x222e, 0x223d), the seg11:0xa234 strike (which needs [bp−0x2c] < 300) and the seg8:0x1cbe clamp never run. The r = −50 store (seg8:0x225b) needs [bp−0x3e] > 3000 and [bp−0x2c] ≥ 300; its second half holds, and it is dead only because [bp−0x3e] = min(d − 300, 60)·50 ≤ 3000. While T's `owner_pool` ≠ −1: +0x10/+0x12 = seg8:0x29ea(T.x, T.y) + T.`size`/2 + (T.v sar s), +0x18/+0x1a = seg8:0x29ea(H.x, H.y) + H.`size`/2 (the current x, y, never `scr_x`/`scr_y`); pen = WHITE_PEN (GetStockObject(6)) while `Bright` DGROUP:0x8ff2 ≠ 0, else DGROUP:0x3b44 + 2·DGROUP:0x915a; draw holder to T. [BYTES seg10:0x4db2-0x4df3 seg10:0x4d87-0x4db0] The hook sheet, image slot 3 (DC DGROUP:0x39ee), is IGRAPHIC 4000/180 (12 × 12, 4 bpp) rendered by seg10:0x561a through seg10:0x6ee6(3, 180, "", 0, 0x63, 3, 0, 1, 0, 12, 12, 0, 0) while DGROUP:0x9300 ≠ 0 and the slot is empty; its callers are the load seg9:0x2722 and the `Size` rebuild seg10:0x9641.
