#### The clipped line (seg12:0x7256)
seg12:0x7256(hdc, x0, y0, x1, y1) (cdecl, `retf`) draws every rope line and erase; LIGHTNING and the race mover (seg12:0x3f29, seg12:0x4044) use it too. W = DGROUP:0x90ea, H = DGROUP:0x90ec; P0 = P1 = (−1, −1) at the start. [BYTES seg12:0x7264-0x7270]
1. All four coordinates inside [0, W] × [0, H] (signed, inclusive): MoveTo/LineTo directly. [BYTES seg12:0x7273-0x72a5 seg12:0x7576]
2. Otherwise x1 = x0 makes x1 += 1 and y1 = y0 makes y1 += 1; both y below 0, both above H, both x below 0 or both above W return with no draw. [BYTES seg12:0x72a8-0x72bb seg12:0x72be-0x72f7]
3. m = ((y1 − y0) << 7)/(x1 − x0) (32-bit through seg5:0x8c0 and seg5:0x110), then x1 = x0 when x1 was x0 + 1; b = y0 − ((m·x0) sar 7) (seg5:0x314, seg5:0x346). A y end below 0 (i = 0 for y0, else 1) or above H: m = 0 returns with no draw; the crossing xi = −((b << 7)/m) or ((H − b) << 7)/m makes P_i = (xi, 0) or (xi, H) only when 0 < xi < W (strict, 32-bit values). [BYTES seg12:0x72fa-0x734d seg12:0x7355-0x73c1 seg12:0x73c6-0x7435 seg12:0x7379 seg12:0x73ea seg5:0x8c0 seg5:0x314 seg5:0x346]
4. m' = ((x1 − x0) << 7)/(y1 − y0), then y1 = y0 when y1 was y0 + 1; b' = x0 − ((m'·y0) sar 7); an x end below 0 or above W: m' = 0 returns; yi = −((b' << 7)/m') or ((W − b') << 7)/m' makes P_i = (0, yi) or (W, yi) only when 0 < yi < H. A later crossing overwrites an earlier one for the same end. [BYTES seg12:0x7438-0x748d seg12:0x7493-0x7573 seg12:0x74b7 seg12:0x7528 seg12:0x7327 seg12:0x7465]
5. A P still (−1, −1) keeps its own end as steps 3 and 4 left it (unclipped when all its crossings were rejected); MoveTo(hdc, P0), LineTo(hdc, P1); LineTo leaves out the last pixel. [BYTES seg12:0x7576-0x75bd seg12:0x75a3]

#### The tethered target in the dispatcher
The [dispatcher](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2)'s rule 5: s = T's signed byte +0x30 is the slot of the tether holding T (or, for a pool-10 hook record dispatched as T, the zero of the never-written byte, so slot 0 whichever slot the hook belongs to). The word DGROUP:0x65da + 0x20·s is the slot's swing direction (+0x0e), toggled 0 ↔ 1 when X's `mass` + 100 > T's `mass`, which reverses a hooked object's swing (read at seg8:0x23ee); [bp−0xc] is T's `mass` (seg8:0x5e1c) and [bp−0xe] is X's, or 20000 for X pool 0x1f (seg8:0x5e25). One `rand()` is drawn whenever T's `owner_pool` ≠ −1. When `rand()` % 12 + 30 < a, a ≠ 100 and T's pool is the ship pool, seg11:0x947a(slot s +0x04, slot s +0x06, 2) runs: the holder lets go, because its +0x36 ≠ −1, and the held ship tumbles for 800 ms. The call passes w = 2 even for a cuffs link, so a ship holder must be on screen, hold a 2 or 15 slot and have `fuel` ≥ W(2)+0x2e = 25, and pays 25; otherwise nothing happens. Credit: a held object attacking credits its holder (rule 8), and a held object hit by anyone credits its holder when the holder is a ship (rule 4). [BYTES seg8:0x5df2 seg8:0x5e0a-0x5e3e seg8:0x5e18 seg8:0x5f3e-0x5fe2 seg8:0x5f4e seg8:0x5f58-0x5f94 seg8:0x5f63 seg8:0x5f9a seg8:0x5fa5-0x5fdd seg8:0x5fbb-0x5fdd seg8:0x606c-0x60b1 seg8:0x5f1b-0x5f3b seg12:0x36ec seg12:0x34bf seg10:0x1309]

#### The throw (seg13:0x6e5-0xa57)
For ship i in the ship mover, after its move, only with `Detail` DGROUP:0x9208 > 1, the ship on screen, ship word +0x38 = 1 and object +0x36 above −1 (signed), never for the player while `PlayMode` DGROUP:0x9134 = 0, never while +0x36 = 10, and only when slot (byte +0x31) +0x0a = 2. The held object G is the ship's goal: for each visibility-list entry (DGROUP:0x507c, DGROUP:0x50e0, j < DGROUP:0x9146) other than G with `mass` > 500, d = sext(lo16(seg8:0x285a(G.x, G.y, C.x, C.y))) must lie in 120 < d < 500; vh = (G.vx, G.vy), sp = sext(lo16(seg8:0x35d0(vh))) (0 skips); t = −((vh.y·dy + vh.x·dx)/d), c = (t << 7)/sp (32-bit throughout), and c > 125 (signed 32-bit) calls seg11:0x947a(DGROUP:0x9324, i, 2): the ship lets go when the swung object heads within about 12 degrees of a heavy object 120 … 500 cells away, a throw. When G is not the goal the same test runs once against the goal object. [BYTES seg13:0x6e5-0xa54 seg13:0x6e5-0x717 seg13:0x71c-0x729 seg13:0x72b-0x754 seg13:0x773-0x90e seg13:0x911-0xa44 seg13:0xa46-0xa54 seg13:0x7a9 seg13:0x842 seg13:0x850 seg13:0x903]

#### What ends a tether
| Cause | Routine | Effects |
|---|---|---|
| Z again, the dispatcher's hard hit on a held ship, the throw, the race mover (seg12:0x3e98: kind-6 waves, the pushed object's `owner_pool` ≠ −1 and its slot's +0x04 ≠ −1; DGROUP:0x9320 = 6) | the release | held ship tumbles, T `owner_pool` −1, O +0x36 −1, slot pools −1, record `state` 1, line erased, hook square blacked |
| the hook's 42nd pass, a `fixed` object other than a tank, a ship that is not the ship-holder's goal | seg12:0x3498 miss | holder +0x36 −1, slot pools −1, +0x08 0, seg7:0x2080 |
| another hook takes T | the steal | the old tether freed and its line erased |
| d above 350 (cuffs) or 450 (hook), the holder's `state` ≠ 0, T the ambulance, a ship holder's `fuel` ≤ 0 | seg8:0x1e32 step 5 | both lines erased, seg7:0x2080, 0x69 then 0x14 at T |
| an end's `state` ≠ 0 (destroyed, captured, through a gate, the holder gone), an end that is a ship with `alive` (+0xec) = 0 or byte +0x147 ≠ 0 | seg12:0x3778 at each frame start before the movers (from seg7:0xbed and seg7:0x19d6 in seg7:0x158e) | record `state` 1, A `owner_pool` −1, B +0x36 −1, slot pools −1, +0x08 0, last line erased and rolled, seg7:0x2080 |
| the wave start | seg9:0x6624 | every slot's pools −1, pool-10 `state` 1, every object's +0x32 and +0x36 −1 (pools 0 … 14) |
| a ship joining the wave | seg13:0xb498-0xb4a7 | that ship's +0x32 and +0x36 −1 only; a tether holding it or held by it dangles until seg12:0x3778 or seg8:0x1e32 finds it |
[BYTES seg11:0x9c30-0x9d9c seg12:0x3728-0x376f seg12:0x35f8-0x36a4 seg8:0x1fda-0x20cd seg12:0x3795-0x38f3 seg12:0x3834-0x38ee seg12:0x379f-0x3831 seg7:0xbed seg7:0x19d6 seg9:0x66ca-0x66e1 seg9:0x68df seg9:0x68e9 seg13:0xb498 seg13:0xb4a7 seg12:0x3e98 seg12:0x3ea8 seg12:0x466d-0x4683 seg12:0x466d-0x468f] Damage alone does not end a tether (a hook takes none, `max_damage` −1); a capture ends it through the icon's `state`, the visit through +0x147.

#### Sounds, rand() and the Enforcer's cuffs
- **Sounds.** 0x1d SLING at the fire (at the user), 0x06 CLUNK at the attach (and from the hook's own dispatch as T in a lower-pool mover, `hit_sound` 6), 0x69 then 0x14 STATIC at T on a seg8:0x1e32 break; the release, the miss and the frame-start break are silent. [BYTES seg11:0x95a3 seg12:0x35cd seg8:0x645d seg8:0x20b8 seg8:0x20c8 SND_FX.DLL 106 120 129]
- **`rand()`.** Two per in-flight hook per pool-10 pass (r1 at seg12:0x3265, r2 at seg12:0x3273); one per dispatch of a target whose `owner_pool` ≠ −1. seg11:0x947a, seg12:0x3498 and seg8:0x1e32 draw none. [BYTES seg12:0x3265 seg12:0x3273 seg8:0x5f9a]
- **HANDCUFFS differ** in the cost (20), the added violence (3), the Enforcer aim above, the physics (no swing and no pull, a 300-cell rope breaking at 350), no throw (the throw tests +0x0a = 2) and the prisoner: an Enforcer leaving through the gate with its held prisoner takes the prisoner along (seg12:0x23fd-0x2472), and the player leaving while held by a team-7 ship sets DGROUP:0x93a4, the Hall of Justice (seg12:0x2478-0x24b1). [BYTES seg12:0x23fd-0x2472 seg12:0x2478-0x24b1]

[BYTES seg4:0x5923-0x5942 seg4:0x5929-0x5942 seg12:0x6e81 seg12:0x6dea seg12:0x6df9 seg12:0x6e73-0x6e93 seg12:0x5636]

### Snowballs and fireballs (seg13:0x1318, seg7:0x40c4, seg7:0x570a)
SNOWBALLS (weapon 4, `mass` 200) and FIREBALLS (weapon 5, `mass` 120) make pool-5 records. The tables are in [structs.md](structs.md#pool-5-entries-0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x5a4c-and-trails-0x24-bytes-at-dgroup0x5eac); P5 is the pool index DGROUP:0x9328 (5 in a wave, 17 in seg18's layout), E(r) the entry and T(r) the trail. A pass is one wave frame, about 27.8 ms at 36 frames per second.
- **The launcher seg13:0x1318(pool, rec, life, kind)** (Pascal, `retf 8`: pool at [bp+0xc], rec [bp+0xa], life [bp+8], kind [bp+6]) is called by the weapon system as seg13:0x1318(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646, 0x1e, 5) for weapon 5 and (…, 4) for weapon 4; computer pilots call it from seg4:0x58fa with the literal pool 14 and their own life (0 from seg4:0x1381 and seg4:0x5408, 0x19 from seg4:0x583a). [BYTES seg13:0x16a7 seg13:0x132f seg13:0x1359 seg11:0xad38-0xad44 seg11:0xad4c-0xad56 seg4:0x58fa seg4:0x584c]
  1. **Ship gate** (pool DGROUP:0x9324): l = 1 … (signed byte +0xcb) − 1; the first slot whose byte +0xb8 + l equals kind stops the search. There, `fuel` above 40 (signed, `jle` skips) makes `fuel` = `fuel` − W(kind)+0x2e (8 for both kinds), floored at 0 (`sub; jns`), and go = 1; `fuel` ≤ 40 or no such slot leaves go = 0 and returns at once with no ball, no sound and no fuel spent. Any other pool: go = 1. [BYTES seg13:0x132c-0x137d seg13:0x1343-0x135c seg13:0x135e seg13:0x136c seg13:0x1370 seg13:0x1381 seg13:0x169f seg10:0x205f seg10:0x2015]
  2. **On screen.** Returns when the launcher's `on_screen` is 0 (−1 passes); the fuel is already spent. **Cap.** Returns unless 2·(DGROUP:0x9208 + 2) > DGROUP:0x9410 (signed): 10 records in use at most at `Detail` 3, 4 at `Detail` 0; the fuel is spent again. DGROUP:0x9410 is pool 5's in-use count: growth sets it (seg13:0x1488, seg7:0x5889, seg12:0x2f58), the wave load sets 0 with the allocated count (seg9:0x24c9), the per-frame clamp seg7:0x1090-0x10aa sets it to the allocated count when it is negative or above it, and [seg7:0x2080](#launch-seg110x7c12) recounts it, after every launch and at every ball's end (seg7:0x472b), to one more than the highest live record. So the cap counts the highest live ball, not the live balls: an old ball at a high index keeps the count up while the ones below it end, and the count falls back to 0 when the last ball ends. A refused ball still costs its fuel, and a split can push the count past the cap. [BYTES seg13:0x1385-0x13a7 seg13:0x1393 seg13:0x139a-0x13a7 seg9:0x24c9 seg9:0x24f6 seg7:0x1090-0x10aa seg7:0x209f seg7:0x210c seg7:0x211b seg7:0x472b] [ORACLE core-0009]
  3. **Free search and growth.** r from −1; r += 1; at r ≥ DGROUP:0x92f6 (allocated) the pool grows by one record: count = r + 1, GlobalUnlock, GlobalReAlloc(handle DGROUP:0x910a, max(count, 1)·0x70, 0x42), GlobalLock into DGROUP:0x1e0c, in-use DGROUP:0x9410 = r + 1, and the record gets `mass` 0x78, `hit_sound` 0x64 (not a slot, so a hit plays nothing), dword `radar_color` 0x000000ff (red), `radar_draw` seg11:0x3914, `on_damage` far seg7:0x570a, `capturable` and `fixed` 0, `owner_pool` −1, `src_y` = `gap`, byte +0x2e = 0 and E+0 = −1; the first record with E+0 = −1 is taken. seg7:0x570a and seg12:0x2db6 grow identically. The block can move, so every pool pointer must be re-read after a creator runs; a reused record keeps everything the template does not rewrite, and its `mass` is rewritten only for kind 4 or 5. [BYTES seg13:0x13aa-0x1495 seg13:0x13b4 seg13:0x13c5 seg13:0x13eb seg13:0x1408-0x1479 seg13:0x1482 seg13:0x1488 seg13:0x1490 seg13:0x168b-0x168f seg7:0x57c2-0x5874 seg12:0x2e8e-0x2f43]
  4. **Take.** E+0 = 0, E+0x10 = 0, E+0x12 = 0, E+0x08/+0x0a (last hit) = (pool, rec), `mass` 200 (kind 4) or 120 (kind 5), `state` 0, byte +0x2e = 0; trail Tx[0] = Ty[0] = −100 written five times (the counter is not in the index), so Tx[1 … 5], Ty[1 … 5] and Ts[0 … 5] keep the previous ball's words: on the first passes the shift carries them to element 5, where they are blacked, and to elements 4 and 3, where they get the PATAND fade; keep them per record. Shooter E+0x04/+0x06 = (pool, rec); life E+0x02. [BYTES seg13:0x1499-0x14fc seg13:0x14a0 seg13:0x14a4 seg13:0x14ab seg13:0x14b2 seg13:0x14c4 seg13:0x14da seg13:0x14e8 seg13:0x14f2 seg13:0x1501-0x151c seg13:0x151e-0x152c seg13:0x1623-0x1629]
  5. **Velocity and position.** With f the launcher's `frame`: `vx` = lo16(6·DGROUP:0x1050[4f]), `vy` = lo16(6·DGROUP:0x1052[4f]) (`imul r, m, 6`), plus the launcher's `vx`, `vy`. With E the launcher's `extent` and q = E/32 + 6 (toward zero): `x` = lo16(DGROUP:0x1050[4f]·q) + E/2 + launcher `x` − 0x20, `y` likewise with DGROUP:0x1052. [BYTES seg13:0x153a-0x1574 seg13:0x1545 seg13:0x1569 seg13:0x1580-0x161f seg13:0x158a seg13:0x1597-0x159d seg13:0x15bd seg13:0x1632-0x164b]
  6. **Sounds.** For pool DGROUP:0x9324 and rec DGROUP:0x9646 only, seg10:0x744c(0x69) (the priority reset, cdecl one word); then seg10:0x754a(id, 5, r, 1) with id 0x1b (FIREBALL.WAV) when life > 24 (signed), else 0x19 (FIRE1.WAV), through the shared call at seg13:0x1676 (the `push 0x19` at seg13:0x1687 jumps into it); the player's life of 30 always gives 0x1b, SNOWBALLS too. The routine ends with seg7:0x2080(5, −1), whose 5 is a literal; only the take path reaches it. It draws no `rand()`, and posts no WM_PAINT. [BYTES seg13:0x164f-0x1666 seg13:0x1667-0x167b seg13:0x1680-0x1689 seg13:0x1693-0x169c seg13:0x1676 seg13:0x1687]
- **The weapon system around it.** W(4)+0x46 = W(5)+0x46 = 0, so each Z use adds 3 to DGROUP:0x9432, then seg13:0x4eec(player, 4) and DGROUP:0x8e28 = now, also when seg13:0x1318 refuses. [BYTES seg10:0x201b seg10:0x2026 seg10:0x206e seg10:0x2065 seg11:0xacd1]
- **The pool-5 mover seg7:0x40c4** (far, no arguments) is called once per pass at seg7:0xcf6 between the fragment mover seg7:0x32d0 and the pool-6 mover, and from seg18:0x324. For r < DGROUP:0x9406 + 2·P5 (re-read every iteration, signed) it skips a record with E+0 ≤ −1; a record split off in the pass at a higher index runs in the same pass. [BYTES seg7:0xcf1 seg7:0xcf6 seg7:0x477c seg7:0x4736-0x474d]
  - **Frame.** From the age t = E+0: for t < 28, f = t/5, side c = 6f + 10, offset o = 3f (28 and 29: f = 7, c = 52, o = 21, from the dword 0x00340007; t ≥ 30: all 0; f = 6 is never used; t ≥ 28 goes to seg7:0x40db). c = (c << `Size`) >> 1; o is applied unscaled in world units. [BYTES seg7:0x474f-0x4771 seg7:0x40db-0x40fc seg7:0x40ff-0x410a]
  - **Shift and position.** For k = 5 … 1 copy element k − 1 to k in Tx, Ty, Ts; `x`, `y` −= 8o; (`scr_x`, `scr_y`) = seg8:0x29ea(`x`, `y`); element 0 = (`scr_x`, `scr_y`, c); `size` = c; `extent` = c << DGROUP:0x9150; `src_x` = (DGROUP:0xb70[f] << `Size`) >> 1 with DGROUP:0xb70 = 0, 10, 26, 48, 76, 110, 150, 196; `src_y` = 0. [BYTES seg7:0x410d-0x4202 seg7:0x413c-0x4164 seg7:0x41ab-0x41ba seg7:0x41be seg7:0x41d3 seg7:0x41e0-0x41f3 seg7:0x4202]
  - **Picture.** Slot [bp−0x12] = 10 for `mass` 0x78 and 11 for 0xc8 (any other mass keeps the previous iteration's value, or stack residue on the first); seg8:0xb76(P5·256 + r, 6, DGROUP:0x39e8[slot], 0) blits `size` × `size` from (`src_x`, `src_y`) with SRCPAINT and erases nothing; `x`, `y` += 8o afterwards. Slot 10 is graphic 170 (248 × 52, eight fireballs of sides 10 … 52, loaded by seg10:0x51de); slot 11 is graphic 175 (196 × 46, seven snowballs at x 0 … 150, loaded by seg10:0x513a). A SNOWBALL at f = 7 reads x 196 … 247, outside its bitmap, so its ages 28 and 29 add nothing; whether Win16 GDI clips that source or ORs black has no visible difference. [BYTES seg7:0x4208-0x4284 seg7:0x421a seg7:0x4238 seg10:0x513a seg10:0x513f seg10:0x51de seg10:0x51e3 IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 175 IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 170]
  - **Erase and fade.** PatBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, Tx[5] − 1, Ty[5] − 1, Ts[5] + 2, Ts[5] + 2, BLACKNESS). With hw, hh = W/2, H/2 (toward zero) and b = (40 << `Size`) >> 1, the test on the raw square of element 5 (x5, y5, s5), signed and strict, hw − s5 < x5, hh − s5 < y5, x5 < hw + b and y5 < hh + b sets the player's ship dword +0x156 = now − 1 (`sub`/`sbb`), the same store as seg13:0x6716's (the blacked square reaches 1 px further on each side, which the test does not count). Then for j = 0, 1: SelectObject(DGROUP:0x5ea8 + 2j), PatBlt(Tx[4 − j] − 1, Ty[4 − j] − 1, Ts[4 − j] + 2, Ts[4 − j] + 2, 0x00A000C9 PATAND), SelectObject back: the picture of 4 passes ago is ANDed with the sparse-dot brush DGROUP:0x5ea8 and that of 3 passes ago with the checkerboard DGROUP:0x5eaa (made at seg10:0xf2/seg10:0x10f, also seg15:0x5863/seg15:0x5880, freed at seg10:0x1d4-0x1e6); a comet tail. [BYTES seg7:0x4288-0x4368 seg7:0x428c-0x429c seg7:0x42af-0x42f2 seg7:0x42f4-0x430b seg7:0x430f-0x4368 seg7:0x432d-0x4342]
  - **Move.** E+0x0c/+0x0e = `vx`, `vy`, then seg8:0x1b54(P5·256 + r, 1), which sets DGROUP:0x9006 = 10 for a P5 record. [BYTES seg7:0x4379-0x4389 seg7:0x439e]
  - **Collision.** Skipped when `on_screen` = 0 and DGROUP:0x9320 ≠ 6. Otherwise DGROUP:0x943c = r, DGROUP:0x943a = P5, h = seg8:0x50aa(P5·256 + r) (0xffff skips), a = seg8:0x546c(h, far &`vx`, far &`vy`, `extent` + 2, `mass`), then seg8:0x5df2(P5, r, h >> 8, h & 0xff, a). The search skips lower pools, so in a wave it finds pools 5 … 14; icons, fragments, defenders, rocks and tanks meet a ball in their own movers (the rock heating, the [icon mover's cases](#icon-effects), the tank's a = 125). The ball's launcher is its first last-hit, so it cannot hit its launcher until it has hit something else. [BYTES seg7:0x43a3-0x4437 seg7:0x43ae-0x43ba seg8:0x52b6 seg8:0x546c seg8:0x5df2]
  - **After the dispatch** (the hit pool byte [bp−9], record byte [bp−0xa]): a rock-pool hit (unreachable in a wave, rocks are pool 3) heats by `mass`; a pool-8 hit of `mass` 0xdac (the gate posts, live in a wave because the post mover's own copy at seg12:0x1d06-0x1d56 cannot find a pool-5 ball while P5 = 5, and hazards of other masses are not heated): `mass` 0x78 sets the signed heat byte +0x2f = min(heat + 1, 9) and dword +0x6a = now + 5000, `mass` 0xc8 with heat > 0 sets heat = −(5·heat) (8-bit `imul byte`, `neg al`); no puff. Any object of `mass` 0xa0 (word compare, any pool, indexing the icon entries with its record number): `mass` 0x78 with word DGROUP:0x57aa + 0x23·i = 0 sets it to 2, dword DGROUP:0x57a4 + 0x23·i = now + 4000 and calls seg7:0x477e(i), the [ignite](#icon-effects); `mass` 0xc8 with that word > 0 (signed) clears the dword DGROUP:0x57a8 + 0x23·i (icon words +0 and +2) and calls seg7:0x5b68(hit pool, hit rec, 1) four times, with no heat change, `capturable` change, sound or backup copy. Icons have `mass` 0xa0 but pool 0, so in a wave only a pool-5 … 14 object of mass exactly 160 reaches the branch, for example a ship whose design mass + `fuel`/20 is 160; the port runs it as read and logs it once. [BYTES seg7:0x443a-0x463d seg7:0x443a-0x44d3 seg7:0x44d7-0x458d seg7:0x4591-0x463d seg7:0x45a9 seg7:0x45c2-0x45f0 seg7:0x45f2-0x463d seg7:0x4623-0x463d seg12:0x1cc1 seg12:0x1cec seg12:0x1d06 seg10:0xbed]
  - **Age and end.** E+0 += 1; when E+0x02 + 4 ≥ E+0 (signed) the record is done for the pass. Otherwise: when seg8:0x7ba8(`scr_x`, `scr_y`) accepts, PatBlt BLACKNESS 54 × 54 at (`scr_x` − 1, `scr_y` − 1); for k = 0 … 5, PatBlt BLACKNESS of (Tx[k] − 1, Ty[k] − 1, `size` + 2, `size` + 2) (a life-30 ball ends at age 35 with `size` 0, so these are 2 × 2 squares and the 54 × 54 square and the per-pass element-5 blackness do the real erasing); E+0 = −1, `state` = 1; with DGROUP:0x9130 ≠ 0 seg18:0x5350(`scr_x`, `scr_y`) between `state` and the recount; seg7:0x2080(P5, r). A life-30 ball is drawn on ages 0 … 34 and ends on the pass that makes its age 35: 35 passes, about 0.97 s. The mover draws no `rand()`; the four seg7:0x5b68 puffs of the mass-160 branch and a tethered target's dispatch draw their own. [BYTES seg7:0x463f-0x4730 seg7:0x4643-0x4652 seg7:0x4704-0x4721 seg7:0x46e9 seg7:0x5b68]
- **The split seg7:0x570a(pool, rec, a)** (cdecl, the `on_damage` of pool 5; `a` is never read; it returns 0 on every path). With E(rec)+0x12 ≠ 0 or `mass` ≠ 0xc8 it sets `vx`, `vy` = E+0x0c, E+0x0e and returns, so a FIREBALL or a snowball half keeps its velocity from before the pass's move. A SNOWBALL with flag 0 splits: a free search and growth on P5 as above with no cap test; the new record n takes E+0 = 0, E+0x10 = 0, `state` 0, E+0x12 = 1, byte +0x2e = 0, `mass` 0xc8, Tx[0 … 4] = Ty[0 … 4] = −100 (the index is used here), the parent's `x`, `y`, shooter and last hit, `vx` = svy/3 + svx and `vy` = svx/(−3) + svy (svx, svy = E+0x0c, E+0x0e; `cwd; idiv`, toward zero), E+0x02 = 16; seg7:0x2080(P5, −1); then the parent's `vx` = svy/(−3) + svx, `vy` = svx/3 + svy, E+0x12 = 1, E+0x02 = 16. The halves leave at about ±18.4 degrees, 5 % faster, and both end once their age passes 20. ### Wildcard defense (seg11:0x9078)
Weapon 13, case 12 of the switch: seg11:0x9078(pool, rec) (cdecl; the weapon system pushes DGROUP:0x9646 then DGROUP:0x9324 and pops 4). It is also called from the shock seg11:0x9daa at its end (seg11:0x9ed4), the computer pilot seg4:0x5973 (pool 14) and the hazard use seg12:0x6ec7 (pool 8). It writes no `fuel`, no count and no sound: W(13)+0x2e = 0 and W(13)+0x46 = 1, so it adds no violence either, and the display reads "FUEL - WILDCARD DEFENSE". [BYTES seg11:0xad66 seg11:0xad9e-0xada7 seg11:0x9ecf-0x9ed7 seg4:0x5973 seg12:0x6ec7 seg10:0x2a38 seg10:0x2a3b seg10:0x29e5 seg10:0x2a2c]
1. **Gate.** For pool DGROUP:0x9324, go only when a slot l = 1 … (signed byte +0xcb) − 1 holds weapon byte 13 or 23 (0x0d or 0x17, the latter so that the shock passes); another pool passes. [BYTES seg11:0x9087-0x90c9 seg11:0x909e-0x90be seg11:0x9092]
2. **Loop** over pool-6 records m < DGROUP:0x9412 (re-read): skip m when its entry age DGROUP:0x66ec + 0x1c·m ≤ 0, when W(id)+0x1e ≤ age (id = DGROUP:0x66e4 + 0x1c·m, signed), when id = 0x1b (27), or when d ≥ 0x28a (650) with d the low word of seg8:0x285a(far &dx, far &dy, user `x`/`y`, missile `x`/`y`), 1/8-unit cells, so 5200 world units. It does not look at the missile's launcher, `state` or `on_screen`: it converts every live pool-6 record within 650 cells, the user's own missiles and other ships' alike, and nothing outside pool 6. [BYTES seg11:0x93ce-0x9439 seg11:0x93d1-0x9439] The manual says it "turns the nearest threatening missile into a random harmless fruit" [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]; [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 122] says "any threatening object within range".
3. **Convert m.** PatBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, m's `scr_x`, `scr_y`, `size`, `size`, BLACKNESS) with no margin, even off screen; e = the highest-index live weapon-27 entry (reset to −1 at seg11:0x9464 before each conversion, so the newly converted m is never included); entry m: weapon DGROUP:0x66e4 = 27, age DGROUP:0x66ec = 1, word +0x0e DGROUP:0x66ee = W(27)+0x1e = 50; weapon-table writes W(27)−4 (DGROUP:0x37c0) = 0x39 and W(27)+0x24 (DGROUP:0x37e8) = 5 (seg10:0x1fb2 stored −1 and 1; nothing restores them, so weapon 27 keeps image slot 0x39 and end kind 5 for the session); m's `on_destroy` +0x66 = far seg12:0x6a60. The converted record keeps its velocity, `mass`, damage, launcher and target, runs on W(27)'s parameters with lifetime 50 and lives 29 passes after the conversion when unlocked, 49 when locked (the half-life turns age 25 into 45). The stale +0x0e carries: it is never reset, so a pool-6 slot that once carried a fruit steers a later kind-1 record only at age > 50. An entry ended by seg12:0x6a60 has weapon −1, so a lifetime test reads DGROUP:0x2f22, which no instruction references (BSS 0) and fails; a port must not index out of bounds there. [BYTES seg11:0x9107-0x913e seg11:0x90d5-0x9105 seg11:0x9107-0x911a seg11:0x9464 seg11:0x911e seg11:0x9124 seg11:0x912a-0x9138 seg10:0x2a6e seg10:0x2a8f seg10:0x2aad seg12:0x14f-0x15e seg12:0x6ac5-0x6adc]
4. **No live fruit (e = −1): the fruit image, slot 0x39.** With DC DGROUP:0x39e8 + 2·0x39 ≠ 0: SelectObject(DC, DGROUP:0x3974 + 2·0x39), SelectPalette(DC, DGROUP:0x3a5c + 2·0x39, 0) when DGROUP:0x920e ≠ 0, DeleteDC, DeleteObject(DGROUP:0x3ad0 + 2·0x39), both words zeroed. Then g = seg10:0x6c02(0x294 + `rand()` % 3, 0, −1): graphic 660 (23 × 23), 661 (27 × 27) or 662 (23 × 23); this `rand()` (seg5:0xdc) is drawn once per rebuild, on the first conversion of any call that finds no live fruit, so a later call after all fruit expired picks a new fruit. Then a memory DC and the slot DC, GetObject(g, 14, &bm) (`size` = bmHeight, `gap` = 5 for an odd height, 6 for even), CreateCompatibleBitmap(2·gap + size)², PatBlt BLACKNESS, BitBlt(slot DC, gap, gap, size, size, memDC, 0, 0, SRCCOPY), cleanup, seg10:0x6308(0x39, −1, −1, −1) (the rescale); `extent` = raw `size` << 3; an odd `size` becomes ((size + 1) << `Size`) >> 1 with `gap` = (4 << `Size`) >> 1, an even one keeps `size` and takes `gap` = (6 << `Size`) >> 1. All three fruits are odd (23, 27, 23): at `Size` 1 drawn 24 × 24 (28 × 28 for 661) from (4, `src_y`) of a 33 × 33 (37 × 37) bitmap whose fruit lies at (5, 5). [BYTES seg11:0x9146-0x93ac seg11:0x91ac-0x91c8 seg11:0x935d-0x93a8 seg11:0x93ca seg11:0x9374 IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 660 IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 661 IGRAPHIC.DLL 4000 662]
5. **A live fruit (e ≠ −1).** m's `size` and `gap` are e's; `extent` is not written; all fruits alive together share the one image. It does not write `src_y` (the launch set it to the missile's own `gap`, so the fruit is read from row 4), `src_x`, `mass`, `max_damage`, `blast_size`, `frame` or the lock, so the fruit still collides as a pool-6 attacker with its old `mass`. [BYTES seg11:0x93ae-0x93ca]
6. **The fruit's life in the [pool-6 mover](#pool-6-mover-seg120x0).** W(27): +0x06 = 5 (animation 5 sets `frame` = 0), +0x0a = 1, +0x1e = 50, +0x24 = 5, +0x3a = 1, +0x40 = 9. On the passes with age 43 … 49 (W+0x1e − 8 < age) seg7:0x5b68(6, m, 1) runs; at age 50 the end kind 5 calls seg12:0x6a60(6, m) with no seg7:0x2080 and then `state` = 1; a dispatched hit with `damage` > `max_damage` ends it through seg7:0x2652 → `on_destroy` seg12:0x6a60. Neither plays 0x45 or makes a fireball. [BYTES seg12:0x473-0x483 seg12:0x4e3-0x4f6 seg12:0x1d6-0x1fa seg7:0x5b68]
7. **seg12:0x6a60(pool, rec)** (cdecl) for every pool: when seg8:0x7ba8 accepts the point seg8:0x29ea(`x`, `y`) it PatBlts (`scr_x`, `scr_y`, `size`, `size`) BLACKNESS; for pool 6 it sets the missile entry age DGROUP:0x66ec + 0x1c·rec = W(id)+0x1e and entry weapon = −1 (id read before the store), and releases no shot slot; then seg7:0x5b68(pool, rec, 1) five times and `state` = 1. [BYTES seg12:0x6a7d-0x6ac0 seg12:0x6ac5-0x6adc seg12:0x6ae2-0x6afe seg12:0x6b0e seg12:0x6a8b-0x6ac0]
- **The key path.** seg11:0x6b64 (Pascal, `retf 8`) never reads lParam, so it tests neither the repeat count nor the previous-state bit. WM_KEYDOWN of Enter or 'Z' calls seg11:0xac86 only when `PlayMode` DGROUP:0x9134 = 0 and the selected byte +0xb8 + `weapon_slot` is 0x0d, so every auto-repeat key-down fires WILDCARD again; WM_KEYUP calls it for any weapon when `PlayMode` = 0, so one press with no repeats makes two seg11:0x9078 calls. The general gate (the player's dword +0x84 above now, or `state` ≠ 0) comes first; each call, repeats included, first checks +0xea, +0xe8 and object byte +0x2e. A second call finds the converted missiles skipped as weapon 27 and rebuilds nothing while a fruit lives. [BYTES seg11:0x6b64 seg11:0x72ae seg11:0x7288 seg11:0x6e19 seg11:0x6e42 seg11:0x6e58 seg11:0x6ece seg11:0x6f58-0x6f74 seg11:0x6d19 seg11:0x6d30 seg11:0x6d4a seg11:0x6e11 seg11:0x6c9b-0x6cd1]

### Launch (seg11:0x7c12)
seg11:0x7c12(w, pool, s) (Pascal, `retf 6`: s at [bp+6], the launcher's pool at [bp+8], w at [bp+0xa]) launches weapon w from record s of the pool. Its callers are the weapon system seg11:0xad21, the computer pilots seg4:0x58e4 and seg12:0x6e09. Pool 6 (DGROUP:0x1e10, allocated count DGROUP:0x92f8, in-use count DGROUP:0x9412) holds every launched weapon, and each record m has a [missile entry](structs.md#missile-entries-0x1c-bytes-at-dgroup0x66e0) at DGROUP:0x66e0 + 0x1c·m. [BYTES seg11:0xad21 seg11:0x8a3d seg8:0x6c67]
1. **Cap.** It returns at once, with no call at all, when DGROUP:0x9412 ≥ 10 (signed). [BYTES seg11:0x7c2b seg11:0x7c30]
2. **The walk.** It walks the records m = 0, 1, … . When m reaches DGROUP:0x92f8 it grows the pool: DGROUP:0x92f8 = m + 1, GlobalReAlloc to max(m + 1, 1)·0x70 bytes with flags 0x42, and DGROUP:0x9412 = m + 1, the only place it raises the in-use count. [BYTES seg11:0x7c3a seg11:0x7c45 seg11:0x7c58 seg11:0x7c63 seg11:0x7c67 seg11:0x7c69 seg11:0x7d41]
   - A grown record gets `state` 1, `mass` 20, `hit_sound` 100, dword `radar_color` 0x7f00, `max_damage` −1, `blast_size` 5, `radar_draw` seg11:0x3914, `on_damage` seg7:0x2652, `capturable` = (w = 16), `fixed` 0, `owner_pool` −1, `src_y` = `gap`, byte +0x2e = 0 and entry weapon −1. The launch never rewrites `hit_sound`, `radar_draw`, `on_damage` or `radar_color`, so a reused record keeps the wave template's ([world.md](world.md#record-templates)): record 0's `hit_sound` is 6, CLUNK, and a grown record's 100 has no WAV ([sound.md](sound.md#slots)), so its hits are silent. [BYTES seg11:0x7c86 seg11:0x7c90 seg11:0x7c9a seg11:0x7ca4 seg11:0x7cb1 seg11:0x7cbb seg11:0x7cc5 seg11:0x7cd5 seg11:0x7ce1 seg11:0x7d07 seg11:0x7d10 seg11:0x7d24 seg11:0x7d2e seg11:0x7d37 seg10:0x109c]
   - **Freeing.** Every record the walk passes with byte +0x51 = 0 is freed (entry weapon = −1) when its `state` ≠ 0, when it has `state` 0 and age 0, or when it has `state` 0 and age ≥ the lifetime W(id)+0x1e with W(id)+0x24 ≠ 6 (a kind-6 record past its lifetime falls through to the age-0 test). A missile launched since the last mover pass still has age 0, so the next launch frees and reuses its record. [BYTES seg11:0x7d4c seg11:0x7d60 seg11:0x7d64 seg11:0x7d66 seg11:0x7d71 seg11:0x7d76 seg11:0x7d80 seg11:0x7d8b]
3. **Ammo test.** A launcher outside DGROUP:0x9324 always passes. A ship searches slots l = 1 … byte +0xcb − 1 for the first weapon byte +0xb8 + l = w and passes when its count byte +0xae + l > 0 (signed) with W(w)+0x2e = 0, or when W(w)+0x2e > 0 with `fuel` (+0x1e) > W(w)+0x2e; no slot holding w fails. The literal-14 test at seg11:0x7ddf inside that branch is dead while DGROUP:0x9324 = 14. [BYTES seg11:0x7d99 seg11:0x7d9c seg11:0x7d9e seg11:0x7dad seg11:0x7dba seg11:0x7dc4 seg11:0x7dce seg11:0x7dd8 seg11:0x7ddf seg11:0x7dee seg11:0x7df4 seg11:0x7dfc seg11:0x7e01]
4. The record is taken only when its entry weapon is −1 and the test passed; otherwise the walk goes on to the next record while no launch has happened and the test passed. [BYTES seg11:0x7e0a seg11:0x7e13 seg11:0x8a05 seg11:0x8a0b seg11:0x8a0f]
5. **Refusal.** It returns when an entry k < DGROUP:0x9412 with weapon ≠ −1 has 0 < age < 15 and the same launcher pool and record. [BYTES seg11:0x7e36 seg11:0x7e3d seg11:0x7e47 seg11:0x7e50 seg11:0x7e56 seg11:0x7e5d seg11:0x8a35]
6. **Ammo.** A ship lowers the found slot's count byte +0xae + l by 1 when W(w)+0x2e = 0, else `fuel` by W(w)+0x2e, floored at 0 (`jns`). Then every launcher, whatever its pool, posts WM_PAINT (0x0f) to DGROUP:0xa7e with no test that the window exists, which repaints the whole [weapon display](world.md#weapon-display-id-0xdc) with the new count. [BYTES seg11:0x7e60 seg11:0x7e66 seg11:0x7e6c seg11:0x7e7b seg11:0x7e7f seg11:0x7e8e seg11:0x7e92 seg11:0x7e94 seg11:0x7e96 seg11:0x7e9a seg11:0x7e9e seg11:0x7ea6]
7. **Entry and record.** Entry age +0x0c, +0x18 and +0x16 = 0, weapon +0x04 = w, mode +0x14 = 2; the record's `scr_x`, `scr_y`, `old_scr_x` and `old_scr_y` = −1000. [BYTES seg11:0x7eb1 seg11:0x7eb5 seg11:0x7ebc seg11:0x7ec0 seg11:0x7ec4 seg11:0x7ed2 seg11:0x7edf seg11:0x7eeb seg11:0x7ef5]
8. **Target.** For kind W(w)+0x0a = 2 from a ship it calls seg3:0xf4(s, dword ship +0x32) unless s is the player in a player game, then copies ship words +0x3e/+0x40 into entry +0x00/+0x02. Otherwise entry +0x00 = −1. Either way the lock +0x1a = 0, `state` = 0 and byte +0x2e = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x7efe seg11:0x7f09 seg11:0x7f0e seg11:0x7f12 seg11:0x7f17 seg11:0x7f22 seg11:0x7f2a seg11:0x7f37 seg11:0x7f43 seg11:0x7f4b seg11:0x7f57 seg11:0x7f5d seg11:0x7f69 seg11:0x7f73]
9. **Size.** With c = W(w)−8: `size` = (c << `Size`) >> 1 and `gap` = (6 << `Size`) >> 1 for an even c; for an odd c `size` = ((c + 1) << `Size`) >> 1 and `gap` = (4 << `Size`) >> 1; `src_y` = `gap`; `extent` = c << 3 from the unrounded c. Then `blast_size` = W(w)+0x22, byte +0x50 = 0, `mass` = W(w)+0x10, `max_damage` = W(w)+0x08, `damage` = 0, and the launcher's `frame`, `x` and `y`. [BYTES seg11:0x7f80 seg11:0x7f84 seg11:0x7f86 seg11:0x7f9a seg11:0x7fa2 seg11:0x7fa9 seg11:0x7fb5 seg11:0x7fc2 seg11:0x7fd1 seg11:0x7fe7 seg11:0x7ff5 seg11:0x7ff9 seg11:0x8000 seg11:0x8010 seg11:0x8015 seg11:0x8021 seg11:0x8031 seg11:0x804b seg11:0x8066 seg11:0x8076]
   - With W(w)−2 = −1 (no graphic, weapon 22) it calls `rand()` once, at seg11:0x80c5, for seg6:0x84fc((r % 2 + 1)·10, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, W+0x1e − 1, m); the other nine arguments, the second `rand()` and the dead Demon's Gate age store are in [Launch: the weapon branches](#launch-the-weapon-branches). [BYTES seg11:0x8087 seg11:0x80c5 seg11:0x80aa-0x80da]
   - `on_destroy` = seg12:0x274a, or seg7:0x2e02 when W(w)+0x24 is 4 or 6. Weapon 16 sets `capturable` = 1 and, for ships below the last whose design team word is 7, writes the ship words +0x06, +0x08, +0x36, +0x3a and +0x94 ([Launch: the weapon branches](#launch-the-weapon-branches)); every other weapon sets `capturable` = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x7c12 seg11:0x8119-0x8181]

[BYTES seg11:0x818a seg11:0x86c3 seg11:0x86ca seg11:0x86d0 seg11:0x880e seg11:0x8815]
    - **Front launch.** With E the launcher's and e the missile's `extent` and f the launcher's `frame`: `x` += (E − e)/2 + lo16(((E + e)/2)·DGROUP:0x1050[4f]) / 15, `y` alike with DGROUP:0x1052[4f]; each /2 is `cwd; sub ax, dx; sar ax, 1` and the product's high word is replaced before the `idiv` by 15. The velocity is the heading vector << 2, and a ship launcher's dword +0xa0 = frame# + 36. The player's launch calls seg10:0x744c(0x69), and every launch then plays seg10:0x7944(W(w)+0x18, 6, m, 1, far DGROUP:0x1118) ([sound.md](sound.md#inspace-wrappers)), whose name argument DGROUP:0x1118 is an empty string; weapon 8's id is 0x1c. ### Launch: the weapon branches
In this section "kind" is W+0x0a (DGROUP:0x2f5e, the launch kind) and "end kind" is W+0x24 (DGROUP:0x2f78); the older text above names W+0x24 the weapon kind in two places, which is the end kind. The branches below run in steps 9 and 10 of [Launch](#launch-seg110x7c12), after M (the pool-6 record m) has the launcher L's `frame`, `x` and `y`; /2 is `cwd; sub ax, dx; sar ax, 1`, /4 and /8 and /16 keep their `and dx, n-1` bias and truncate toward zero. [BYTES seg11:0x804b-0x8080]
- **Graphic −1 (weapon 22 ENLIGHTENMENT only,** tested on W(w)−2 = −1 at seg11:0x8087**).** With DGROUP:0x92e8 ≠ 0 the entry age is stored W+0x1e/4, a dead store: step 11 writes age 0 at seg11:0x8981 before anything reads it. The launcher then draws one `rand()` (seg11:0x80c5) and calls seg6:0x84fc((r % 2 + 1)·10, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, W+0x1e − 1, m) (cdecl, ten words, `add sp, 0x14`). seg6:0x84fc returns 1 at once when DGROUP:0x94da = 0 and 0 when DGROUP:0x94c4 ≠ 0; it then scans the DGROUP:0x94ce slots of 0x229 bytes at the far pointer DGROUP:0x6b08 for byte +2 = 0 and returns 0 with no `rand()` when none is free; only after a free slot does it draw `rand()` (seg6:0x8569) and choose sound 0x1b (r % 3 = 0) or 0x4e. Argument 8 becomes clamp(a8·600/word DGROUP:0x10, 0x1c, 0x1d), so 0x1c. The slot gets byte +0 = 0, words +0x27 = +0x29 = 0, byte +2 = a0 (10 or 20), word +5 = a9, word +7 = 0, dwords +0x13/+0x17 = a1/a2 through seg5:0x8c0 with cl = 8, dwords +0x1b/+0x1f = sext a3/a4, +0xb/+0xd = a5/a6, +0x11 = 0, +0xf = the clamped a7 and byte +1 = a10 (m); the sound is seg10:0x754a(snd, 6, m, 1) when DGROUP:0x9330 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x9130 = 0, else seg10:0x754a(snd, 0, 0, 0). The wave generator calls seg6:0x72d8(5, 5), sets DGROUP:0x94c4 = 0 and DGROUP:0x9330 = 1, and the wave frame calls the particle movers seg6:0x73f6 and seg6:0x7c14 right after seg12:0x0; what they do with kinds 10 and 20 belongs to the Noble weapons (M6) [OPEN]. So per launch: one `rand()` always, a second only with a free particle slot. [BYTES seg11:0x8087-0x80da seg11:0x808e-0x80a6 seg11:0x80aa-0x80da seg11:0x80c5 seg11:0x80ca-0x80d1 seg11:0x897d seg11:0x8981 seg6:0x850b-0x8521 seg6:0x8551-0x8557 seg6:0x8518 seg6:0x8569-0x8584 seg6:0x8584-0x85a4 seg6:0x851f seg9:0x4683-0x4695 seg9:0x4687 seg9:0x468f seg9:0x4695 seg6:0x72d8 seg6:0x72ed seg6:0x7327 seg7:0xcfb seg7:0xd00 seg7:0xd05 seg6:0x73f6 seg6:0x7c14 seg9:0x6d8f]
- **Weapon 16 DOUGHNUTS** (seg11:0x8119-0x8181): the missile's `capturable` = 1 (every other weapon sets 0); for ships k < DGROUP:0x9316 (signed `jge`, so the last ship is skipped, and `state` is not tested) whose design team word DGROUP:0x970a + 0xad·(ship +0x4e) is 7, it writes ship +0x06 = 6, +0x08 = m, +0x3a = 2, +0x36 = 2 and dword +0x94 = DGROUP:0x1e50 + 100, not the goal words +0x32/+0x34. Only the computer pilots read these (the Enforcers chase the doughnut: strings 512 ".I Want That Doughnut" and 961), so with the player alone nothing runs. [BYTES seg11:0x8119-0x8181 seg11:0x8127 seg11:0x8136-0x813c seg11:0x813e-0x814e seg11:0x8150-0x8172 seg11:0x8181 seg11:0x7ce1-0x7cfa] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 33] id 512 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 61] id 961
- **The kind-5 aim** (weapons 17 and 21; seg11:0x8193-0x86bc) replaces the front and rear launch. M.v = L.v; M.x += L.extent/2 − M.extent/2 and M.y likewise; tp = −1. (a) When W(w)+0x46 ≠ 0 (both weapons): for pool-6 records k < DGROUP:0x9412 whose entry launcher is not (pool, s), d = lo16 seg8:0x285a(M.x/y, record k's x/y); the last k with d < 350 (signed) is the target (6, k), with no weapon, `state` or k ≠ m test. (b) With none, the best visible object: best = 20000; for the visibility list i < DGROUP:0x9146 skip (P, R) = (pool, s) and `mass` ≤ 1 (signed), d = lo16 seg8:0x285a(cand x/y, L x/y) (L pushed last, so dx = L − cand); for a ship candidate d = lo16(3d)/4, then with a ship launcher whose goal (+0x32, +0x34) is the candidate d/6, else lo16((d + 50)·5) when seg13:0x2688(s, R) > 150 (signed, cdecl, the launcher's record pushed last) or the two design team words are equal; h = seg8:0x19b0(den = −dx, num = −dy), and L.`frame` − 1 ≤ h ≤ L.`frame` + 1 (plain signed range, no wrap: frame 0 does not take 15, 15 does not take 0) makes d = lo16(2d)/3; a smaller d (signed) becomes (P, R). For a non-ship launcher only the 3/4 applies. (c) With a target: seg8:0x348e(6, m, tp, tr, W(w)+0x0e) ([the aim](#the-aim-seg80x348e): V = 120 for weapon 17, 95 for weapon 21), then h = seg8:0x19b0(den = M.vx − L.vx, num = M.vy − L.vy) and each axis gets += lo16(5·lo16((M.extent/2 + L.extent/8)·heading[h]))/20. (d) When M.v still equals L.v on both axes: M.v = L.v + (heading[L.`frame`] << 3), h = seg8:0x19b0(M.v − L.v) and each axis += lo16((M.extent/2 + L.extent/2)·heading[h])/20 with no ×5. heading = DGROUP:0x1050/0x1052 + 4h. Step 11 skips the launcher-velocity add for kind 5, and a kind-5 launch never writes the launcher's dword +0xa0. [BYTES seg11:0x8193-0x86bc seg11:0x81a3-0x81c7 seg11:0x81d7-0x8235 seg11:0x8239 seg11:0x8240-0x82a5 seg11:0x824e-0x8262 seg11:0x8264-0x8299 seg11:0x828c seg11:0x82a7-0x82ab seg11:0x82af-0x837b seg11:0x837e-0x83a3 seg11:0x8390-0x839d seg11:0x83bc-0x8400 seg11:0x8403-0x844a seg11:0x82c1-0x8312 seg11:0x8315-0x8326 seg11:0x8329-0x8357 seg11:0x835a-0x8378 seg11:0x844d-0x8451 seg11:0x8455-0x855a seg11:0x855e-0x86b8 seg11:0x898b-0x8990 seg11:0x87e3-0x8803]
  - **The self-aim.** Loop (a) has no k ≠ m test and the only writers of the entry launcher words are seg11:0x89cb and seg11:0x89d2, which run after this block, so when m < DGROUP:0x9412 (always for a grown record) and record m's stale launcher words differ from (pool, s), loop (a) adopts (6, m) itself at d = 1: seg8:0x348e then sees dx = dy = M.extent/2 (both points are M's corner; only the target's is moved by its `extent`/2), and for a pellet (extent 72) vx = L.vx + 120·51/36 = L.vx + 170, vy = L.vy + 120·36/51 = L.vy + 84: the first pellet of every fresh pool-6 record flies down and to the right. When dx = 0 the original faults (R1). [BYTES seg11:0x8247-0x82a5 seg11:0x89cb seg11:0x89d2 seg8:0x29db seg8:0x3507-0x3523 seg8:0x348e]

With f = L.`frame`, n = ship +0x4e, f' = (f + 8) % 16 (the remainder of `cwd; idiv 16`, the quotient discarded) and the rear muzzle entry (rx, ry) = DGROUP:0x7ba0/0x7ba2 + 4·(64n + f) (entry 16 + f of seg10:0x1620): M.x += (rx << 3) + lo16(11·hx[f'])/4 − M.extent/2, M.y likewise with ry and hy; M.v = −(heading[f] << 2); M.x −= L.vx, M.y −= L.vy (the ship-pool record); step 11 then adds L.v to M.v, so the record leaves at L.v − 4·heading from a point behind the cell's corner. It writes no dword +0xa0. A dead path for a kind other than 5 with W+0x04 outside {1, 2} and a ship launcher (seg11:0x880a-0x881a jumps to seg11:0x897d) leaves M at L's position with whatever velocity the record held; no table weapon reaches it. ### Pool-6 mover (seg12:0x0)
seg12:0x0 walks m < DGROUP:0x9412, with id = the entry weapon and W = W(id). [BYTES seg12:0x519 seg12:0x51c seg12:0x522]
- **Run condition.** A record runs only when (W+0x1e > age or W+0x24 = 6), id ≠ −1 and `state` = 0; any other record calls seg12:0x129e(6, m) when its byte +0x51 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg12:0x536 seg12:0x53c seg12:0x543 seg12:0x551 seg12:0x506 seg12:0x513]
- Then, in order: [BYTES seg12:0x55c seg12:0x445]
  1. **Animation** by W+0x06: 1 runs seg12:0x17, 4 seg12:0x2b1, 5 seg12:0x2e8, and 2 (weapon 8) or any other value nothing. [BYTES seg12:0x55c seg12:0x561 seg12:0x567 seg12:0x56e seg12:0x574 seg12:0x579]
  2. seg4:0x5514 every 4 frames when W+0x38 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg12:0x0]
  3. **Retarget** seg12:0x586(m, age << 3) when age % 13 = 0 (`cwd; idiv`), age > 8, the kind W+0x0a is neither 1 nor 5, and W+0x3c ≠ 0, no target (entry +0x00 = −1) or age = 13. [BYTES seg12:0xa7 seg12:0xab seg12:0xad seg12:0xb6 seg12:0xbd seg12:0xc4 seg12:0xca seg12:0xd1 seg12:0xe0 seg12:0xe5]
  4. **Half-life.** With the lock +0x1a = 0 and age = W+0x1e / 2 (truncated) it sets age = W+0x1e − 5. [BYTES seg12:0xec seg12:0xfd seg12:0x100 seg12:0x102 seg12:0x108 seg12:0x10b]
  5. **Off screen.** With W+0x3a = 0, `on_screen` = 0 and age > 20 it sets age = W+0x1e. [BYTES seg12:0x113 seg12:0x124 seg12:0x12f seg12:0x13c]
  6. **Steer** seg12:0x7c0(m) while locked, and for kind 1 only once age > entry word +0x0e. [BYTES seg12:0x144 seg12:0x14f seg12:0x156 seg12:0x15a seg12:0x164]
  7. Drag through W+0x40, and the helpers of kinds that weapon 8 does not use (seg7:0x5b68 for W+0x24 = 5, seg12:0x64aa for W+0x14 ≠ 0). [BYTES seg12:0x0]
  8. **Move** seg8:0x1b54(0x600 | m, 1), then `src_x` = lo16((`size` + `gap`)·`frame`) + `gap`. [BYTES seg12:0x23a seg12:0x23d seg12:0x23e seg12:0x240 seg12:0x255 seg12:0x259 seg12:0x25d seg12:0x261 seg12:0x265]
  9. **Draw**, only while `state` = 0: with W−2 ≠ −1 seg8:0xb76(0x600 | m, 3, DGROUP:0x39e8[W−4], 0), the ordinary erase-and-SRCCOPY path of [world.md](world.md#sprites-seg80xb76); with W−2 = −1 it draws nothing and sets `scr_x`/`scr_y` = seg8:0x29ea(`x`, `y`) instead. [BYTES seg12:0x26f seg12:0x274 seg12:0x27d seg12:0x282 seg12:0x284-0x2ab seg12:0x2f9 seg12:0x2fd seg12:0x300 seg12:0x301 seg12:0x307 seg12:0x30d seg12:0x311 seg12:0x313]
  10. **Collision**, only when `on_screen` ≠ 0 and age > 3: DGROUP:0x943a = 6, DGROUP:0x943c = m, h = seg8:0x50aa(0x600 | m), a = seg8:0x546c(h, &`vx`, &`vy`, `extent`, `mass`), passing `extent` without the + 2 of the other movers (the word is never read). A hit object with `capturable` ≠ 0 in the ship pool whose ship byte +0xea = 0 goes to seg13:0x16ac(ship, 0x600 | m); the kind-6 pick-up of [Ship against ship](#ship-against-ship-seg130x0) has its copy here; any other hit goes to seg8:0x5df2(6, m, hit pool, hit rec, a). [BYTES seg12:0x320 seg12:0x32b seg12:0x330 seg12:0x332 seg12:0x33b seg12:0x345 seg12:0x36c seg12:0x370 seg12:0x376 seg12:0x3a3 seg12:0x3fb seg12:0x40d seg12:0x410 seg12:0x416 seg12:0x41a seg12:0x41d seg12:0x41f]
  11. **Age.** A record with `state` ≠ 0 skips the rest. Otherwise the age rises by 1, and at age ≥ W+0x1e a W+0x24 = 1 record calls seg12:0x274a(6, m) and seg7:0x2080(6, m), and every kind but 6 gets `state` 1. [BYTES seg12:0x42f seg12:0x445 seg12:0x449 seg12:0x452 seg12:0x45f seg12:0x4db seg12:0x4e7 seg12:0x4f6]
- **Weapon 8's life.** It ends on the pass that raises the age to 200, 200 passes after launch, when it is locked at age 100; unlocked, step 4 turns age 100 into 195 and it ends 105 passes after launch. With W+0x3a = 1 an off-screen missile lives its full time without drawing or colliding. It is first tested for a collision at age 4, and the pair test ignores its launcher while age < 10 ([Pair test](#pair-test-seg80x6afa)). [BYTES seg12:0xfb seg12:0x100 seg12:0x102 seg12:0x108 seg12:0x445 seg12:0x449 seg12:0x32b seg10:0x22c6 seg10:0x22ea]
  - core-0002's missiles that never hit end at age 200 with no sound, and the entry's weapon goes −1; DGROUP:0x9412 drops when the last one ends. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **seg12:0x274a** on a pool-6 record sets the entry age to W+0x1e and the entry weapon to −1 before it plays seg10:0x754a(0x45, 6, m, 1); its early exits for a record beyond both counts skip both stores and the sound. [BYTES seg12:0x2771 seg12:0x2784 seg12:0x2ae6 seg12:0x2af0 seg12:0x2af5 seg12:0x2af9 seg12:0x2afd seg12:0x2b0b seg12:0x2b0d]
- **Pairs.** A missile meets rocks, icons and every pool below 6 in those pools' movers, which find the pair ([Who tests whom](#who-tests-whom-seg80x50aa)); the missile mover finds only ships and the pools above 6. Its `on_damage` seg7:0x2652 ([structs.md](structs.md#object-record-0x70-bytes)) destroys it once `damage` > `max_damage`, so weapon 8 (`max_damage` 1) dies on one dispatched hit of 2 or more. [BYTES seg8:0x52b6 seg7:0x2652 seg7:0x2699 seg7:0x26a7 seg10:0x22e4]
- **Contact comes first.** In a wave frame seg7:0x0 calls the rock mover seg7:0x3bae and the icon mover seg7:0x309c before the pool-6 mover seg12:0x0, so a missile that meets a rock is found by the rock mover's search before its own pass could fuse it. When the rock's response seg8:0x546c (seg7:0x4049) gets bounce result 1, it dispatches the missile inside itself (seg8:0x698a(1) at seg8:0x5867 or seg8:0x6a44(1) at seg8:0x5a9d), and that dispatch plays the missile record's `hit_sound` +0x42 at seg8:0x645d; the rock's own dispatch seg8:0x5df2 follows at seg7:0x4060. On bounce result 0 neither party is dispatched: seg8:0x546c returns −1 and the rock's dispatch returns 0 at its −1 test. [BYTES seg7:0xcd8 seg7:0xcec seg7:0xcfb seg7:0x4049 seg8:0x57e3 seg8:0x5867 seg8:0x5a18 seg8:0x5a9d seg8:0x645d seg7:0x4060]
  - The wave template's loop over pool-6 records 0 … DGROUP:0x92f8 − 1 writes `mass` +0x44 = 20, `hit_sound` +0x42 = 6, dword `radar_color` 0x7f00, `max_damage` −1, `blast_size` 5, `radar_draw` seg11:0x3914, `on_damage` seg7:0x2652 and bytes +0x53 and +0x54 = 0, so a contact plays 0x06 CLUNK. The missile then ends with 0x45 when its `damage` exceeds `max_damage` W(w)+0x08 (1 for weapons 7 and 8), through `on_damage` and `on_destroy`: seg12:0x274a for end kinds other than 4 and 6, which stores the entry age W+0x1e and weapon −1 and plays seg10:0x754a(0x45, 6, m, 1), and seg7:0x2e02 for kinds 4 and 6. [BYTES seg10:0x1092-0x1101 seg11:0x8029 seg7:0x26a7 seg7:0x26b1 seg11:0x80f7 seg11:0x810d seg12:0x2af5 seg12:0x2af9 seg12:0x2afd seg12:0x2b0d]
  - **The fuse** measures only the locked target, and only in the steer, which runs while the lock +0x1a ≠ 0; every launch writes lock 0, and the retarget runs only at age % 13 = 0 with age > 8, so an unlocked missile cannot fuse before age 13. The steer's target point is the target's world x/y plus its `size` +0x22 sar 1 and the missile's point its x/y plus its `extent` +0x20 sar 1: the fuse point lies `size`/16 px inside the target's top-left corner at `Size` 1, while the missile's point is its centre. So a missile closing on a rock from the left or from above comes within the fuse distance before its square meets the rock and fuses, and one from the right or from below touches first. [BYTES seg11:0x7f4b seg11:0x7f5d seg12:0xa7 seg12:0xab seg12:0xb6 seg12:0x144-0x164 seg12:0x7eb-0x831 seg12:0x963-0x994]
  - core-0005's ten RADAR-GUIDED launches end six times with 0x06 followed 3–8 ms later by 0x08 and 0x45, drawn at the rock's edge, three times with two 0x45 alone short of the rock, after a lock at age 14, and once silent. core-0006's HEATSEEKER MISSILES (weapon 7, no fuse) end on rocks with 0x06, 0x08 and 0x45 within 10 ms. [ORACLE core-0005] [ORACLE core-0006]

### Pool-6 mover: the weapon branches
- **The dodge seg4:0x5514(m)** (Pascal, `retf 2`), called every 4 passes when W+0x38 ≠ 0 (weapons 6 and 9), before the retarget. M.`on_screen` = 0 sets entry mode +0x14 = 2 and returns; v = seg13:0x1076(6, m), 0xffff sets mode 2 and returns; otherwise entry +0x10/+0x12 = (v >> 8, v & 0xff) (stored before the launcher test, so a launcher result is stored too, with mode 2, where nothing reads it), a result equal to the entry launcher sets mode 2, else with T = that record, dx, dy = T − M positions and dvx, dvy = T − M velocities (16-bit, then `cwd`), cross = sext(dx)·sext(dvy) − sext(dy)·sext(dvx) (32-bit through seg5:0x314 twice) > 0 gives mode 4 and ≤ 0 mode 3. [BYTES seg12:0x7b-0x94 seg4:0x552f-0x5534 seg4:0x563e seg4:0x5538-0x554d seg4:0x5551-0x5561 seg4:0x5565-0x5573 seg4:0x5577-0x55fd seg4:0x55fe-0x561a seg4:0x561c-0x5636 seg4:0x5514-0x5650]
  - **seg13:0x1076(pool, rec)** (Pascal, `retf 4`: pool at [bp+8], rec at [bp+6]) is the most urgent object on a collision course. The goal for pool 6 is the entry target (+0x00/+0x02, not +0x10/+0x12) with goal mode 2, a pool-6 caller always setting the local mode word to 2; a ship caller sets it from ship +0x38 and takes its goal from ship words +0x32/+0x34 (M5). It scans i < DGROUP:0x9318 + DGROUP:0x9146: the visibility list for i < DGROUP:0x9146, then pool 12 record 2(i − DGROUP:0x9146) + 1 (the gate midpoints). A candidate is skipped when it is the object itself, in pool 2, 9 or 4, the goal, or (goal mode 2) in pool 0. Then d = lo16 seg8:0x285a(self, cand) gives cand − self and the candidate needs 0 < d < 300; dvx, dvy = self.v − cand.v; q = (dx·dvx + dy·dvy)/d (32-bit) must be > 0; score = d − lo16(seg5:0x110(3q, 2)); a score ≥ the best so far (from 20000) is rejected; a score ≥ 60 is kept only for pool 8 with rec < 2·DGROUP:0x9318 and score < 120; in a kind-6 wave (DGROUP:0x9320 = 6) with byte DGROUP:0x9eb5 = 0 every pool-8 candidate is rejected (M6). It returns (pool << 8) | cbw(rec) or 0xffff; seg8:0x35d0(dvx, dvy) at seg13:0x11f4 is computed and never used; no `rand()`. [BYTES seg13:0x108b-0x10d0 seg13:0x10d0 seg13:0x10ef seg13:0x10fc-0x110b seg13:0x1113-0x1157 seg13:0x1164-0x127e seg13:0x11f4-0x1200 seg13:0x1281-0x12c8 seg13:0x12a5-0x12b7 seg13:0x12ce-0x12f4 seg13:0x12f7-0x130a seg13:0x1315]
  - **The turn in modes 3 and 4.** The steer passes the mode to seg13:0xd6c, which sends every mode other than 2 to seg13:0xf2d before its race test (the per-axis wrap tests on DGROUP:0x9320 at seg13:0xdcf and seg13:0xe0c run first), so in every wave: mode 4 h = seg8:0x19b0(den = dy − (dx sar 2), num = −(dx + (dy sar 2))), mode 3 h = seg8:0x19b0(den = −(dy + (dx sar 2)), num = dx − (dy sar 2)), mode 5 or 0x1e h = seg8:0x19b0(−dx, −dy), with dx = tx − mx, dy = ty − my in cells: a turn at right angles to the threat. These match `turn_toward` cases 3, 4 and 5. The steer then aims at the alternate target with doubled thrust and no cloak jitter, its fuse measuring to the alternate target too, so a TARGETTING MISSILE (fuse 50) fuses on the threat it dodges; the mode stays until the next seg4:0x5514 call. [BYTES seg13:0xe2f seg13:0xe33 seg13:0xf2d-0xf7f seg13:0xdcf seg13:0xe0c seg13:0xe37 seg13:0xd6c seg12:0x843 seg12:0x963-0x99e seg12:0x805 seg12:0x848]
- **Fire from a missile seg12:0x64aa(m, id)** (Pascal, `retf 4`: m at [bp+8], id at [bp+6]; no other caller), called whenever W+0x14 ≠ 0, after the W+0x14 = 3 charge step (+4 to byte +0x50 per pass, capped at 0x4b, signed) and before the integrator. It returns unless (age & 3) = 0 and age ≥ 13 (signed): the first firing pass is age 16. The fire rule is W+0x16: 1 always; 2 when `rand()` % 3 = 0 (seg12:0x64f4, once per qualifying pass, after the steer's two); 3, only while M is on screen, for ships k = 0 … DGROUP:0x9316 inclusive with `state` 0 and `on_screen` ≠ 0, h = seg8:0x19b0(den = S.`scr_x` − M.`scr_x`, num = S.`scr_y` − M.`scr_y`) (screen coordinates +0x00/+0x02) and h − 1 ≤ M.`frame` ≤ h + 1 (plain signed range, no wrap), the launcher included; 4, when the entry target (+0x00/+0x02) has `state` 0, is on screen and M.`frame` is within one of its heading (no table weapon has it). The action is W+0x14: 1 seg12:0x2db6(6, m, M.`frame`, 0xb, 5) (a FIREBALL), 2 the same with kind 4, 3 seg11:0x9fdc(6, m) only when the signed byte +0x50 > 0x19 (it never lowers the charge, so a charged battery stays at 0x4b and discharges on every age ≡ 0 mod 4 from 16 on), 4 seg13:0x1fc2(6, m). [BYTES seg12:0x1fd-0x233 seg12:0x208-0x227 seg12:0x64b9-0x64cc seg12:0x64d4-0x6512 seg12:0x64f1 seg12:0x64f4 seg12:0x6514-0x65c6 seg12:0x654c-0x6558 seg12:0x655a-0x65ad seg12:0x65b0-0x65cb seg12:0x65ce-0x667a seg12:0x6681-0x66f7 seg12:0x6691-0x66a7 seg12:0x66ac-0x66c1 seg12:0x66cf-0x66e8 seg12:0x66c3-0x66c8 seg12:0x6701 seg12:0x64d8 seg12:0x668b seg12:0x66d7]
  - **seg13:0x1fc2(pool, rec)** (Pascal, `retf 4`) fires one gun shot from an object into the shot slots DGROUP:0x6098 ([Shot slots](structs.md#shot-slots-9--0x94-bytes-at-dgroup0x6098)). With `Detail` > 0 it returns unless some slot j < DGROUP:0x594 has owner words (+0x7a, +0x7c) = (DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x9646) (the player's, free or busy) or at least two slots have +0x76 = −1. In the first free slot: +0x76 = 0, +0x7e = 0, +0x80 = 0; −100 to +0x30 + 2b and +0x48 + 2b for b = 0, 1, each followed by seg8:0x764(j, b), which see the previous barrel count; +0x30 = e/2 + (hx[`frame`] << 3) + x and +0x48 likewise (no world wrap); owner +0x7a/+0x7c, +0x78 = 1, +0x84 = 1, +0x82 = 0; velocity +0x70/+0x72 = 3·heading[`frame`] with no owner velocity; lifetime +0x74 = seg5:0x110(20·max(DGROUP:0x90ea, DGROUP:0x90ec), 1000) (36 in a kind-6 wave, which also calls seg11:0x5626(j)); sound seg10:0x754a(3, 0x1e, j, 2). It writes no +0x86, +0x88, +0x8a or dword +0x90 and leaves trail points 2 … 5 as they were. At 1024 the lifetime is 20 sub-steps; the shot's power is always 23, drawn in the brush row DGROUP:0x3b60, the head is protected from its own shot by the shooter exclusion, the player's ship can be hit, and the shot scores nothing and credits nobody. Other callers: seg12:0x60cb and seg12:0x6260 in seg12:0x5636. [BYTES seg13:0x1fc2-0x2187 seg13:0x1fd1-0x1fe3 seg13:0x1fe6-0x203a seg13:0x1ffa-0x2011 seg13:0x2016-0x2025 seg13:0x2046-0x206a seg13:0x206c-0x20b7 seg13:0x20bb-0x20d4 seg13:0x20da-0x20f6 seg13:0x20fa-0x2115 seg13:0x2116-0x213d seg13:0x2141-0x214a seg13:0x2154-0x2169 seg13:0x216b-0x2175 seg13:0x2107-0x2115 seg12:0x60cb seg12:0x6260 seg8:0x764 seg8:0x6f3c]
- **The ends.** W+0x24 = 1: seg12:0x274a(6, m), seg7:0x2080(6, m), `state` 1. W+0x24 = 4: seg7:0x2e02(6, m) as a direct far call (not through +0x66), seg7:0x2080(6, m), `state` 1. W+0x24 = 5: seg12:0x6a60(6, m), no seg7:0x2080, `state` 1 (reachable only after a WILDCARD, [Wildcard defense](#wildcard-defense-seg110x9078)). W+0x24 = 6: entry target = entry launcher and lock = 1; only when the launcher's record has `state` ≠ 0 does it call seg7:0x2e02(6, m) and seg7:0x2080(6, m), and it never stores `state`; seg7:0x2e02 itself sets `state` 1 when it finds 0, so a boomerang whose launcher is dead ends. [BYTES seg12:0x452-0x4f6 seg12:0x464-0x46d seg12:0x4c9-0x4f6 seg12:0x473-0x483 seg12:0x485-0x4ec seg12:0x4e0-0x4f6 seg12:0x4e7 seg12:0x462 seg12:0x4d3 seg12:0x4d6 seg12:0x4ee seg7:0x2e28]
  - **seg7:0x2e02 for pool 6** returns and does nothing when `state` ≠ 0 (pool 1 still gets seg7:0x65aa); otherwise it sets `state` 1 and erases with PatBlt, takes the image slot DGROUP:0x39e8[W(w)−4], and with no effect entry among 0 … 3 (DGROUP:0x1e90) free or of type ≤ 5 it plays no sound and starts no effect. It plays seg10:0x754a(0x1a FLANGE, 6, m, 1) for W+0x24 = 6 and seg10:0x754a(0x1f SHATTER, 6, m, 1) for any other end kind of a pool-6 record. It never frees the missile entry. Frame 5 of the type-6 entry calls the blast: the BEER BOTTLE's shock blast. [BYTES seg7:0x2e24 seg7:0x2e28-0x2e4f seg7:0x2e82-0x2e9a seg7:0x2e7c-0x2ec2 seg7:0x306e-0x3076 seg7:0x2ffd-0x3013 seg7:0x3079 seg7:0x2e9e-0x2ea3 seg7:0x2eba-0x2ec2]
- **The kind-6 catch** (seg12:0x3ad-0x40b): a hit on a ship whose selected weapon (byte DGROUP:0x7148 + 0x15a·s + ship word +0x18) has W+0x24 = 6, by a record of W+0x24 = 6, sets the record's `capturable` = 1, calls seg13:0x16ac(s, 0x600 | m) and increments byte DGROUP:0x713e + 0x15a·s + (ship word +0x18) with no cap and no test of the launcher, so any ship with BOOMERANGS selected catches any boomerang; before it, a record with `capturable` ≠ 0 (DOUGHNUTS, or a boomerang already caught) hitting a ship with byte +0xea = 0 goes to seg13:0x16ac only. Both skip the dispatch; the dispatcher's own case clears its flag for the same pair. [BYTES seg12:0x3ad-0x3dd seg12:0x37e-0x3ab seg12:0x382-0x3ab seg12:0x3b1-0x407 seg12:0x3eb seg12:0x3fb seg12:0x407 seg8:0x6040-0x6067]
- **The dissolve.** The not-run branch is exactly "byte +0x51 ≠ 0 → seg12:0x129e(6, m)", the pickup dissolve of pools 6 and 11 with no wave-kind test; for pool 6 the byte is the counter the touch sets to 10, so an eaten doughnut dissolves from its last sheet frame and a caught boomerang from frame 0. [BYTES seg12:0x4fe-0x516 seg12:0x129e seg12:0x1398 seg12:0x13af seg12:0x12cb seg12:0x16b7 seg13:0x1d50]
- **BATTERIES** keep their charge: seg11:0x9fdc never writes +0x50. **Entry word +0x0e** (DGROUP:0x66ee) has one writer, seg11:0x911a (the fruit, 50), never reset and never cleared between waves, and entry words +0x10/+0x12 and +0x14 have the writers seg4:0x555b, seg4:0x5561 and seg11:0x7ec4/seg4:0x562a/0x5636/0x5642; a pool-6 slot that once carried a fruit therefore steers a later kind-1 record (weapons 10, 11, 16, 18, 19, 20) only at age > 50. [BYTES seg11:0x911a seg12:0x156 seg4:0x555b seg4:0x5561 seg11:0x7ec4 seg4:0x562a seg4:0x5636 seg4:0x5642]

#### The launched weapons, word by word
Every word is stored by seg10:0x1fb2; the table is identical for DGROUP:0x920c = 0 and −1 and differs for 3 only in the 23 words +0x36. [BYTES seg10:0x1fb2-0x2ff8] Weapon w is string 186 + w (6 TARGETTING MISSILES, 7 HEATSEEKER MISSILES, 8 RADAR-GUIDED MISSILES, 9 DEATH HEADS, 10 REAR-DEPLOYED MINES, 11 DUMB MISSILES, 12 FIRE DEMONS, 14 BATTERIES, 16 DOUGHNUTS, 17 PROTECTIVE PELLETS, 18 BEER BOTTLES, 19 BOOMERANGS, 20 SPUD MISSILES, 21 THROWING STARS, 22 ENLIGHTENMENT). The manual text of each is [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 109] + w.

[BYTES seg10:0x2249 seg10:0x221c seg10:0x2201 seg10:0x221f seg10:0x2284 seg10:0x22a0 seg10:0x223d seg10:0x2228 seg10:0x2951 seg10:0x291b seg10:0x290c seg10:0x2954 seg10:0x290f seg10:0x2960 seg10:0x2957 seg10:0x2912 seg10:0x2921 seg10:0x292d seg10:0x2939 seg10:0x27dd seg10:0x27f8 seg10:0x279e seg10:0x2792 seg10:0x27ad seg10:0x27b0 seg10:0x27ce seg10:0x27c2 seg10:0x285d seg10:0x2845 seg10:0x2848 seg10:0x2445 seg10:0x2469 seg10:0x246f seg10:0x24a2 seg10:0x24a5 seg10:0x2499 seg10:0x2478 seg10:0x2b06 seg10:0x2b60 seg10:0x2b27 seg10:0x2b18 seg10:0x2b39 seg10:0x2d66 seg10:0x2dbe seg10:0x2d7e seg10:0x2d84 seg10:0x2d90 seg10:0x2dca seg10:0x2c1a seg10:0x2bd2 seg10:0x2bde seg10:0x2bea seg10:0x2c76 seg10:0x2c7e seg10:0x2589 seg10:0x256e seg10:0x2525 seg10:0x25f9 seg10:0x2641 seg10:0x270d seg10:0x26c8 seg10:0x2cff seg10:0x2ca2 seg10:0x2cc9 seg10:0x2cdb seg10:0x2d48 seg10:0x2cae seg10:0x2e6c seg10:0x2e7b seg10:0x2eae seg10:0x2e42]

The other words (cell −8, gap −6, +0x08 `max_damage` 1 except 3 for weapon 21, +0x0c thrust, +0x0e clamp, `mass` +0x10 = 20 for all, spin +0x12, +0x1a/+0x1c, fuse +0x20 = 50 for 6 and 40 for 8, blast +0x22, frames +0x26, fuel +0x2e = 150 for 22 only, +0x3a = 1 for every launched weapon, +0x3c = 1 for 7, 8 and 22, +0x3e = 1 for 9, 11, 14, 17 and 22, +0x42 = 1 for 16, +0x46 = 1 for 17 and 21) are in the weapon-record rows of [structs.md](structs.md#weapon-records-28--0x50-bytes-at-dgroup0x2f54). No record holds W+0x24 = 5 as seg10:0x1fb2 fills them; no weapon is heavier than another (the SPUD's "very heavy" text has no word of its own), and the off-screen expiry (W+0x3a = 0) never runs for a launched weapon. Weapon 27, the WILDCARD fruit, has name word −1, kind 1, animation 5, drag 9 and lifetime 50, and no name string.

#### What each launched weapon does
- **6 TARGETTING MISSILES**: pursues the player's selected target (the launch copies the ship's +0x3e/+0x40 for kind 2, without calling seg3:0xf4 for the player in a player game; the retarget at age 13 locks only when that target's `state` is 0, and W+0x3c = 0 allows no later retarget while a target is set) and dodges through [seg4:0x5514](#pool-6-mover-the-weapon-branches). With the player's target pool −1 the retarget's lock test reads record (−1, rec) from the far pointer DGROUP:0x1df4 [OPEN]. [BYTES seg11:0x7efe-0x7f4b ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 115]
- **9 DEATH HEADS**: rear launch, kind-4 retarget onto hot objects (thrust 9, clamp 70, drag 5), spinning (animation 1, W+0x12 = 2: a frame every third pass, ZOOM 0x02 per turn), fires a gun shot at any on-screen ship its face points at, launcher included, and dodges; at its end it explodes with the shock blast. [BYTES seg12:0x17-0x2b ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 118]
- **10 REAR-DEPLOYED MINES**: rear launch, kind 1 (never steers), animation 4 over 6 frames, drag 6, ends unlocked at age 32 → 59 → 64 (37 passes) with seg12:0x274a and the blast, which stuns a live ship through the dispatcher's negative-amount tumble ([ORACLE core-0006]'s mine lived 0.95 s and ended with 0x45 × 7). [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 119]
- **11 DUMB MISSILES**: front launch, kind 1, ends at age 100 (55 passes unlocked, half-life 50 → 95) with seg12:0x274a and the blast of range (min(8, 7) + 8)·15 = 225 cells; there is no near-miss detector, the shock blast is the end explosion ([ORACLE core-0006]). [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 120]
- **12 FIRE DEMONS**: spinning, kind 4 (W+0x14 = 1 also leaves pool 5 out of the retarget), a FIREBALL in the spin direction on 1 of 3 qualifying passes (W+0x16 = 2), launch sound WHISTLE 0x4e. [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 121]
- **14 BATTERIES**: kind 4, thrust 7, clamp 100, charge byte +0x50 += 4 per pass to 0x4b, lightning from age 16 on every fourth pass while the charge > 25. [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 123]
- **16 DOUGHNUTS**: rear launch, `capturable` 1, a ship that touches one gets `tumbling` and the 1200 ms lock-out and 20 `fuel` through seg13:0x16ac, then the dissolve; unconsumed it ends unlocked at 175 → 345 → 350 (180 passes) with seg12:0x274a and the blast; the pair test keeps the launcher from eating its own only while age < 10. [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 125]
- **17 PROTECTIVE PELLETS** and **21 THROWING STARS**: the kind-5 aim above; interception is the ordinary pool-6 collision (a pool-6 record finds another pool-6 record) and a `max_damage` 1 missile dies on one hit; the stars (`max_damage` 3) survive a hit of 3 and end at 80 → 85 with seg12:0x274a (blast 3). [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 126 ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 130]
- **18 BEER BOTTLES** and **20 SPUD MISSILES**: front launch, kind 1, end kind 4 with `on_destroy` seg7:0x2e02 (SHATTER 0x1f, 25 shards from the sheet, blast at frame 5; blast 4 and 5); at their end (58 passes unlocked) the mover's seg7:0x2e02 call. [BYTES seg11:0x80e1-0x8103 ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 127 ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 129]
- **19 BOOMERANGS**: kind 1, end kind 6; at age 106 the mover turns it home with the lock and the steer aims at the launcher every pass (a kind-1 record steers once locked at any age above entry word +0x0e); it runs past its lifetime (run condition W+0x24 = 6); catch and reuse by the kind-6 catch above (sound 0x23); a dead launcher breaks it up with FLANGE 0x1a. [BYTES seg12:0x144-0x164 ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 128]
- **22 ENLIGHTENMENT**: a Noble weapon ([BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL 5000 131]): 150 `fuel` per launch, kind 3, no sheet, blast 20 at its end, and the particles (M6).

### The retarget (seg12:0x586)
seg12:0x586(m, R) (Pascal, `retf 4`: R at [bp+6], m at [bp+8]) looks for a target within R cells; the mover passes R = 8·age. [BYTES seg12:0x5aa seg12:0x7bd]
- It returns at once for weapon 22 in Demon's Gate (DGROUP:0x92e8 ≠ 0). For kind 2 it sets the lock +0x1a = 1 when the current target's `state` is 0 and returns either way. [BYTES seg12:0x5aa seg12:0x5b1 seg12:0x5bc seg12:0x5df seg12:0x5e9]
- **Candidates.** Pools 0 … DGROUP:0x9324 (signed bound) except pool 2, and pool DGROUP:0x9328 when W+0x14 = 1; records below the pool's in-use count, the missile itself excluded; `state` 0, `mass` > 30 (signed), and kind 3 or byte +0x2f ≠ 0. For weapon 8 (kind 3, W+0x14 = 0) that is every record in play with `mass` > 30 outside pool 2, the ships, the rocks, the icons and the ambulance included; pool 6 (`mass` 20) never qualifies. [BYTES seg12:0x751 seg12:0x757 seg12:0x75d seg12:0x764 seg12:0x76c seg12:0x63d seg12:0x646 seg12:0x64c seg12:0x660 seg12:0x667 seg12:0x672 seg12:0x679]
- **Distance.** d = the seg8:0x285a distance in cells between the candidate's and the missile's `x`, `y`; × 4 (16-bit `shl`) for the launcher; × 7 (low word) when launcher and candidate are both ships of the same design team DGROUP:0x970a outside Demon's Gate; then for kinds other than 4 a ship candidate's d becomes (2d)/3 (`cwd; idiv`), and kind 4 adds (int8)0xe7 × byte +0x2f (an 8-bit `imul`) and halves d for pool DGROUP:0x9328. [BYTES seg12:0x692 seg12:0x6a8 seg12:0x6b0 seg12:0x6bf seg12:0x6c8 seg12:0x6ce seg12:0x6d9 seg12:0x6df seg12:0x6ef seg12:0x6ff seg12:0x705 seg12:0x70c seg12:0x70f seg12:0x5fa seg12:0x605 seg12:0x60b seg12:0x719 seg12:0x723 seg12:0x733 seg12:0x737 seg12:0x73d seg12:0x745]
  - So a launching ship's own record counts lo16(2·lo16(28d))/3, every step 16-bit (`shl`, `imul word`, `add ax, ax`, `cwd; idiv`): from 586 cells on 56d passes 32767 and d turns negative, and a negative d beats every positive one, so after age 60 such a missile can lock on its own launcher. [BYTES seg12:0x6ce seg12:0x70c seg12:0x70f seg12:0x712 seg12:0x602 seg12:0x605 seg12:0x60a seg12:0x60b]
- **Choice.** It keeps the candidate with d < R and d < the best so far (from 20000, both signed) and adopts it with the lock +0x1a = 1 unless it is the launcher while age ≤ 60. With no candidate the target and the lock stay as they were. [BYTES seg12:0x5a0 seg12:0x613 seg12:0x61b seg12:0x77a seg12:0x787 seg12:0x790 seg12:0x796 seg12:0x7a1 seg12:0x7aa seg12:0x7b1]
  - core-0002's two missiles that circled switched every 13 frames between a rock and the player's own ship, and the two that hit locked on a rock at age 13 and 27. [ORACLE core-0002]
  - That a dead target keeps the lock, so that the steer keeps aiming at its last point until a retarget finds another, has an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s2-missile.md §4.2). [OPEN]

### The steer (seg12:0x7c0)
seg12:0x7c0(m) (Pascal, `retf 2`) aims and thrusts one pass. [BYTES seg12:0x7c0]
1. **Points.** From the missile's centre (`extent` >> 1) + `x`/`y` to the target's (`size` >> 1) + `x`/`y`, the target being entry +0x00/+0x02 in mode +0x14 = 2 and the alternate target +0x10/+0x12 otherwise. [BYTES seg12:0x7eb seg12:0x7f1 seg12:0x7fe seg12:0x805 seg12:0x81e seg12:0x824 seg12:0x831 seg12:0x843 seg12:0x848 seg12:0x86f]
2. **Jitter.** c = 1 for a mode-2 ship target with `cloaked` (+0xe8) ≠ 0 when W+0x3e = 0, else 0. It calls `rand()` twice on every call, x at seg12:0x8ad and then y at seg12:0x8c6, and adds lo16((r % 300 − 150)·c) << 3 to the target point; c = 1 also clears the lock. [BYTES seg12:0x886 seg12:0x896 seg12:0x8a1 seg12:0x8a8 seg12:0x8ad seg12:0x8b2 seg12:0x8b6 seg12:0x8ba seg12:0x8bd seg12:0x8c0 seg12:0x8c3 seg12:0x8c6 seg12:0x8dc]
3. **Turn.** With W+0x06 = 2 `frame` = seg13:0xd6c(…) with S = −1 and the current `frame`; otherwise it passes S = −100 and 0 and keeps `frame` ([The turn](#the-turn-seg130xd6c)). e is the result. [BYTES seg12:0x8f3 seg12:0x910 seg12:0x916 seg12:0x92b seg12:0x933 seg12:0x93f seg12:0x947 seg12:0x950]
4. **Fuse.** When W+0x20 ≠ 0, c = 0 and W+0x20 > the seg8:0x285a distance, it doubles `blast_size` and calls seg12:0x274a(6, m) and seg7:0x2080(6, m), and does not return: the thrust and the clamp below still run. Weapon 8 fuses within 40 cells with `blast_size` 18. [BYTES seg12:0x963 seg12:0x96a seg12:0x988 seg12:0x990 seg12:0x99e seg12:0x9a8 seg12:0x9b3 seg12:0x9bb seg10:0x22cc]
   - The record then has `state` 1, so the mover skips the draw and the age step, while its collision step still runs on `on_screen` and `collided` ([Who tests whom](#who-tests-whom-seg80x50aa)). [BYTES seg12:0x26f seg12:0x320 seg12:0x42f seg8:0x50d7]
5. **Thrust.** `vx` += lo16(DGROUP:0x1050[4e]·W+0x0c) and `vy` likewise with DGROUP:0x1052, doubled on the alternate-target path, then seg8:0x1cbe(0x600 | m, W+0x0e). Weapon 8: 10·heading per pass, ±120 per axis. [BYTES seg12:0x9c1 seg12:0x9c9 seg12:0x9d6 seg12:0x9dd seg12:0x9e3 seg12:0x9e8 seg12:0x9f7 seg12:0xa02 seg12:0xa0d seg12:0xa12 seg12:0xa16]
- So every steer draws two `rand()`, whatever c is: a locked missile draws two per pass ([rand() sites](#rand-sites)). [BYTES seg12:0x8ad seg12:0x8c6]
- core-0002's missile: an 11 × 19 px sprite nose first at about 310 px/s; the first, fired at a rock 136 px ahead, blew up at age 13. [ORACLE core-0002]

### The turn (seg13:0xd6c)
seg13:0xd6c (Pascal, `retf 0x14`) returns a new heading. Its words, from [bp+6] up: a0, a1, a2, S (a ship, or −1 or −100), mode, cur, tx, ty, mx, my. [BYTES seg13:0xd6c]
1. It shifts my, ty, tx and mx right by 3 (`sar`), into cells. dx = tx − mx, and outside a race wave (DGROUP:0x9320 ≠ 6) a |dx| above DGROUP:0x5a0 >> 1 (seg9:0x88ba) wraps by DGROUP:0x5a0; dy alike with DGROUP:0x5a2. [BYTES seg13:0xda5 seg13:0xdb8 seg13:0xdc5 seg13:0xdcb seg13:0xdcf seg13:0xdec seg13:0xe2f seg13:0xe37]
2. In mode 2 outside a race with a0 = 0, h = seg8:0x19b0(dx, dy) (den = dx, num = dy). [BYTES seg13:0xef4 seg13:0xefa seg13:0xf27 seg13:0xf7f]
3. It returns cur for h outside 0 … 15, and h itself in mode 0x1e, for S = −100 or when h = cur. Otherwise it turns cur one step toward h: down when 0 < cur − h < 9, up when cur − h ≥ 9, up when 0 < h − cur < 9, down when h − cur ≥ 9, wrapping 16 → 0 and −1 → 15. [BYTES seg13:0xf87 seg13:0xf93 seg13:0xf96 seg13:0xf9c seg13:0xfa7 seg13:0xfbf seg13:0xfc2 seg13:0xfc7 seg13:0xfcd seg13:0xfd8 seg13:0xfe0 seg13:0xfe6 seg13:0xfeb seg13:0xff1 seg13:0xff8]
   - A ship S > −1 makes the early test at seg13:0xd7a; a missile (S = −1 or −100) never does. [BYTES seg13:0xd7a]
   - So cur 0 with h = 8 turns up and returns 1: the `cmp ax, 9; jge` comes before the `inc`, and a difference of exactly 8 turns toward h either way. [BYTES seg13:0xfe0 seg13:0xfe6 seg13:0xfe9 seg13:0xfeb seg13:0xfbf]
4. Before a one-step turn it calls seg13:0x6716(S). For S = −1, which is not the player, that writes now − 1 to the dword DGROUP:0x71e6 − 0x15a = DGROUP:0x708c, 4 bytes below ship record 0 ([globals.md](globals.md#global-table)). [BYTES seg13:0xfaf seg13:0xfb3 seg13:0x6720 seg13:0x6744 seg13:0x674a seg13:0x674f seg13:0x6753]
- seg8:0x19b0(den, num) is Pascal with num at [bp+6] and den at [bp+8], 0 counting as 1 ([world.md](world.md#helpers)). [BYTES seg8:0x19bf seg8:0x19c8 seg8:0x19f2]
- Every mode other than 2 goes to seg13:0xf2d before the race test, so in every wave mode 4 gives h = seg8:0x19b0(den = dy − (dx sar 2), num = −(dx + (dy sar 2))), mode 3 h = seg8:0x19b0(den = −(dy + (dx sar 2)), num = dx − (dy sar 2)), and mode 5 or 0x1e h = seg8:0x19b0(−dx, −dy), the modes the [dodge](#pool-6-mover-the-weapon-branches) sets. [BYTES seg13:0xe2f seg13:0xe33 seg13:0xf2d-0xf7f]
- The a0 ≠ 0 path and the race-wave branch have an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s2-missile.md §4.4). [OPEN]

### The clamp (seg8:0x1cbe)
- seg8:0x1cbe(pool·256 + rec, L) (Pascal, `retf 4`: L at [bp+6]) clamps `vx` and `vy` separately: at most L, then at least −L, signed 16-bit. It is a per-axis box, not a speed magnitude. [BYTES seg8:0x1ccc seg8:0x1cd4 seg8:0x1ceb seg8:0x1cf1 seg8:0x1d06 seg8:0x1d0c seg8:0x1d23 seg8:0x1d29 seg8:0x1d40 seg8:0x1d46 seg8:0x1d51]

### Movers and pools
- seg12:0x532c moves pool 9, seg8:0x8256 pool 2, seg7:0x309c pool 0, seg7:0x32d0 pool 1, seg12:0x3148 pool 10, seg12:0x6b1e pool 11, seg12:0xae4 pool 4 and seg12:0x3e98 pool 8, by the pool pointers DGROUP:0x1e1c, 0x1e00, 0x1df8, 0x1dfc, 0x1e20, 0x1e24, 0x1e08 and 0x1e18; seg12:0x3e98 and seg8:0x8256 also read pool 14. [BYTES seg12:0x532c seg8:0x8256 seg7:0x309c seg7:0x32d0 seg12:0x3148 seg12:0x6b1e seg12:0xae4 seg12:0x3e98]

### Selection and the weapon display
- **Tab.** WM_KEYDOWN VK_TAB runs seg11:0xadf0 and then seg11:0x2f8c, with no `PlayMode` test; only the general gate of seg11:0x6b64 ([world.md](world.md#key-messages-seg110x6b64)) and the demo filter, which lets VK_TAB through only in a player game, stand before it. [BYTES seg11:0x6c6c seg11:0x6c9b seg11:0x6cd1 seg11:0x6e19 seg11:0x6e50 seg11:0x6e52 seg11:0x71e4 seg11:0x71e7]
- **seg11:0xadf0** saves the old slot (ship word +0x18) in DGROUP:0x9524. With the signed slot count byte +0xcb ≤ 1 it selects slot 0. Otherwise it takes slot + 1, skips one slot whose weapon byte is 0, and when that is not below +0xcb (signed) wraps to 0 in Strategy mode (`PlayMode` ≠ 0) and to 1 in Action mode; the new slot goes to ship word +0x18 and DGROUP:0x9524. When the weapon byte of the old slot is 3 (LIGHTNING) it clears the player's object byte +0x50. [BYTES seg11:0xae04 seg11:0xae0b seg11:0xae0e seg11:0xae13 seg11:0xae1b seg11:0xae23 seg11:0xae2c seg11:0xae31 seg11:0xae35 seg11:0xae37 seg11:0xae3e seg11:0xae46 seg11:0xae61 seg11:0xae6d seg11:0xae70 seg11:0xae87]
  - seg11:0x6d6c, the WM_KEYUP VK_F12 case ([world.md](world.md#key-messages-seg110x6b64)), does not write the weapon slot. [BYTES seg11:0x6d25 seg11:0x6d28 seg11:0x6d6c seg11:0x6dae seg11:0x6dc1]
  - In core-0002, with two slots (+0xcb = 2) and weapon 8 in slot 1, two Tab presses leave the display unchanged: slot 2 is not below +0xcb, so Action mode wraps to slot 1. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Wave start** (seg11:0xae94): unless DGROUP:0x92e4 ≠ 0, slot 1 when byte +0xcb ≥ 2, else 0. Its callers are the wave set-up seg9:0x695e and the player-ship switch seg11:0xb088, each followed by seg11:0x2f8c. [BYTES seg11:0xae94 seg11:0xae9e seg11:0xaec0 seg9:0x695e seg9:0x6963 seg11:0xb088 seg11:0xb08b]
- **seg11:0x2f8c** deletes DGROUP:0x5040, rebuilds it with seg11:0x2ff6(w) when the slot ≠ 0, and posts WM_PAINT to DGROUP:0xa7e when that window exists. [BYTES seg11:0x2f8c]
- **Picture seg11:0x2ff6(w).** It returns 0 for w = 0, and seg10:0x6c02(W(w)+0x02, 0, −1), the plain graphic, when the kind W(w)+0x0a is 0 or W(w)−2 = −1 and w ≠ 24. [BYTES seg11:0x3003 seg11:0x300b seg11:0x3012 seg11:0x3019 seg11:0x3020 seg11:0x302e seg11:0x3032]
  1. **Cell.** A = W(w)−8 and B = W(w)−6 when W(w)+0x0a ≠ 0; for w = 24 with W(w)+0x0a = 0 the sign-extended roster bytes +0x7a/+0x7b of the record DGROUP:0x6e24 + 0x7c·DGROUP:0x931c. [BYTES seg11:0x3044 seg11:0x3049 seg11:0x304b seg11:0x3052 seg11:0x3058 seg11:0x305e seg11:0x306b seg11:0x3073]
  2. **Overlay.** By W(w)+0x14: 1 or 4 load graphic 0xbf, 2 0xc4, 3 0xc5, and 0 loads 0xbd for w = 24 and nothing otherwise (seg10:0x6c02(id, 0, −1)); a value outside 0 … 4 leaves the handle uninitialised. [BYTES seg11:0x307f seg11:0x308a seg11:0x3094 seg11:0x3096 seg11:0x30aa seg11:0x30b3 seg11:0x30bc seg11:0x30c6 seg11:0x30cb]
  3. **Size.** With an overlay of bmWidth gw and bmHeight gh, S = A for w = 24 and 2A otherwise, the bitmap is (gw + S) × max(gh, S), the stretched cell goes to y = (H − S)/2 when H > S and the overlay to (S, (S − gh)/2) when H = S, else to (S, 0). Without one the bitmap is S × S with S = (W(w)−8 << 2)/3 (signed `idiv`) for names 0xc4, 0xc6 and 0xc3 and 2·(W(w)−8) otherwise, every offset 0. S takes A before step 5. [BYTES seg11:0x30e2 seg11:0x30f3 seg11:0x3106 seg11:0x311e seg11:0x312f seg11:0x313f seg11:0x317d seg11:0x3193 seg11:0x319b seg11:0x31a2 seg11:0x31aa seg11:0x31b0 seg11:0x31bc seg11:0x31d2]
  4. It PatBlts the whole bitmap BLACKNESS. [BYTES seg11:0x320a seg11:0x3215 seg11:0x3219]
  5. **Source.** It drops B: for an odd A it sets A = A + 1 and B = 4, else B = 6; then B = (B << `Size`) >> 1 and A = (A << `Size`) >> 1. The source is (B, B) when the animation W(w)+0x06 = 5 or the name is 0xc6 or 0xc3; ((W(w)+0x26 − 1)·(A + B) + B, B) (a 16-bit `imul`) when W(w)+0x06 = 4; else (4A + 5B, B), frame 4 of the sheet. [BYTES seg11:0x3229 seg11:0x322f seg11:0x3232 seg11:0x3239 seg11:0x323e seg11:0x3245 seg11:0x3247 seg11:0x324f seg11:0x3251 seg11:0x325a seg11:0x3261 seg11:0x3268 seg11:0x326f seg11:0x327e seg11:0x3285 seg11:0x3290 seg11:0x3297 seg11:0x329a]
  6. SetStretchBltMode(COLORONCOLOR) and StretchBlt(dc, 0, yS, S, S, src, sx, sy, A, A, SRCCOPY) from DC DGROUP:0x3a38[DGROUP:0x9646] for w = 24 and DGROUP:0x39e8[W(w)−4] otherwise; then the overlay is BitBlt SRCCOPY. [BYTES seg11:0x32ac seg11:0x32b3 seg11:0x32c7 seg11:0x32cd seg11:0x32e5 seg11:0x32eb seg11:0x32fb seg11:0x3300 seg11:0x3326]
  - Only the source rectangle scales with `Size`. Weapon 8 (A = 23, no overlay) gives a 46 × 46 bitmap stretched from (116, 4), 24 × 24, at `Size` 1. [BYTES seg11:0x2ff6 seg10:0x22d8 seg10:0x22f0] Its non-black pixels lie at x 828 … 871, y 728 … 751 on a 1024 × 768 screen, a missile pointing right. [ORACLE wave-0001] [ORACLE core-0002]
  - Weapon 10's words, as seg10:0x1fb2 fills them, are W(10)+0x00 = 196 (0xc4), +0x06 = 4, +0x14 = 0, +0x26 = 6 and W(10)−8 (block 9's +0x48, DGROUP:0x326c) = 22. So seg11:0x2ff6(10) makes no overlay, S = (22 << 2)/3 = 29, B = 6 for the even A, and the source x = (6 − 1)·(22 + 6) + 6 = 146 at `Size` 1: a 29 × 29 bitmap stretched from (146, 6), 22 × 22. - **Image slot** seg10:0x533a(w) gives weapon w a slot 0x30 … 0x38 in W(w)−4. It does nothing for w = 22 or 27. It reuses a slot k whose DC DGROUP:0x39e8[k] ≠ 0 when some weapon j ≤ 27 with W(j)+0x0a ≠ 0 has W(j)−4 = k and the same W(j)−2, writing only W(w)−4. Otherwise it takes the first k with DC 0 (none: it returns), stores W(w)−4 = k, writes W(w)−8 and W(w)−6 into `size` and `gap` of pool-6 record 0, and renders the sheet: seg10:0x4412(k, 6, W(w)−2, W(w)+0x26) for animation 4, else seg10:0x6ee6(k, W(w)−2, far "" (DGROUP:0x76d), 2, 0x63, W(w)+0x1a, W(w)+0x1c, 1, 0, 0xc, 0xc, 0, sel), with sel = 1 when DGROUP:0x931e = 2, or when DGROUP:0x90f4 = 1, frame# ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x931e = 1 [BYTES seg10:0x5348 seg10:0x534e seg10:0x5354 seg10:0x5357 seg10:0x5361 seg10:0x5365 seg10:0x536b seg10:0x5375 seg10:0x5383 seg10:0x5389 seg10:0x5393 seg10:0x539e seg10:0x5414 seg10:0x5418 seg10:0x541f seg10:0x542d seg10:0x5431 seg10:0x5439 seg10:0x543d seg10:0x5445 seg10:0x5449 seg10:0x5452 seg10:0x545e seg10:0x53b3-0x540c].
  - **seg10:0x4412(k, pool, id, n), the animation-4 sheet** (cdecl, reached only from seg10:0x545e), loads graphic id with seg10:0x6c02(id, 3, −1), reads its bmHeight h with GetObject into a 14-byte BITMAP and stores the gap, 5 for an odd h and 6 for an even h, in word +0x24 of the pool's record 0. It creates the sheet as CreateCompatibleBitmap(DGROUP:0x13dc, lo16(h·n) + lo16(gap·(n + 1)), 2·gap + h) in a new DC, with the DC, the bitmap and the old bitmap in DGROUP:0x39e8[k], DGROUP:0x3ad0[k] and DGROUP:0x3974[k], and blacks it with PatBlt 0x00000042. For n = 1 it BitBlts the h × h graphic to (gap, gap) SRCCOPY; otherwise it sets stretch mode 3 and, for i = 0 … n − 1, calls seg10:0x9962(0, sheet DC, x, gap, s, s, graphic DC, 0, 0, h, h, SRCCOPY) with s = h + 2(i − n) and x from gap in steps of gap + h, so the frames grow toward the last. It ends with the rescale seg10:0x6308(k, −1, −1, −1).
