  - seg10:0x1fb2 gives animation 4 to weapons 10 (W+0x26 = 6 frames), 16 (8) and 17 (1): REAR-DEPLOYED MINES, DOUGHNUTS and PROTECTIVE PELLETS, all listed by the registered shop. With DGROUP:0x920e = 0 the palette restore at seg10:0x4602 tests [bp−0xa], which only the palette branch at seg10:0x44fb writes ([ADR 0016](../decisions/0016-undefined-reads.md)). [BYTES seg10:0x27f8 seg10:0x27ad seg10:0x2ddd seg10:0x2d84 seg10:0x2bcc seg10:0x2c76 seg10:0x44fb seg10:0x4602] [ORACLE core-0006]
  - The ship-sheet builder seg14:0x11a8 ends by setting ship bytes +0xad and +0xac to 1 and calling seg10:0x533a for the weapon of every slot k < byte +0xcb, slot 0 included, whose W+0x0a ≠ 0. [BYTES seg14:0x13eb seg14:0x13ef seg14:0x13f3 seg14:0x13ff seg14:0x1404 seg14:0x1407 seg14:0x140f seg14:0x141d seg14:0x1425]
  - The other writers of the table are the W−4 stores seg10:0x5389, seg10:0x542d and seg10:0x5560; every other word stays as seg10:0x1fb2 filled it. [BYTES seg10:0x5389 seg10:0x542d seg10:0x5560]
- **The display's repaint.** The IS_INFO window procedure seg11:0x22fa answers WM_PAINT with InvalidateRect(hwnd, NULL, TRUE), BeginPaint, seg11:0x238e(hwnd, hdc) and EndPaint, and seg11:0x238e first PatBlts the whole client rectangle BLACKNESS. So a launch's WM_PAINT redraws the title, the count, the rule and the picture ([world.md](world.md#weapon-display-id-0xdc)). [BYTES seg11:0x230a seg11:0x232f seg11:0x233c seg11:0x2349 seg11:0x2357 seg11:0x23e8 seg11:0x23f4 seg11:0x23fe seg11:0x2400 seg11:0x2405]

## Fuel

### The words
- `fuel` is ship word +0x1e; its cap, the tank, is design word DGROUP:0x96f0. [BYTES seg11:0x1d03 seg14:0x1915]
- **Wave start without a roster record:** `fuel` = 40 × SHP byte +0 (8-bit imul), `resources` = 100 and `credit` = 0. [BYTES seg14:0x1b25 seg14:0x1b2f seg14:0x1b48 seg14:0x1b51]
- **With a roster record** (seg14:0x1430, every placement): `fuel` = 40 × roster byte +0x1c by an unsigned 8-bit `mul`, raised to (2·tank)/3 for a ship other than DGROUP:0x9646, then capped at the tank; only a capped value is written back to roster +0x1c, as tank/40. credit = 10 × roster word +0x02 (signed 16 × 16 → 32) and resources = roster word +0x04 ([Ship roster records](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)). [BYTES seg14:0x18c9 seg14:0x18d3 seg14:0x18dc seg14:0x18e0-0x1903 seg14:0x1915-0x1931 seg14:0x1997 seg14:0x199e seg14:0x19a6 seg14:0x19aa]
  - So `fuel` crosses a wave end as `fuel`/40 and comes back as a multiple of 40: core-0003's 1209 at the exit is 1200 after Navigation Enter, and a restored 1188 is 1160. [ORACLE core-0003]
- A new game's player roster record 0 gets +0x02 = 15 and +0x04 = 0: the player starts with 1.50 icons of credit and no resources. [BYTES seg14:0x3cc seg14:0x3d8] [ORACLE wave-0001]
- The set-up's refuel seg9:0x6db0 runs only in a race wave ([world.md](world.md#placement-seg90x6624), step 8).

### Consumption (seg11:0x1bcc, every 1000 ms)
The block runs for ships i = 0 … DGROUP:0x9316 once the deadline DGROUP:0x5074 has passed ([world.md](world.md#mass-thrust-and-drag)). Per ship, in this order: [BYTES seg11:0x1c3e seg11:0x1c54 seg11:0x1d33]
1. While `thrusting`: `fuel` = max(0, `fuel` + `thrust`/−40 − 5·`cloaked` (+0xe8)); otherwise `fuel` = max(0, `fuel` − 5·`cloaked`). Both paths floor at 0 before the store. [BYTES seg11:0x1d3a seg11:0x1d43 seg11:0x1d55 seg11:0x1d5d seg11:0x1c5c seg11:0x1c6d seg11:0x1c6f seg11:0x1c71 seg11:0x1c73]
2. With `cloaked` ≠ 0 it subtracts 3 more without a floor. When the result is below 120 (signed) it calls the cloak toggle seg11:0x4c72(i), discarding its result, and then seg3:0x332e(1033, 1048), the [command-line notice](#the-command-line-notice-seg30x332e), for every such ship, computer ships included. [BYTES seg11:0x1c83 seg11:0x1c88 seg11:0x1c8d seg11:0x1c92 seg11:0x1c97 seg11:0x1c98 seg11:0x1c9e]
   - A toggle that refuses ([The cloak](#the-cloak-seg110x4c72)) leaves the cloak on, so the word goes on losing 3 + 5 per tick below 120 and can stand at −3 after a tick ([ADR 0014](../decisions/0014-saved-games.md) decision 4). [BYTES seg11:0x4c8e seg11:0x4cd2 seg11:0x1c83]
3. **The LIGHTNING charge.** While the ship's selected weapon (byte +0xb8 + word +0x18) is 3, its object byte +0x50 (signed c) gets c += (109 − c)/2 for the player and c += (109 − c)/5 for another ship ([Lightning](#lightning-seg110x9fdc-seg110xa234-seg110xa9ea)). [BYTES seg11:0x1ca3-0x1cfa seg11:0x1cac seg11:0x1ccf seg11:0x1cef]
4. Then the `mass` update from the new `fuel` ([world.md](world.md#mass-thrust-and-drag)). [BYTES seg11:0x1cfe seg11:0x1d26]
- The TRANSFORMER (`thrust` 205) burns 5 per second thrusting and 13 cloaked and thrusting. [BYTES seg11:0x1d55 seg11:0x1c83] [ORACLE probe-0001] core-0003 reads 1320 → 1215 in 21 steps of 5, 1.003–1.004 s apart, over 20 s of thrust, and no burn at rest. [ORACLE core-0003]
- No instruction of the ship damage handler seg8:0x45e0 touches `fuel`; the shield uses no fuel. [BYTES seg8:0x45e0] [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]
- **Message 9**, the low-fuel message, is case 8 of the dispatcher's switch (seg3:0xcee, the switch runs on code − 1): seg10:0x744c(0x2c), seg10:0xa332(DGROUP:0x8db0, 121, 2) through the shared tail seg3:0x8d9, then seg3:0x14d6. String 121 is "FUEL LEVEL LOW. SEEK FUEL ASAP". The fuel gauge raises it ([world.md](world.md#fuel-gauge)). [BYTES seg3:0x1346 seg3:0xcee seg3:0xcf0 seg3:0xcf6 seg3:0xcf8 seg3:0xcfa seg3:0x8d9 seg3:0x8dd seg3:0x8e5] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 8] id 121

### The command-line notice (seg3:0x332e)
- seg3:0x332e(a, b) is Pascal (`retf 4`). It writes DGROUP:0x8630 = the pilot name DGROUP:0x380c + ": " (DGROUP:0x13d6) + LoadString(a) + LoadString(b), each LoadString with hInstance DGROUP:0x9212 into a 40-byte buffer, then sets DGROUP:0x58c = 1 and dword DGROUP:0x5c0 = now + 1200. [BYTES seg3:0x3343 seg3:0x3350 seg3:0x3359 seg3:0x3363 seg3:0x3371 seg3:0x337a seg3:0x3384 seg3:0x3392 seg3:0x3397 seg3:0x33a7 seg3:0x33b1]
- The fuel tick's push of the dword 0x04090418 makes a = 1033 and b = 1048: "<pilot>: Uncloaking Because I'm Low On Gas.". The ship mover's shield drop ([The damage drop](#the-damage-drop)) passes 1033 and 1047, "Uncloaking Due To System Damage.". [BYTES seg11:0x1c98 seg13:0x4a3] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 65] id 1033 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 66] ids 1047–1048
- DGROUP:0x8630 is the Strategy-mode command line ([Messages](#messages-seg30x6ec), The command line), drawn only while `PlayMode` = 1. Every reference to dword DGROUP:0x5c0 by its address is a write; the frame's gate compares DGROUP:0x5bc, which no instruction writes and which is 0 in the file, so the line is drawn on the next frame and the 1200 ms never matter. [BYTES seg3:0x33a7 seg3:0x340e seg3:0x3455 seg4:0x69af seg7:0x187e seg11:0x65a2 seg13:0x19bc seg13:0x69dc seg7:0x7f3] [BYTES DGROUP:0x5bc]

### The cloak (seg11:0x4c72)
seg11:0x4c72(i) is cdecl and returns AX. [BYTES seg11:0x4c72]
1. **Gates.** It returns 0 without toggling when byte +0xe7 (`has_cloak`) = 0; when the ship is not the player with `PlayMode` 0 and frame# − dword +0x152 < 150 (32-bit, unsigned); when +0xe8 = 0 and `fuel` < 120 (signed); or when byte +0xeb ≠ 0. [BYTES seg11:0x4c82 seg11:0x4c8e seg11:0x4c96 seg11:0x4ca9 seg11:0x4cad seg11:0x4cb5 seg11:0x4cc6 seg11:0x4cd2]
2. **Off** (+0xe8 ≠ 0): seg12:0x2556(DGROUP:0x9324, i), ship word +0x2e = 2, seg7:0x13b2(2, DGROUP:0x9324, i) ([The vanish](#the-vanish-seg70x13b2-and-seg70x158e)) and +0xe8 = 0. [BYTES seg11:0x4ce9 seg11:0x4cf1 seg11:0x4cfe seg11:0x4d00 seg11:0x4d08]
3. **On** (+0xe8 = 0) needs word +0x2c ≠ 0, and > 40 (signed) unless the ship is the player with `PlayMode` 0. It stores the object's dword +0x04 (x, y) in ship dword +0x14a and now in +0x14e, calls seg12:0x2556(DGROUP:0x9324, i), sets ship word +0x2e = 2, calls seg7:0x13b2(1, DGROUP:0x9324, i) and sets +0xe8 = 1. [BYTES seg11:0x4d21 seg11:0x4d26 seg11:0x4d3e seg11:0x4d43 seg11:0x4d5a seg11:0x4d67 seg11:0x4d73 seg11:0x4d96 seg11:0x4d98 seg11:0x4da0]
4. Both directions then store frame# in ship dword +0x152 and return 1. [BYTES seg11:0x4d0d seg11:0x4d11 seg11:0x4d16]
- The cloak is toggled with CAPSLOCK and uses fuel. [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]

### The shield (seg11:0x4b00)
- **CapsLock.** WM_KEYUP with wParam 0x14 needs DGROUP:0x95bc ≠ 0 (the WM_KEYUP branch), does nothing while `PlayMode` ≠ 0, calls seg11:0x4b00(player) when the player's `has_shield` (+0xe9) ≠ 0, and otherwise seg11:0x4c72(player) when `has_cloak` (+0xe7) ≠ 0. [BYTES seg11:0x6d19 seg11:0x6d36 seg11:0x7290 seg11:0x6dd6 seg11:0x6ddb seg11:0x6de5 seg11:0x6df1 seg11:0x6dfc seg11:0x6e01 seg11:0x6e0a]
- **Gates.** seg11:0x4b00(i) (cdecl, returns AX) returns 0 when word +0x2c < 50 (signed) with byte +0xea = 0, when ship word +0x10 is 1 … 6, or when the ship is not the player with `PlayMode` 0 and frame# − dword +0x152 < 150 (32-bit, unsigned). [BYTES seg11:0x4b10 seg11:0x4b17 seg11:0x4b28 seg11:0x4b2f seg11:0x4b36 seg11:0x4b45 seg11:0x4b51 seg11:0x4b55 seg11:0x4b5d seg11:0x4b60]
- **Toggle.** From +0xea ≠ 0 it writes ship word +0x10 = 6, DGROUP:0x9522 = −1, ship word +0x20 = the object's `size` − 2, ship word +0x22 = 1 and +0xea = 0; from 0 it writes +0x10 = 1, DGROUP:0x9522 = 1, +0x20 = `size` − 12, +0x22 = 7 and +0xea = 1. [BYTES seg11:0x4b6e seg11:0x4b74 seg11:0x4b97 seg11:0x4b9b seg11:0x4ba1 seg11:0x4bb0 seg11:0x4bb4 seg11:0x4bd5 seg11:0x4bd9 seg11:0x4bdf]
- **Then**, on both paths, it calls seg10:0x7328(design +0x36, far DS:design +0x84, dword design +0xa9, byte design +0x18, byte design +0x1a, object `frame`, DC DGROUP:0x3a38[i], 1, 0x63): the graphic id, the file name, the scheme words and bytes of the sheet render seg14:0x11a8, the heading and the ship's own sheet. seg10:0x7328 hands them to ISPACE.DLL [ordinal 17](ispace.md#ordinal-notes), which renders body frames 1 and 2 at that heading into the sheet's column 16, rows 0 and 1. [BYTES seg11:0x4ba6 seg11:0x4be4-0x4c37 seg10:0x7335-0x7365] It then stores frame# in +0x152, calls seg11:0xb09c(i, 1) when `thrusting` (+0x02) ≠ 0, plays seg10:0x754a(5, DGROUP:0x9324, i, 1), sound 5 SHIELD, and returns 1. [BYTES seg11:0x4be4 seg11:0x4bed seg11:0x4c32 seg11:0x4c37 seg11:0x4c3e seg11:0x4c43 seg11:0x4c50 seg11:0x4c5c seg11:0x4c5e seg11:0x4c66]
- **So byte +0xea is the shield-on flag.** CapsLock with `has_shield` toggles it with the SHIELD sound, and its effects ([structs.md](structs.md#ship-records-8--0x15a-bytes-at-dgroup0x7090) +0xea) are the manual's shield, which stops captures and weapon systems and uses no fuel. [BYTES seg11:0x6df1 seg11:0x4ba1 seg11:0x4bdf seg11:0x4c5e] [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]
- **The step.** Ship word +0x10 is the shield's animation step: the ship mover adds 1 to it while the sign-extended +0xea ≠ 0 and subtracts 1 otherwise, only for a ship with `has_shield`, on screen, whose +0x10 read at the top of the iteration is 1 … 6, and then calls seg11:0xb09c(i, `thrusting`). So the raise runs 1 → 7 and the lowering 6 → 0. The mover reaches the step for a ship with `has_flame` (+0xe4) ≠ 0, and for a body-thrust ship (+0xe4 = 0) only when its thrust animation (seg13:0x2a3–0x33e, ship bytes +0x5a … +0x5c) neither runs (`thrusting`, object byte +0x2e = 0, and on screen or seg13:0x59e8(i, 14) ≠ 0) nor resets a non-zero frame byte +0x5c on screen. [BYTES seg13:0x25a-0x29f seg13:0x2a3-0x2b1 seg13:0x33e seg13:0x341-0x37d] [BYTES seg13:0xd28 seg13:0xd34 seg13:0x34b seg13:0x352 seg13:0x36a seg13:0x384 seg13:0x3a0 seg13:0x3a7 seg13:0x3ad seg13:0x3b3 seg13:0x3bb seg13:0x3c6 seg13:0x3d2 seg13:0x3d6]
- **The sprite's cell.** seg7:0x1208 takes the source x 16·`size` + 17·`gap` (column 16) while ship word +0x10 is neither 0 nor 7, and the source y 2·`gap` + `size` (row 1) while it is above 3 ([world.md](world.md#the-players-sprite-seg70x1208)); the ship mover's own draw applies the same rule to the step it latched before stepping ([bp−0x22], from seg13:0xd2c). So a raise shows column 16 row 0, body frame 1, at steps 1 … 3, row 1, frame 2, at 4 … 6 and the heading's cell of row 1 at 7; a lowering runs back to the heading's cell of row 0 at 0. [BYTES seg7:0x12ee-0x1384 seg13:0xaa1-0xb37 seg13:0xd28-0xd2c] core-0005 shows the TRANSFORMER's raise with one step per game frame and four pictures: the normal sprite at 0, frame 1 for steps 1–3, frame 2 for 4–6 and the ball for 7. It also sees damage take the shield down at system 3 = 44 with +0x10 and +0xea going to 0 in one poll and no 0x05, which the damage drop's seg11:0x4b00 would not do. The lead is the damage rule of [Damage to a ship](#damage-to-a-ship-seg80x45e0) step 11, which for a wing ship (design word DGROUP:0x96fa = 3) below 50 with both wings on it tears the wing on the hit side and clears +0x10 and +0xea; core-0005's watch of the record bytes shows wing byte +0xad going from 1 to 0 14 ms after the shield's drop, the only change in that 50 ms poll, with system 3 at 44, so the hit that took the shield down tore a wing, as step 11 does; the two watches (5 ms and 50 ms polls) do not order the two stores within the hit. [ORACLE core-0005]

### The damage drop
- The ship mover, per ship, runs this block only behind a frame gate: with c = DGROUP:0x134e and d = `Detail` DGROUP:0x9208, when c % (10 − max(5, 2d)) = 0 and the ship's object is on screen (+0x1a ≠ 0), or c % (18 − 4d) = 0, or DGROUP:0x9320 = 6; otherwise it jumps to seg13:0x4dd. [BYTES seg13:0x3e3-0x430]
- A cloaked ship (+0xe8 ≠ 0) with word +0x2c < 50 calls seg11:0x4c72(i) when `rand()` % (2·+0x2c + 1) = 0. Otherwise a ship with +0xea ≠ 0 and +0x2c < 50 makes the same roll and calls seg11:0x4b00(i). A non-zero return of either call, for the player, leads to seg3:0x332e(1033, 1047). [BYTES seg13:0x439 seg13:0x440 seg13:0x449 seg13:0x452 seg13:0x456 seg13:0x45f seg13:0x464 seg13:0x466 seg13:0x46b seg13:0x472 seg13:0x47b seg13:0x491 seg13:0x496 seg13:0x497 seg13:0x49e seg13:0x4a3 seg13:0x4a9]
- The manual: a damaged ship may drop out of cloak by itself. [MANUAL Special Ship Systems]

## The small movers (pools 1, 4 and 11)
The three movers run in step 21 of the frame ([world.md](world.md#update-loop)); their record templates are in [world.md](world.md#record-templates) and their fields in [structs.md](structs.md#pool-4-tanks). In this section u(n) = (n << `Size`) >> 1. [DESIGN]

### The fuel tank (pool 4, seg12:0xae4)
- **Run.** The frame calls seg12:0xae4 while DGROUP:0x9600 ≠ 0. Its only writer, seg9:0x6833, stores 1 and nothing clears it, so from the first wave placement on the mover runs on every frame of the session. [BYTES seg9:0x6833 seg7:0xd20]
- **Head.** It sets word +0x1c (`collided`) = 0 for every record below DGROUP:0x940e before its main loop, so a tank another mover has already collided with this frame can collide again. [BYTES seg12:0xb05 seg12:0xb14 seg12:0xb1d]
- **Taken tank.** A record with `state` ≠ 0 only gets seg12:0xe5c(i) ([The pickup dissolve](#the-pickup-dissolve-seg120xe5c)), and only when byte +0x51 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg12:0xc6b seg12:0xc70 seg12:0xc40 seg12:0xc4b]
- **Off screen** it moves only when DGROUP:0x9142 % 3 = 0, by seg8:0x1d54(h, 0x28) and seg8:0x1b54(h, 3) (h = 0x400 | i), and does not collide. [BYTES seg12:0xc9a seg12:0xb2d seg12:0xb38 seg12:0xb46 seg12:0xb4e seg12:0xb53 seg12:0xb5f]
- **On screen** it moves by seg8:0x1d54(h, 0x28) and seg8:0x1b54(h, 1), takes `src_x` = (+0x26 / 5)·(`size` + `gap`) + `gap` (signed idiv) with the `size` and `gap` of pool-4 record 0, and is drawn by seg8:0xb76(h, 1, DC DGROUP:0x39f0, 0). [BYTES seg12:0xaf7 seg12:0xafe seg12:0xcc2 seg12:0xcd5 seg12:0xcd8 seg12:0xcdd seg12:0xce4 seg12:0xcec]
- **Wobble.** With DGROUP:0x9214 = 0 (no special time of day) it adds the signed byte +0x2f to +0x26; when +0x2f ≠ 0 after the add it clamps +0x26 to 5 (setting +0x2f = +0x50) and to 21 (setting +0x2f = −+0x50). With DGROUP:0x9214 ≠ 0 it goes straight to the collision block. [BYTES seg12:0xcf1 seg12:0xd0c seg12:0xd16 seg12:0xd23 seg12:0xd29 seg12:0xd37 seg12:0xd43 seg12:0xd4a seg12:0xd5c seg12:0xd5e]
- **Volatile.** Only on that path (DGROUP:0x9214 = 0, +0x2f ≠ 0), when the signed +0x50 > `rand()` % 120 and then +0x50 > `rand()` % 20, the tank explodes by seg12:0x274a(4, i) and seg7:0x2080(4, i). The mover then still runs the collision block for it in that frame. [BYTES seg12:0xd62 seg12:0xd6b seg12:0xd7a seg12:0xd81 seg12:0xd8a seg12:0xd95 seg12:0xda2 seg12:0xdad seg12:0xdb2]
- **Collision.** With `on_screen` ≠ 0 and `collided` = 0 it registers pool 4 in DGROUP:0x943a and calls seg8:0x50aa(h). A hit on a ship whose bytes +0xea and +0xe8 are 0 calls the touch seg13:0x16ac(ship, (DGROUP:0x943a << 8) | cwde(byte DGROUP:0x943c)) and is not dispatched ([Other touches](#other-touches)). Any other hit is dispatched with the tank as target, seg8:0x5df2(4, i, hit pool, hit rec, a), a = 125 when the hit is pool 0 with word DGROUP:0x57aa + 0x23·rec ≠ 0 or the hit pool is DGROUP:0x9328, else a = seg8:0x546c(hit, &vx, &vy, `extent` + 2, `mass`). So a shielded or cloaked ship bounces off a tank. [BYTES seg12:0xb91 seg12:0xb9e seg12:0xba4 seg12:0xbba seg12:0xbc5 seg12:0xbd3 seg12:0xbdf seg12:0xbe5 seg12:0xc19 seg12:0xc2c seg12:0xc2e]
- **The tank's `on_damage`** seg12:0xdc4(pool, rec, amount) (cdecl) adds amount to `damage`, sets byte +0x2f = 1 when it is 0, sets byte +0x50 = the low byte of max(1, `damage` / 10) (signed idiv), and when that byte is > 5 (signed) calls `on_destroy` and seg7:0x2080(pool, rec) and returns 1, else 0. So 60 damage destroys a tank at once, and less sets it wobbling at speed +0x50, which arms the volatile test. [BYTES seg12:0xde1 seg12:0xded seg12:0xdf4 seg12:0xe15 seg12:0xe17 seg12:0xe1f seg12:0xe29 seg12:0xe36 seg12:0xe43 seg12:0xe4b seg12:0xe50]
- **Respawns.** The race respawn seg12:0x4f4e ([Other touches](#other-touches)) has one call, seg12:0x3ee5 in seg12:0x3e98, the last mover of step 21, which returns at once unless the wave kind DGROUP:0x9320 is 6. [BYTES seg12:0x3ea8 seg12:0x3eaf seg12:0x3ee5]
  - In a kind-7 (duel) wave the wave frame calls seg12:0x515e instead (seg7:0xe78). While some ship whose object word +0x18 is 0 has under 40 % of its tank (100 · ship +0x1e / design +0x1e < 40) and fewer than two of pool 4's first DGROUP:0x92f4 records are in play, it puts the first idle tank whose byte +0x51 is 0 at ((`rand()` % DGROUP:0x5a0)·8, (`rand()` % DGROUP:0x5a2)·8), and when no ship is within 60 of that spot it brings the tank back (+0x18 = 0, seg7:0x2080(4, −1)). [BYTES seg7:0xe71 seg7:0xe78 seg12:0x5192 seg12:0x51c2 seg12:0x51d8 seg12:0x51f1 seg12:0x5241 seg12:0x5256 seg12:0x5281 seg12:0x52c8 seg12:0x5307 seg12:0x5312]
  - The only other writer of `state` = 0 through the pool-4 pointer DGROUP:0x1e08 is the wave placement seg9:0xdf6. [BYTES seg9:0xf55]
- core-0003 shows the tank as a red jerrycan of about 34 × 32 px; a pickup plays 0x24 then 0x25 0.68 s later and sets `fuel` to the tank (1221, 1297 and 1184 → 1320), with no message. [ORACLE core-0003]

### The pickup dissolve (seg12:0xe5c)
seg12:0xe5c(i) (cdecl) runs once from the touch and then once per frame from the mover. It reads `size` and `gap` from record i itself. sx, sy are the record's (`scr_x`, `scr_y`), p is the phase and every PatBlt is BLACKNESS. [BYTES seg12:0xe5c seg12:0xe6b seg12:0xe77 seg12:0xe7e]
1. It evaluates its frame-clear flag (DGROUP:0x8ffe ≠ 0 and the signed +0x51 > 8) before it subtracts the step DGROUP:0x8ffe from byte +0x51, floored at 0; p = 9 − +0x51. [BYTES seg12:0xe85 seg12:0xe8c seg12:0xe93 seg12:0xeaa]
2. SetStretchBltMode(3) comes first. On an odd p, when the record's word +0x48 equals the player DGROUP:0x9646 and DGROUP:0x9208 > 1, it calls seg7:0x1208; the touch stores the ship in +0x48. [BYTES seg12:0xef8]
3. The source x offset x0 is `gap` + `size` when DGROUP:0x9214 is 3 or 4 and +0x26 ≠ 0, else 0. With `on_screen` = 0 it returns before the switch, so an off-screen tank never runs its end. [BYTES seg12:0xefd seg12:0xf04 seg12:0xf13 seg12:0xf1a seg12:0xf25 seg12:0xf39]
4. **p = 0:** StretchBlt(sx, sy, `size`, `size`) from DC DGROUP:0x39f0 at (x0 + `gap` − u(3), `gap` − u(3)) with source side `size` + u(6), SRCCOPY. [BYTES seg12:0xf42 seg12:0xf61 seg12:0xf73 seg12:0xf7c seg12:0xf84 seg12:0xf8e]
5. **p = 1 … 4:** with q = (p << `Size`) & 0xfffe, StretchBlt to (sx + q, sy − u(2)), width `size` − ((p << (`Size` + 1)) & 0xfffc), height `size` + u(3), from (x0, 0) with source side 2·`gap` + `size`. With DGROUP:0x8ffe ≠ 0 it also PatBlts two strips of width u(2) and height `size` at x = sx + q − u(2) and x = sx + `size` − q. [BYTES seg12:0xfa6 seg12:0xfab seg12:0xfbc seg12:0xfc3 seg12:0xfca seg12:0xfde seg12:0xfe6 seg12:0xff0 seg12:0xffc seg12:0x1001 seg12:0x1042 seg12:0x1078]
6. **p = 5 … 8:** with p − 4, StretchBlt to (sx + u(8), sy + u(2)·(p − 4) − 1), width `size` − u(16), height `size` − u(4)·(p − 4) + 1, from the same source. With DGROUP:0x8ffe ≠ 0 it PatBlts two strips of width u(2) at sx + u(6) and sx + `size` − u(16) + u(8). [BYTES seg12:0x1080 seg12:0x109b seg12:0x10ae seg12:0x10b5 seg12:0x10be seg12:0x10cb seg12:0x10d3 seg12:0x10ed seg12:0x10f2 seg12:0x112c seg12:0x1152]
7. **p = 9:** PatBlt(sx, sy, `size`, `size`), seg7:0x2080(4, −1) and seg7:0x1208. No case touches `fuel` or plays a sound: the touch has done both. [BYTES seg12:0x1155 seg12:0x1174 seg12:0x117d seg12:0x1185]
8. With the frame-clear flag set it ends with four PatBlts that form a frame of width u(3) inside the square: top (sx, sy, `size`, u(3)), bottom (sx, sy + `size` − u(3), `size`, u(3)), left (sx, sy + u(3), u(3), `size` − u(6)) and right (sx + `size` − u(3), sy + u(3), u(3), `size` − u(6)). [BYTES seg12:0x11ad seg12:0x11e2 seg12:0x1217 seg12:0x1251 seg12:0x128f]
- DGROUP:0x8ffe is 3, 2, 1 and 1 for `Detail` 0 … 3 ([globals.md](globals.md#global-table)), so the phases run 2, 5, 8, 9; 1, 3, 5, 7, 9; or 0 … 9. core-0003 sees the can shrink in two 0.15 s frames and gone in the third. [BYTES seg6:0xe36] [ORACLE core-0003]

### Icon fragments (pool 1, seg7:0x32d0)
- **Loop.** seg7:0x32d0 runs over i < DGROUP:0x9408 and skips records whose `state` ≠ 0. A fragment off screen or without its bitmap DGROUP:0x4e94 + 2i moves only when DGROUP:0x9142 % 3 = 0, through seg7:0x3400(i) (Pascal, `retf 2`), which is seg8:0x1d54(h, 0x28) and seg8:0x1b54(h, 3). [BYTES seg7:0x330b seg7:0x3323 seg7:0x3328 seg7:0x332a seg7:0x3336 seg7:0x32f4 seg7:0x32ff seg7:0x3305 seg7:0x3417 seg7:0x341f]
- **Draw.** An on-screen fragment selects DGROUP:0x4e94[i] into DC DGROUP:0x9288, sets `src_x` = u(6), calls seg8:0x1d54(h, 0x28), seg8:0x1b54(h, 1) and seg8:0xb76(h, 3, DGROUP:0x9288, 0), and reselects the old bitmap. [BYTES seg7:0x3347 seg7:0x3356 seg7:0x3366 seg7:0x336e seg7:0x337c seg7:0x3388]
- **Collision.** With `collided` = 0 it registers pool 1 in DGROUP:0x943a, calls seg8:0x50aa and on a hit seg8:0x5df2(1, i, hit pool, hit rec, seg8:0x546c(hit, &vx, &vy, `extent` + 2, `mass`)). Unlike pools 4 and 11 it has no ship-touch branch and no 125 case. [BYTES seg7:0x3391 seg7:0x339a seg7:0x33a7 seg7:0x33d6 seg7:0x33e9 seg7:0x33eb]
- **End.** A fragment has no timer: it ends through its `on_destroy` seg7:0x2e02 once `damage` passes `max_damage` 14. [BYTES seg10:0xcaa seg10:0xcec]
- **Target text.** For a pool-1 target seg3:0xf4 copies the icon's file name DGROUP:0x57b4 + 0x23·(word DGROUP:0x6038 + 2·rec) into DGROUP:0x9628 and the name seg3:0x1542(0, 1, rec, buf, 0), "Icon Fragment", into DGROUP:0x8718. [BYTES seg3:0x2cf seg3:0x2de seg3:0x2e3 seg3:0x2e8 seg3:0x2ed seg3:0x419 seg3:0x428] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 56] id 892

### The break-up (seg7:0x2e02)
seg7:0x2e02(pool, rec) (cdecl) is the `on_destroy` of pool 1 and of the time-of-day tanks ([world.md](world.md#pool-9-and-pool-4)). [BYTES seg7:0x2e02]
- Only when `state` was 0 does it set `state` 1, black the square and set up an effect. It takes the first of effect entries 0 … 3 that is free or of type ≤ 5; an entry in use is first copied into the first free entry among 4 … 44. The entry gets dword +0 = 0x00140001, type +0x0a = 6, +0x0c = `size` / 5, +0x04 = 2 when the entry index > `Detail`, else 1, pool, rec, the screen point (DGROUP:0x5170) and the world point (DGROUP:0x5224), and DGROUP:0x9430 rises by 1 ([structs.md](structs.md#effect-table-45--0x14-bytes-at-dgroup0x1e90)). With no entry among 0 … 3 it does nothing more. [BYTES seg7:0x2e1f seg7:0x2e24 seg7:0x2e28 seg7:0x2e4f seg7:0x2eca-0x2f5e seg7:0x2f66 seg7:0x2f73 seg7:0x2f99 seg7:0x2f9b seg7:0x2fa2 seg7:0x2fa7 seg7:0x2fb3 seg7:0x3007 seg7:0x300e]
- With an entry it plays seg10:0x754a(0x1a, 1, rec, 1) for pool 1 and seg10:0x754a(0x1f, 4, rec, 1) for pool 4; pool 8, and pool 6 with W(w)+0x24 = 6, share the 0x1a call, while pool 6 with any other end kind plays seg10:0x754a(0x1f SHATTER, 6, rec, 1). [BYTES seg7:0x2e9e-0x2ea3 seg7:0x2eba-0x2ec2] [BYTES seg7:0x303f seg7:0x306e seg7:0x3074 seg7:0x2ea8 seg7:0x2eba seg7:0x2ec0 seg7:0x2ec2]
- For pool 1 it always ends with seg7:0x65aa(0, word DGROUP:0x6038 + 2·rec), the icon's row, also when `state` was already 1. [BYTES seg7:0x3079 seg7:0x3084 seg7:0x308b]
- It does not raise DGROUP:0x9434, so a type-6 entry above that index is not visited until another entry raises it or an ending entry's rescan reaches it. Before it fills the entry it releases DGROUP:0x5e98[e], the piece source of an earlier break-up, with SelectObject(DGROUP:0x5ea0[e]) and DeleteDC whatever pool filled it. Then pool 1 gets a new DC holding the fragment's own bitmap DGROUP:0x4e94[rec] (old object in DGROUP:0x5ea0[e]), pool 8 the image slot DGROUP:0x39e8[word DGROUP:0x3d6e + 0x34·rec], pool 6 the slot of its weapon, DGROUP:0x39e8[W(w)−4], and pool 4 the tank's slot 4, DGROUP:0x39f0; another pool gets no DC and no sound. [BYTES seg7:0x2e02-0x3099 seg7:0x3015-0x3039 seg7:0x3047-0x306a seg7:0x2e5c-0x2ec2]
- **The teardown.** seg10:0x22e also releases the four piece sources: for e < 4 with DGROUP:0x5e98[e] ≠ 0 it calls SelectObject(DGROUP:0x5e98[e], DGROUP:0x5ea0[e]) and DeleteDC, then stores 0, for any pool, as seg7:0x301a-0x3039 does. A pool-8, pool-6 or pool-4 source is the image slot's own DC, so a type-6 entry still live at the teardown deletes the DC the slot still holds. The port deletes only the DCs it made and leaves another pool's slot DC to the image table ([ADR 0016](../decisions/0016-undefined-reads.md), Proposed line). [BYTES seg10:0x2cb-0x301 seg7:0x301a-0x3039 seg7:0x2e5c-0x2ec2]
- The entry's 25 pieces, cut from that DC, are animated by the effect frame's type-6 branch ([world.md](world.md#effect-frame-seg80x3fbe), Pieces).

### Pods (pool 11, seg12:0x6b1e)
- **Pod record.** seg13:0x4ba0 stores the dword 0x01f40000 at DGROUP:0x8564 + 0x34·rec, so a new pod has age word 0 and limit word 500 ([structs.md](structs.md#pool-11-pods)). [BYTES seg13:0x4bb3 seg13:0x4c88]
- **Taken pod.** A record in `state` ≠ 0 with byte +0x51 ≠ 0 gets seg12:0x129e(11, i), the pickup dissolve of pools 6 and 11. Its image slot comes from object word +0x4a for pool 11 (source x offset 0) and from DGROUP:0x2f50 + 0x50·W for pool 6, W = word DGROUP:0x66e4 + 0x1c·rec. A record index outside 0 … 100 increments DGROUP:0x9498, writes 0x10 into word DGROUP:0x66e4 + 0x1c·rec (the missile table, also for a pod) and uses DGROUP:0x3450 = W(16)−4 (DGROUP:0x2f54 + 0x50·16 − 4), the image slot of weapon 16, the weapon it has just written; seg10:0x1fb2 stores −1 there and seg10:0x533a a slot. Its cases are [OPEN]. [BYTES seg12:0x6beb seg12:0x6bbc seg12:0x6bc1 seg12:0x6bc9 seg12:0x1336 seg12:0x1340 seg12:0x1347 seg12:0x134e seg12:0x135d seg12:0x1375 seg12:0x1378 seg12:0x1384 seg12:0x138c seg12:0x1392 seg10:0x2da8]
- **Live pod.** For every record in `state` 0 the mover clears `collided` and sets `capturable` = 1 once the age word exceeds 2 (signed). It calls seg8:0x1b54(h, 1) before seg8:0x1d54(h, 0x28), with no on-screen test, then draws the pod with seg8:0xb76(h, 3, DGROUP:0x39e8[2·word +0x4a], 0) at `src_x` = (word DGROUP:0x8560 + 0x34·i − 1)·(`size` + `gap`) + `gap`, and adds 1 to the age. [BYTES seg12:0x6bf2 seg12:0x6bfc seg12:0x6c01 seg12:0x6c09 seg12:0x6c1a seg12:0x6c22 seg12:0x6c39 seg12:0x6c3d seg12:0x6c4b seg12:0x6c51 seg12:0x6c61 seg12:0x6c67 seg12:0x6c6f seg12:0x6c74]
- **Collision.** A hit on a ship with +0xea = 0 and +0xe8 = 0 calls the touch seg13:0x16ac(ship, 0x0b00 | rec); any other hit calls seg8:0x546c with the constant mass 0x6e, not the record's 101, and then seg8:0x5df2(11, i, hit pool, hit rec, result). [BYTES seg12:0x6cb1 seg12:0x6cc1 seg12:0x6cc7 seg12:0x6ce0 seg12:0x6b61 seg12:0x6b63 seg12:0x6b76 seg12:0x6b78]
- **Lifetime.** A pod in `state` 0 whose age exceeds its limit (signed) while DGROUP:0x92e8 = 0 gets its `on_destroy` as (11, i), then seg7:0x2080(11, −1); in Demon's Gate pods never expire. 500 frames are about 13.9 s at 36 frames per second. [BYTES seg12:0x6b80 seg12:0x6b84 seg12:0x6b88 seg12:0x6b8c seg12:0x6b8e seg12:0x6ba2 seg12:0x6bad seg12:0x6bcc]
- **The pod's `on_destroy`** seg12:0x6a60(pool, rec) blacks the record's square when seg8:0x7ba8 accepts its screen point from seg8:0x29ea(x, y), calls seg7:0x5b68(pool, rec, 1) five times and sets `state` 1; for pool 6 it sets the missile entry age DGROUP:0x66ec + 0x1c·rec = W(id)+0x1e and the entry weapon = −1 (id read first) between the square's PatBlt and the puffs, and releases no shot slot; pool 11 skips that branch. [BYTES seg12:0x6ac5-0x6adc seg12:0x6a8b seg12:0x6a97 seg12:0x6aa1 seg12:0x6ac0 seg12:0x6aef seg12:0x6afa seg12:0x6b0e]
- Which routine renders image slot 0x1d, the pods' cells, is [OPEN].

## The ambulance
The ambulance is pool 7 record 0 (A below). Its state words are in [globals.md](globals.md#global-table) (DGROUP:0x9e41 … DGROUP:0x9e5d, DGROUP:0x111e, DGROUP:0x1c42, DGROUP:0x6e10). [DESIGN]

### Calling it (H)
- WM_KEYDOWN 'H' without Ctrl or Alt calls seg16:0xb4e(player) only in a player game. Ctrl+H calls seg13:0x81c2(player) and Alt+H seg13:0x9ae8(player); Ctrl+Alt+H runs both. [BYTES seg11:0x6e8e seg11:0x6cdc seg11:0x6cfb seg11:0x6f7a seg11:0x6f84 seg11:0x6f94 seg11:0x6f9c seg11:0x6fa2 seg11:0x6fb0]
- **Reply.** seg3:0x6ec(player, 7, 0, code) (cdecl) with code 7 when seg16:0xb4e returns non-zero; when it returns 0, code 132 when the 16-bit sum lo16(credit) + resources < −300 (signed `jge` on 0xfed4), else code 8. [BYTES seg11:0x6fb6 seg11:0x6fc4 seg11:0x6fc8 seg11:0x6fcc seg11:0x6fcf seg11:0x6fd8 seg11:0x6fe5]
  - Code 7 (switch case 6): voice 0x33 and seg10:0xa332(DGROUP:0x8db0, 110, 2, far DGROUP:0x380c), "Hold Your Horses, %s. Rescue Unit Dispatched." with the pilot name. [BYTES seg3:0xcbb seg3:0xcc4 seg3:0xcc9] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 7] id 110 [ORACLE wave-0001] [ORACLE core-0003]
  - Code 8: voice 0x2c and string 348 "Sorry! Ambulance Service Is Busy At This Time."; code 132: voice 0x2c and string 1136 "Sorry! You Have No Credit. Help Is Not Coming.". All three end at seg3:0x14d6. [BYTES seg3:0xcde seg3:0xce8 seg3:0xcce seg3:0xcd8 seg3:0xbb4 seg3:0xbb8 seg3:0xbc0 seg3:0x8d9 seg3:0x8e5] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 22] id 348 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 72] id 1136 [ORACLE core-0003]
  - seg16:0xb4e refuses below −350 but the reply says "no credit" below −300, so a busy refusal with credit + resources below −300 reads "no credit". [BYTES seg16:0xb92 seg11:0x6fcc]
- **seg16:0xb4e(ship)** returns 0 while the wave clock DGROUP:0x934e < 3 s, DGROUP:0x913a ≠ 0, DGROUP:0x913c ≠ 0, (DGROUP:0x92e8 ≠ 0 and DGROUP:0x94b2 = 0), or the wave kind is 6 or 7. [BYTES seg16:0xb5c seg16:0xb68 seg16:0xb6f seg16:0xb76 seg16:0xb84 seg16:0xb8b seg16:0xb90]
  - With r = resources + credit (32-bit) it refuses every ship when r < −350 (dx:ax below 0xffff:0xfea2) and resources < 200 (signed), and a ship other than the player also when r < 200: a negative high word, or a zero high word and a low word below 200 unsigned. [BYTES seg16:0xb92 seg16:0xb9e seg16:0xba2 seg16:0xbac seg16:0xbb3 seg16:0xbb8 seg16:0xbbe seg16:0xbc4 seg16:0xbc6 seg16:0xbca seg16:0xbcc seg16:0xbd1]

### The mover (seg16:0xa2)
The frame calls seg16:0xa2 while A has `state` 0 ([world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 20). [BYTES seg7:0xc61 seg7:0xc65 seg7:0xc6c]
1. **Busy flag.** Every call sets DGROUP:0x111e = 1 when 1 ≤ DGROUP:0x9e49 < 6 and 0 otherwise; state 1 also writes 1, and state 9, the abort, the park and seg16:0x32a0 write 0. No INSPACE.EXE instruction reads DGROUP:0x111e or DGROUP:0x9596. 2. **Lift.** With DGROUP:0x9e49 = 0 the lift DGROUP:0x9e47 is first forced to 0. With the lift ≠ 0 and A's byte +0x2e = 0 the cell DGROUP:0x9e45 = min(lift/5, 3) + 10 (signed idiv) and the lift gains DGROUP:0x9e41; above 15 it becomes 15 with DGROUP:0x9e41 = 0, at 0 or below both become 0. When that clamp to 0 comes in state 8 the state becomes 9 and byte +0x54 (`fixed`) of the served ship's object and of A becomes 0. 4. **Draw.** The sprite draw is skipped only when (state 3, or state 6 with lift 15) and byte +0x2e = 0. It sets A's `src_x` = (`gap` + `size`)·DGROUP:0x9e45 + `gap` (16-bit imul, `size` and `gap` copied at entry) and calls seg8:0xb76(0x700, 1, DC DGROUP:0x39f2, 0). A's `size` is u(40) and `gap` u(6), u(n) = (n << `Size`) >> 1. DGROUP:0x9e4f has one reader, seg16:0x210, which follows its write at seg16:0x1f6 on every path, so its other writes are dead. ### The intercept (seg8:0x2658)
seg8:0x2658(chaser pool, chaser rec, target pool, target rec) (Pascal, `retf 8`) returns a heading 0 … 15. [BYTES seg8:0x2658]
1. dx, dy = target − chaser in cells (seg8:0x285a, target first) and d their distance. T = seg5:0x110(target `vx`·dy, d) − seg5:0x110(target `vy`·dx, d), two signed 32-bit divides of the full 16 × 16 products. V = seg8:0x35d0(chaser `vx`, `vy`) (cdecl, sign-extended), 50 when V < 30 (signed 32-bit). [BYTES seg8:0x26bc seg8:0x26cd seg8:0x26e1 seg8:0x26ec seg8:0x26f7 seg8:0x2717 seg8:0x272f]
2. When V < T (signed dword) it returns seg8:0x19b0(dx, dy) ([world.md](world.md#helpers)). [BYTES seg8:0x273b seg8:0x2741 seg8:0x2746]
3. Otherwise W = the low word, sign-extended, of seg8:0x1290(V² − T², \|V\| − \|T\|); X = W·dx/d + T·dy/d and Y = −T·dx/d + W·dy/d, and it returns seg8:0x19b0(X, Y). W·dx/d, T·dy/d and W·dy/d are each a 32-bit `imul`, a `cdq` that drops the product's high dword and a 32-bit `idiv`; T·dx/d goes through seg5:0x110 and is negated in 32 bits; X and Y are 16-bit sums of the low words. [BYTES seg8:0x2754 seg8:0x2765 seg8:0x2774 seg8:0x277a seg8:0x278a seg8:0x279f seg8:0x27a2 seg8:0x27b7-0x27bc seg8:0x27cf seg8:0x27d6 seg8:0x27fd seg8:0x2804 seg8:0x280b seg8:0x2822 seg8:0x2827 seg8:0x283a seg8:0x2840 seg8:0x2848 seg8:0x284b]
- seg8:0x1290(N, g) (Pascal, `retf 8`, g at [bp+6], N at [bp+0xa]) sets g = 3 when g < 3 (signed dword) and returns (N / g + g) `sar` 1 in DX:AX. Its loop at seg8:0x12c6 is reached only from inside the same unreachable block. [BYTES seg8:0x129d seg8:0x12a4 seg8:0x12b2 seg8:0x12b6 seg8:0x12ba seg8:0x12c4 seg8:0x1314]

### The ship's part (seg13:0x3748)
seg13:0x3748(i) moves the served ship into A in state 3 and out of it in state 6; the ship mover calls it only for i = DGROUP:0x952e while DGROUP:0x9e49 is 1 … 6. seg13:0x3d40 draws the shrinking or growing ship over A. [BYTES seg13:0x7c seg13:0xac seg13:0x3757 seg13:0x3976]
- **Offsets.** Both states take D = seg8:0x285a(A's `x`/`y`, the ship's `x`/`y`) on the raw positions, A first, with the offsets dx, dy = A − ship; the centre sums the routine builds at [bp−0x16] … [bp−0x10] are never read. [BYTES seg13:0x3796 seg13:0x37c8 seg13:0x37ea seg13:0x37f3 seg13:0x37f8 seg13:0x39ff seg13:0x3a08]
- **State 3, into A.**
  - It sets DGROUP:0x9e5b = 0 when the ship's `alive` (+0xec) is 0, and sets `alive` to 1 on every call. While DGROUP:0x9e5b = 0 each call sets the ship object's word +0x26 = 1; when ship word +0x16 (DGROUP:0x70a6) < 1 (signed) it turns the heading +0x28 with the Pascal seg13:0x100c(ship, heading), calls seg13:0x6716(ship) and sets +0x16 = 1, and otherwise it decrements +0x16, so from +0x16 = 0 or 1 the heading steps by +1 (mod 16) on every other call. seg13:0x100c leaves the heading unchanged while ship byte +0xeb ≠ 0 or ship word +0x10 is 1 … 6. [BYTES seg13:0x3766 seg13:0x3772 seg13:0x377b seg13:0x3800 seg13:0x3812 seg13:0x381d seg13:0x3841 seg13:0x384f seg13:0x3853 seg13:0x3860 seg13:0x101c seg13:0x102d seg13:0x104e seg13:0x1072]
  - **Below distance 5** (signed) it zeroes the ship's `vx` and `vy`, frees the private buffers of the ship (DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x952e) and of A (7, 0) with seg12:0x26a4, sets DGROUP:0x9e41 = −1 and DGROUP:0x9e43 = 50 in one dword store, DGROUP:0x9e45 = 5 and DGROUP:0x9e4f = 0, plays seg10:0x754a(0x23, 7, 0, 1), and sets DGROUP:0x9e49 = 4, the dword DGROUP:0x6e10 = now + 1100 and the ship's x = A's x + 2. [BYTES seg13:0x3864 seg13:0x387b seg13:0x3890 seg13:0x389e seg13:0x38aa seg13:0x38b2 seg13:0x38bb seg13:0x38c5 seg13:0x38cd seg13:0x38d5 seg13:0x38e5 seg13:0x38fe]
  - **Otherwise** it sets A's `frame` to DGROUP:0x9e45, calls seg13:0x3d40(DGROUP:0x9e53 + 50, D + 50, 0, 7, i, DGROUP:0x9324) and sets the ship's velocity per axis to lo16(d·70)/D (16-bit `imul`, `cwd`, signed `idiv`), then moves it with seg8:0x1b54(1, (DGROUP:0x9324 << 8) | i). [BYTES seg13:0x3904 seg13:0x390b seg13:0x390f seg13:0x391d seg13:0x3924 seg13:0x3929 seg13:0x392c seg13:0x3931 seg13:0x3947 seg13:0x394c seg13:0x3971]
- **State 6, out of A,** runs while A's byte +0x2e is 0. [BYTES seg13:0x3976 seg13:0x3983]
  - **Lift below 15.** At lift 2 it calls seg10:0x754a(0x23, 7, 0, 1); at lift 14 it calls seg10:0x744c(0x6e) when the carried ship DGROUP:0x952e is the player, and then seg10:0x754a(0x1c, 7, 0, 1) for any carried ship. [BYTES seg13:0x398c seg13:0x3b46 seg13:0x3b4d seg13:0x3b57 seg13:0x3b5e seg13:0x3b65 seg13:0x3b67 seg13:0x3b6f seg13:0x3b77 seg16:0x129 seg16:0x13e]

  - **The drop-off ends** when D > DGROUP:0x9e53 (signed) or the ship's `alive` is 0: seg10:0x754a(0x23, 7, 0, 1); seg12:0x26a4(DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x952e) and seg12:0x26a4(7, 0) free both buffers; then DGROUP:0x9e43 = 50, DGROUP:0x9e4f = 0, DGROUP:0x9e41 = −1, seg7:0x2080(7, −1), DGROUP:0x9e49 = 8 and the ship's byte +0x147 = 0. [BYTES seg13:0x397f seg13:0x3a29 seg13:0x3a2f seg13:0x3a37 seg13:0x3a46 seg13:0x3a56 seg13:0x3a62 seg13:0x3a6a seg13:0x3a70 seg13:0x3a79 seg13:0x3a7f seg13:0x3a87 seg13:0x3a93]
    - D is the seg8:0x285a distance taken at the call's start (seg13:0x3a0d), from the positions before this frame's move, so the last 0x23 (seg13:0x3a46) plays on the first call that finds the ship past DGROUP:0x9e53, the pickup distance that state 2 stored (seg16:0x506). seg13:0x3d40 plays no sound, and it, the push and seg8:0x1b54 run only on calls that do not end the drop-off. seg13:0x3748 runs once per wave frame (state 1), so none of the drop-off's three sounds depends on state 6's pace. [BYTES seg13:0x3a0d seg13:0x3a12 seg13:0x3a29-0x3a2f seg13:0x3a3c seg13:0x3a9b seg13:0x3ad1 seg13:0x3afe seg13:0x3b3f seg13:0x3d40-0x4479 seg16:0x506]
    - So the drop-off lasts longer the farther the pickup was: core-0003 reads DGROUP:0x9e53 = 86 in visit 1's drop-off and 110 in visit 2's, and the lift-14 0x1c to the last 0x23 takes 0.397 s and 0.504 s. A comparison of the drop-off's sounds takes the run's own DGROUP:0x9e53. [ORACLE core-0003] [DESIGN]
  - **Otherwise,** with D ≠ 0, it sets A's `frame` to DGROUP:0x9e45 and calls seg13:0x3d40(DGROUP:0x9e53 + 50, min(D, DGROUP:0x9e53) + 50, 0, 7, ship, DGROUP:0x9324), whose second argument is D + 50 on this path. Then it pushes the ship out with velocity lo16(d·−50)/D per axis (16-bit `imul`, signed `idiv`), sets its heading to seg8:0x19b0(vy, vx) and moves it with seg8:0x1b54(1, (DGROUP:0x9324 << 8) | ship); the composite therefore draws the ship at the heading it had before this frame. [BYTES seg13:0x3a9b seg13:0x3aaa seg13:0x3abc seg13:0x3ac2 seg13:0x3ac5 seg13:0x3ad1 seg13:0x3ad4 seg13:0x3afe seg13:0x3b19 seg13:0x3b1d seg13:0x3b25 seg13:0x3b2c seg13:0x3b3f]
- **seg13:0x3d40(a1, a2, outer rec, outer pool, ship rec, ship pool)** (Pascal, `retf 0xc`) draws the ship scaled by a2/a1 over the outer object; a1 = 0 counts as 1. [BYTES seg13:0x3d40 seg13:0x3d4f]
  1. **Scale.** The scaled side is lo16(`size`·a2)/a1, the product's high word replaced by `cwd`, placed at `gap` + (`size` − scaled)/2 (toward zero), `size` and `gap` being the ship object's. [BYTES seg13:0x3de2 seg13:0x3de5 seg13:0x3de6 seg13:0x3df0 seg13:0x3df5 seg13:0x3df7]
  2. **Overlap.** With ox, oy the outer object's screen position minus the ship's, it composites only when −2 − outer `size` ≤ ox, oy ≤ ship `size` + 2 (signed) and the outer pool is not 15. [BYTES seg13:0x3e2c seg13:0x3e35 seg13:0x3e3a seg13:0x3e44 seg13:0x3e49 seg13:0x3e4f]
  3. **No overlap.** It clears the outer record's buffer (PatBlt BLACKNESS) and BitBlts its cell into it, unless the outer pool is 15, and refreshes the ship's buffer from its own cell: a BitBlt when the scaled side equals `size`, else SetStretchBltMode 3, PatBlt BLACKNESS and StretchBlt. [BYTES seg13:0x3e57 seg13:0x3e7f seg13:0x3eb6 seg13:0x3ec1 seg13:0x3efa seg13:0x3f18 seg13:0x3f37 seg13:0x3f74]
  4. **The composite** clears the ship's buffer +0x3c and fills it with the overlapping part of the outer object's image, stretches the ship into a new bitmap and builds its mask with seg10:0x6ec6, and applies the mask (SRCAND 0x8800c6) and then the scaled ship (SRCPAINT 0xee0086) to the ship's buffer and, after clearing it and filling it with its own image, to the outer object's buffer. Both buffers end with the scaled ship painted over the outer object. [BYTES seg13:0x4037 seg13:0x408e seg13:0x4191 seg13:0x41e4 seg13:0x420e seg13:0x42d7 seg13:0x430e seg13:0x4340 seg13:0x4378]
  5. **The draw.** It sets the ship's word +0x1a to 1 and its `src_x` to lo16(`frame`·(`size` + `gap`)) + `gap`, draws it through seg8:0xb76 from its word +0x3a and sets +0x1a back to 0. Unless the outer pool is 15 it then sets the outer object's `src_x` the same way from that object's `frame`, `size` and `gap` and draws it through seg8:0xb76, leaving its +0x1a as it was. Both `src_x` writes stay in the records. [BYTES seg13:0x43db seg13:0x43f6 seg13:0x4416 seg13:0x441f seg13:0x4425 seg13:0x444e seg13:0x446a]
- core-0003 sees, after the re-entry, the sphere form at the view's centre, 0x23, 0x1c and 0x23 within 0.75 s, the ship appear to its right and state 8 then 9 0.4 s apart. [ORACLE core-0003]

### Into the visit and back
- **The deadline.** seg7:0x5ce–0x60b clears DGROUP:0x1e88 when it falls due (≠ 0 and ≤ now, unsigned). Only when the served ship is DGROUP:0x9646 and DGROUP:0x951a = 0 does it enter the visit (DGROUP:0x90f4 = 6, DGROUP:0x914a = 0, seg16:0xcfe(1) through seg7:0x18f); every other case calls seg16:0x32a0. [BYTES seg7:0x5ce seg7:0x5fa seg7:0x60b]
- **seg16:0x32a0,** the drop-off without a visit, writes the dword DGROUP:0x9e41/0x9e43 = (−1, 50) and the dword DGROUP:0x9e45/0x9e47 = (5, 15), runs seg16:0x872 and sets A `state` = 0. When A's byte +0x2e = 0xff it also calls seg12:0x2556(7, 0) and seg7:0x13b2(2, 7, 0) and sets DGROUP:0x9e41 = 1. It ends with DGROUP:0x9e49 = 6 and DGROUP:0x111e = 0, and writes no `mass`, no ship `state` and no DGROUP:0x9e4b. [BYTES seg16:0x32aa seg16:0x32b3 seg16:0x32bd seg16:0x32c4 seg16:0x32ce-0x32ec seg16:0x32f2 seg16:0x32f8]
- **seg16:0x872** calls seg16:0x3784(DGROUP:0x952e, 0) ([Purchases](#purchases-seg160x3784)) and seg3:0x6ec(DGROUP:0x952e, 7, 0, 0x54): code 84, "Repairs Complete, %s. …" ([Lamp messages](#lamp-messages)), the message the oracle sees back in the wave. Its callers are phase 64 (seg16:0x1e54) and seg16:0x32a0 (seg16:0x32bd). [BYTES seg16:0x87c-0x893 seg16:0x1e54 seg16:0x32bd] [ORACLE wave-0001] [ORACLE core-0003]
- **Re-entry.** While A's `state` ≠ 0, DGROUP:0x6da8 ≠ 0 and now > DGROUP:0x6da8 (unsigned), the frame calls seg16:0x33a2, and seg16:0x3306 when that returns non-zero ([world.md](world.md#update-loop) step 20). [BYTES seg7:0xc73-0xc8f]
  - seg16:0x33a2 returns 0 when any `state`-0 record below the in-use count DGROUP:0x9406 + 2p of pools p = 0 … DGROUP:0x9324, pool 7 and the served ship excepted, has its dword +0x04 point inside the rectangle (A.x − 560, A.y − 640, A.x + 1600, A.y + 640) by USER PtInRect, which excludes the right and bottom edges; else 1. [BYTES seg16:0x33b1-0x345c seg16:0x33c8 seg16:0x33d6 seg16:0x33e4 seg16:0x33fb seg16:0x340a seg16:0x341d seg16:0x3434 seg16:0x343f seg16:0x344f]
  - seg16:0x3306 sets the served ship's and A's `state` to 0, clears dword DGROUP:0x6da8, calls seg12:0x26a4 and seg12:0x2556 on (DGROUP:0x9324, DGROUP:0x952e) and seg11:0x2f8c, and sets DGROUP:0x9e49 = 6. When A's byte +0x2e = 0xff it also calls seg12:0x2556(7, 0) and seg7:0x13b2(2, 7, 0), sets DGROUP:0x9e41 and the lift to 1 and A's `mass` to 800. [BYTES seg16:0x3311-0x3356 seg16:0x335f-0x338d seg16:0x3393]
  - So the ship comes back 3000 ms after the visit, or later while an object sits in that box. core-0003 measures 3.0 s from DGROUP:0x6da8's store to DGROUP:0x6da8 = 0 with DGROUP:0x9e49 = 6 and the HYPER sound, in both visits. [ORACLE core-0003]
- Another ship's call (seg4:0x4f06, seg13:0x7456, seg13:0x7f4d, seg13:0x80ec) and its drop-off through seg16:0x32a0 have an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s3-ambflight.md §6). [BYTES seg4:0x4f06 seg13:0x7456 seg13:0x7f4d seg13:0x80ec] [OPEN]

### The visit (state 6, seg16:0xcfe)
seg16:0xcfe(repaint) runs on the phase DGROUP:0x914a. The state-6 case of the tick calls seg16:0xcfe(0) once per state-6 frame, whatever the phase, and runs no phase outside that call; the entry pass calls seg16:0xcfe(1), the repaint, which draws nothing at phase 0 ([timing.md](timing.md#adaptive-screen-threshold)). [BYTES seg16:0xcfe seg7:0xff-0x194 seg7:0x18f seg7:0x5fa-0x608 seg16:0xd13-0xd2d]
- **Messages.** Before its frame test the state-6 case hands a pending message DGROUP:0x8e62 to seg11:0x785e(DGROUP:0x8e62, DGROUP:0x8e64, dword DGROUP:0x1d38), a visit route of its own: on WM_KEYUP (0x101) with wParam 0x1b and DGROUP:0x93a6 = 0 it calls seg16:0x638a(0, 0, 0, 1) (seg11:0x788b), and with DGROUP:0x952c ≠ 0 it calls seg16:0x614c (seg11:0x78a9), which sets DGROUP:0x9530 = 5 and calls seg16:0x64fa. These change the phase outside seg16:0xcfe: seg16:0x6464 and seg16:0x646a save DGROUP:0x914a to DGROUP:0x958c and set it to −1, and seg16:0x64aa/0x64ad and seg16:0x6875/0x6878 restore it from DGROUP:0x958c. The phase raises happen only inside seg16:0xcfe's calls, but the −1 pause and the restore can come from a message in the same state-6 pass. [BYTES seg7:0x105-0x119 seg11:0x788b seg11:0x78a9 seg16:0x6464 seg16:0x646a seg16:0x64aa seg16:0x64ad seg16:0x6875 seg16:0x6878]
- **Repaint.** With repaint ≠ 0 it calls the screen redraw seg16:0x2144 only when (phase > 0 or phase = −1) and (phase ≥ 2 or DGROUP:0x92a2 ≥ 1, signed), then returns. [BYTES seg16:0xd0d seg16:0xd13 seg16:0xd1a seg16:0xd21 seg16:0xd28 seg16:0xd30 seg16:0xd37]
- **Guard.** With repaint = 0 it returns at once while DGROUP:0x9532 ≠ 0 (a dialog of the visit is open). [BYTES seg16:0xd3b seg16:0xd40]
- **Fast flag.** Ctrl held (GetKeyState(0x11) & 0x8000) with DGROUP:0x952c = 0 and DGROUP:0x92a2 ≠ 1, or DGROUP:0x8f70 > 1 (signed), sets DGROUP:0x928 = 1. The test runs before the phase switch on every call that passes the guard. [BYTES seg16:0xd44 seg16:0xd4b seg16:0xd50 seg16:0xd57 seg16:0xd5e seg16:0xd64 seg16:0xd6b seg16:0xd74]
  - **Its window.** DGROUP:0x952c is 0 from phase 0 (and from seg16:0x631c at the previous phase 64) until the descent's first call stores seg16:0x619e()'s 1 through seg16:0x27ea ([shell.md](shell.md#screen-states), State 6). So Ctrl counts at phase 0's frame, which reaches the landing scene's and seg15:0x1a42's tests in the same frame, and at the sub-step-0 frames of the incoming jet; while DGROUP:0x92a2 = 1 (the descent and its 1000 ms wait) the test fails, and from the descent's first call DGROUP:0x952c = 1 blocks it. Its readers are all in the landing ([shell.md](shell.md#screen-states), Ctrl skip). [BYTES seg16:0xd85 seg16:0x637d seg16:0x2e31 seg15:0x3859 seg16:0x2052]
  - core-0007's visits with Ctrl held set the flag in the poll of the state's change to 6 and clear it with phase 64; only the descent changes. [ORACLE core-0007]
- **Phases.** The switch reads the jump table at seg16:0x1e6e, 65 words for phases 0 … 64; a phase above 64 as an unsigned word, −1 included, goes straight to the tail. "type" is the monitor typing seg16:0x2e3c(0, blank) below, which itself advances the phase at the text's end; a332(id, …) is seg10:0xa332 into the monitor text DGROUP:0x6db8 in mode 1. - **Timers.** The 50 ms steps of phases 24, 29 and 35 use dword DGROUP:0x1c48 against now. Phase 60 arms DGROUP:0x1c48 from GetCurrentTime and compares it with DGROUP:0x1e50, two clocks. [BYTES seg16:0x1b17 seg16:0x1b49]
- **What a phase waits on.** Every phase runs once per frame; what sets its length is its own wait: a deadline against the pass sample DGROUP:0x1e50 (the typing's DGROUP:0x1c60, phase 1's DGROUP:0x17f2, phases 11, 24, 29, 35 and 60), a wait inside one frame, busy (phases 15, 40, 50, 53 and the 2000 ms of seg15:0x4c76's new body) or in seg10:0x740c calls (43, 47, 61), or the dialog (15); every other phase takes one frame per step. seg10:0x740c is no message pump: it calls ISPACE.DLL ordinal 7 with 9 and dword DGROUP:0x5a0 through DGROUP:0x3c54, the WaveMix pump, only when the active window's task (GetActiveWindow, GetWindowTask twice) is the game's, and makes no other call. [BYTES seg16:0x2edc-0x2eed seg15:0x36c3-0x370e seg16:0x11c6 seg16:0x145c-0x1465 seg16:0x1b17-0x1b4e seg16:0x123b-0x1258 seg16:0x1831-0x184e seg16:0x1968-0x1985 seg16:0x1a29-0x1a46 seg15:0x4ce7 seg16:0x1894-0x18ad seg16:0x1906-0x191d seg15:0xef3-0xf18 seg10:0x7419-0x7441]

#### The descent (seg15:0x374c)
Phase 1's seg15:0x3590 calls seg15:0x374c(1) once and seg15:0x374c(0) on each later step. In state 6 (DGROUP:0x90f4 = 6) it lowers the landing ship to the pad: [BYTES seg15:0x36b9 seg15:0x36bc seg15:0x36cc seg15:0x36cf]
1. **First call.** It sets the end y DGROUP:0x92d6 = pad top DGROUP:0x3cb2 − s4 + pad height DGROUP:0x3c9a − 10 and the start y DGROUP:0x92d8 = DGROUP:0x92d6 − 300, s4 being that of [Phase 2](#phase-2), then the position DGROUP:0x17fa = 10·DGROUP:0x92d8 and DGROUP:0x17fc = DGROUP:0x17f8 = 0, calls seg15:0x3bfa(s4, s4 + 40) and sets the speed DGROUP:0x17f6 = 150. [BYTES seg15:0x385e seg15:0x386d seg15:0x3870 seg15:0x38d7 seg15:0x38f2 seg15:0x38f8]
2. **Fast flag.** While DGROUP:0x928 ≠ 0, the first call (before the position store) and every later call set DGROUP:0x92d8 = DGROUP:0x92d6 + 1 and the position DGROUP:0x17fa = 10·DGROUP:0x92d8. [BYTES seg15:0x3876 seg15:0x38d2 seg15:0x3900 seg15:0x3907 seg15:0x390b seg15:0x3911]
3. **The flag DGROUP:0x17fc.** Every call then sets it to 1 when (DGROUP:0x92d6 − 10)·10 < DGROUP:0x17fa, else to 0 when (DGROUP:0x92d6 − 60)·10 < DGROUP:0x17fa, else to 1 when (DGROUP:0x92d8 + 60)·10 < DGROUP:0x17fa, and else leaves it, all signed 16-bit. [BYTES seg15:0x3914 seg15:0x3921 seg15:0x3923 seg15:0x3930 seg15:0x3932 seg15:0x393a seg15:0x3947 seg15:0x3949]
4. **Speed.** Every call lowers DGROUP:0x17f6 by 3 when Calibrate DGROUP:0x8ffc < 1300 (signed), else by 5, clamps it to at least 50 and at most 120, and sets it to 0 when DGROUP:0x928 ≠ 0. The first call's 150 therefore never survives it: the descent starts at 120, or 0 with DGROUP:0x928 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg15:0x3914 seg15:0x394f seg15:0x3955 seg15:0x395a seg15:0x3962 seg15:0x3965 seg15:0x396d seg15:0x3970 seg15:0x3978 seg15:0x397b seg15:0x3982]
5. **Step.** At its end it adds 2·DGROUP:0x17f6 / d to the position DGROUP:0x17fa (signed `idiv`), with d = 5 while DGROUP:0x17fc = 0 and 3 while it is set when DGROUP:0x8ffc < 1300, else 6 and 4. It returns 1, setting DGROUP:0x92d8 = −1 when DGROUP:0x90f4 = 8, once 10·DGROUP:0x92d6 < DGROUP:0x17fa, else 0, and does not clamp the position. [BYTES seg15:0x3b97 seg15:0x3b9f seg15:0x3ba4 seg15:0x3bab seg15:0x3bb0 seg15:0x3bb5 seg15:0x3bc2 seg15:0x3bcd seg15:0x3bd0 seg15:0x3bd4 seg15:0x3bdd seg15:0x3be6 seg15:0x3bec seg15:0x3bf1]
- **Calls.** With the fast flag clear the rule gives 134 calls, the flag's 1 → 0 change (0x20, IGNITE) at call 100, when Calibrate ≥ 1300, and 96 calls with the change at call 70 below 1300, whatever the end y. The traced oracle's negative Calibrate takes the second: core-0008's paced SKY TALON descent moves the position in 96 polls, by +46 … +30 with the flag 0, +48 falling to +33 with it set, +20 after the flag's return to 0 and +33 for the last steps, to 5750. [BYTES seg15:0x3914-0x3949 seg15:0x394f seg15:0x3955 seg15:0x395a seg15:0x3962 seg15:0x3970 seg15:0x3b97-0x3bf1] [ORACLE core-0008]
- **The first call in state 6** also makes the visit's back buffer DGROUP:0x9580 with the pad in it and calls seg16:0x1f78, the visit's screen ([shell.md](shell.md#the-landing-scene), seg16:0x2144). [BYTES seg15:0x379b-0x3859]
- **The drawing**, on every call after the speed step, with s4 that of [Phase 2](#phase-2): [BYTES seg15:0x39b6-0x3b92]
  1. The ship rectangle DGROUP:0x17fe … 0x1804 = (half(W) − half(s4), trunc(DGROUP:0x17fa/10), + s4, + s4 + 40). [BYTES seg15:0x39b6-0x39ee]
  2. PatBlt(DGROUP:0x92b8, 0, 0, s4, s4 + 40, BLACKNESS); the part of the ship rectangle inside the pad rectangle DGROUP:0x3cb0 (IntersectRect) is copied from the pad DC DGROUP:0x92b6 in state 6 and from the scene DC DGROUP:0x929a in state 8. [BYTES seg15:0x39f1-0x3a8f]
  3. **The shadow,** when hgt = trunc(DGROUP:0x17fa/−10) + DGROUP:0x92d6 is below 0x78 or in state 8, at depth ≤ 8 only: BitBlt(DGROUP:0x92b8, 0, half(hgt) + 9, s4, s4, DGROUP:0x3cde, 0, DGROUP:0x3cea, 0x00A803A9) under the brush DGROUP:0x5ea8 (depth < 8) or DGROUP:0x5eaa, the checkerboard over frame 1's silhouette ([ADR 0007](../decisions/0007-palette.md)). [BYTES seg15:0x3a94-0x3b12]
  4. Frame f = DGROUP:0x17fc of seg15:0x1eac's two, with y_f the word at DGROUP:0x3ce8 + 2f: BitBlt(DGROUP:0x92b8, 0, 5, s4, s4, DGROUP:0x3cde, 0, y_f, SRCAND), then the same from DGROUP:0x3cdc with SRCPAINT. Flag 0 draws frame 0, the ship with its flame, and flag 1 frame 1, the ship without ([shell.md](shell.md#the-landing-scene), seg15:0x1eac). [BYTES seg15:0x3b17-0x3b64]
  5. BitBlt(DGROUP:0x13dc, half(W) − half(s4), trunc(DGROUP:0x17fa/10), s4, s4 + 40, DGROUP:0x92b8, 0, 0, SRCCOPY). [BYTES seg15:0x3b69-0x3b92]
  - So the ship comes in with its flame, glides without it, fires it again 60 px above the pad with 0x20 on the 1 → 0 change and shuts it for the last 10 px. core-0008 sees the drawn image 124 × 130 px while the flag is 0 and 124 × 110 while it is 1. [ORACLE core-0008]
- **Sub-steps.** seg15:0x3590's sub-step DGROUP:0x92a2 is 0 during the incoming jet ([shell.md](shell.md#the-landing-scene), the descent's set-up) and 1 during the descent: while dword DGROUP:0x17f2 = 0 each frame calls seg15:0x374c(0), and the call that returns non-zero is followed by sound 9 and DGROUP:0x17f2 = DGROUP:0x1e50 + 1000. With DGROUP:0x17f2 ≠ 0 the frame sets DGROUP:0x92a2 = 2 only once DGROUP:0x17f2 < DGROUP:0x1e50 (unsigned dword) and otherwise keeps sub-step 1, so the fast flag's test stays off for the second as well. Sub-step 2 raises DGROUP:0x914a in state 6 inside phase 1's call. So if frame N sets DGROUP:0x92a2 = 2, frame N + 1 raises the phase to 2 and phase 2's handler first runs on frame N + 2. [BYTES seg15:0x36c3-0x36ef seg15:0x36f5-0x370a seg15:0x3710-0x3717 seg15:0x373e-0x3742 seg16:0xdb9 seg16:0x1ef0]
  - core-0007 and core-0008 hold the ship on the pad 1.005 – 1.039 s from the descent's last step to DGROUP:0x92a2 = 2 in every visit, Ctrl held or not; in their first visits phase 2 follows 30 and 26 ms later. [ORACLE core-0007] [ORACLE core-0008]

#### The monitor typing (seg16:0x2e3c)
seg16:0x2e3c(init, blank) types the monitor text DGROUP:0x6db8 on the right monitor, whose rectangle seg16:0x27ea sets ([The screen](#the-screen)). L = DGROUP:0x6e18 + DGROUP:0x3d48 and T = DGROUP:0x6e1a + DGROUP:0x3d4a. [BYTES seg16:0x2e3c]
- **Init** (init ≠ 0) calls the CRT flash seg15:0x84b0(DGROUP:0x6e14/2 + L + 3, DGROUP:0x6e16/2 + T + 3), resets the index DGROUP:0x1c5c and the line counter DGROUP:0x1c5e, sets the pen to (L + 8, T + 7) and the deadline dword DGROUP:0x1c60 = now + 500, and returns without drawing. [BYTES seg16:0x2e79 seg16:0x2ea1 seg16:0x2ea9 seg16:0x2eb1 seg16:0x2ebe seg16:0x2ecb seg16:0x2ed9]
- **A call** draws only when DGROUP:0x1c60 ≤ now, sets it to now + 40 and runs at most 4 characters. The 40 ms is the phase's own deadline against the pass sample, checked once per frame, not a rate: core-0007 sees the steps 41 – 47 ms apart, 4 characters each, the first 0.49 s after the line's init. [BYTES seg16:0x2e6e seg16:0x2edc seg16:0x2eed seg16:0x31a5] [ORACLE core-0007]
- **Font.** CreateFont(h − 1, 5, nine zeros, quality 2, pitch 0x22, "Arial") with h = 12 below W 700, 13 below 860 and 14 otherwise; SetBkColor 0, SetBkMode TRANSPARENT and text colour 0x0000FF00. [BYTES seg16:0x2e4b seg16:0x2e5a seg16:0x2f07 seg16:0x2f28 seg16:0x2f54 seg16:0x2f6c]
- **A character.** It clears the previous cursor block with a BLACKNESS PatBlt at (DGROUP:0x1c58 + 2, DGROUP:0x1c5a), TextOuts the character at the pen, moves the pen right by its GetTextExtent width, PATCOPYs a 6 × 10 block with CreateSolidBrush(0x0000FF80) at (pen x + 2, pen y) and plays seg10:0x754a(0x2b, 0, 0, 0). [BYTES seg16:0x305d seg16:0x313d seg16:0x316b seg16:0x318f seg16:0x3197]
- **The end.** At the NUL it resets the pen x, increments DGROUP:0x914a, clears the index and falls into the new-line code. [BYTES seg16:0x2fb8 seg16:0x2fbf seg16:0x2fc3]
- **A new line** scrolls the monitor with ScrollDC(dx 0, dy −1) 12 times and lowers DGROUP:0x1c5a by 12 when (DGROUP:0x6e1a + DGROUP:0x6e16 + DGROUP:0x3d4a) − 13 ≤ pen y, else moves the pen down 12. [BYTES seg16:0x2fcd seg16:0x2fdd seg16:0x301c seg16:0x3032 seg16:0x303b seg16:0x304a]
- **Blank line.** With blank ≠ 0, at index 0 the line counter rises first; at 9 or more the monitor scrolls 12 × 1 px, otherwise the pen moves down 12. [BYTES seg16:0x3078 seg16:0x307f seg16:0x3087 seg16:0x308b seg16:0x3100]
- **Wrap.** Before a space it copies the following word, up to and including the next space, NUL or CR, and counts n bytes with the terminator. It wraps when right − margin < pen x + extent, with right = DGROUP:0x6e18 + DGROUP:0x6e14 + DGROUP:0x3d48 + 3 and margin 22 for n ≤ 5 (a word of at most 4 letters), else 9. The bytes are stored from [bp−0x77] while GetTextExtent measures n bytes from [bp−0x78], so the first byte measured is never written and the terminator is not measured; that byte's value is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg16:0x31b5 seg16:0x3207 seg16:0x3217 seg16:0x323d seg16:0x3243 seg16:0x3250 seg16:0x3257 seg16:0x2f82 seg16:0x2f8d]
- core-0003 hears 0x2b once per character from the second line on and sees the monitor scroll a line at a time once full. [ORACLE core-0003]

#### Phase 2
- The ship is blitted at (W/2 − s4/2, DGROUP:0x92d6 + 2), s4 = 4·(roster byte +0x7a + (roster byte +0x7b − 6)/2) of the player's roster record (signed), SRCAND 0x8800c6 from DC DGROUP:0x3cde and then SRCPAINT 0xee0086 from DC DGROUP:0x3cdc, s4 × s4 from (0, DGROUP:0x3cea). [BYTES seg16:0xdc1 seg16:0xdd4 seg16:0xddb seg16:0xdea seg16:0xe0b seg16:0xe2a seg16:0xe4f]
  - (0, DGROUP:0x3cea) is frame 1 of seg15:0x1eac, the stretch of DGROUP:0x92b4: the ship without its flame, with both wings ([shell.md](shell.md#the-landing-scene)). Phase 2 draws no pad and erases nothing, so the blit lands over the descent's last frame. [BYTES seg16:0xdc1-0xe4f]
  - core-0008's landed SKY TALON and TRANSFORMER have the same pixels as on the desk scene's pad (5184 blue and 1360 green pixels), 3 px higher. [ORACLE core-0008]
- **The strip.** With sh = (word +0x22 of the player's object, far DGROUP:0x1e30 + 0x70·DGROUP:0x9646, << 3) sar byte DGROUP:0x959e, it creates DGROUP:0x953c = CreateCompatibleBitmap(DGROUP:0x13dc, padW, sh + 0x43) when that is 0 and, in a temporary DC with DGROUP:0x920e, PatBlts it black and copies BitBlt(dc, 0, 5, padW, sh + 0x3e, DGROUP:0x13dc, half(W) − half(padW), DGROUP:0x10 − sh − 0x3e, SRCCOPY): the screen's pad and ship, which the pad's downward move later slides ([The pad](#the-pad-seg150x4c76)). [BYTES seg16:0xe54-0xf0e]

#### Phases 4 and 6
- **Phase 4, the shape line.** The mean of systems 0 … 3 when lamp row 3 (DGROUP:0x96fa) ≠ −1, else 0 … 2 (signed idiv), gives string 311 GOOD (> 70), 312 FAIR (> 45), 313 BAD (> 27) or 314 SORRY, formatted with 1183 "YOU'RE IN %S SHAPE, %s" and the pilot; 2021 "YOU'RE DEAD, %s" when DGROUP:0x9e5b = 0; 2020 "WELL DONE, %s" when DGROUP:0x93a6 ≠ 0, a path that never sets the mean, so its stack word [bp−0xa] is uninitialised. Then seg11:0x3fd4(DGROUP:0x6db8, 1) upper-cases it, and it gets ". " (DGROUP:0x9ee) when the mean > 27 and DGROUP:0x9e5b ≠ 0, else "!" (DGROUP:0x1124). [BYTES seg16:0xf86 seg16:0xf8d seg16:0xf94 seg16:0xfad seg16:0xfc3 seg16:0x1014 seg16:0x1022 seg16:0x102a seg16:0x1030 seg16:0x103c seg16:0x1046] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 20] ids 311–314 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 74] id 1183 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 127] ids 2020–2021 [ORACLE core-0003]
- Strings 311 … 315 are also picked from the header word +0x15e at seg10:0xa2be and seg15:0x560a, not from the system words ([formats.md](formats.md#header)). [BYTES seg10:0xa2b9 seg10:0xa2be seg15:0x5605 seg15:0x560a] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 20] ids 311–315
- **Phase 6, the required work,** on the served ship with tank = its design word DGROUP:0x96f0: 1177 REFUELING when tank/5 > `fuel`, else 2516 BASIC REPAIRS when the mean of systems 0 … 2 < 50, else 2517 WING REPAIR when lamp row 3 ≠ −1 and system 3 < 50, else 1177 when tank/2 > `fuel`; then a332(1157 "%S REQUIRED", id) + ". ". With none of them the phase becomes 8 and no line is typed. The comparisons are signed, tank/5 by idiv and tank/2 by a signed halving. [BYTES seg16:0x109b seg16:0x10a1 seg16:0x10a7 seg16:0x10c0 seg16:0x10c7 seg16:0x10e5 seg16:0x10ea seg16:0x1109 seg16:0x110c seg16:0x1037] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 73] id 1157 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 74] id 1177 [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 158] ids 2516–2517

#### The pad (seg15:0x4c76)
- seg15:0x4c76(first, down) moves the pad in state 6. With down ≠ 0 the offset DGROUP:0x181c grows by 2 up to the routine's maximum [bp−0x26], and once it is at the maximum DGROUP:0x914a rises by 1. With down = 0 it shrinks by 2 to a minimum of 5, and at 5 or below it raises DGROUP:0x914a only when DGROUP:0x90f4 ≠ 0. [BYTES seg15:0x5238 seg15:0x5241 seg15:0x524a seg15:0x5258 seg15:0x525d-0x527d seg15:0x52d8]
- seg15:0x4c76(1, 0) with DGROUP:0x90f4 ≠ 0 and the new-body flag DGROUP:0x9563 deletes the three objects DGROUP:0x92b0, 0x92b2 and 0x92b4, rebuilds the ship image with seg15:0x2a08 and busy-waits 2000 ms; this rebuild runs before GetObject(DGROUP:0x92b0) and s4, and s4 + 0x3e is computed on every call, before the first-call test. [BYTES seg15:0x4c84-0x4cea seg15:0x4cec-0x4d1c seg15:0x4dab-0x4db4] [BYTES seg15:0x4c84 seg15:0x4c8b seg15:0x4c91 seg15:0x4c97 seg15:0x4cc0 seg15:0x4cd5 seg15:0x4ce7]
- The downward move never rebuilds the strip, so it slides phase 2's screen capture down; every upward first call rebuilds it from the pad DC and the body image DGROUP:0x92b0, so the ship that comes up is the new body, uncut and without its flame. The blits are in [shell.md](shell.md#game-start-dialogs), item 7, Rise. [BYTES seg15:0x4dbd-0x4dde seg15:0x4de1-0x4f67]
- core-0008's dead ship's visit sinks the pad with the burnt ship in 96 polls of DGROUP:0x181c (2 → 194), shows no pad for 2.0 s and brings it up with the new TRANSFORMER, whole and with both wings, with 0x05 at each start. [ORACLE core-0008]

#### Repairs and refuel
- **Basic repairs (phase 24).** Once dword DGROUP:0x1c48 < now it writes 100 to roster bytes +0x38 … +0x3a of record DGROUP:0x931c, the player's, not of the served ship's record, and re-arms DGROUP:0x1c48 = now + 50; each system k = 0 … 2 below 100 with a lamp row gains 2 with no clamp, so an odd value ends at 101 ([ADR 0014](../decisions/0014-saved-games.md) decision 6). [BYTES seg16:0x1472 seg16:0x147e seg16:0x150d seg16:0x1527 seg16:0x1537 seg16:0x153b seg16:0x1543] [ORACLE core-0003]

#### Phase 64
- The phase runs in one frame. It blacks the client, restores the palette, writes DGROUP:0x90f4 = 9 when DGROUP:0x93a6 ≠ 0, else 1, directly and not through the request DGROUP:0x13de, so the next pass runs the new state's case with no state-change step, calls seg16:0x631c ([shell.md](shell.md#screen-states), State 6) and then clears the fast flag DGROUP:0x928. [BYTES seg16:0x1b87-0x1e5c seg16:0x1bc0 seg16:0x1bc7 seg16:0x1bcf seg16:0x1bd6 seg16:0x1bd9]
- **DGROUP:0x93a6 = 1** (seg16:0x1bdf), a separate exit: seg10:0xa90c (seg16:0x1be6), DGROUP:0x92be = 0, seg16:0x7210(1) and the tail through seg16:0x11e8, with no DGROUP:0x6da8, no free spot, no music, no seg16:0x872 and no seg11:0xafe. [BYTES seg16:0x1bdf seg16:0x1be6-0x1bf8 seg16:0x11e8]
- **Otherwise:** distance 1200, the ship's and A's `state` = 1, and dword DGROUP:0x6da8 = DGROUP:0x1e50 + 3000 from this pass's sample (seg16:0x1c21–0x1c2b), before seg10:0xa90c (seg16:0x1c2f), the free spot (seg16:0x1c59–0x1e44), seg16:0x872 (seg16:0x1e54) and seg11:0xafe (seg16:0x1e57). [BYTES seg16:0x1bfb seg16:0x1c21-0x1c2b seg16:0x1c2f seg16:0x1c59-0x1e44 seg16:0x1e54 seg16:0x1e57]
- core-0007's first visit hears 0x14 and 0x15 at the launch's end and sees phase 64 and state 1 28 and 31 ms later; in its visits `v1` … `v4` the re-entry's 0x27 comes 3.02 – 3.07 s after the 0x14, as core-0003's 3.037 s. [ORACLE core-0007] [ORACLE core-0003]
  - **Free spot.** The rectangle x − 800 … x + 2240, y ± 1200 around a candidate point, starting at A's (x, y), is tested with PtInRect against the `x`, `y` of every record below the in-use count of pools 0 … DGROUP:0x9324 except pool 7 and the carried ship, with no `state` test. A record inside rejects the candidate. For a record outside, the candidate is also rejected when x < 1040, y < 720, x > (DGROUP:0x5a0 − 130)·8 or y > (DGROUP:0x5a2 − 90)·8 (signed 16-bit), so these bounds are tested only when some record lies outside the rectangle. Each retry moves x and then y by (`rand()` % 150 + 100)·8 (seg16:0x1d0a, seg16:0x1d1e), a coordinate past the field edge wrapping to 0. [BYTES seg16:0x1c59 seg16:0x1c9d seg16:0x1caa seg16:0x1cb1 seg16:0x1cbf seg16:0x1cc4 seg16:0x1ccd seg16:0x1cd4 seg16:0x1cdb seg16:0x1ce4 seg16:0x1ce9 seg16:0x1cf2 seg16:0x1d0a seg16:0x1d1e seg16:0x1e37]

- **Template.** ENG_LANG.DLL DIALOG 194 "Ambulance Options (To Buy)" has the listboxes 101 (Options) and 102 (To Be Done), both of style 0x50a10853 (LBS_NOTIFY, LBS_SORT, LBS_OWNERDRAWFIXED, LBS_HASSTRINGS, LBS_EXTENDEDSEL), Add >> 105, Remove 106, Sell.. 195, the edits Total 109, Credit 110 and Remaining 113, and OK. - **Procedure** seg16:0x4104 (INSPACE export 7): WM_DRAWITEM (0x2b) returns 1 via seg16:0x4279 and WM_COMMAND IDOK returns 1 after seg16:0x5b24; WM_INITDIALOG, WM_MEASUREITEM and everything else return 0. A double click (notification HIWORD(lParam) = 2) in list 101 posts PostMessage(hDlg, WM_COMMAND, 105, 0) and in list 102 posts 106. Sell.. plays sound 0x13 and opens DialogBox(DGROUP:0x9212, 378, DGROUP:0x9532, seg16:0x42a2), then runs seg16:0x5820, seg16:0x43f2 and UpdateWindow(list 101). Removing a "BODY" item writes WritePrivateProfileString(DGROUP:0x9690, "MainShip", the served ship's design name DGROUP:0x9742 + 0xad·n, DGROUP:0x96a9). [BYTES seg16:0x4900 seg16:0x499b seg16:0x49b4]
