- **OK** (seg16:0x5b24) busy-waits 1500 ms in the demo, clears the purchase flags, and for each To Be Done line sets the flag of the first suffix it matches (seg16:0x5f0e(a, b) is "a ends with b", with no guard when b is longer): 2516 → DGROUP:0x953e, REFUEL → 0x9540, 1153 " REPAIR" → 0x953f, DOUBLE GUNS → 0x9541, 1970 → 0x957f, 2308 "BODY" → 0x9563. Otherwise byte DGROUP:0x955d + k = 1 for the first k = 0 … 5 whose "POWERUP " + (1973 + k) the line ends with, a flag and not a count, and the weapon loop w = 1 … 26 still runs on the same line, setting DGROUP:0x9542 + w. It clears DGROUP:0x9532 before EndDialog(1). [BYTES seg16:0x5b24 seg16:0x5f0e] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 145] id 2308
- **Sell** (seg16:0x541c) lists, for each weapon w = 1 … 26 with DGROUP:0x9564 + w = 0, the first of all ten player slots holding w, at W(w)+0x34 (DGROUP:0x2f88), times the slot's ammo / 20 when W(w)+0x0a ≠ 0, at least 250, with flag 1; a zero price is skipped. RADAR-GUIDED 20/20 gives 7.5 and 19/20 gives 7.1. [BYTES seg16:0x541c] [ORACLE wave-0001]
  - Sell OK (seg16:0x5db0) takes, for each To Be Sold line, the first w whose name the line ends with and sets DGROUP:0x9564 + w = 1. It then deletes the Options items ending with that name, but its loop keeps the count read before deleting and steps its index after each LB_DELETESTRING, so an item that slides into a deleted slot is not tested and of two adjacent matches the second stays. The loop reuses [bp−6], so no further weapon is tested for that line. [BYTES seg16:0x5db0 seg16:0x5e10-0x5e6a]
- **The body chooser,** ISPACE.DLL ordinal 10 command 6 with the template "New Ship Body" ([shell.md](shell.md#game-start-dialogs)), ends on OK through ordinal 28 (ISPACE.DLL seg10:0xf9c, which calls seg10:0x2862(hwnd, 1) at seg10:0x1005): with ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1aa ≠ 0 a ship other than "TRANSFORMER" (lstrcmp of ISPACE.DLL DGROUP:0x1554) is refused with MessageBox 2326 + 2327; otherwise it writes WritePrivateProfileString("Inner Space", "MainShip", DGROUP:0x1554, "INSPACE.INI") and EndDialog(hwnd, 1). Its effect on the visit is that INI value. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg10:0x2862 seg10:0x1005] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 146] ids 2326–2327
- The manual: only items you can afford can be bought, except basic repairs and refueling. [MANUAL Resource Management]

### Purchases (seg16:0x3784)
seg16:0x3784(ship, mode) applies the flags of the shop. Its callers are seg16:0x872 (mode 0), the set-up seg14:0x0 (mode 1, [The ship between waves](#the-ship-between-waves)), seg13:0xb0dd, seg18:0x684 and seg18:0x84e. All credit arithmetic is on the dword +0xdc with each helper's 16-bit result sign-extended. [BYTES seg16:0x3784 seg14:0xd32]
1. Mode 0 first moves `resources` into `credit`. It calls seg16:0x3b18(ship, mode), the computer shop, when the ship is not the player or the mode is not 0, and saves the credit when the mode is not 0 or the ship is the player. [BYTES seg16:0x3792-0x37a8 seg16:0x37ae seg16:0x37b4 seg16:0x37b6 seg16:0x37c1 seg16:0x37c7 seg16:0x37cd seg16:0x37da]
2. DGROUP:0x9541 → seg16:0x3ece; DGROUP:0x957f → seg16:0x3f88; each set byte DGROUP:0x955d + k → seg16:0x4030(ship, k). [BYTES seg16:0x3784]
3. The basic repairs seg16:0x3468(ship, k), k = 0 … 2, run when DGROUP:0x953e ≠ 0, or when the mode is not 0 and DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0. The wing repair seg16:0x3468(ship, 3) always runs in a mode ≠ 0, and in mode 0 with DGROUP:0x953f when the ship is the player or its credit > 100. [BYTES seg16:0x3867 seg16:0x386e seg16:0x3874 seg16:0x3879 seg16:0x38a4-0x38d2 seg16:0x38ab seg16:0x38c6]
4. The new-body path (seg16:0x38e8), reached only with DGROUP:0x953f = 0 and mode 0, needs DGROUP:0x9563 ≠ 0: it sets +0xac = 0 and calls seg16:0x3468 for k = 0 … 3 without subtracting their costs. [BYTES seg16:0x38e8 seg16:0x38f4 seg16:0x3905]
5. For w = 1 … 26: a sold w removes the first slot below +0xcb holding it, shifting the later slots down, zeroing slot 9 (+0xc1, +0xb7) and lowering +0xcb by 1. A bought w (not sold) already owned is reloaded by seg16:0x3730(ship, slot); one not owned is added by [seg12:0x6d64](#loading-a-weapon-seg120x6d64)(ship, w) and costs the sign-extended W(w)+0x34, whether or not a slot was added. [BYTES seg16:0x391c seg16:0x3944 seg16:0x3948 seg16:0x394c seg16:0x3974 seg16:0x39b7 seg16:0x39bc seg16:0x39e2 seg16:0x39ee seg16:0x39f4] core-0006's visits sell the ship's missiles first and append the bought weapons in weapon order: the SKY TALON's slot 1 holds DUMB MISSILES (11) with count 20 at the re-entry, and the TRANSFORMER's slots read [0, 10, 11] with counts 20 and +0xcb 3. [ORACLE core-0006]
6. The refuel seg16:0x3e80 runs with DGROUP:0x9540 or in any mode ≠ 0. Then the saved credit is restored, so the helpers' costs do not reach the player, who pays at phase 58, and mode 1 costs nothing; a computer ship in mode 0 pays each helper's cost. [BYTES seg16:0x3a1d seg16:0x3a39 seg16:0x3a3f seg16:0x3a52]
7. It calls seg13:0x33d6(ship) and zeroes `thrusting` (+0x02) and object word +0x26. When DGROUP:0x90f4 = 1 and the ship is the player, in any mode, it posts WM_PAINT (0x0f) to DGROUP:0xa7e and DGROUP:0xa7c, each only when non-zero. [BYTES seg16:0x3a81 seg16:0x3a88 seg16:0x3a90 seg16:0x3aa3 seg16:0x3abb]
8. It clears every purchase flag and ends with the dword write DGROUP:0x9e5b = 1, which also zeroes the Sell total DGROUP:0x9e5d. [BYTES seg16:0x3b07]

- **The helpers:**

  | Helper | Writes | Returns | Evidence |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | seg16:0x3ece(ship), DOUBLE GUNS | nothing when +0x0a = 2; else +0x0a = +0x24 = 2, +0xe0 = 1 unless (+0xac = 0 and lamp row 3, design word +0x28, = 3), +0xe1 likewise with +0xad; with both wings 0 and lamp row 3 = 3 also +0x24 = 1 and +0xe0 = 1. It makes no call, so no muzzle table is rebuilt | 0 or 250 | [BYTES seg16:0x3ede seg16:0x3ef2 seg16:0x3ef6 seg16:0x3efa seg16:0x3f06 seg16:0x3f12 seg16:0x3f1e seg16:0x3f41 seg16:0x3f4d seg16:0x3f57 seg16:0x3f5e seg16:0x3f6b seg16:0x3f72 seg16:0x3f78 seg16:0x3f7d] |
  | seg16:0x3f88(ship), UNIFY | acts on the player DGROUP:0x9646, not on its argument: removes the first occurrence of weapons 24 and 25 from slots 1 … +0xcb − 1, lowers +0xcb by 2 even when neither is found, and loads weapon 26 with seg12:0x6d64 | 250 | [BYTES seg16:0x3f95 seg16:0x3fd7 seg16:0x4016 seg16:0x401b seg16:0x4021] |
  | seg16:0x4030(ship, k), POWERUP | roster byte +0x32 + k += 1 before the roster index is tested for −1, so index −1 writes at DGROUP:0x6e24 − 0x7c + 0x32 + k; with an index it recomputes `turn` (+0x14) = seg5:0x110(1200·DGROUP:0x96f2, ((roster +0x33 + 10)·10 (16-bit imul) · `thrust` +0x0c)/100), 32-bit | (b + 1)·100 for k > 1, (b + 3)·100 otherwise, b the new byte | [BYTES seg16:0x405e seg16:0x4065 seg16:0x4069 seg16:0x406e seg16:0x407e seg16:0x4081 seg16:0x4086 seg16:0x4094 seg16:0x4099 seg16:0x40a2 seg16:0x40a8 seg16:0x40ad] |
  | seg16:0x3730(ship, slot), reload | nothing when the slot's ammo +0xae + slot is 0xff; else the slot's ammo = 20 and slot 0's ammo +0xae = 0xff | 0, or ((20 − ammo)·10 / 10)·10 | [BYTES seg16:0x3746-0x374f seg16:0x375b seg16:0x3760 seg16:0x3766 seg16:0x376b seg16:0x3779] |
  | seg16:0x3e80(ship), refuel | q = (tank − `fuel`)/7 first, the ship's own design tank DGROUP:0x96f0; `fuel` = tank only when DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0 | (q/10)·10 | [BYTES seg16:0x3e99 seg16:0x3e9f seg16:0x3ea9 seg16:0x3eae seg16:0x3eb5 seg16:0x3ec0 seg16:0x3ec2] |
  | seg16:0x3468(ship, k), repair | below | ((100 − old system k)/10)·10 | [BYTES seg16:0x3468] |

- **seg16:0x3468(ship, k).** It returns 0 at once when DGROUP:0x9563 = 0 and design row k = −1, unless k = 3 with DGROUP:0x3a38 + 2·ship = 0, which continues while the ship has no sheet DC. Otherwise: for k = 0 with system 0 < 40 (signed) the rating DGROUP:0x5078 becomes rating·(system 0 + 200)/300 (32-bit signed); system k = 100 only when DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0; byte +0xec (`alive`) = 1; for k = 1 dword +0x8c = 2, the gun interval. [BYTES seg16:0x34a7 seg16:0x34b7 seg16:0x34c3-0x34e0 seg16:0x34f8 seg16:0x34ff seg16:0x350a seg16:0x3513 seg16:0x3520]
  - For k = 3 with DGROUP:0x9563, the new body, it reads INI MainShip (GetPrivateProfileString(DGROUP:0x9690, "MainShip", "EAGLE", buf, 20, DGROUP:0x96a9)) for the player, or takes the design name DGROUP:0x9742 + 0xad·n for another ship, and calls seg16:0x5f78 with the index of the first of the DGROUP:0x95aa SHP records (0x41 bytes at far DGROUP:0x6e20, name at +0x0a) that matches, or DGROUP:0x95aa when none does, bracketed by seg14:0x3eda and seg14:0x4074. [BYTES seg16:0x3547 seg16:0x355e seg16:0x3574 seg16:0x35bd seg16:0x35cd]
  - **The wing repair,** k = 3 with DGROUP:0x9563 = 0 (seg16:0x35db): ship word +0x20 = object `size` − 2, +0x22 = 1, object `mass` +0x44 = design +0x32, byte +0xac = 0 with both wing bytes saved, then seg14:0x11a8(ship), whose skip test then fails, so the sheet is rendered again with both wings; object +0x3a takes the new DC DGROUP:0x3a38[ship]; with DGROUP:0x92e4 ≠ 0 the saved wing bytes are put back, and with +0xd6 ≥ 0 the wing bytes go to roster bytes +0x72/+0x73. In a restored game seg14:0x0 cuts the lost wings again after the flame strip ([The ship between waves](#the-ship-between-waves)). The wing bytes at that re-cut (seg14:0xc84-0xcce) are the ones seg16:0x3468(i, 3) saved at seg16:0x3611/0x3619 and put back at seg16:0x3650-0x365a: seg14:0x1430's copies of roster +0x72/+0x73, made in seg14:0x0's first loop, or 1 when ship DGROUP:0x9646's +0xe4 is 0. In the set-up's seg16:0x3784(i, 1), seg16:0x3b18(i, 1) first clears DGROUP:0x9563, so the call reaches the wing-repair path seg16:0x35db and not the new-body path. [BYTES seg16:0x35db-0x3696 seg16:0x3611-0x3621 seg16:0x362b seg14:0x1209-0x1225 seg14:0xb3e seg14:0xb73 seg14:0x1935-0x1970 seg16:0x3611-0x365a seg16:0x37bc seg16:0x3b26-0x3b2e seg16:0x3b86-0x3b8c seg16:0x38a4-0x38d2 seg16:0x3547] core-0006 sees both wing bytes back 3.0 s after the visit's OK, words +0x20/+0x22 going 19/7 → 31/1 at the re-entry, and the sprite drawn with both wings in the next wave. [ORACLE core-0006]
  - Every path past the early return reaches seg16:0x3529, which sets `barrels` +0x24 = +0x0a; +0xe0 = 1 when +0xac ≠ 0, else 0 when design word +0x28 = 3; +0xe1 likewise from +0xad; and +0xe0 = 1 with +0x24 = 1 when +0x0a = 1 or (+0xac = +0xad = 0 and design +0x28 = 3). So +0x0a is the ship's gun count. [BYTES seg16:0x351b seg16:0x3529-0x353f seg16:0x3547 seg16:0x35d8 seg16:0x3668 seg16:0x3696 seg16:0x3699-0x36ab seg16:0x36b0-0x36d5 seg16:0x36df-0x370b]
- **Five powerups.** A new game's roster record comes from GlobalAlloc(0x42, 0x7c), zero-filled, and seg14:0x0 writes no byte of +0x30 … +0x37, so the powerup bytes +0x32 … +0x37 start at 0. Their only other writers are seg16:0x4065 and the cheat keys seg11:0x72fa and seg11:0x7496, which write 6 into +0x32 and +0x33 of record DGROUP:0x931c (and the saved-game reader's whole-record copy). An entry is listed while b < 5, so each powerup can be bought five times, against the manual's four stages. [BYTES seg14:0x252 seg14:0x254 seg14:0x256 seg14:0x258 seg14:0x2b0-0x3f3 seg11:0x72f1 seg11:0x72fa seg11:0x748d seg11:0x7496 seg16:0x4065] [MANUAL Resource Management]
- **The computer shop** seg16:0x3b18(ship, mode) shops for a computer ship: with its budget B (a signed dword, the credit) above 100 it buys double guns when DGROUP:0x91f8 > 2 and +0x0a < 2, then draws r = `rand()` % 3 once; r = 0 with B ≥ 700 (signed 32-bit) makes ten weapon tries of `rand()` % 20 + 1, skipping a try when DGROUP:0x11aa = 0 and W(w)+0x36 ≠ −1, and its owned-weapon reload test reads the ammo of slot i, the try index, not of the slot found; r = 1 with B ≥ 400 and a roster index ≥ 0 makes three powerup tries of `rand()` % 6. Its wing test reads design word DGROUP:0x96fc and system word +0x2e (index 4), and the ship's credit (> 250), not the budget, with +0xac = 0 or +0xad = 0. The rest of the routine is an analyst's reading (work/analysis/m4/s3-visit.md §4.4). [BYTES seg16:0x3c60 seg16:0x3c8f seg16:0x3c97 seg16:0x3c9c seg16:0x3ca9 seg16:0x3d03 seg16:0x3d08 seg16:0x3d0b seg16:0x3d4a seg16:0x3d52 seg16:0x3d5d seg16:0x3d7f seg16:0x3d84 seg16:0x3d93 seg16:0x3da2 seg16:0x3da7 seg16:0x3e2e seg16:0x3e4c seg16:0x3e6e] [OPEN]

### Loading a weapon (seg12:0x6d64)
seg12:0x6d64(s, w) (cdecl: ship record s at [bp+6], weapon w at [bp+8]) puts weapon w into a new slot of ship s. [BYTES seg12:0x6d72 seg12:0x6de9]
1. It searches slots l = 1 while sext8(+0xcb) > l (signed) and returns with no write when one holds weapon byte w (sign-extended): an owned weapon is neither added nor reloaded, and slot 0 is never searched. It also returns when the signed +0xcb > 9, ten slots full. [BYTES seg12:0x6d72 seg12:0x6d81 seg12:0x6d86 seg12:0x6d89 seg12:0x6d90 seg12:0x6d95 seg12:0x6d9a seg12:0x6da1 seg12:0x6da6]
2. Otherwise it adds 1 to byte +0xcb and, with l = sext8(+0xcb) − 1, writes weapon byte +0xb8 + l = lo8(w) and count byte +0xae + l = 0x14 (20). [BYTES seg12:0x6dad seg12:0x6db1 seg12:0x6db6 seg12:0x6dbf seg12:0x6dc6]
3. Its only call is the [image slot](#selection-and-the-weapon-display) seg10:0x533a(the new weapon byte, sign-extended), made when W(w)+0x0a ≠ 0. It plays nothing, posts no repaint and leaves `weapon_slot` +0x18, so the new slot shows on the weapon display only after the caller's repaint, a Tab or a wave start. [BYTES seg12:0x6dcb seg12:0x6dcf seg12:0x6dd4 seg12:0x6dd6 seg12:0x6ddc]
- **Callers:** the kind-1 pod's hand-over seg13:0x1e4e ([Other touches](#other-touches)), which then writes the pod's count into byte +0xad + (+0xcb), the new slot's count, or slot 9's when no slot was added; the purchase seg16:0x39e2 ([Purchases](#purchases-seg160x3784), step 5); UNIFY seg16:0x4021; the placement seg14:0x1764 (weapon 13 when DGROUP:0x104e ≠ 0); seg7:0xed3 and seg11:0x9f26, which reload the player's weapons below 0x16; the cheat keys seg11:0x7362, seg11:0x7370 and seg11:0x737e; and seg18:0x5251. [BYTES seg13:0x1e4e seg13:0x1e56-0x1e68 seg16:0x39e2 seg16:0x4021 seg14:0x1764 seg7:0xed3 seg11:0x9f26 seg11:0x7362 seg11:0x7370 seg11:0x737e seg18:0x5251]

### The ship between waves
- **Set-up.** seg14:0x0 calls seg16:0x3784(i, 1) for ship i only when its local flag [bp−0x54 + 2i] ≠ 0, DGROUP:0x91f8 = 1, or its own argument ≠ −1; in later waves it clears the player's flag only when DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0, and wave 1 sets it to 1. So in mode 1 the basic repairs run when DGROUP:0x953e ≠ 0 or DGROUP:0x92e4 = 0, the wing repair and the refuel run always, pending shop flags are applied, and the restored credit makes it free. [BYTES seg14:0xd1a seg14:0xd1e seg14:0xd20 seg14:0xd25 seg14:0xd27 seg14:0xd2b seg14:0xd32 seg14:0x425 seg14:0x453 seg14:0x458 seg14:0x45f seg16:0x37bc seg16:0x3914-0x3a13]
- **Roster to ship.** seg14:0x1430 copies the roster record into the ship at every placement ([formats.md](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)): `systems` from +0x38 … +0x3b, `fuel` from +0x1c ([The words](#the-words)), the weapons, the wings, `barrels`, the gun count, `credit`, `resources` and +0x82. It writes no ship field +0x18, +0xe0 or +0xe1. [BYTES seg14:0x1775-0x179b seg14:0x1430-0x1b82]
- **Ship to roster.** The wave end seg9:0x2828 and the save step seg6:0x99b6 write the ship back ([formats.md](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)). [BYTES seg9:0x2b5d-0x2e59 seg6:0x99d7-0x9cda]
- **Weapon picture.** seg11:0xae94 sets the player's `weapon_slot` (+0x18) = 1 when +0xcb > 1, else 0, and does nothing when DGROUP:0x92e4 ≠ 0; the placement calls it only when DGROUP:0x91f8 = 1 or DGROUP:0x92e4 ≠ 0 ([world.md](world.md#placement-seg90x6624) step 7). [BYTES seg11:0xae9e seg11:0xaea3 seg11:0xaeab seg11:0xaeb2 seg11:0xaec0 seg9:0x6951 seg9:0x6956 seg9:0x6958 seg9:0x695e]
- **After a restore.** In the first wave after a restore (DGROUP:0x92e4 ≠ 0) seg14:0x0 calls seg8:0x2cd8(i, 0) for a ship with +0xe4 ≠ 0, design word +0x28 = 3 and +0xac = 0, and seg8:0x2cd8(i, 1) likewise for +0xad = 0, so a lost wing stays lost ([Wings torn off](#wings-torn-off-seg80x2cd8)). [BYTES seg14:0xc84 seg14:0xc89 seg14:0xc90 seg14:0xc9d seg14:0xca4 seg14:0xcb0 seg14:0xcbd seg14:0xcc9]
- core-0001 and core-0003 show the carry-over: systems, credit, ammo and score cross the wave end unchanged, `fuel` as a multiple of 40. [ORACLE core-0001] [ORACLE core-0003]

### Credit and resources
- `credit` is ship dword +0xdc (DGROUP:0x716c), `resources` word +0xce (DGROUP:0x715e), captured word +0xd2 (DGROUP:0x7162) and destroyed word +0xd0 (DGROUP:0x7160). [BYTES seg16:0x11f8 seg13:0x1b0e seg13:0x1b12 seg7:0x5b39]
- Resources come from captures ([Capturing an icon](#capturing-an-icon)) and move to credit at visit phase 13 and in seg16:0x3784. [BYTES seg13:0x1b0e seg16:0x11f2 seg16:0x3784]
- The bill of phase 58 has no floor: core-0003's second visit takes `credit` from 40 to −30 (dword 0xffffffe2) with no refusal. [BYTES seg16:0x1aef] [ORACLE core-0003]
- At a wave end the roster keeps `credit` / 10 after capping the ship's dword at 0x4fff6 in place ([formats.md](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)), and the next placement multiplies by 10, so the last digit is lost. [BYTES seg6:0x9b61 seg14:0x1997]
- **Number formats.** seg6:0x1010 is in [Capturing an icon](#capturing-an-icon). seg6:0xf54(v, d, n, dest) is its 32-bit twin: v is a dword, d a word sign-extended to 32 bits; for v < 0 it writes "-" (DGROUP:0x48d) and negates in 32 bits, then prints the 32-bit idiv quotient, "." (DGROUP:0x48f) and the remainder through seg6:0x10ce cut at index n. seg6:0x1010 uses its own "-" (DGROUP:0x491) and "." (DGROUP:0x493) and a 16-bit `neg`. [BYTES seg6:0xf6a seg6:0xf73 seg6:0xf7b seg6:0xf7e seg6:0xf82 seg6:0xfa2 seg6:0xfa8 seg6:0xfc1 seg6:0xfd7 seg6:0xfec seg6:0xff5 seg6:0x1010]
- The briefing's ship page shows the player's credit with seg6:0xf54(·, 100, 2) and resources with seg6:0x1010(·, 100, 2). [BYTES seg17:0x33fd seg17:0x3417 seg17:0x3433]

## Messages (seg3:0x6ec)
- seg3:0x6ec(recipient, pool, rec, code) shows a message only when the recipient is the player; it also returns for sender pool 14 = the player, for message 0xb7 from a ship heading to pool 12, and for senders other than −1, 14, 7 and 2. [BYTES seg3:0x6fa seg3:0x6fd seg3:0x700 seg3:0x704 seg3:0x70d seg3:0x711 seg3:0x72b seg3:0x7d6]
- Sender pool −1 loads string 2213 "NO MESSAGE" with an end time now + 3000. [BYTES seg3:0x77d] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 139] id 2213
- For senders 14, 7 and 2 the name comes from seg3:0x1542(0, pool, rec, DGROUP:0x8740, 0), and the display switches to message mode with seg7:0x119c(3, 0) unless DGROUP:0xa76 = 3. [BYTES seg3:0x75e seg3:0x765 seg3:0x769 seg3:0x7da seg3:0x7e7]
- **End time** DGROUP:0x870c: now + 7000 outside a race (DGROUP:0x94e = 0); in a race now + 2500 for pool 7 and now + 5000 for pools 14 and 2. The cases of the switch may override it (case 0 sets 9000). [BYTES seg3:0x7c6 seg3:0x7f3 seg3:0x7fa seg3:0x800 seg3:0x807 seg3:0x80c seg3:0x813 seg3:0x818 seg3:0x81f seg3:0x825 seg3:0x845]
- seg10:0xa332 in mode 2 splits DGROUP:0x8db0 into the lines DGROUP:0x8768 and DGROUP:0x87b8 with seg3:0x3498, and seg3:0x14d6 upper-cases both when W > 635: "GOOD ICON CAPTURE! / 1.0 RESOURCE GAINED.". [BYTES seg10:0xa332 seg10:0xa831 seg10:0xa843 seg3:0x14d6 seg3:0x14e8 seg3:0x14f6 seg3:0x1524] [ORACLE wave-0001]
  - core-0002 shows that message from "Ambulance" from the first screenshot after the capture until 7.0–7.2 s after it, and then the target display again. [ORACLE core-0002]
- **Ship-credited codes** of a [destroyed target](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2) 0x75, 0x76 and 0x77 play sound 0x3b through seg10:0x744c. Code 0x75 formats string 0x581 (1409 "Not Bad! Lets Get Every Last Virus!") and code 0x76 string 0x582 (1410 "Right On!!! You're Quite A Pilot!"). Code 0x77 formats 0x583 (1411 "He's Gone! You Don't Know How Much I Appreciate That!") when the player's goal pool (ship word +0x32) is 14, and 0x584 (1412 "Thanks For Helping Me With That. It Makes Life Easier.") otherwise. [BYTES seg3:0x1346 seg3:0x11d0 seg3:0x11da seg3:0x11e0 seg3:0x11ea seg3:0x11f0 seg3:0x11fe seg3:0x1207 seg3:0x120f] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 89] ids 1409–1412
- **Capture codes.** 0x55 is in [Capturing an icon](#capturing-an-icon), 0x49 in [The rivals](#the-rivals-seg130x3008), 0x5b and 0x5e in [Shot hit test](#shot-hit-test-seg80x3688-and-seg80x6f3c). Code 0x56 (case 85) formats string 680 "Resource Pack Captured! %s Resource Gained." in mode 2 with seg6:0x1010(DGROUP:0x9604, 100, 2). [BYTES seg3:0xe59 seg3:0xe62 seg3:0xe66 seg3:0xe75] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 43] id 680
- **The command line.** String 478 "Capturing %s" has one use, seg3:0x2e15 in the ship activity text seg3:0x1d0a(a, ship, mode), which formats it (seg10:0xa332) with seg3:0x1542(1, goal pool, goal rec, buf, 2) of the ship's goal words +0x32/+0x34 when the goal object's `capturable` ≠ 0, or when it is 0 and the ship is the player with `PlayMode` < 2 (signed). seg3:0x31d0(mode, line 1, line 2) copies line 1 into DGROUP:0x8630 for mode 0 and line 1 + DGROUP:0x13d4 + line 2 for mode 1. seg13:0x2f30(player) calls seg3:0x1d0a(0x64, player, `PlayMode` ≠ 0 ? 1 : 0) and sets DGROUP:0x58c = 1 when DGROUP:0x8630 changed. [BYTES seg3:0x2dde seg3:0x2de5 seg3:0x2def seg3:0x2df4 seg3:0x2e08 seg3:0x2e15 seg3:0x2e1e seg3:0x3313 seg3:0x331a seg3:0x32e4 seg3:0x32ec seg3:0x32f1 seg3:0x32ff seg3:0x330c seg13:0x2f90 seg13:0x2f98 seg13:0x2f9f seg13:0x2fa7 seg13:0x2fb6 seg13:0x2fc8 seg13:0x2fd1] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 30] id 478
  - The frame calls seg7:0x1f38 when DGROUP:0x58c ≠ 0, DGROUP:0x951a = 0 and dword DGROUP:0x5bc < now (unsigned). seg7:0x1f38 draws only while `PlayMode` = 1, clears DGROUP:0x58c, and writes string 2345 "Cmd Mode: " + DGROUP:0x8630 with ExtTextOut(ETO_CLIPPED) at (DGROUP:0x1d28 + 10, DGROUP:0x1d2a) in a 14 × 9 Arial font, colour 0x02FFFF00, OPAQUE on black, after a BLACKNESS PatBlt of (DGROUP:0x1d28 + 10, DGROUP:0x1d2a, DGROUP:0x1d2c − 10, DGROUP:0x1d2e), the [message line](world.md#clip-region-and-message-line). So "Capturing …" is the Strategy-mode command line, not an IS_INFO text. [BYTES seg7:0x7e1 seg7:0x7e8 seg7:0x7ef seg7:0x7f3 seg7:0x7f8 seg7:0x7fb seg7:0x1f45 seg7:0x1f4e seg7:0x1f5d seg7:0x1f71 seg7:0x1f8e seg7:0x1fb3 seg7:0x1fe2 seg7:0x1ff8 seg7:0x2006 seg7:0x2032] [BYTES ENG_LANG.DLL STRING 147] id 2345
  - What seg3:0x1d0a requires before seg3:0x2cfb, where the "Capturing" branch starts, is [OPEN].

## rand() sites
In the order they run within a frame and per event; the generator is in [rng.md](rng.md). [DESIGN]
1. **The damage drop.** The ship mover draws `rand()` % (2·+0x2c + 1) for a cloaked ship (seg13:0x449) or a shielded one (seg13:0x47b) with system 3 below 50, on the passes its gate admits ([The damage drop](#the-damage-drop)). [BYTES seg13:0x449 seg13:0x47b]
2. **Rock mover.** The cold-rock `rand()` % 7 ([world.md](world.md#rocks-seg70x3bae)), before its collision test. [BYTES seg7:0x3c54]
3. **Steering a missile.** seg12:0x7c0: two draws on every call, x (seg12:0x8ad) then y (seg12:0x8c6), so two per locked missile per frame ([The steer](#the-steer-seg120x7c0)). [BYTES seg12:0x8ad seg12:0x8c6]
4. **A volatile tank.** seg12:0xae4 draws `rand()` % 120 for each on-screen wobbling tank outside a special time of day, and `rand()` % 20 only when the first test passes ([The fuel tank](#the-fuel-tank-pool-4-seg120xae4)). [BYTES seg12:0xd62 seg12:0xd81]
5. **View shake.** seg8:0x0 while DGROUP:0x238c ≠ 0: y position % H, x position % W, x jitter % 3, y jitter % 3. [BYTES seg8:0x25b seg8:0x266 seg8:0x289 seg8:0x29a]
6. **A ship's death.** seg16:0x8e4: for entries 1 … 7, x then y % 60, each only with a free effect slot. [BYTES seg16:0x95d seg16:0x96e seg16:0x991 seg16:0x9a4]
7. **An explosion.** seg12:0x274a per fragment with a free slot: % 110; for j > `Detail`/2 + 2, % 10 and, when that is ≤ 6, % 4; for j > 0, x then y. [BYTES seg12:0x28ec seg12:0x292d seg12:0x2938 seg12:0x293d seg12:0x29a0 seg12:0x29f6]
8. **A tethered target.** seg8:0x5df2 draws one whenever the target's `owner_pool` ≠ −1 and the amount is not −1 ([Hit dispatcher](#hit-dispatcher-seg80x5df2), rule 5). [BYTES seg8:0x5f4e seg8:0x5f9a]
9. **A launch with W(w)−2 = −1.** One draw at seg11:0x80c5, and a second at seg6:0x8569 only when the particle table has a free slot ([Launch: the weapon branches](#launch-the-weapon-branches)). [BYTES seg11:0x80c5 seg6:0x8569]
10. **The wave end.** seg9:0x2828 draws at most one ([world.md](world.md#difficulty-inputs)). [BYTES seg9:0x2b25 seg9:0x2b3d seg9:0x2b4d]
11. **The debriefing page.** seg9:0x6fee draws one per dead ship, not of team 7, whose killer is itself ([The debriefing](#the-debriefing), step 6). [BYTES seg9:0x81c4]
12. **The visit.** Two per free-spot retry at phase 64, x then y. [BYTES seg16:0x1d0a seg16:0x1d1e]
13. **The computer shop.** seg16:0x3b18 with a budget above 100 draws `rand()` % 3 once, then ten `rand()` % 20 + 1 (r = 0, budget ≥ 700) or three `rand()` % 6 (r = 1, budget ≥ 400, a roster index) ([Purchases](#purchases-seg160x3784)). [BYTES seg16:0x3c8f seg16:0x3d52 seg16:0x3da2]
14. **A new roster record.** seg14:0xdd6 draws word +0x02 = `rand()` % ((DGROUP:0x9334 + 1)·10) + 10 and then word +0x04 = `rand()` % ((DGROUP:0x9334 + 1)·50) + 150 ([formats.md](formats.md#ship-roster-records-0x7c-bytes)). [BYTES seg14:0x101b seg14:0x106c seg14:0x1071-0x1083 seg14:0x1087-0x109f]
15. **A gun kill's message.** Messages 0x5b and 0x5e draw one each for `%R` at seg10:0xa7c4 when they reach the player ([Shot hit test](#shot-hit-test-seg80x3688-and-seg80x6f3c)), and so does message 0x70 of the 800 award ([The vanish](#the-vanish-seg70x13b2-and-seg70x158e)). [BYTES seg10:0xa406 seg10:0xa7c4 seg3:0x1089]
16. **Effects.** Frame 1 of every type-6 and type-7 effect entry draws 25 `rand()` in step 7 ([world.md](world.md#effect-frame-seg80x3fbe)); every smoke start draws up to four and every melt step four or five ([rng.md](rng.md#callers-and-range-mapping)). [BYTES seg7:0x3730 seg8:0x9998 seg7:0x5b68 seg7:0x4da7-0x4e0d]
17. **Stage 7 weapons**, in the frame's order: the pool-10 mover draws two per in-flight hook, % 128 for blue then % 128 for red, after the smoke and before the pods ([The tether weapons](#the-tether-weapons-seg110x947a)); the pool-6 mover draws FIRE DEMONS' `rand()` % 3 once per qualifying pass (age ≥ 13, age & 3 = 0), after the steer's two of the same pass ([Pool-6 mover: the weapon branches](#pool-6-mover-the-weapon-branches)); every lightning bolt calls srand(GetCurrentTime()) before its point draws, then the bolt's draws, and a virus source draws its strike amount after them, so every bolt reseeds the wave's generator; the visibility pass's `rand()` % 9 gates the discharges ([Lightning](#lightning-seg110x9fdc-seg110xa234-seg110xa9ea)); a WILDCARD conversion with no live fruit draws one `rand()` % 3 per fruit rebuild ([Wildcard defense](#wildcard-defense-seg110x9078)); the virus mover draws `rand()` % 100 for pools other than 0 and 6 per target. [BYTES seg12:0x3265 seg12:0x3273 seg12:0x64f4 seg11:0xa4b5-0xa4bb seg11:0xa37d seg8:0x4ffb-0x5008 seg11:0x91ac-0x91c8 seg12:0x55f5-0x5604]
- The player's capture path draws no INSPACE `rand()`; capture sound 0x66 draws from ISPACE.DLL's own. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg9:0xd0e seg3:0x668]
- ISPACE.DLL ordinal 29 draws none. [BYTES ISPACE.DLL seg2:0x3c]
- The player's gun in Action mode draws none: it skips seg11:0x5ab6 ([The gun](#the-gun-seg110x4e0a)). [BYTES seg11:0x4ec5]
- Draws reachable through callees and not yet placed: a computer ship's seg11:0x4e0a → seg11:0x5ab6 → seg11:0x63b4, and seg8:0x6f3c → seg13:0x2714, seg4:0x7636, seg13:0xb6f2 and seg18:0x3f02. Whether they run on each shot or hit is [OPEN]. [BYTES seg11:0x5ab6 seg11:0x63b4 seg8:0x6f3c seg13:0x2714]
- The GAME OVER particle bursts of state 5 (seg6:0x5f7a) and its state-9 entry (seg6:0x5d58) draw too; their sites are an analyst's reading only (work/analysis/m4/s3-save.md §5). [BYTES seg6:0x5f7a seg6:0x5d58] [OPEN]
